# Release Notes

## build 90256 — 2026-10-27

### Features

- **A second operator's TV web app has a navigation tree** (TASK-177) — A new navigation tree for the operator's TV web app: 21 nodes and 21 edges, one node per screen of the app's navigation (home, live TV, replay, VOD, radio, the settings page and the deeper screens each of them opens), every node carrying one language-free URL verification and every edge a forward **and** a back action. The app is browsable as a guest, so the tree needs no account at all. The app's own route table was read out of its JavaScript bundles, which is what made the deeper screens reachable, and the playbook — the cookie overlay that has to be dismissed before the navigation is clickable, the nav items that are clickable while the links inside them are not, the two redirects the app does, the settings page that blocks the browser for half a minute after it opens, and the two live channels whose players never stop the browser from loading — is in the internal operator notes. The tree carries the `settings` and `info` nodes every tree needs, and the software version under test (`2.10.7+0-g49134c0b2`) is read from the settings page: the `info` node waits for the **version row itself** (`"Version de l'application"`), the edge into it opens the collapsed section and scrolls that row into view, and the value is read by a script over the page text. The Android mobile and Android TV trees are still blocked: the mobile app wants the operator's account (the key names are in the owner's machine only, on no host of the lab) and no Android TV device of the lab is usable. In plain words: the operator's TV app on the web can now be walked end to end by the platform · TASK-177 · area: testing

### Improvements

- **The server picker reads the platform server list, and Settings edits it (TASK-179)** — the servers in the picker are no longer a build-time `VITE_SLAVE_SERVER_URL` in the frontend `.env`: the web app keeps `VITE_SERVER_URL` as its one start address and, once you are signed in, reads the other servers from the platform server list (`GET /server/servers`), enabled ones in the table's order, and refreshes its hosts as soon as the list changes. Settings → Frontend has a Servers card (name, URL, order, enabled; add and remove) that a platform admin saves into the list; everyone else sees it read-only, and a server that is not in the list shows the server's warning there. The last list is kept in the browser so the picker is there at the first paint after a reload. In plain words: add or switch off a server in Settings and every user's picker follows, with no rebuild · [TASK-179](../tasks/TASK-179-atlas-multi-server.md) · area: platform
- **Atlas follows the server picker (TASK-179)** — with "All servers" picked, Atlas answers for the whole platform: "list the hosts" or "how many devices" gives one row per server (a Server line on each host) and the platform total, and names any server it could not ask (for example `gcloud-standalone: 401`). With one server picked it answers for that server only. An action on a device goes to the server that owns it, with your own login (the lock names you, not a service account): take control, press a key, get the screenshot, release. If two servers have a host of the same name, Atlas asks which one. The chat sends your login for that one message and the server keeps it in memory only; Atlas and the MCP tools take an optional `server` argument. In plain words: pick All servers and Atlas sees and drives every server's devices as you · [TASK-179](../tasks/TASK-179-atlas-multi-server.md) · area: ai

### Bug fixes

- **Atlas no longer says a device does not exist when the chat sent no device list** — Atlas's quick first step checks a device against the list of devices the chat page sends. A caller that sends no list (the chat script, an API client) had every device answered with "I don't see any device on this server", even one on another server that Atlas could reach. With no list there is nothing to check against, so the request now goes on to Atlas, which finds the server that owns the device. In plain words: "release device1 on lenovo-local-low" works from a script too · [BUG-0969](../bugs/BUG-0969-2026-10-27-atlas-said-a-device-did-not-exist-when-the-chat-sent-no-device-list.md)
- **The Pokémon runner walks through the gate house of Route 15 on its way to Fuchsia City, instead of walking back and forth over the border with Route 14** — the runner was asked to go from Route 15 to Fuchsia City, but the road to Fuchsia passes through the gate house across Route 15 (its two doors are on the route itself, and the route is cut in two without them). The map of the world only learns a place when the runner has been inside it, and the gate house had never been visited: the runner's map knew the door led to an unknown place, so it knew no way to Fuchsia. The guide then walked the runner back to the waypoint it came from — Route 14 — whose own next waypoint is Route 15 again, and the two swapped the runner for ever: no milestone, no new map and no new square for 5 min of game time until the stall detector wrote the run down (2026-10-27, `fuchsia`, 2261 walks over the same two squares). The places a milestone's stretch passes through are the way on when the next waypoint cannot be reached yet, so the gate house of Route 15 joins the ones of Route 12: the runner goes through it, learns its doors and walks on to Fuchsia. In plain words: the runner keeps walking south to Fuchsia instead of pacing at the top of Route 14 · [BUG-0968](../bugs/BUG-0968-2026-10-27-the-runner-walked-back-to-route-14-instead-of-through-the-gate-house-of-route-15.md)
- **A navigation tree write answered with an empty 500 when the platform could not check whether the tree was a prod tree** — the platform's own guard that keeps an edit off a prod tree (it re-aims the write at the dev tree the prod one was published from) reads the tree from the database. When the database could not be reached, the guard fell over instead of answering: the request died with a status code and an empty body, so the save looked like a crash with no cause, and the route it was aimed at never ran. The guard now asks the database only when there is one, and when the lookup itself fails the write is refused with a message the caller can read — the rule "an edit never lands on a prod tree" still holds. In plain words: a tree save that could not be checked now says why, instead of failing blank · [BUG-0967](../bugs/BUG-0967-2026-10-27-a-navigation-tree-write-answered-with-an-empty-500-when-the-platform-could-not-check-whether-the-tree-was-a-prod-tree.md)
- **The Pokémon runner fires the door it stands on instead of walking between its two halves** — the player came through the elevator's door of the Rocket Hideout and stood on it, and the world graph knew no way out of the corridor he was in (the elevator is the only door of that part, and the elevator's cabin leads back to the floor it came from). The runner's way out was "the door next to me" — the other half of the same two-tile door — and moving between the two halves of a door never triggers a warp, so it walked (24, 19) and (25, 19) back and forth for 5 minutes until the stall detector wrote the run down (2026-10-27, `poke_flute`, no progress in 5 min). The door under the player's feet is now fired instead: the press goes into the wall next of it, which is what makes the Rocket elevator open. The elevator also no longer takes the floor it came from — the floor the map knows a way out of is taken, so the runner rides up to B2F and walks out through the Game Corner. In plain words: the runner rides the elevator out of the hideout instead of pacing in front of it · [BUG-0966](../bugs/BUG-0966-2026-10-27-the-runner-walked-between-the-two-halves-of-the-elevators-door.md)
- **The Pokémon runner walks out of a room it cannot leave** — a door is not part of the walkable squares of a map (stepping on it takes the player to another room), so the square the player stands on and the door can end up in two different parts of the map, and the map then looks like a place with no way out. The runner answered "no route" on every tick and stood still — 607 ticks in a row, on the one square next to the only door of the house on Route 5 — until the stall detector wrote the run down (2026-10-26, `vermilion`, same spot for 5 min). The door next to the player is now used: the runner steps on it and is out. In plain words: the runner leaves the room instead of standing in it · [BUG-0965](../bugs/BUG-0965-2026-10-26-the-runner-stood-still-in-the-house-of-route-5-next-to-the-only-door.md)
- **The Pokémon runner goes through Cerulean's trashed house on its way to Vermilion, instead of walking back and forth over the border with Route 24** — the runner was asked to go from Cerulean City to Route 5, but Cerulean's south exit lies in another part of the city (the trashed house's two doors are the only way between the parts, and stepping on them warps the player into the house), so the world graph knew no way to Route 5. The guide then walked the runner back to the waypoint it came from — Route 24 — whose own next waypoint is Cerulean again, and the two swapped the runner for ever: no milestone, no new map and no new square for 5 min of game time until the stall detector wrote the run down (2026-10-26, `vermilion`, 29 walks over the same border). The maps a milestone lists as its stretch are the way on when the next waypoint cannot be reached yet, so they now win over the walk back: the runner explores the trashed house and takes the path under Route 5 to the underground path. In plain words: the runner keeps walking south to Vermilion instead of pacing at the top of Route 24 · [BUG-0963](../bugs/BUG-0963-2026-10-26-the-runner-walked-back-to-route-24-instead-of-through-the-trashed-house.md)
- **The Pokémon runner no longer ping-pongs between the two floors of Victory Road** — the last mile's list of places to go sent the runner from Victory Road 2F up into Victory Road 3F, a floor whose four exits all lead back down to 2F (measured on the live save). The runner solved the puzzle of 2F, climbed up, could not use the one exit that leads to the way out (a boulder was in the way), fell back down — and was sent up again, for ever, until the stall detector wrote the run down (2026-10-26, `hall_of_fame`, no progress in 5 min). The upper floors of Victory Road are a dead end on the way to the Elite Four (the way is Route 23's north edge), so they are no longer on the list: the runner leaves Victory Road and walks north to Indigo Plateau. In plain words: the runner goes to the Elite Four instead of going up and down a dead end · [BUG-0962](../bugs/BUG-0962-2026-10-26-victory-road-3f-is-a-dead-end-and-the-runner-ping-ponged-between-the-floors.md)
- **The Pokémon runner no longer ping-pongs in a gym** — the leader of a gym was looked for again on every single tick, and who is on screen depends on where the player stands: in the Cerulean gym the trainer at (8, 7) was on screen, the runner walked one square down to reach him, the trainer went off screen, the leader at the top of the room became the candidate again, and the runner walked back up — two squares, for ever, until the stall detector wrote the run down (2026-10-26, `cascade_badge`, no progress in 5 min, 220 walks and nothing else). The candidate is now remembered with the map it was seen in: it only changes when the person is gone, judged not to be the leader, out of reach, or when the runner is in another gym. In plain words: the runner walks up to the gym leader instead of walking up and down in front of him · [BUG-0957](../bugs/BUG-0957-2026-10-26-the-gym-candidate-changed-under-the-runners-feet.md)
- **Atlas and Masterchief can tell which device streams are down** — a stuck or stopped capture stream (the device tile that stays on "loading") was logged by the host but no agent could read it, so "is any device stream loading?" got screenshots and guesses. The new `get_stream_status` tool lists every stream's live state, failing ones first with since when; Atlas picks it for stream, loading or tile questions and Masterchief has it too. The answer is a short table (device, host, problem, for how long); a Tello, which streams only while paired, is listed as idle and never counted as a failure. Each host restart also used to open the same failure again and never close the old one (1 464 incidents left open); a failing stream now has exactly one open incident, closed when it recovers. In plain words: ask "is any stream down?" and get the list, not a guess · [BUG-0960](../bugs/BUG-0960-2026-10-26-stream-failures-logged-but-unseen-and-duplicated.md)
- **A changed device profile shows without clearing the browser** — when a host changed a device's monitoring profile (the Tello on host-clone-1 from PC to Drone), a browser that had opened the Device page before kept the old badge, even after a reload. The page kept the cached copy of each host's stats over the new one. A host's fresh values now win. In plain words: change a device's profile and the badge follows on the next reload · [BUG-0956](../bugs/BUG-0956-2026-10-26-device-profile-badge-stuck-on-cached-value.md)
- **Victory Road 2F's boulder plan is the published one** — the plan of the floor named three boulders the floor does not have ((10, 12), (12, 8), (8, 6)), so the runner never found a rock of the plan, gave the floor up and the Pokémon lane ping-ponged between the two floors of Victory Road (the player goes up and down) until the stall detector wrote it down. The plan is now the walkthrough's, measured on the live save: the boulder to the south of the landing square — the one at (4, 14) — is pushed southwestward (←1, ↓2, ←2) onto the switch at (1, 16), which lowers the barrier of the floor, and the run crosses to Victory Road 3F instead of standing still. In plain words: the runner solves the last puzzle of Victory Road instead of shoving a rock into a dead end · [BUG-0955](../bugs/BUG-0955-2026-10-26-the-plan-of-victory-road-2f-named-boulders-the-floor-does-not-have.md)
- **Apple TV on an IRTrans box works with a named remote, and any captured box can use the power API** — every model with power control (Apple TV, Android tablet, Fire TV Vega included) now accepts a Tapo plug, the client power API or Wake-on-LAN, and the `.env` picks (`DEVICE<N>_POWER_API_URL` → power API). **patch-2** brings this to older client builds together with the named-remote keymaps for Apple TV, Fire TV and Samsung (`irtrans_conf/`), which builds before 2026-09-28 lack, so a device with `DEVICE<N>_IR_REMOTE` set answered every key with "Command failed"; its `--rollback` now also removes the files it added. In plain words: rerun patch-2 on the client and the Apple TV takes keys and power · [BUG-0953](../bugs/BUG-0953-2026-10-26-irtrans-named-apple-tv-and-power-api-on-older-builds.md)
- **A VNC tile no longer borrows another server's password** — in All-servers mode, two servers can each have a host with the same name (host-clone-1 on node1 and on QualiAI). The Device page cached each host's VNC password by name only, so one tile could connect with the other machine's password; TigerVNC refused it and then blocked every tile of that host. The password is now cached per server and host. In plain words: same-named desktops on two servers each get their own password · [BUG-0952](../bugs/BUG-0952-2026-10-26-vnc-password-cache-shared-across-servers.md)
- **An IRTrans key that fails now says why** — a key the IRTrans box refuses (unknown remote or command), a key missing from the keymap, or a remote whose keymap did not load used to show only "Command failed" in Take Control; the host now returns the reason, including the box's own `RESULT` line. In plain words: when an IR key does not go out, the console tells you what the box answered · [BUG-0951](../bugs/BUG-0951-2026-10-26-irtrans-refused-key-shows-command-failed.md)

- **A signed-out server offers its login again, instead of showing a padlock and nothing to do** — the mobile app's server picker opened the login dialog only when the picked server *changed*: MUI fires `onChange` only on a real change, so on a fresh install — where the one configured server is already the selected one — nothing ever fired, and the case that most needed the prompt was the case it never reached (2026-09-17, `ServerSelector.tsx`). The picker showed the server with a padlock and "No servers connected", with no way to sign in. The dialog now opens whenever the selected server needs an auth, and cancelling it stays cancelled until that server is picked again. In plain words: a fresh mobile install can sign in again · [BUG-0147](../bugs/BUG-0147-2026-09-17-signed-out-server-never-offers-a-login.md)
### Security

- **Image decoding moved to Pillow 13, which closes a critical JPEG decoder overflow** — the backend server and hosts capped Pillow below 13, so the dependency scanner kept one critical (GHSA-8g5g-8vmr-9782, a JPEG decoder buffer overflow), six high and two medium advisories open on TIFF, PDF, GIF and JPEG decoding; Pillow 13.0.0 is the first release with the fixes. The server now pins `Pillow==13.0.0` (installed on the running servers) and the host manifest pins it exactly as well, so the host deploys install it on the hosts too (BUG-0961). None of the APIs Pillow 13 removed is used, and every server and host runs Python 3.11. The other alerts on the public snapshot (PyJWT, certifi, compression, brace-expansion) were already fixed in build 90145. `datasets` (training only, never imported by the platform) stays below 5.0.1 because unsloth still requires `<5.0.0`. In plain words: the image library the platform uses to read screenshots and captures is now the patched one · [BUG-0954](../bugs/BUG-0954-2026-10-26-pillow-12-image-decoder-advisories.md)

### Internal

- **The operator-tree inventory now reads each tree's rule state from the platform** — every tree is checked for the `settings` node and the `info` node the rule asks for, the inventory prints what the `info` node actually waits for and whether any string in the tree mentions a version, and a tree that was published without them is visible instead of silent. The trees that do not meet the rule are fixed with the sessions that own them and stay tracked in the bug. In plain words: "is this tree finished?" is answered by the platform instead of by memory · [BUG-0964](../bugs/BUG-0964-2026-10-26-the-software-version-under-test-cannot-be-read-from-the-operator-trees.md) · area: platform
- **The platform has one server list in the database (TASK-179)** — a new platform-level table `platform_servers` (name = `SERVER_NAME`, public origin, enabled, order) with `GET /server/servers` (any signed-in user; also reports this server's name, whether it is in the list, and a warning when `SERVER_NAME` is unset or missing from the list) and `PUT /server/servers` (platform admin; replaces the list). Each server caches the list 60 s. A database without the table answers an empty list with a warning. Migration `20261026a` is written, not applied; the frontend reads it since P2
- **`scope=all` on the hosts and locks routes (TASK-179)** — `GET /server/system/getAllHosts?scope=all` and `GET /server/control/lockedDevices?scope=all` ask every other server of the list in parallel (4 s budget) with the caller's own `Authorization` / `X-Auto-Sign` (never the service key; a service-key-only call gets 403) and return one merged answer: each host or lock carries `server`, hosts get an absolute `host_url`, and `servers` lists every server with `ok`, `error` (`timeout`, `unreachable`, `401`, ...) and counts, failed ones included. Peers are called with `scope=local` so nothing loops. Built, not deployed; Atlas uses it in P4
- **A host deploy now installs the Python packages the host manifest declares** — a host deploy shipped the code, restarted the service and reported success, but never touched the venv: the packages in a host venv were frozen at install time, so a fixed dependency never reached the host service. The dependency step the server got now runs for the host service too, each against its own manifest (`backend_server/requirements.txt` for a server deploy, `backend_host/requirements.txt` for a host deploy), so a host deploy installs the host's pins and never the server's. Only exact pins are installed, as before — which is why the host manifest's Pillow became an exact pin: a range is precisely what a deploy cannot act on. One lab host has a narrower sudoers rule that refuses the venv's user, so the install falls back to root there and puts the venv ownership back instead of failing the deploy. In plain words: when a dependency is fixed, the hosts get it on the next deploy instead of staying on whatever they were built with · [BUG-0961](../bugs/BUG-0961-2026-10-26-a-host-deploy-shipped-the-code-but-never-installed-the-python-packages.md)
- **IRTrans remotes written, not learned** — an IRTrans `.rem` file is a protocol description plus each key's data bits, so a remote whose codes are known can be generated instead of learned in the box. `scripts/ir/gen_irtrans_rem.py <type>` decodes `ir_conf/<type>.json` (NEC, Samsung32) and writes `irtrans_rem/<type>.rem` with nominal timings; `apple_tv.rem` (checked on an IRTrans, 2026-10-26), `fire_tv.rem` and the EOS STB remote `eos.rem` (Nokia32, the box's own export) are in the repo, with an identity map for `fire_tv`. The format, the decoding of each protocol and the Shield gap (FLIRC files hold hashes, not codes) are in `docs/agent/devices/IRTRANS_REM.md`.
- **Operator trees renamed `<platform>-<operator>` (TASK-176)** — the generic tree names (`stb_tv`, `stb_android_tv`, `stb_android_mobile`) and the first web tree are renamed `stb-<operator>`, `androidtv-<operator>`, `androidmobile-<operator>` and `web-<operator>` in the interface list (dev and prod), the knowledge base, scripts, tests, Grafana dashboards, docs and skills. Dataset scope is matched by the `stb-` / `web-` prefix, `fold_temp_trees.py` takes `--canonical NAME`, the human screen-group merges live in an internal data file, and the localize scripts read `VPT_LOCALIZE_UI`. Older reports keep the names they were written with.

### Upgrade

**Compared with** `main-2026.10.26-90145` → `main-2026.10.27-90256` (338 files changed).

**Database** — apply these once per database:
- `setup/db/migrations/20261026a_platform_servers.sql`

_Do **not** apply:_ `20261026a_platform_servers_rollback.sql` — they undo a migration above.

**Settings** — no new key.

**Dependencies** — no new package.

**Services** — restart by hand (the deploy restarts the rest itself): `vpt-discard-incidents`, `vpt-discard-scripts`, `vpt-heatmap`, `vpt-emulator-fifo@`, `vpt-procon`, `vpt-timeline`

**Grafana** — `device-information-get-info.json`, `kpi-measurement.json`

## build 90145 — 2026-10-26

### Features

- **Atlas can remember what you asked it before (TASK-172)** — an opt-in memory, one markdown file per user per day (`<ATLAS_MEMORY_DIR>/<team>/<user>/<YYYY-MM-DD>.md`, outside the repo by default), so the second time you ask the same thing Atlas answers from what it did the first time instead of re-deriving it. Only a turn that actually did something is remembered (at least one tool call that worked), with the tools that ran, the artefacts they produced and the answer; the write is done by the code, so remembering costs no tokens. A recall is dated and says it is a recollection; saying "again", or asking the same thing again in the same session, runs it for real. Device and host commands, live-state questions ("is stb3 free?") and script runs are never answered from the memory, the FAQ keeps priority, and a reworded question is a miss rather than a wrong hit. Files are kept 30 days at most. **On demand**: it is off for you until you turn it on — the book icon in the AI agent page's header, or the "Atlas memory" card in Settings → AI, which also shows how much is kept and has "Clear my memory". Shared logins (auto-sign, API key) get no memory. `ATLAS_MEMORY=off` removes it from a server, `on` makes it everybody's default. Measured on 126 asks (off / recall / context, 3 rounds): on an exact repeat of "create a script / user interface named …", 5 of 5 recalls named the artefact that exists, in 0.06 s with no tokens, against 7.5–29 s and 12–162 k tokens; device commands, look-only questions and "again" were never answered from memory, and a memory dated yesterday is recalled the next day. Only turns that acted or created something are remembered, with the artefacts of the tools that acted. A memory of a script or user interface that was deleted is dropped instead of recalled, and Settings → AI shows how often your memory answered. In plain words: flip the memory switch and Atlas can skip the work it already did for you · [TASK-172](../tasks/TASK-172-atlas-memory-system.md) · [BUG-0911](../bugs/BUG-0911-2026-10-25-atlas-memory-replayed-failures-and-commands.md) · area: ai

- **One script for the whole content surface (TASK-168)** — the new `content_suite` script tests radio, replay, video on demand and recording as four separate cases, on a set-top box, on the TV web app or on the mobile app. One reusable harness runs the same case on whichever platform the device is, and every case prints one machine-readable line (`VPT_CASE_RESULT`) with PASS or FAIL and the reason. Recording is a real three-step case: it records, checks the recording is listed, then plays it back — a recording that is listed but does not play is a failure. A fleet runner (`scripts/agent/vod_radio_dispatch.py`) spreads the suite over the hosts, one whole device per job, claims the lease before touching anything and never forces a busy one. Every failure says whether it is a product defect or an infra problem (lease, deploy, connectivity, geo-block), because mixing the two invalidated an earlier benchmark. An offline check (`scripts/agent/vod_radio_offline_check.py`) proves those rules without a device, so a rule can never drift from what the suite says. In plain words: ask the lab "does radio, replay, movies and recording actually work?" and get one line per case, with the defects separated from the noise. · [TASK-168](../tasks/TASK-168-vod-radio-content-suite.md) · area: testing
- **Showcase web userinterfaces: real trees for nine well-known free websites (TASK-161)** — Wikipedia, Hacker News, Internet Archive, OpenStreetMap, Pluto TV, Twitch (browse), Dailymotion, PBS and YouTube are now navigation trees in the platform (`web-wikipedia`, `web-hackernews`, `web-internetarchive`, `web-openstreetmap`, `web-pluto`, `web-twitch`, `web-dailymotion`, `web-pbs`, plus `youtube-web` extended rather than duplicated). Each node carries a URL-path verification and a screenshot-backed Localize fingerprint, every edge has a forward **and** a back action, and the whole tree walks on a live browser: `test_scripts/web/web_<site>_smoke.py` goes entry → every screen (navigation + its own verifications) → home, two passes in a row. The walk uses the platform's own navigation executor, so a node whose edge works but whose verification does not still fails the run — **five of the nine trees pass that walk twice in a row** (Hacker News, OpenStreetMap, Twitch, Dailymotion, PBS), and the four that do not each fail for a named reason that is written down. Built with Forge and the `build-tree-web` skill on the free `MiniMax-M3.1-Flash-Preview`; the site list and the run recipe live in `scripts/agent/web_showcase.py`, and no account, cookie or credential is involved. In plain words: point a test at a famous website and the platform already knows how to walk it · [TASK-161](../tasks/TASK-161-web-showcase-trees.md) · area: testing

- **Notifications you control (TASK-145)** — Every error you see as a toast is also a notification with a link to the page that shows the cause, and you choose which ones you want. A Notifications page (bell → View all) holds the History (filter by category and severity, open, dismiss) and the Settings: for each category (devices, hosts, scripts, capture, wasted AI tokens, storage, deployments, security, support, action errors) switch the bell and the toast, pick the lowest severity that pops a toast, and mute a category for an hour, a day or a week. The same choices can be set for a project, a workspace or the whole team; a team admin sets the defaults and can lock a row so it cannot be muted. Defaults: scripts reach the bell only, devices and hosts toast on errors, capture, storage and AI spend toast from warning up, and an error from something you just did always shows. A platform problem is no longer a 4-second toast that shows once: it has a Show button (keyboard too), a "Monitoring problem" chip stays beside the "in error" pill while it is open, and the bell keeps your last 200, saved in your account so they follow you to another browser. Every down device is its own notification, and "Mute this device" on a history entry silences just that device for you (Settings lists the muted devices with an Unmute button). The AI budget raises a warning at 80 % and an error at 100 %, and so does a day where 20 % or more of the AI spend (at least $1) went on calls that failed or gave nothing usable. A "host disk 93 %" problem now has a **Fix now** button: it shows how much expired capture data (old captures, thumbnails, chunks, reports) it would delete on that host, asks you to confirm, then reports "Freed 4.2 GB, disk 93 % -> 61 %" and refreshes the dashboard; it refuses while someone else is using the host. **TASK-100 follow-up:** the bell now matches what the platform actually knows. Each `dash-*` entry (every platform-detected problem) is auto-resolved on the next dashboard poll when `detectProblems()` no longer sees it, rendered muted with a check icon and "Fixed 2 min ago", and auto-purged 2 h after the resolution timestamp. Other notifications (script errors, action results) have no condition to prove cleared: they keep the same × and are auto-purged 48 h after they happened. The bell default is "show everything that is not dismissed" — live first, fixed underneath, newest of each on top; a re-toast of a problem that came back clears `resolved_at` automatically. New optional column `public.notifications.resolved_at` (BIGINT, partial index), no other schema change. In plain words: you hear about what you care about, and nothing is lost when you do not want the pop-up · [TASK-142](../tasks/TASK-142-notification-center.md) · [TASK-100](../tasks/TASK-100-notification-resolve-state.md) · [TASK-145](../tasks/TASK-145-notification-preferences.md) · 🗄 DB migrations `20261020d_notification_prefs.sql`, `20261020e_notification_prefs_device_scope.sql`, `20261020f_notifications_history.sql`, `20261021a_notifications_resolve_state.sql` · area: platform

- **Talk to Atlas (TASK-140)** — Ask AI and the Atlas chat input get two new controls next to Send. The mic records one voice message: the bar becomes cancel · waveform · stop · send, where send goes straight to Atlas and stop puts the words in the field so you can fix them first. The Voice pill starts a voice conversation: Atlas keeps listening and each sentence you say becomes a turn ("show me the devices page", "take control of stb1"); a strip under the bar shows what it heard, what it is doing, a Stop button, the Silent / Interactive switch and what to read out loud (answer, thinking, questions). Saying "stop" (or arrête, stopp, annule) cancels the turn and cuts speech at once, and talking over Atlas stops its voice. Typing works exactly as before. This first version uses the browser's own speech recognition and voice (Chrome, Edge, Safari; free); a voice settings button next to the mic sets the voice speed (1.2× by default, 1.5×, 2×) and how a spoken command reaches Atlas: its words, or the audio itself (Voxtral Small or Gemini 3.5 Flash-Lite pick the command and Atlas checks and runs it). Short commands, typed or spoken, now run in about two seconds: Atlas starts every turn with a quick step (one small call to its own model with your quick tools: open a page, take control, goto, screenshot, plus the tools you use most, learned per user and dropped after repeated misses), the action shows in the chat and Atlas remembers it; anything else is the normal turn. Atlas speaks with the MiniMax Trustworth Man voice (English, French, German; browser voice as fallback). Stop, or saying "stop", now also cancels a goto already running on the device: the current step finishes and no further step starts. Every quick step and voice call is in the AI call log and Langfuse (`agent.atlas.quick`, `agent.voice`, `agent.voice.speak`). In plain words: talk to Atlas instead of typing, or keep typing, and simple commands just happen · [TASK-140](../tasks/TASK-140-atlas-continuous-voice.md) · 🗄 DB migration `setup/db/migrations/20261020b_atlas_quick_tool_stats.sql` · area: ai

- **Is this device actually playing sound and video? (TASK-138)** — the new `av_check` script watches a device for 30 seconds and gives two plain answers: **video** (is the picture really moving frame after frame, with no long freeze and no long black screen) and **audio** (is there sound for at least 60 % of the window, measured against the platform's own -50 dB floor). Before it watches, it puts the sound up: it asks the device whether it is muted (a browser player says so, a set-top box shows no mute icon and the report says so instead of guessing), clears a mute it can see, and raises the volume to the maximum — it never presses MUTE, because muting the device to make the check fail would be the script creating the failure it then reports. The report shows both verdicts, the two screenshots with the motion percentage between them, a **table** with the sound level of every second in dB, the silence / freeze / black windows and the two overall percentages; the executive summary stays short, so it can be read at a glance. It runs from the Run page like `goto.py` on a phone, a set-top box, a browser or a live stream, it reuses the platform's existing capture analysis, navigation and verification code instead of writing new detection, and it speaks English, French and German. The runs also have their own Grafana dashboard, **AV Check Results**: the pass rate, the verdict split over time (video / audio), the failure reasons, and a link from every run to its report. In plain words: ask the lab "is this box really playing?" and it answers with the proof. · [TASK-138](../tasks/TASK-138-av-check-script.md) · area: testing

- **Custom pages** — a Pages menu in the top bar holds pages you build from the platform's own blocks (device picker, go to node, Localize result, live stream, recent runs, a saved report, text), by hand with Edit page or by asking Atlas ("make me a page with only my STBs, a node dropdown and the Localize result"). Atlas builds a draft you check and save; later you ask it to add or remove blocks. A page is saved data, never code. Share it with named team members (view or edit) or the whole team: sharing grants no right, each person sees a block with their own permissions and a block they may not use shows as locked, and the share dialog says how many blocks each person would see locked. You can only add blocks you may use yourself; Atlas lists what you can and cannot use and why. New permissions `pages.custom:view` (every role) and `pages.custom:edit` (manager, developer, tester). In plain words: ask for the page you want instead of waiting for someone to build it · [TASK-139](../tasks/TASK-139-custom-pages.md) · 🗄 DB migration `setup/db/migrations/20261020a_custom_pages.sql` · area: platform

- **Fixnet call test script (TASK-71)** — `test_scripts/gw/fixnet_call.py` places a call from one FX voice modem to another through the gateways and checks that the called modem rings, with one report step per phase (arguments, open, prepare, dial, ring, hang up). `--from_fx`, `--to_fx` and `--number` default from `FIXNET_FROM_FX`, `FIXNET_TO_FX` and `FIXNET_NUMBER` in the host `.env` (fx3 and fx4 when unset); The called modem also picks up and the call must reach `CONNECT` — that is the default, no flag needed (`--answer false` is the opt-out for a far end that answers on its own). The modem country code is set to Switzerland on every run, because the modem's US default makes it refuse the line. · [TASK-71](../tasks/TASK-71-fixnet.md) · area: testing

- **A deploy never holds a lease for more than 10 minutes** — `update_core.sh` used to claim its deploy lease for 60 minutes, so a deploy that crashed or hung kept the node (or the Windows box) locked for an hour. It now claims for 10 minutes (real deploys measured: median 0.8 min, longest 6.2 min), counted from the moment the lease is granted, not from when the deploy queued; nothing renews it. If a deploy is still running when the 10 minutes are up, the lease is gone and other agents may start, so the deploy stops before the next server or host step with `!!! DEPLOY LEASE EXPIRED` and exit code 6 instead of restarting hosts without a lease (the step in flight finishes; hosts already done stay deployed). Rerun the deploy to finish the rest. In plain words: a stuck deploy frees the lab after 10 minutes, and it tells you loudly that it stopped · area: platform

- **League of Legends overlay on the stream** — the stream modal of a desktop device has a target button that draws the bot's danger zones (red border), caution zones (amber) and safe zones (green) over the video, plus each input the bot sends: right click as a fading ring, left click as a dot, minimap click on the minimap, keys (Q, W, E, R…) as a badge next to Garen. Live mode polls the bot's `lol-overlay-live.json` every 500 ms through `/host/lol-overlay/live` and says "No live data" when the bot is not running. The `/lol-replay` page replays a recorded run (`zones.jsonl`, `turns.jsonl`, the video and its game-0:00 offset) with health, mana, attack, defense, timer and the bot's decision beside the video. `scripts/lol_overlay_mock.py` writes sample files in the contract format. · area: testing

- **League of Legends: a Garen bot plays a Practice Tool game (TASK-132)** — on `vpt-windows`, `scripts/lol_practice/run_garen_bot.py` starts a Practice Tool game as Garen, plays it with a rule-based bot (walks top lane, last-hits minions, E/Q/R, recalls and buys the next item from the Garen build, never stands still for more than 4 s), records it and writes a scorecard, then leaves the game. All input goes through a guard that refuses unless the game is a running Practice Tool game. First two games scored 23.7 and 36.9. · [TASK-132](../tasks/TASK-132-lol-garen-bot-v0.md) · area: ai

- **League of Legends game scorecard (TASK-131)** — after a game on `vpt-windows`, one command scores it 0-100 per area (lane, farming, deaths, kills, objectives, items, recalls) and overall, with the raw numbers and the rule next to a Garen-top reference built from cited sources (LosingQueue, op.gg, lolalytics, the wiki). It only reads Riot's Live Client Data API and the screen; it never sends input, so a human game and a bot game are scored the same way. The player's lane comes from the white camera box on the minimap (97 % agreement with hand labels on the 37 of 50 real frames where the camera follows the player), a score below the reference floor still ranks games instead of collapsing to 0, every finished game folder gets `lol-scorecard.json` + HTML written next to it automatically, farm uses the exact CS from the HUD counter (the API's `creepScore` only moves in steps of 10), a `health` area scores damage taken per minute and time below half health (no enemy laner exists, so losing health is a mistake), a `cannons` area scores last-hits missed by gold once the recorder has seen minion deaths, the laning phase (first 8 minutes) is the default view with headline numbers at 5:00 and 8:00 (the full game stays as a secondary score), and every game gets a clickable review timeline, a training-data row per last-hit attempt and a reviewer-model note with one rule change to try. · [TASK-131](../tasks/TASK-131-lol-game-scorecard.md) · area: ai
- **League of Legends practice game from one run (TASK-130)** — on `vpt-windows`, `scripts/lol_practice/run_practice.py` waits until the League Client's local API reports an idle client, creates a Practice Tool lobby (queue 3140), adds a full 5v5 of bots, locks a champion and waits for `League of Legends.exe` and the Live Client Data API. Every client call goes through an endpoint allowlist that refuses any queue other than Practice Tool, and the run refuses to start if a game is already running. No credentials are read or typed. · [TASK-130](../tasks/TASK-130-lol-practice-game.md) · area: ai
- **League of Legends phase-0 operator-driven launch chain (TASK-121 waves 0-9, BUG-0610/0622/0615/0616/0617/0618/0619/0620/0621)** — an end-to-end orchestrator on `vpt-windows` that launches the Riot Client, finds its window, clicks the LoL launcher icon in the sidebar, waits for the League Client, and clicks Play — but never fills credentials, picks a champion, or accepts a queue pop. The agent's FR-15 three-layer defense stays intact; this orchestrator runs outside the agent as the operator's smoke driver. Five new modules in `shared/src/lib/game/leagueoflegends/` (drift-aware window translator, `Win32WindowTracker` read-only Win32 queries, `Win32ClickExecutor` SendInput, `NavTargetDetector` with multi-scale `cv2.matchTemplate`, and the `Phase0Runner` orchestrator). 172 unit tests + 2 macOS-skip passing in 4.5 s. AST guard clean — no `pyautogui`. Five production fixes landed in the same merge: (a) the `wt.TRUE` / `wt.FALSE` Win32 enumproc callback fix (BUG-0618; `ctypes.wintypes` does not define those — bare `True` / `False` instead); (b) the Riot Client window title is `"Riot Client"`, not `"Home"` (BUG-0619); (c) `translate_decision_to_screen()` now accepts a `screenshot_extent: Optional[WindowGeometry]` keyword so the operator's full-desktop screenshot denormalizes correctly to desktop pixels instead of being treated as window-relative (BUG-0615); (d) the `NavTargetDetector` default sweep is 9 scale fractions 0.4× → 1.6× (BUG-0621) — single-scale matching failed because the operator's GDI capture is at a different zoom than the template; (e) the `lol_icon.png` template is now a 90×90 RGB crop extracted from a live vpt-windows capture (BUG-0622) — the original 130×168 RGBA crop was at the wrong scale and matched at 0.46. Operational fixes: chain-wide mouse-jiggle keepalive (BUG-0616) so the Riot Client's renderer does not auto-exit between steps; `_detect_and_click` falls back to the wait-step geometry when `query_by_hwnd` is flaky (BUG-0617 — Vanguard's `vgk` driver occasionally blocks `GetWindowRect` for 200 ms); periodic progress logging every 5 s (BUG-0610) so a stuck chain leaves a trail in `phase0-run.log` instead of hanging for 12 hours. On `vpt-windows` the chain now runs green end to end (2026-10-18, run V20): LoL icon clicked, Play clicked on the Riot Client's LoL page, League Client window found after about 25 s; the in-game wait is non-blocking (BUG-0626, BUG-0627). A new `TODO-handover.md` in `docs/tasks/` catalogs the open issues, improvement suggestions, and a 5-step checklist for the next agent. The simulator-only path (`LeagueRunner`, `SimulatorTarget` registry) is unchanged. · [BUG-0610](../bugs/BUG-0610-2026-10-18-phase0-runner-progress-logging.md) · [BUG-0622](../bugs/BUG-0622-2026-10-18-lol-icon-template-clean-crop.md) · [BUG-0615](../bugs/BUG-0615-2026-10-18-translate-decision-screenshot-extent.md) · [BUG-0616](../bugs/BUG-0616-2026-10-18-phase0-chain-wide-keepalive.md) · [BUG-0617](../bugs/BUG-0617-2026-10-18-phase0-detect-click-window-vanish-tolerance.md) · [BUG-0618](../bugs/BUG-0618-2026-10-18-window-tracker-wt-true-fix.md) · [BUG-0619](../bugs/BUG-0619-2026-10-18-riot-client-window-title-not-home.md) · [BUG-0620](../bugs/BUG-0620-2026-10-18-phase0-bbox-screenshot-bad-roi.md) · [BUG-0621](../bugs/BUG-0621-2026-10-18-vision-nav-multi-scale-detector.md) · [BUG-0626](../bugs/BUG-0626-2026-10-18-phase0-lol-icon-play-button-templates-and-order.md) · [BUG-0627](../bugs/BUG-0627-2026-10-18-phase0-window-refind-class-fallback-nonblocking-game.md) · [TASK-121](../tasks/TASK-121-league-of-legends-windows-vision-agent.md) · [Handover](../tasks/TODO-handover.md) · area: ai
- **Live run page** — Opening a running script shows what it is doing now: the current step with a percent bar and a chip per step (done, current, failed, skipped), the device's live screen, a strip of step screenshots you can click, a Steps panel, and a log of every step with All / Steps / Warnings & errors filters, search, follow, wrap, copy and download. The Run Tests live dialog and running rows on Run → Deployments use the same parts, and scheduled deployment runs now stream their steps live too. · TASK-127 · area: testing
- **Integrations page** — One Integrations page lists every connected tool with tiles and tabs. Each tool has a status chip, what it enables, links to create a token and to its docs, and for Jira and TestRail the connection form with Test connection. The Plugins menu opens it first. · TASK-127 · area: integrations
- **Atlas Companion** — Atlas answers in the chat, then offers the real app page already filtered (the reports of one device, a run summary, the open defects) in a panel beside the chat. Click Open companion, or switch Auto-open on to skip the offer. The header Companion toggle shows or hides the panel and pulses when Atlas updates it while hidden. Atlas always knows which page and filters are shown, so a follow-up like "only failed" or "this month" edits the same page, even when Atlas does not query again. Test Reports now read `host`, `device` and `script` from the URL, Incidents and Heatmap read `host` and `device`, and `?embed=1` shows any page without the top bar. A user guide page describes it. · TASK-126 · area: ai
- **Dashboard tabs and cards** — The Dashboard now opens on tabs: Hosts and devices (the previous view, with a Favourites strip and an Only favourites filter), Overview, Scripts, Devices and, for admins, System. Overview opens first, every card's content is centred (single-number tiles both up-down and left-right), links out of the dashboard open in a new tab (your favourites open Device Control filtered), and one ⋮ menu in the server card holds the server and all-hosts actions. Everything sits on one compact, strict grid (about half the height of the first version): Overview has four KPI tiles (pass rate with a sparkline, running now, devices up, failures), then a pass-rate chart with 0-100 % axes, time labels and a hover tooltip, then a merged "Needs attention" list (devices down, hosts and machines in trouble, scripts that keep failing) and your favourites on one line; Scripts groups failing runs per script; Devices shows who is down or locked and a per-host list; System (admins) shows services healthy, server CPU / RAM / disk and a table of every VM; each card shows one answer in a fixed-height tile with very few words (lists page three rows at a time), every number and row opens the page that answers the follow-up, and a time range (24 h, 7 d, 30 d) drives the results cards. Star a tab to make it the one that opens first, switch the cards of a tab on or off from the gear (reset to default), and start a full-screen wall display that rotates the tabs every 30 s. A problem (device down, inodes at 80 %, a monitoring host with trouble, a failure spike) shows one toast per session instead of moving the page. The Dashboard is view-only. · TASK-123 · area: platform
- **vpt-windows host_vnc desktop audio HLS pipeline (BUG-0597)** — Windows host_vnc devices now play audio alongside the noVNC iframe. Five layers, all wired together · area: device-control
  - `backend_host/scripts/capture_audio_stereo_mix.py` reads raw PCM from the Realtek *Mixage stéréo* / *Stereo Mix* device (Win10/11 hides it from `dshow` but `sounddevice` (PortAudio via WDM-KS) sees it; hint-aware with `Mixage_stereo` / `stereo_mix` short-circuits and a `Stereo-Mix` / `What-U-Hear` scan-fallback covers other localizations).
  - `backend_host/scripts/run_audio_stereo_mix.ps1` owns the (capture, ffmpeg) pair with a pidfile lock, 5 s cooldown, in-place ffmpeg restart (BUG-0592 pattern); `-re` is the critical flag — without it ffmpeg drains the rolling PCM at 217x speed and exits at EOF, restarting every 2 s with no segments produced.
  - Scheduled Task `vpt-windows-audio` (`schtasks /create /sc onlogon /ru SYSTEM`) — `Register-ScheduledTask` from ssh'd sessions fails with `HRESULT 0x80041318`; `schtasks` goes through a separate code path that works.
  - Backend: `buildAudioStreamUrl` in `shared/src/lib/utils/build_url_utils.py` joins `audio_stream_path` from the device dict through the same `/host/<name>/` prefix `buildStreamUrl` uses for the video path. `Device.__init__` and `Device.to_dict()` learned the new `audio_stream_path` field; `controller_manager.py` reads `DEVICE{N}_HOST_VNC_AUDIO_STREAM_PATH` and honours `DEVICE{N}_HOST_VNC_STREAM_PATH` for `device{i}` even when `DEVICE{N}_VIDEO_STREAM_PATH` is unset. `/host/av/getStreamUrl` returns `audio_stream_url` alongside `stream_url`.
  - Frontend: `useStream` cache stores `(videoUrl, audioUrl)`; `<VNCStream>` renders a small `<VNCAudioTrack>` chip in the bottom-right corner bound to the audio HLS through `hls.js@1.6.5`, behind a `<Tooltip>` mute/unmute toggle (`VolumeOff` / `VolumeUp` icons). Muted by default because Chrome blocks unmuted autoplay; a click on the chip un-mutes and the audio plays in sync with the noVNC iframe (both consume the same ffmpeg wall-clock segment timestamps).
  The new Scheduled Task uses PortAudio's WDM-KS backend, which talks to `Audiosrv` in Session 0 — no interactive desktop needed at boot. Same shape as Linux `run_ffmpeg.sh` `pulse -i default` muxing into the device HLS; Windows just splits into two playlists because noVNC already has the video. Smoke test on vpt-windows: `curl /host/av/getStreamUrl?device_id=device1` now returns `audio_stream_url=/host/vpt-windows/stream/device1_audio/segments/output.m3u8` next to `stream_url=/host/vpt-windows/vnc_lite.html`. Direct fetch of the audio m3u8 from `192.168.x.70:6109` returns HTTP 200, growing segments every 2 s · [BUG-0597](../bugs/BUG-0597-2026-10-17-vpt-windows-host-vnc-desktop-audio-hls-pipeline.md)
- **Project page in-scope section (BUG-0606, BUG-0608)** — the new `/server/projects/<id>/in-scope` endpoint finally has a consumer. `ProjectDetail` now renders three groups below the stat tiles: **Devices** grouped by host, **Executables** as a flat peer list of scripts + testcases + virtuals (the v3 mockup's "different concept" fix — they're peers, not nested), and **Campaigns** in a separate full-width band (orchestrators, not executables). Each row carries `X runs · Y% pass` coloured by `≥90 / ≥75 / <75`, plus a `×` that calls `DELETE /server/projects/<id>/link` with optimistic UI. The two stat tiles that overlap with the in-scope data now reflect the in-scope count: `Executables` shows the G11 sum (scripts + testcases + virtuals) with a `X scripts · Y testcases · Z virtual` sub-line; `Devices` shows `in scope · of N total`. The "add device / add script / add campaign" buttons render in the section chrome but their click handlers are no-op stubs (a follow-on routes them to the existing device-tag editor and the existing script / campaign pickers). tsc + i18n + ui-check + eslint green at merge. Second round, add/remove wired: three dialog components land on top of BUG-0606 + BUG-0579. `ProjectTagsEditor` lives in the page header next to the member chips: scope tag (locked) + user tags (× to remove) + a `+` button that opens a Dialog with a comma-separated input, hitting `POST /server/projects/<id>/tags/names`. `AddDeviceDialog` opens a search list of every device NOT already in scope (pulled from the existing `/server/device-flags/batch`); picking one unions the project's tag names into the device's flags via `PUT /server/device-flags/<host>/<device>`. `AddExecutableDialog` shares the same chip-row type filter for script / testcase / virtual_script / campaign; the four kinds each fetch from their own list endpoint and POST `/server/projects/<id>/link` with `{type, id}`. The `×` on a device row no longer a stub — it filters the project's tag_names out of the device's current flags and PUTs the remainder back. Every `+ add` and every `×` is gated on `canEdit` (admin OR project owner). Inline error beside the chip row when the server returns 400 (e.g. trying to remove the scope tag) instead of a silent `console.error`. 28 new i18n keys × 2 locales. tsc + i18n + ui-check + eslint green. Originally filed as BUG-0607 — renumbered to 0608 because a parallel pokemon agent claimed 0607 for the saffron-step fix during the same merge window. **Auto-tag-on-save is now deprecated (BUG-0611)** — `projects_db.auto_tag_with_project` is a no-op; the manual `+ add` is the supported path going forward. · [BUG-0606](../bugs/BUG-0606-2026-10-17-project-detail-in-scope-section.md) · [BUG-0608](../bugs/BUG-0608-2026-10-18-project-page-v2-add-remove-wired.md) · TASK-65 · area: platform
- **All servers** — pick "All servers" in the top-bar scope picker and every list (Dashboard, Settings, Reports, Scripts, Hosts, Projects) fans out to every configured backend. Each row carries a small server chip so you know which server the row came from, and clicking Run / Take Control / Edit / Delete on a row sends the action to that row's home server, not the one you are looking at. The browser is the only client of every server: no inter-server API, no shared DB, no peer discovery, no auth forwarding. The picker uses URL-as-identity (a renamed server is a new server; a hint prompts the user); Atlas stays single-server in v1. The desktop ScopePicker (3-column popover) and the mobile ServerSelector both grow the new "All servers" row. Settings in ALL mode shows a per-server card grid (edit, restart host, get all hosts) instead of a single combined table. The `selectedServer` slot accepts the `__all__` sentinel and rehydrates it across reloads; React Query keys always carry the real `serverId` (never `'*'`); the per-row action dispatch resolves the target from the trusted registry, not from a mutable row field. Five-server PoC against `virtualpytest.angelstreet.io` + `<vpt-pi4>` is green: 39×200, 1×401 (the customer's gcloud standalone has no auto-signed session), 5 distinct server origins hit. · TASK-113 · area: platform
- **Atlas embeds demo videos in chat** — when you ask Atlas "how do I fly a drone" / "show me a tutorial on Grafana" / "is there a video on KPI", the answer now includes a click-to-play YouTube card alongside the prose. The chat renderer turns `[[video:<id>]]` tokens into lite-embed cards (poster + circular play button → iframe on click), accepts 1-2 brackets on each side (defensive against the model dropping a `]`), and is a brand-new `search_videos` MCP tool backed by `docs/videos/videos.json` (tag > title > summary scoring). The tool is a baseline in `assistant.yaml` so it loads in every chat (not just the ones the video-library skill selector picks), and a new `video-library` micro-skill anchors the answer in VirtualPyTest context so the LLM doesn't fall into safety-overdrive on "drone" questions. `docs/videos/videos.json` is bind-mounted into the vpt-server container at `/app/docs`, so the tool can read it at runtime. In plain words: "how do I…" questions in the agent chat now answer with text + a 90-second demo · area: ai
- **AI budget per bucket** — the monthly AI budget is no longer only per team. One budget row can be keyed by `team`, `user`, `model`, `endpoint`, `usage`, or `project`; every row carries a `team_scope` so the tenant-defending team FK is unchanged. The resolver walks the specificity ladder `usage > model > endpoint > user > project > team`; any block bucket over its limit refuses the call; warn buckets report on `Monitoring → AI usage` without blocking. New endpoints: `GET /server/ai/usage/budget?team_id=` returns one card per bucket, `POST /server/ai/usage/budget/evaluate` resolves the buckets a hypothetical call would hit, `PUT /server/ai/budget` accepts `bucket_type + bucket_id + team_scope + label`, `DELETE /server/ai/budget?bucket_type=&bucket_id=` removes one. The Budgets panel on `Monitoring → AI usage` gains an "Add bucket" editor for super admins. Cost comes from `ai_usage_stats` for team buckets and from `ai_calls` (90-day per-call log, gains a `project_id` column from this change) for finer buckets. In plain words: cap Opus on `text.plan` without locking the whole team, isolate a noisy single user, or bound a project's Atlas calls independently · [BUG-0559](../bugs/BUG-0559-2026-10-16-ai-budget-only-per-team-no-finer-buckets.md) · 🗄 DB migration `setup/db/migrations/20261016d_ai_budgets_finer_buckets.sql` · area: ai
- **Monitoring profiles** — Choose per device what is monitored, and see the profile on Device Control and the Dashboard. · TASK-90 · TASK-95 · 🗄 DB migration `20261014c_monitor_status_off.sql` · area: monitoring
- **Caption playback** — Watch a device 150 s behind live with machine captions, translated into English, French or German. · TASK-103 · area: monitoring
- **Localize** — Localize gives one answer (signal, live, screen or unknown), shows its evidence and takes a correction when it is wrong. · TASK-104 · 🗄 DB migration `setup/db/migrations/20261014e_localize_verdicts.sql` · area: ai
- **GitLab** — Connect your own GitLab and browse it like a GitHub repo home, one click from the navbar. · TASK-91 · area: integrations
- **Support tickets** — Open a ticket from inside the app with a type, a severity, a priority and a workflow. · [Docs](../user-guide/support-tickets.md) · TASK-96 · 🗄 DB migration `features/zammad/db/003_priority_severity.sql`, `features/zammad/db/001_support_tickets.sql`, `features/zammad/db/002_ticket_model.sql` · area: integrations
- **Platform update** — One guided page updates an install in seven steps: it checks the machines, backs up the database (made by the database VM) and the settings files, builds the bundle from any source and deploys it. A **Settings** button sets the frontend host, SSH user and key and the database connection without editing `.env`, **Set up DB backup** makes the agent token and shows the one command to run on the database VM, a failing **Sudo rights** check shows the exact root command per machine with a Copy button, **Server key** creates the server's SSH key for the frontend VM and shows the key, the `authorized_keys` line and the one command to run there, **Set up frontend VM** does it for you (type the VM's address, an admin account and its password once: the server creates the deploy account, installs the key and sudo rights, saves the settings and forgets the password), a host that does not answer shows the command that starts it, **Free space** on the Disk check deletes old backups, bundles and kept trees beyond the newest five (after a dry-run list), and every saved `.env` in the backup result has a **Restore** button (the current file is kept, nothing is restarted, the restart command is shown; the database dump shows its restore command). Settings are now saved in the database per server (with a GitHub token field; the file store is used until the migration is applied), and a host that cannot download names the `SERVER_URL` or `API_KEY` that differs from the server with the `.env` line to set. A missing tool on the Tools check shows one ready install line per machine for its own package manager (apt, dnf, apk or pacman; installing needs root, so you copy the line), and an old host that cannot pull yet gets an **Update host once** button (it uses the shared source the host already has) or the manual `git pull` / copy command. A running deploy can be paused, resumed or aborted between machines, and after an abort or a failed deploy the page shows each machine as on the new version, rolled back or untouched with the migrations applied, and offers **Roll back** (hosts, then the frontend VM, then the server last, each from the copy saved before it was updated), **Undo migrations** (the rollback files, newest first, after a second confirmation) or **Leave partial** with a warning; a deploy now refuses to start without the step 1 backup. · [Docs](../user-guide/platform-update.md) · TASK-101 · TASK-99 · TASK-109 · TASK-128 · 🗄 DB migration `20261014a_applied_migrations.sql` · 🗄 DB migration `20261018g_platform_update_settings.sql` · area: platform
- **Projects (first-class concept, TASK-65 G1–G10)** — A project is a tagged scope of executables (scripts / testcases / virtual scripts / campaigns) plus devices. The top-bar `ProjectSelector` writes `localStorage:virtualpytest:project_id`; `/projects` lists projects (create / own / scope the team's work); `/projects/<id>` shows six tiles (Members, Runs 30 d, Devices, Failures, AI tokens 30 d, Active users); `/projects/<id>/executive-summary` is the manager stand-up (Health, Pass rate trend 30 d, Top 5 failures, Last 5 runs, AI tokens 30 d with budget, Members). When a project is active, the project filter applies on Dashboard, Rec, Run Tests, Test Cases, Campaigns, Build Campaigns, QuickTest, Virtual Scripts, Agent chat Test Case + Campaign pickers — every device list and every script list is filtered by the project's tag. "All projects" keeps the current behaviour. G5 (Scope Atlas) wires the project into the Atlas agent: `save_virtual_script` / `save_testcase` / `create_campaign` MCP tools auto-tag new artefacts, `list_*` tools forward `project_id`, and the Atlas system prompt carries a "Current Project" line. Migrations `setup/db/migrations/20261006a_projects.sql`, `20261006b_team_members_last_seen.sql`, `20261006c_report_snapshots_project_ids.sql`, `20261015a_executable_tags_virtual_script_campaign.sql` must be applied BEFORE the server deploy. Each project carries an audit log on every create / update / delete / member add / member remove (grep `[PROJECT_AUDIT]`). · area: platform


### Improvements
- **Edit the prod view of a navigation tree without switching to dev (TASK-173)** — the navigation editor's production view is no longer locked. Move a node or edit an edge there and Save: the server applies the change to the dev draft (the tree the prod version was published from) and leaves prod untouched, then the editor moves to the dev view in place with a note "Saved to the draft (dev). Publish to make it live." The DEV chip reads **DEV · DRAFT** while dev holds changes newer than the last publish; Publish makes them live as before. Running scripts keep using prod until you publish. In plain words: just edit, and Publish when you are happy · [TASK-173](../tasks/TASK-173-nav-editor-edit-prod-as-dev.md) · area: platform

 **Review the ad breaks the meter found, with their pictures** — the ad meter kept only the times of each commercial break, so nobody could check a break by eye. It now keeps a small picture at the start of every break and every 30 s while it lasts (kept 3 days with the break), and the stream modal of an ad-meter device has an **Ad breaks** button: the last 24 h of breaks, newest first, each with its time, length, whether it was *seen before* or is *new*, its pictures, and a **Not an ad** button that takes a mistaken break out of the totals and teaches the meter (the correction existed in the analyzer but nothing could call it). Checked on a loop clip with a known answer (a cartoon with a channel mark, then about 5 min of commercials): one break per pass, about 95 % of the ad time, no false break. The red **AD** sign no longer covers the FPS line under the title, and the demo device is called **Ads loop**. In plain words: you can now see what the meter called an ad, and say when it was wrong · area: monitoring
- **A cleaner egg and car overlay** — four things the owner found confusing are gone or fixed: the yellow "locked to the analysed frame" label next to the FPS line, the "analysed frame / live stream" switch in the bottom-right corner (the analysed frame is now always the picture under a counting overlay, the only one on which the boxes are right by construction), and the purple "…" with "check #2 · item #261" under *Last egg* on a profile that grades nothing. And *Last egg* with its gold outline now shows **the last egg counted**, the one that just crossed the count line, instead of the newest track to be looked at, which was often a stray low-confidence box on bare belt. The demo devices are renamed **Eggs count** and **Cars count**. In plain words: the overlay shows the eggs, the count and the last egg counted, nothing else · area: monitoring
- **The agents split the work, and one-tool asks cost a tenth (TASK-175)** — creating or changing a script, a test case or a virtual script now goes to **Forge**: ask Atlas and the chat hands it over and switches to Forge, before Atlas spends a token ("run the script" stays with Atlas). Lists (test cases, scripts, campaigns, user interfaces), remote keys, screen dumps, alerts and short questions like "which devices are online?" are answered in one small call. A vague two-word ask ("cap 5") gets one clarifying question instead of Atlas guessing and exploring the lab; restarting a service or rebooting gets a button to **Masterchief** (it makes the change behind a confirm card); "why did the last run fail?" gets Atlas's answer and a button to **Sherlock**, which now has its result tools. A device name that is not there is answered with the devices that are. Measured before/after on the same 22 prompts (44 asks): 40 of 44 handled as designed (10 before), 4.35 M → 0.60 M tokens, 202 → 83 model calls, median ask 12 k → 1 k tokens; "create a script" 1.48 M → 9.5 k. In plain words: each agent does its own job, and simple asks are fast and cheap · [TASK-175](../tasks/TASK-175-agent-skills-and-routing.md) · area: ai
- **Counting devices show their circles without a click** — opening the egg line or the car counter used to show the bare video until you pressed Monitoring, although the overlay is what those devices are for. Each host now says which of its devices run a profile that draws its own overlay (a `ui` block: eggs, cars — `monitor_overlay_on` in the host's system stats), and the stream modal opens with monitoring on for them; TV and phone devices are unchanged, and the button still turns it off. In plain words: open the egg line and you see the eggs counted · area: monitoring
- **The car counter boxes the cars it can see, and says what it shows (BUG-0927)** — on the street camera of `Synthetic cars` the detector boxed 7.7 vehicles a frame out of ~150 in view: a 1280x720 frame squeezed into the model's 416x416 input makes every car a third of its size. `coco_detect` can now run on overlapping tiles (`tiles: [2, 2]`): **~57 vehicles boxed a frame**, at the same 2 analyses a second the box (`vpt-lenovo4`, now hosting the device) can keep up with. The headline was "cars in the last hour", a number that changed with the sampling rate and mostly re-counted parked cars; it is now **Vehicles in view**. A real traffic count is out of reach at that rate — a car moves five of its own lengths between two samples — and is written up in the monitor-profile doc. In plain words: the car view now shows the cars, and stops showing a count it cannot back · [BUG-0927](../bugs/BUG-0927-2026-10-26-the-car-counter-saw-almost-none-of-the-cars.md) · area: monitoring
- **AI routing tab: collapse, search and filter** — Settings → AI → Routing now folds the usage tree: a chevron on each parent, Expand all / Collapse all, and Level 1 / Level 2 shortcuts; what you leave open is remembered in your browser. A search box finds a usage by name, key, profile, model or endpoint and opens the parents of every match; the chips Overridden only (team overrides), Has calls (24h) and Used fallback narrow the list. Rows that only inherit their parent's model no longer repeat its fallback chain. In plain words: find the one usage you want to change without scrolling the whole tree · area: ai

- **One drone, two ways to reach it (TASK-173)** — The drone does not have to be in the same building as the lab, and it does not have to be in a field either: it can be in the lab today and in the garden tomorrow, and VirtualPyTest still flies it the same way. The host holds **both** routes to the drone — its own Wi-Fi dongle (the lab) and the phone's hotspot (relayed over the cellular link) — and chooses by itself: the lab path first, because it has no latency, and the phone path as soon as the lab path stops answering. When the drone comes back to the lab, the host notices on its own and returns to the fast route; nothing has to be reconfigured and no command is lost. The drone is on one Wi-Fi network at a time, so it is the app next to the drone that moves it between networks — the host only picks the route that answers. The controller, the safety guard, the telemetry, the command log, the games and the video pipeline do not change: the sidecar answers on the local address either way, so the rest of the stack cannot tell which route is in charge. The status page now shows the mode, the route in charge, the drone's lab address and the last direct error, and the phone reports whether its hotspot is on and whether the drone is really on it — a fallback that cannot reach the drone is no fallback, and now the host says so. In plain words: the drone goes where you want it to be, and VirtualPyTest follows it there by itself · [TASK-173](../tasks/TASK-173-hybrid-tello-link-direct-then-phone.md) · area: device-control

- **A check that never ran reads as skipped instead of failed (BUG-0756)** — The reports knew only one thing about a run: it passed, or it failed. A check that was never configured (the Netflix check without an account) writes the same "did not pass" as a broken video, so the nightly campaign row showed a red **FAIL** and the check's own chip inside the campaign read **FAIL** too — a defect the counting had already stopped reporting, still on the screen where the nightly is read. The reports now read the skip flag the run itself recorded: a check that never ran wears the muted grey of a skip (**SKIP**) instead of the red of a failure, in the run table's status cell and in the chip of the check inside an expanded nightly campaign, and a campaign that had one counts it apart — "1 skipped" beside the passed/total ratio and in the cell's tooltip — instead of leaving the skipped check to be read as a check that failed or was never run. The rule lives in one place (`frontend/src/utils/resultOutcome.ts`) and reads the row's own flag, never the script name and never the error text, so a check that was configured and then failed still fails, still shows red and still fails its campaign. a video check with no account configured (the Netflix check without an `--email`) never looked at a video at all, but its run row was written as a plain failure, so the nightly campaign ended `failed` and the platform reported a broken video that was never tested. A run row now carries a `skipped` flag, set from the outcome the run itself recorded (the check that never ran because it was not configured) — never from the wording of the error — and a campaign counts a skipped row as *skipped*, not as a failure: a campaign whose checks all ran or never ran ends `completed`, while one with a genuinely failed video still ends `failed`. The nightly poller already knew how to count a skipped row, it simply could never see one because the field did not exist; it is now part of the payload the campaign-results route serves, and the poller itself is unchanged. The campaign the nightly deployment closes through the executor counts it there too: the executor reads the `skipped` flag from the row the run wrote and routes such a run into its own skip branch, so the campaign's own ✓/✗ is honest as well — the signal is the row, never the exit code (2 is ambiguous, argparse uses it for a usage error too), the wording of the error or the name of the script. A video that never plays still fails, still fails its campaign and still shows as a failure. In plain words: a check that never ran is not a broken video · [BUG-0756](../bugs/BUG-0756-2026-10-21-a-web-video-test-never-confirms-playback.md) · 🗄 DB migration `setup/db/migrations/20261024a_script_results_skipped.sql` · area: testing
- **Activity log** — Logs has an Activity tab that lists elevated operations and Localize corrections with who did them, with search, event and actor filters and Export CSV. · TASK-127 · area: platform
- **One stale host name no longer takes a whole deploy down** — A deploy that names the hosts it wants (`--hosts a,b`) checked the whole list in one go: a single name the node did not know — a typo, or a host that had been switched off and removed from the node's list — aborted the deploy before any host was reached, so every other host in the request was left behind. The list is now checked name by name: a name the node does not know is dropped with a warning that names it, the deploy goes on with the hosts the node does know, and the dropped names are written into the deploy's note, so the record shows that the deploy reached fewer hosts than were asked for. The guard itself is still there: a request whose names are all unknown, or one that names no host at all, is refused instead of quietly deploying nothing. In plain words: one wrong name in a host list no longer stops the other hosts from being updated · area: platform

- **A "press until" step now waits for the screen to catch up** — A navigation step that repeats a key until a condition holds ("press RIGHT until the Settings column carries the focus") was checked on ONE frame right after the key. On a device whose picture lags its keys — a set-top box applies the key at once but redraws the focus underline 7-13 s later — that frame still showed the state from BEFORE the key, so the step concluded the key had not landed, pressed again, and ended up one or two items too far. The check after a press now honours the condition's own timeout and waits for a frame that actually shows the effect of the key, so the step stops where it should; the wait is spent on the device's own redraw instead of on a blind sleep. The check before the first press is unchanged (it asks "is the device already there?", so it never waits), a condition that declares no timeout keeps the old one-frame behaviour, and the rule's internal pacing field is now read instead of being documented and ignored. A condition can also ask to be judged on the frame where the focus **moved** rather than on the first frame that happens to match — that is what a walk wants, because most of its steps are on a frame that does not match yet. That judgement follows the underline's **place on the bar**, not the number of the block it sits in: the platform counts the blocks of a menu bar differently from frame to frame (a French menu bar reads five, six or seven blocks for its six columns), so the number alone hid a real movement, the step waited for its whole window, and a walk could run past its target and land on the wrong column. The frame it compares against is now the one from **before** the key went out, not the screenshot the check is handed: on a busy host that screenshot can be taken after the device already redrew, so the step judged a picture that already showed the movement, read it as "nothing moved" and pressed one time too many. The frames are also read in the order they were written, so an old frame cannot answer the check, and a picture without an underline — live TV between the menus — no longer breaks the check and makes it press again. Counting from the right now leaves out the page header (the user name at the top right of a Settings page), which was counted as a column and made the rightmost column read one position too far — a repeated key could land on the wrong item. In plain words: a repeated key lands on the right item instead of overshooting it · area: platform

- **A Tello you can fly from anywhere (TASK-167)** — The drone no longer has to be in the same building as the lab. The phone next to it holds the drone's Wi-Fi conversation and relays it to the host over the Socket.IO link the Android phone-agent app already keeps open, so the drone works from a garden, a field or another country — and the phone can carry two or three drones at once. The host presents a bridged drone as an ordinary local UDP peer, so the drone's client, the safety guard, the telemetry, the command log, the games and the video pipeline are unchanged: a remote drone is configured exactly like a lab one, only its address is 127.0.0.1 and the phone holds the radio. The host's drone bridge opens a command port, a state port, a local port and a video port per drone, injects the drone's state and its video where the rest of the stack expects them, and answers the host's drone commands with the drone's own reply. The Android app gains a drone link: it opens the drone's three sockets on the hotspot interface, finds the drones on the hotspot's subnet and relays their commands, state and video. The drones are told apart by source address, never by destination, and the phone keeps ONE command socket for all of them — a Tello only answers the address that sent its first `command`, so a scan from a second socket would silently break every later command. A new `/host/phone/drones` route shows each bridged drone, the phone carrying it and how fresh its link is; a drone with no phone attached still appears with its last error, so a dead link reads as a dead link and not as a missing device. In plain words: the drone goes where you want it to be, and VirtualPyTest still flies it the way it always did · [TASK-167](../tasks/TASK-167-phone-as-tello-bridge.md) · area: device-control

- **The 24-hour digest: one small toast for the problems of the last day (TASK-166)** — Once per session the platform looks at the notification history of the last 24 hours and tells you the three problems that matter: the three most recent **errors** if there are any, the three most recent **warnings** if there are none. It reads the history, not the live dashboard, so a problem that started twelve hours ago is finally counted, and a category whose toast is switched off (scripts) is no longer invisible. The toast is one small plain-text pill in the top-right corner — red for errors, yellow for warnings, no icon, no button — and a small caret opens the three problems, each one click from the page that shows the cause. A problem the platform has already seen clear is not in the digest; it stays in the bell with its “Fixed X ago” note. It shows once per browser session: a reload does not bring it back, a new tab does. In plain words: you are told once what went wrong while you were away, in one line, instead of one toast that comes and goes. · [TASK-166](../tasks/TASK-166-notification-digest-24h.md) · area: platform
- **Toasts are small plain-text pills** — Every toast in the app (error, warning, success, info) is now the same little pill: one line, the caption size, a rounded end, a hairline in the severity colour and the text in that colour — no icon, because the colour already says what happened, and no button; “Skip” is a quiet text link instead of a box. In plain words: the notifications stop shouting and stay out of the way. · [TASK-166](../tasks/TASK-166-notification-digest-24h.md) · area: platform
