- **Every egg gets a circle, and no egg gets two (BUG-0858)** — the egg overlay drew 95 circles on a frame with about 200 eggs in it, and drew two or three circles on some of the ones it did find. Both defects are now fixed and the fix is measured on that same fixed frame. **The model was being fed the wrong picture**: it was trained on a portrait video of big white eggs (150-250 px) on yellow rods in daylight, and the device3 feed is a landscape shot of small white eggs (20-70 px) on a saturated blue belt. Four knobs close that gap, each one measured rather than guessed: `fill: stretch` (a square 640 tile fills the model's square input with no grey padding to confuse it — and a square tile stretched is not distorted), `preprocess: [whitebalance, unsharp]` (pull the saturated blue out of the scene, bring back the edges the belt's blur takes away — 89 → 226 candidates), `flip_tta` (the same tile seen from the other side: a model confused by a neighbour in a crowd often finds the egg in the mirrored crop, and this is the single biggest recall lever — 89 → 111 unique eggs) and a square 640 tile instead of a 640x360 one. **What decides that a box is an egg is now the scene's own colour**, not the detector's confidence: the belt is saturated blue and an egg is not, so a box that is mostly blue belt is dropped. That is what makes the very low `min_conf: 0.01` safe — the detector offers ~220 candidates and the belt filter removes the 28 that are bare belt, leaving **198 circles, none on bare belt**. (A brightness test is the *wrong* filter here: "bright and unsaturated" rejected 84 candidates, and most of those were real eggs lying in the shadow of the pile.) **Two circles on one egg were an NMS limitation, not a model one**: two boxes on the same egg are offset by a few pixels, so their IoU lands around 0.3-0.5 and some survive the 0.30 threshold, while two different eggs that touch overlap by ~0.04 — so the test for "same egg" is centre **distance**, not overlap. Boxes within 0.35 of a diameter of each other, of a similar size, are now one circle: **0 duplicated eggs, and no pair of neighbouring eggs merged**. One scale, 640, wins: a 320 tile finds the small far eggs but at 2.5x the cost and with boxes that disagree with the 640 pass, and merging the two scales produced doubled circles all over the far rows. The cost is stated: 12 forwards, ~12.4 s a frame on the box, so the picture still refreshes slowly — this is a still frame that updates, not live video. The far rows and the deep pile still lose a few small dim eggs, and **good/bad per egg still means nothing on this footage** (no candling) · [BUG-0858](../bugs/BUG-0858-2026-10-23-the-egg-circles-missed-half-the-eggs-and-drew-two-on-some.md) · area: monitoring
- **Clash of Clans and Pokémon play on the free MiniMax model, back to back (TASK-164)** — the two game players ran, but not on the model the owner wants and not without gaps. **Clash of Clans** already served its 1816 calls of the last day on the free MiniMax M3.1 Flash for $0, but the model had a paid fallback (so a failing Flash call was silently answered by Haiku — real money) and the budget meter priced the free model like Haiku ($0.0249 a run, $3.92 "spent" in the month). The free profile has no fallback any more, the free model is priced at zero, and the extra `coc-flash` job is switched on, so the phone keeps playing between the hourly runs. **Pokémon** had not played since 2026-10-20: the save was locked by a stall and the watcher that starts a fixer only looked at a three-hour window, so the lock waited two and a half days for a human; the nightly `game_pokemon` step died every night on a flag that carried its dashes twice; and the strategist ran on a paid OpenRouter model. The watcher now re-arms on the lock itself, the nightly step starts, and the strategist runs on Flash. A new `flash_run_log.py` writes the per-run record (model, steps, progress, the money it really cost, wall time) and the daily summary of the task doc, and the doc lists the other volume jobs that could move to Flash — caption translation (it spent $1.87 of real money on 2026-10-22 before the best-effort guard stopped it from paying a second hop, and it still eats the owner's M3 quota) and alert triage (99 % of its paid calls fail) first. **What the loop hit on its first day** is now fixed too: every free call of the platform answered `rate_limit` for an hour (the account is shared with the paid models of the same provider, and another job made 1 685 calls in that hour), so the CoC run died 21 seconds after it started and the Pokémon's strategist never answered — a free model that is throttled is now asked again on the same hop instead of being written off, and a throttled game step is skipped instead of ending the run (BUG-0864). And the lease of the running game was released two minutes after it was claimed (its holder name was a session that had ended), so a second game landed on the same 2-core host and neither moved: the holder name is one no ended session can release now, and both dispatchers renew their lease every 3 minutes (BUG-0863). In plain words: the two games play on the free model, back to back, and you can see what each run cost · [TASK-164](../tasks/TASK-164-flash-play.md) · area: ai
- **The recurring screens finally get names, and the process shows its work (TASK-162)** — a new read-only report answers "is every screen we see often enough actually named?", one device at a time, split into labelled + fingerprinted, labelled but not fingerprinted, still `unknown_N`, and no name at all, and prints the one line this is measured on: **"screens >10 seen, not live, missing a label or a fingerprint"**. It landed on **25** of the 31 recurring screens on the lab and reads **5** after the day's worker run; 30 of those 31 were still being seen this week — the gap was in the screens currently on the boxes, not in old history. The same pass is what found that the bigger gap was fingerprints rather than names, and the fix for that is in the bug fixes below. The report also has a `--metric` mode that prints only that one line for a cron or alert to watch, and an `--sql` mode for the same numbers in psql. A daily job then closes the naming gap, on the free MiniMax model, and it reaches further down than the number above on purpose: it names anything the registry still keeps (three sightings or more, the same floor the platform's own cleanup uses), so a splash that boots once a day is named instead of sitting unnamed for ever — while the "seen more than 10 times" number you watch stays exactly where it was. It takes the most-seen screen first, and it asks before it names: first *is this even a picture of a screen, or a photo of the real world?*, then *is it moving video?*, then *is it a splash or a poster?* — and only then does it name. Every one of those questions is asked more than once and has to be answered the same way each time, because that model does not always answer the same thing twice (measured: one frame came back MOVING, STILL, STILL). A name is written **once**, through the same single writer as everything else, never over an existing name, and anything the model is not sure of is left as `unknown_N` and listed for a person rather than guessed. When it recognises a screen that matches an existing navigation node, it links it and keeps that node's fingerprint; when nothing matches it queues the name on the page you already use for unnamed screens, rather than inventing a node. A second pass re-checks what was written with a different question and reports how often it agrees — disagreements are listed, never applied — which is how it caught itself naming two photographs of a wall "Full-screen poster". The daily schedule ships **switched off**: the report runs read-only, the worker needs `--apply` before it can write anything, and the two timers are handed over as files to install rather than installed for you, because adding a systemd unit is an owner-only step on this platform. In plain words: the screens your boxes keep showing get proper names on their own, and you can check whether the machine is doing it right · [TASK-162](../tasks/TASK-162-screen-label-process.md) · 🗄 DB migration `setup/db/migrations/20261023a_screen_label_worker.sql` · area: monitoring
- **A web navigation no longer hangs on a busy page (TASK-161)** — walking a website's screens could stop dead. `page.title()` and `page.content()` are browser calls with no timeout of their own, so on a page whose script was still busy (a donation modal, a long-running widget) the title read never came back: the page had actually loaded — the address bar already showed the new address — but the navigation never returned, and every screen after it waited the full 180 s and then failed. The title read and the DOM capture now have their own budget, and the **address** (not the title) decides that a page loaded. The same round fixed an edge whose `params` were not a mapping (`"params": ""`, which an agent had written): the executor used to raise `'str' object has no attribute 'items'` and that killed the navigation and the whole test run; params that are not a mapping now mean "no params". Measured on the Internet Archive and Wikipedia trees: a walk that used to burn 21 of its 30 minutes on screenshot timeouts, then another 180 s per screen on a stuck page, now finishes a screen every 3-6 seconds. In plain words: a website that keeps a script busy no longer freezes the test that walks it · [TASK-161](../tasks/TASK-161-web-showcase-trees.md) · area: testing

- **The drone remote stops covering its own controls (BUG-0846)** — holding the move pad or the round **Up** / **Down** / **Rotate left** / **Rotate right** buttons popped a tooltip ("Move: forward, back, left, right") that stayed for the whole flight. Those controls are press-and-hold, so the pointer never leaves them and MUI holds the tooltip open for exactly as long as you fly: the panel covered the stick you were steering with, at the one moment you need to read it. The five held controls now carry no hover label at all — the pad already draws its four direction arrows inside the ring and the stick shows the vector — and each one keeps its accessible name for screen readers. The labels on the controls you *click* (battery, link, flight history, emergency stop, the four flips) are unchanged. In plain words: the flight controls no longer hide behind their own tooltips while you fly · [BUG-0846](../bugs/BUG-0846-2026-10-22-drone-remote-tooltip-covers-the-controls.md) · area: device-control
- **A circle that fits the egg, and a number that says what the detector missed (BUG-0848)** — the egg overlay drew 45 px circles on eggs that are 55-70 px across: `egg_detect method: blob` finds each egg by its own local contrast (so it never merges two) but its box is the LoG *response diameter*, not the egg. The circles were too small and the picture looked like a detector guessing even where it had found the right egg. A new detector, **`yolo_seg_detect`**, runs an Ultralytics YOLOv8 **segmentation** model in ONNX through `cv2.dnn` — no torch on the host, the same runtime `coco_detect` already uses — and fits an ellipse to the box, so the circle is the egg. The model is a third-party YOLOv8s trained on the public Roboflow `egg-seg-1` set (GitHub, no account, no key; the HuggingFace egg repos are gated and the Roboflow one needs a key), exported outside the project folder and pointed at by `MONITOR_YOLO_MODEL`. One 640x640 pass is not enough for this scene, and the reason is specific: the model on the whole 720p frame finds 47 eggs, and on a 480x240 **crop of the same frame** finds 22 of the 26 in it. It is not blind to the rest, it is losing them to context, so the frame is **sliced** into overlapping tiles and the tiles merged — the shipped profile (640x360 tiles, a quarter overlap, 9 forwards) finds **95**, more than the 82 detections the owner's own reference clip shows. What it will not do is box every egg in a crowd: hand-counted at ~220 in that frame, 95 is 43 %, and no threshold, tile size, zoom or NMS setting tested (30+ configurations) moved that. So the honest numbers ship with it: the overlay now reads **`Eggs 253 · Detections 82`**, a new `ui.stats` row where a big Eggs next to a small Detections says eggs are on the belt that were never boxed — one total cannot say that. The cost is real and stated in the profile: one forward is **1029 ms** on the host through `cv2.dnn` and 1083 ms through onnxruntime (that box has no OpenVINO, and int8 quantisation made it 3.5x *slower*, 3780 ms), so this profile updates about every **10 s** against 41 ms for the blob detector. It therefore ships as a **second profile**, `egg_quality_yolo`, beside the fast `egg_quality` rather than replacing it, and at that rate the frame-lock from BUG-0836 is what keeps the circles on the eggs. Three mistakes along the way all *reported success* and are pinned by tests: `cv2.dnn.NMSBoxes` takes (x, y, w, h) not xyxy (handed xyxy it deleted half the boxes and made slicing look twice as bad as it was), a segmentation head is `4 + classes + 32 mask coefficients` and nothing marks the split once the prototypes are dropped (reading it wrong gives 618 boxes a frame instead of 47), and `tile_overlap` was applied as the step, so "0.25" ran a 75 % overlap and 240 forwards. **Good/bad per egg is unchanged and still means nothing here**: the classifier grades crops of white eggs on a blue belt in daylight, where there is no image evidence of quality, and reports ~99 % bad because that is what the model says. A real grade needs candling · [BUG-0848](../bugs/BUG-0848-2026-10-22-the-egg-circles-did-not-fit-the-eggs.md) · TASK-90 · area: monitoring

- **The monitoring overlay sits on the eggs (BUG-0836)** — the boxes were drawn from one frame on top of a *live* video, with nothing relating the two, so on a moving belt they showed where each egg was up to three quarters of a second ago: the monitor analyses one frame per sample period and takes the newest it has (250 ms at 4 Hz), the analysis costs ~57 ms, the browser then asked for the newest JSON only every 500 ms, and the stream underneath is later again. Nothing said so — the FPS line reported the capture rates, which were fine. Each box now carries the velocity the tracker already computes for matching (`v`, px/s) and the frame JSON carries `captured_at`, the capture time of the frame actually analysed, so the client puts every box back on its own object and prints the real distance in the FPS line as `sync 250 ms`; boxes fade out rather than glide away once the prediction is older than 0.8 s. The saving that pays for it: `detect_width` finds the eggs on an 800 px copy and scales the boxes back — the **same 71 boxes per frame for 32 ms instead of 54** — so `egg_quality` now samples at 8 Hz instead of 4, and the browser polls every 120 ms instead of 500. Measured on the host afterwards: the overlay is now **311 ms median / 747 ms worst case**, down from a worst case of ~750 ms — and the analysis turned out to run at 4.9 Hz, not 8, because that device's ffmpeg captures at 5 fps and `sample_hz` is a ceiling; the cheaper detector and the faster poll are what actually bought the drop. Three things were unreadable on top of that and are fixed: the counters wrapped onto two lines with a number 200 px from its label (one row per class now, count **and its share of the total**, under a single stacked bar), every one of 90 eggs wore a `3/3` badge (only the focused item keeps one past 12 objects), and the "Last checked" verdict named an egg you could not find in the picture (it is drawn in **gold** with a heavier stroke, and the panel says so). Correction for a lag is not enough, and the owner's own screenshot after the first deploy proved it: a constant-velocity prediction is only right while the motion is constant, and eggs get picked up and piles shift. So when a profile overlay is drawing, **the picture shown is the exact capture the boxes were measured on** — `capture_NNNNNN.jpg` sits above the video and below the boxes, the boxes cannot drift because there is nothing to drift from, and the overlay stops carrying them forward. A small switch in the corner gives the smooth live stream back for watching the device itself. The cost is stated plainly: frame-locked advances at the capture rate (5 fps here), which was the ceiling on box freshness anyway. `counting_cars` gets the velocity and the capture time too, at its existing 2 Hz · [BUG-0836](../bugs/BUG-0836-2026-10-22-the-monitor-overlay-did-not-sit-on-the-eggs.md) · TASK-90 · area: monitoring
- **Tello takes off however warm it reads** — the software refused take-off at 85 °C or more. A Tello cools itself with its propellers in the air and runs at 80 °C and above in normal flights, so the check is gone and the drone decides for itself. In plain words: a warm drone is no longer grounded by the platform · area: device-control
- **Pairing a Tello answers in seconds** — joining a drone that is off, asleep or too hot now fails after a few seconds with a plain message instead of waiting up to a minute. In plain words: press Pair and you know quickly whether the drone is there · area: device-control

- **Egg quality control counts real eggs and restarts with the clip (BUG-0833, BUG-0834)** — the "Synthetic eggs" and "Synthetic cars" devices on `vpt-lenovo1` now play real factory and traffic footage instead of flat synthetic shapes, and what the overlay shows is true. Two fixes: the deployed egg classifier was a structurally invalid ONNX (32 px input, a transposed final layer) so the `egg_quality` profile **never once ran** on the host — it is retrained from the documented CC BY Mendeley set (held-out accuracy 0.985) and loads in both cv2.dnn and onnxruntime; and a looping device counted the same clip for ever, so "cars in the last hour" read 2 900 after 80 minutes of the same traffic scene. The new `img.reset_counters` starts the counters again at every pass (`every_s`, plus an optional scene-cut trigger that stays off by default), which measures 38 instead of 134 over two passes. The egg detector grew a `blob` method: it finds each egg by its own local contrast, so on real footage every box lands on a real egg instead of a whole pile counting as one "egg" for the length of the clip. Recorded honestly in TASK-90 §6, including what is still not true: the good/bad grade is meaningless outside candling imagery (it reads almost everything bad on factory footage), and the count over a moving pile is a detector figure, not a census you can check by hand. New `scripts/render_egg_overlay.py` draws the overlay on a clip so it can be checked without a host · [BUG-0833](../bugs/BUG-0833-2026-10-22-egg-quality-never-ran-the-classifier-model-is-invalid.md) · [BUG-0834](../bugs/BUG-0834-2026-10-22-looping-device-counts-for-ever-and-eggs-weld-into-one-box.md) · TASK-90 · area: monitoring

- **Incident triage knows when a key was last sent, and can wake a box to check (TASK-158)** — every new black-screen, no-signal and freeze alert now carries the last key that reached the device before it (`seconds_since_last_key`, who sent it: a script, a take-control session or the remote) and the expanded row on the Incidents page shows it as "Last key: 42 min · remote". The triage prompt uses it: black or no signal on a TV box nobody touched for 30 min (`ALERT_STANDBY_AFTER_S`) reads as standby, a freeze seconds after a key stays a real fault. When triage still cannot conclude on a black or no-signal alert (flat frame, or the model gave no answer), a **wake probe** can send one safe key (OK, SELECT or BACK; never POWER or HOME), look again and discard the alert with a named reason ("probe: woke_from_standby", "probe: splash_to_home") or keep it open ("probe: no_change"). Off by default (`ALERT_PROBE_MODE=off|dryrun|on`), opt-in per device (`ALERT_PROBE_DEVICES=host/device=KEY`), never while the device is in a take-control, script or deployment session, cooldown 1 h per device and 6 probes an hour, each logged in the audit trail; `dryrun` only writes what it would send. · area: monitoring
- **Image telemetry (TASK-157)** — every device on the `tv_app` monitoring profile (`img.telemetry: true`; other profiles are off, and `IMG_TELEMETRY=false` switches a host off) gets its frames counted: the image monitor counts per device how many frames arrive per second, how many JPEG bytes (an estimated image throughput, not the stream bitrate and not network), how old a frame is when the monitor starts on it (p95) and how many it drops to stay on the newest, and it samples the monitor's and ffmpeg's CPU and memory and the load per core. Counting costs one file-size read per frame, no decoding. Every 60 s the numbers go to the new `image_telemetry_buckets` table (7 days kept; a database outage drops rows, never frames) and to the host status. The stream stats overlay (bottom-left of the stream modal) gets **Img in**, **Img age** and **Monitor** rows and the load per core; a new Grafana dashboard **Image Telemetry** shows the same history. A reading that cannot be taken is empty, never 0. In plain words: you can see when the monitor falls behind a device, and what that costs the host · [TASK-157](../tasks/TASK-157-image-telemetry-worker.md) · [review](../tasks/REVIEW-image-telemetry.md) · 🗄 DB migration `20261022c_image_telemetry.sql` · area: monitoring
- **The website "Ask AI" answers common questions instantly (Fixnet, IR remotes, pricing, devices, getting started)** — the widget on virtualpytest.com used to send every question to the model, so "how is fixnet working" took 41 s (five model calls). It now reads the same prepared FAQ as Atlas (`agent_faq`), restricted to entries flagged `public` (docs-only, English and French rows): a question that matches answers in milliseconds with no model call and no cost, in the visitor's language when a row exists. A question that does not match finds the best doc sections first and puts them in the prompt, so most answer in one model call, with a 10-second wall budget (the top doc excerpt and its sources come back if the model is slow; the old multi-call loop stays only for questions the docs do not obviously cover). The reply now carries `faq: true` / `model_calls`. Seed: `scripts/agent/faq_load_public.py`; benchmark: `scripts/agent/public_ask_bench.py`. Every model answer that cites docs is also remembered in the database (until a cited doc changes), so asking the same question a second time is instant, even after a deploy. In plain words: the visitor gets the answer before they look away · 🗄 DB migration `setup/db/migrations/20261022c_agent_faq_public.sql` (additive `public` column, default false) · area: ai

- **Filter the Interface page by model, from the badge or a dropdown** — on `/configuration/interface` the model badges (`web`, `android_tv`, `stb`…) are now filter buttons: click one and the list shows only the interfaces that use it, click it again and that filter lifts. A **Models** dropdown beside the search box does the same thing and shows how many of your interfaces use each model, so you can narrow without scanning the table first. Both controls are one filter: a model chosen in the dropdown lights up as a filled badge on every row that has it, and a badge clicked in a row ticks in the dropdown. Several models can be on at once and they **AND** — `web` + `android_tv` shows only the interfaces carrying both — so each click narrows further and each click off widens by exactly that step. The dropdown only lists models your interfaces actually use, because a model nothing uses filters the list to nothing with no visible cause. Selecting a model keeps working when you also use the search box or the Dev/Prod toggle, and it drops itself if the interface that used it is deleted while the page is open. A model badge is `filled` while it filters and outlined when it is not, and reports its state as pressed, so it is not distinguished by colour alone. The badge behind a `+N` opens the row's other models, where each one filters the same way. **Variant** names stay read-only text — a variant is not something the list can be filtered by, so the two badge columns are deliberately different. In plain words: click the badge of the model you care about and see only those interfaces · area: platform

- **A full disk can no longer look healthy (TASK-156)** — a host card turns amber from 85 % disk and red from 95 % on its fullest mount (it used to read green at 100 %), the dashboard raises the same warning or error with the Fix now button, and a host that gained 10 points in 6 hours and would be full within a day warns as "disk 62% filling, full in ~9 h" before it reaches the line. New hosts also cap the system journal at 512 MB. In plain words: you hear about a filling disk hours before the cameras go black · [runbook](../agent/platform/HOST_DISK_FULL.md) · area: monitoring

- **Audit trail: who did what (TASK-154)** — one table, `public.audit_events`, now records what every user, AI agent, host and cron job did: mutating and denied API calls (health and status polls, plain reads and OPTIONS are left out), logins (a database trigger on `auth.users`, so password, Google and token sign-ins are all seen), device control take and release, the host, device, command and key name of a remote key press (never typed text), AI calls (`ai_calls` stays the AI system of record) and the nightly Localize benchmark. Internal identities (auto-sign, the shared service key, open mode, the public key) are recorded as system or service, never as users. The Logs → Activity tab lists these rows beside the elevated operations and Localize corrections. The caller IP is stored as a hash; the raw address is kept only when `VPT_AUDIT_STORE_RAW_IP=1`. Rows older than 90 days (`VPT_AUDIT_RETENTION_DAYS`, kept between 7 and 3650, a malformed value falls back to 90) are deleted by the server itself. The trail is best-effort: a failed write is retried three times, and rows that still cannot be saved, or arrive while 10,000 are already waiting, are counted and logged each minute instead of vanishing silently; a team the caller does not belong to is stored as no team. Sign-outs are recorded from the Supabase auth log; failed logins are not, because Supabase does not log them. In plain words: ask the database what any person, AI or timer did, and when · [TASK-154](../tasks/TASK-154-comprehensive-audit-trail.md) · 🗄 DB migrations `20261022a_audit_events.sql`, `20261022b_audit_events_logout.sql` · area: platform
- **Heatmap on All servers** — With All servers picked, the 24h Heatmap shows one server at a time, chosen from a server dropdown in its header (remembered in this browser), with the usual All / OK / KO filter, Latest, Generate report and History for that server. Before, All servers asked a server called `__all__` and showed an empty page. Devices with no monitor (`tv_lite` and `pc` profiles, such as the vpt-nipogi and lenovo streams) used to be grey NO CAPTURE cells; they now show their newest screenshot with a blue border (picture only, no OK/KO verdict). In plain words: pick a server and see all its screens, monitored or not · area: monitoring

- **Phones and Fire TV over USB work on a new host without manual setup** — the host installer now adds the standard Android USB rules and lets the host service open USB devices, so `adb devices` sees a phone plugged into a fresh host and its "Allow USB debugging" prompt appears. If a host still shows nothing, the device page doc gives the one-line fix (restart adb as the host service user). In plain words: plug the phone in, accept the prompt, done · area: device-control
- **Atlas chat keeps the reasoning in view during long runs** — the live reasoning now sits above the tool calls, and tool calls list newest first with only the last 5 shown; "Show N earlier tool calls" reveals the rest · area: ai
- **Android and Android mobile devices work over a USB cable** — a phone, tablet or Android TV plugged into the host is driven by its adb serial: set `DEVICE<N>_ADB_SERIAL=<serial from adb devices>` in the host `.env` (and leave `DEVICE<N>_IP` / `_PORT` out) and Take Control, scripts and the ADB verifications use the cable, no Wi-Fi adb needed. A serial wins when both are set, and the host no longer runs `adb connect` for a serial. Builds before this only knew ADB over the network (`device_ip is required`). For an install that has not taken the update, **patch-3** (`patch/patch_host_3_android-usb-serial.sh`) applies the same six small edits to `backend_host`, checked on the 2026.09.08 / 09.11 / 09.15 / 09.17 releases: run `--check` (exit 10 = needed), then the script, then restart `vpt-host`; `--rollback` restores the backups. In plain words: plug the phone in and use it, instead of setting up Wi-Fi debugging · 🩹 patch-3 · [TASK-151](../tasks/TASK-151-client-patches.md) · area: device-control

- **The Alerts page header is only the title and the tiles** — the sentence under "Alerts" ("Incidents the monitor opened on each device: what is happening now and what was resolved") repeated what the four tiles above it already say, and the small × in front of the Clear button read like "close this" instead of "clear the filters". The header now carries the title alone, and the Clear button says what it does. In plain words: the page shows its numbers, not a description of them · area: monitoring

- **Hovering a triage verdict tells you why** — the Triage column said "Discarded" and left you to expand the row to learn why. Hovering the verdict now shows the reason the triage gave — the model's own words for a kept or a discarded incident, the cost rule that skipped it ("the alert was shorter than 30 s"), or the screen it was recognised as — with the category above it. In plain words: you learn why an incident was thrown away without opening it · area: monitoring

- **The AI usage page prices the MiniMax calls the way they are actually billed** — the `minimax` endpoint is the MiniMax **coding plan**, a flat subscription: every call on it is included in the plan and costs 0. A price row synced from Langfuse for `minimax-m2.7-highspeed` was billing that model (0.25 / 1.00 USD per million tokens) while its siblings on the same endpoint were free — about $3 of calls the subscription already covers. And the VLM endpoint reports no token usage, so its 14 866 calls (a third of all AI calls) were logged with "no price" instead of "included in plan". A synced price row never bills a plan endpoint any more (only a typed one does), and a call whose provider reports no tokens is priced 0 when the endpoint is free and stays "no price" when it is metered. In plain words: the AI spend you see is the spend you have · [BUG-0726](../bugs/BUG-0726-2026-10-20-coding-plan-billed-by-a-synced-price-row.md) · area: ai
- **The hourly Clash of Clans run gives the phone back as soon as somebody else needs it** — TASK-100. CoC is low priority: the guard that keeps other agents off the phone while the game plays now takes the lease as a **background job (P3)** with a 10-minute TTL, and the registry itself refuses to extend it while a claim of higher priority waits. The moment somebody queues a claim for the phone (or a host deploy waits on it), the guard asks the game to stop: it writes a stop order on the phone, the player reads it at its next turn and the run ends cleanly with "yielded" instead of being killed in the middle of a step. The guard then hands the phone back - the lease is released, never left to expire - and the next hourly run tries again; the phone is never held more than one turn (about 10 minutes) past a waiting claim. In plain words: the game gives up the phone as soon as someone else asks for it · [TASK-100](../tasks/TASK-100-coc-llm-player-platform-budget.md) · area: platform

- **Notification centre (TASK-142)** — A platform problem is no longer a 4-second toast that shows once. The toast now has a Show button and is clickable (keyboard too): "Monitoring problem on labox-backend: disk 93%" opens Status on that host. While a monitoring problem is open, a "Monitoring problem" chip stays beside the "in error" pill on Hosts and devices; it toasts again after 30 minutes or when the dashboard is reopened, and a problem that cleared and came back toasts again. A bell in the top bar shows an unread count and the last 50 notifications (newest first; click opens the page, dismiss one, Mark all read, Clear), kept per browser. In plain words: a problem stays visible, one click takes you to its cause, and you can find it again later · [TASK-142](../tasks/TASK-142-notification-center.md) · area: platform
- **Every AI call can be benchmarked against any model (TASK-143)** — each of the platform's 56 AI usage keys (every `agent.*`, `vision.*`, `text.*` and companion call) now has a frozen set of 1–10 test cases and can be re-run against any named model on demand, so "is this model better for this call?" is a command instead of an opinion. `python3 scripts/ai_bench.py --key text.plan --profile minimax-m3 --profile claude-sonnet-5-5` runs the case set of that key through the **production call path** (the model is pinned, no fallback, the call is logged, traced and priced like the real one) and writes the result twice: a git-tracked line in `docs/agent/ai/benchmarks/bench-<key>.jsonl` and one row per case in the AI call log (`ai_calls`, labelled `eval:<usage key>` — a benchmark case is an AI call, so it needs no table of its own). The cases come from the labelled eval screens where they exist (the banner, the screen classifier, the localize and the verification decisions) and are hand-authored elsewhere; the checks are `contains`, `equals`, `regex`, `json_field`, `choice`, `nonempty` and `manual`. The predefined models per key and per family live in `benchmarks/models.json`. The results are shown in **Monitoring → AI usage → Benchmarks** (one row per run: the key, the model, the cases that passed, the cost, the latency; click a run for its cases) and in a new Grafana dashboard "AI benchmarks" (pass rate by key and model, cost, pass rate over time, cases per day, latency, the failing cases). In plain words: ask the lab "which model should answer this call?" and it answers with the cases, the cost and the proof · [TASK-143](../tasks/TASK-143-ai-call-benchmark-coverage.md) · area: ai
- **Project slice follow-ups (BUG-0609)** — five paper-cuts from wrapping the project page v2 slice: project permissions helpers (`can_edit_project`, `caller_project_role`, the `_can_edit_project` private) extracted to `backend_server/src/lib/project_permissions.py` so future route modules don't import a leading-underscore name across blueprints; the existing `server_projects_routes` keeps a thin re-export so callers using the old path still resolve. ProjectDetail / ProjectInScope section header now carries a "AI (30d): X tokens · Y calls · $Z" chip that pulls per-project totals from `ai_calls` (filtered by `project_id`, the column BUG-0559 added) — per-script breakdown still deferred to a slice that lands a `script_id` column on `ai_calls`. `ProjectTagsEditor` surfaces an inline error chip next to the tag row when a `DELETE /tags/names/<name>` returns 400 (e.g. trying to remove the project's scope tag) instead of just `console.error`-ing; the friendly copy is a regex match on the route's user-facing message. `TASK-101-project-page-mockup-v3.html` is now committed in `docs/tasks/` (the worktree file was lost when the v2 worktree was removed) and BUG-0606 + BUG-0608 docs link to it from a "Visual reference" section. BUG-0608 doc gets a "Renumber note" section explaining the 0607 → 0608 id collision and cross-linking the real BUG-0607 (pokemon saffron-step fix). 5 new i18n keys × 2 locales. tsc + i18n + ui-check + eslint + 34 in-memory pytest all green. · [BUG-0609](../bugs/BUG-0609-2026-10-18-projects-slice-followups.md) · TASK-65 · area: platform
- **A host runs only the services its profiles need** — when the monitoring profiles are applied (host start, profile change), every profile-driven service no device needs (image, audio, subtitle, archiver, KPI, AVQ monitors and the Live-TV companion) is now stopped instead of left idling, and started again when a profile needs it. Units stay enabled, so a reboot starts them and the next apply stops them again; stream, host and VNC are never touched. The Dashboard row of a stopped one reads "not needed" and the apply status lists `stopped` and `started`. In plain words: a host only runs what its devices use · [TASK-144](../tasks/TASK-144-profile-stops-unneeded-services.md) · area: monitoring

- **Web trees: one command validates every UI language** — `scripts/agent/validate_languages.py <userinterface> --languages de,fr,it,en` switches the web app's language on Settings → Language, proves the switch (address and a sidebar label before and after), runs `validation.py`, reads that run's own result and report link, recovers a stuck login or a restarted Chrome, and ends back in English. In plain words: test a web app in all its languages without driving it by hand · area: testing
- **Web screens are fingerprinted on the page, not the whole desktop** — a web device's capture includes the taskbar, clock and browser bars; the Localize fingerprint is now computed on the browser page area (box read from the browser, any window size), so screens sit about 5-6 bits further apart. In plain words: the screen's identity no longer includes the clock · area: testing
- **Every Grafana dashboard ends with a collapsed "Details" section** — the house shape of the Script Results dashboard is now the rule for all of them: a dashboard opens on its overview, and the detail tables sit in a "Details" row at the bottom that is collapsed by default (the row keeps its old name in its description). The dashboards that had no row at all were split into an "Overview" row and a collapsed "Details" row, and the AV Check dashboard's recent runs now link to the **signed** report url, which is the one that really opens the report — plus a "Device runs" link to the app's report list for that device, which never expires. · area: monitoring

- **One project page** — a project now has a single page with Overview, Scope, Usage and Team, where Overview shows pass rate, devices online, runs in progress and AI tokens, what needs attention, and the same Last executions table as Run Tests narrowed to the project. One Add button covers scripts, testcases, campaigns, devices and users; members can be added and removed from Team. The old Executive summary address opens Overview. · area: platform

- **A web test that fails on a captcha says so** — a test that ended on the FAILURE block used to report only "Test case reached FAILURE block". The failed step is now named, and when the page it failed on shows an hCaptcha, reCAPTCHA or Cloudflare Turnstile widget, the step and the run say "Blocked by hCaptcha captcha at step 'Login and reach Dashboard'" (an automated test cannot pass a captcha). Found on the Sauce Demo login tests, which failed every run for that reason. Host restart needed (`update_core.sh main --host`). · area: testing

- **Delete several projects at once** — on the Projects page, Select shows a checkbox on every card, Select all picks them all, and Delete selected removes them after one confirmation. Projects you are not allowed to delete (admins and project owners only) stay selected with a message. · area: platform

- **A Localize page users understand (TASK-137)** — the Localize monitoring page was reviewed by five users with real questions (tester, tree builder, manager, on-call, customer) and none could answer theirs. Its tabs are now **Live results** (how often Localize named the screen in real runs) and **Screens taught** (which screens of each app it has a reference for), each with a one-line explanation and a one-sentence summary; the named rate shows the change against the previous window. Test, bench and copy interfaces and devices where nothing was named are hidden behind a switch. A Screens taught row opens the screens still to fix with the reason and a link to the tree; a device row opens its recent runs with the top 3 guesses and the screenshot shown in the app; repeated close calls are grouped; a screen name opens that screen in the tree editor (new `?node=` link), and a run from Verify or the Localize button has "Wrong screen?" with Undo, the same correction as the Localize button (migration `20261019a`, monitor runs carry no fingerprint and cannot be corrected). The Live results tab is now translated (French, German), and both tabs become cards on a phone. In plain words: the page now says what Localize is, whether it works on your device and what to fix · [TASK-137](../tasks/TASK-137-localize-page-ux.md) · area: monitoring
- **Search box on the Interface page** — a search field left of the All / Dev / Prod switch filters the interface list by name or model as you type. · area: platform
- **AI usage Calls: the conversation opens in a dialog** — the conversation icon on a call used to open Langfuse in a new tab (a 404 on the lab Langfuse); it now opens a dialog over the table with the prompt and answer of each model attempt, read through `GET /server/ai/usage/trace/<id>`. The Latency and Details column headers no longer truncate, and every header shows its full name on hover. In plain words: you read what the AI was asked and answered without leaving the page · area: ai

- **Last verified frame per node** — a real run that passes a node's verifications now keeps that frame in storage (one per node and variant, refreshed at most every 10 minutes), and the node panel shows it with its age (the node card above already shows the stored screenshot). Editor test runs never store one. · area: testing

- **Interface list stays one line per interface** — on Configuration → Interface, the Variants and Models columns show the first item as a chip and the rest behind a "+N" chip; click it to see the full list. An interface with four variants no longer makes its row four lines tall. · area: platform
- **Phones, PC captures and voice lines get their own monitoring profile** — a phone (Android mobile, phone agent, cloud Android) now defaults to the new `mobile` profile: blackscreen, freeze and motion stay on, no audio or subtitle service, and the video badge shows live for a game or video instead of staying empty (no screen tree is needed). A host_vnc capture defaults to `pc`. A USB voice modem (`fx_dongle`, the fixnet rack) defaults to the new `fixnet` profile: a telephone has no picture, so the image, audio, subtitle and archiver services are all switched off and only the KPI scan and the per-minute A/V quality score are kept. On a rack where every modem is a `fixnet` device those four services are stopped outright, and because they are recorded as "no device needs this" the host reads **ok** rather than error. Nothing to set; an explicit `MONITOR_PROFILE` still wins. On an adb phone the badge names the app in the foreground (for example "Clash of Clans"), read at most every 30 s and never while a script or take-control is driving the phone. · [TASK-151](../tasks/TASK-151-fixnet-monitor-profile.md) · area: monitoring
- **Edit node and Edit edge dialogs are easier to read** — verifications and actions are one line each (reference or key, timeout, threshold, area) and open their editor on click; Run and Delete are the only row icons. Depth, priority, parent, live and description (node) and priority, threshold, final wait and sibling shortcuts (edge) sit in a "Details" line that starts collapsed and shows the values. Empty retry and on-failure lists are an "Add" link instead of an empty box. A node is checked either by its verifications or by its fingerprint: one Verifications / Fingerprint switch shows only the chosen one, the Fingerprint tab says whether a fingerprint is ready, and it holds Regenerate (fingerprint and DOM together) and Open DOM, a dialog with the screenshot, the element boxes and the element list. Run runs whichever is chosen. "Use target node checks" is a switch that greys out the checks it replaces. The Live switch is gone: Localize's live node is now simply the node named `live` in each tree. In plain words: the two editors show what a node or edge does at a glance · area: testing

- **`IsStandby` verification and HDMI signal state** — scripts and nodes can check that a box is asleep (`IsStandby`, or `awake: true` for the opposite) with the other video verifications; it reads the HDMI controller's new `get_signal_state()` (the dongle's HDMI flag, else the monitor's no_signal / blackscreen). The grid tile and stream modal keep showing the real picture with the "No Signal" chip (the picture is never covered, so what an operator sees is what a verification reads). · area: monitoring

- **Background images** — the app can show an image behind every page instead of the flat page colour, set separately for light and dark: the Theme choice (Light, Dark or System, which follows your computer) decides which one you see and edit. Settings menu (or Settings → Appearance) → Background: None (the default, the app looks exactly as before), the two images made for the current mode (dark: Nebula and Amber; light: Dawn and Contour), or Your image. The pencil on that row opens Edit background, whose Library button opens a grid of 23 ready-made photos and artworks (landscapes, space, abstract) usable in both modes, which changes the page live behind it (Cancel puts it back, Save keeps it): the image tint (Theme follows your Colours choice, or pick a fixed colour; it never changes the Colours setting), the image transparency (0-90 %), and Upload (then Replace or Remove) for your own 16:9 picture, 2560 × 1440 recommended, up to 15 MB, compressed in the browser to WebP and kept on this device. The same dialog sets the colour of the navbar menu icons: Default (the text colour), Theme (gold on Gold), one of the theme's palette colours or your own colour. The card transparency (0-60 %) is set in Edit your theme instead, where the preview cards show the image behind them and the app changes live (Cancel puts it back). Reset goes back to None and default icons. An image that does not suit the mode (a dark picture in light mode) gets an extra veil so page titles stay readable; dialogs stay solid. In plain words: each mode can wear its own wallpaper, tinted as you like · [TASK-136](../tasks/TASK-136-theme-backgrounds.md) · area: platform

- **No-signal read from the capture dongle** — MacroSilicon MS2130 HDMI grabbers show black (or the box's own bars) when HDMI is gone, and V4L2 cannot tell. The monitor now reads the dongle's HID signal flag (`shared/src/lib/utils/hdmi_signal.py`, register `0xF6E9`, verified on stbv1 waking from standby) and raises `no_signal` instead of `blackscreen`/`freeze` when it reports no HDMI; dongles it does not know fall back to the image detector. Colour bars the box sends in standby are now also caught (edge limit 2.0 to 4.0). The udev rule that lets the monitor read it (`71-vpt-hid-signal.rules`) is installed by every deploy (`ensure_host_prereqs.sh`) and by `install_host.sh`. · area: monitoring

- **A host-only deploy no longer queues server deploys** — `update_core.sh` claimed its lease without a kind, so the lease client made it `kind=server` and a deploy that only restarts `vpt-host` (`--host`, `--hosts`, restart-only) blocked every `server` deploy until it was done, including while it waited for a device lease. A run with no server and no frontend part now claims `--kind host`: it still waits for any lease on the same host or its devices and for other deploys of that host, but server deploys no longer queue behind it. Runs that include the server or frontend, the Windows claim, and a host that also runs a secondary server (for example a Pi) are unchanged. In plain words: restarting a host stops holding up the server deploy · area: platform

- **Localize names the Home tabs in every menu language** — a tab bar was recognised by where its red underline sits, and a long label (French "Enregistrements", "Films & Séries") pushes every tab after it to the right by about 0.036 of the width, past the 0.03 tolerance: four of the seven Home tabs were not recognised on a French box (measured on the operator's set-top box, German 6/9, English 6/9, French 5/9 frames named). The fingerprint of a tab bar now also stores which tab of the bar is focused and how many tabs the bar has (`focus.tab_index`, `focus.tab_count`, read from the text blocks above the underline, icons count); when both sides have it the position in the bar decides, and the underline x only has to be in the same neighbourhood. Older fingerprints without the position keep the x match. A lone candidate on a tab bar whose focused tab matches is no longer left to the AI second opinion because the rotating hero banner moved the screen hashes (it was reported at 0.6 before). Same frames afterwards: German, English and French all 9/9, no frame named wrong in any language. In plain words: switching the box menu to French no longer makes Localize lose the Home tabs · area: testing

- **Monitoring profile badge on device tiles and in the stream modal** — each Device Control tile shows a short profile name (TV, Lite, PC, Captions…) in its header next to the device name, so devices of the same model can be told apart; it is hidden above 5 columns in a custom grid. The stream modal title carries the same badge, and the name tooltip in both places now reads "name - host - model - profile". Hovering the badge gives a one-line meaning; clicking it opens a card with the description, the services the profile runs (with their real unit names, e.g. vpt-monitor-img) and a collapsed list of the devices using it. Settings has the same card beside a one-line list of all profiles. · area: monitoring


- **Monitor records where the picture moved** — each analysed frame's `motion` now has `mask` (which of the 16 cells of a 4×4 grid changed) and `bbox` (the changed area as frame fractions), so a picture-in-picture in the bottom-right, a carousel band or full-screen live TV can be located, not only counted. · area: monitoring
- **STB host USB devices pinned to their physical port** — capture dongles, their audio and the USB IR dongle are named after the USB port they sit in (`DEVICEn_VIDEO=/dev/stb<n>`, `DEVICEn_VIDEO_AUDIO=plughw:stb<n>,0`, `DEVICEn_IR_PATH=/dev/lirc-stb<n>`), so a reboot or replug no longer points a `DEVICE` at the wrong box. `setup/local/linux/backend_host/pin_usb_ports.sh N=PORT … irN=PORT` writes the udev rules and the `.env` keys in one step; applied on vpt-pi1 and vpt-mf1. A replugged IR dongle in the same port comes back under the same name. · area: device-control
- **Game reports show the AI usage and no longer carry task numbers** — every game that calls a model (2048, Pokémon, Super Kirby Clash, Clash of Clans vision player) now ends its report summary with one AI block: provider and model, calls (failed, on fallback), total tokens in / out, cache read and cost when known, then one line per AI step with tokens in / out and the round-trip time in ms. The same lines are printed in the run log and stored in the run metadata (`ai_usage`). A game with no model says `AI: none`. Tetris, Flappy Bird and the Clash of Clans menu bot no longer print `TASK-NN` in their report titles or script description. In plain words: you can see what the AI used and how long each answer took · area: testing
- **Pokémon run shows its steps live** — The Live run view of the Pokémon game now lists each milestone, heal, training stop and gym leader as it happens. Before, it showed only "Navigation step" until the run ended. · area: entertainment
- **Pokémon long-play plays on any free web host** — the save, its memory, backups and stall reports now live in one store on codebox, and a dispatcher plays every 15 minutes on the first web host that is free (host-clone-1, then host-clone-2): it takes the host's lease, copies the save there, runs the game, and brings everything back before the next slot. A host held by someone else no longer skips play, a failed copy-back never replaces the good save, and two runs of the same save cannot overlap. A host is added with one line. In plain words: Pokémon keeps playing when its usual machine is busy · TASK-33 · area: entertainment

- **Pokémon long-play: a stall is caught in 5 minutes and fixed by an agent** — the stuck detector stops a run after 5 minutes of game time without a milestone, a new map or a new square (was 30 minutes, and walking in circles on known squares no longer hides a stall); training and the Safari Surf hunt, which end themselves, do not count. Within a minute of a stall a watcher on codebox starts an Opus session that diagnoses it, ships the fix to host-clone-1 and clears the lock, so play resumes on the next scheduled run instead of waiting for a person. A story that ends only because goals were skipped (Surf or Strength deferred) is a stall too, so skipping a goal can no longer pass for finishing the game. · area: entertainment
- **Docs: one layout for every section** — Security Reports and API Reference now open inside the docs page (left menu, title bar) like every other section, and the left menu keeps only the section you are reading open. · area: platform
- **Targets dropdown shows the devices** — The All Targets menu is taller and split in two: a host list on the left (one row per host, names never wrap, long ones are cut) and that host's devices on the right, with a whole-host checkbox. Before, the wrapping host pills filled the whole menu and no device was visible. · area: device-control
- **Tetris showcase: profiles, vision mode, predictive loop (TASK-51 G1–G4)** — `game/game_tetris` now reads the board from a captured frame (`--perception vision`) with the JS state as the oracle (a `vision_match_pct` KPI tracks the reader's accuracy over the run), plans the next piece from a deterministic prediction (`--predict`, so per-piece time stops including capture lag), and accepts a game profile (`jakesgordon` for the web, `guideline`/SRS for the planned Switch and Android UIs) with kick-aware reachability. `--userinterface auto` resolves the right `tetris-*` tree from the device model. The shared `VisionReader` reads a court by ROI + cell saturation and names the next piece by its 4-cell shape (colour never enters the lookup), so jakesgordon's flat colours, Tetris 99's brighter ones, and GB greys all map to the same name. `--perception vision` plays ≥ 80 pieces locally with 100 % vision-match and 0 resyncs; with a 1 s injected frame delay, `piece_ms_p50` stays under 500 ms with the predictive loop · [TASK-51](../tasks/TASK-51-tetris-any-platform.md) · area: ai
- **Stream profiles set the default stream quality** — A host on `stream_local_hd` now streams HD after every stream restart; the low profiles (`stream_local_360p`, `stream_hls_*`) start on low. Set `STREAM_QUALITY=low|sd|hd` in the host `.env` to override the profile. · area: device-control
- **Docs in plain words** — The user docs (Get Started, User Guide, Features, Integrations, FAQ, MCP overview) are rewritten for people who are not engineers: short sentences, what to choose and what you will see. Commands, settings files and internals are still there, behind a new Show technical details switch in the page header (off by default, remembered). · area: platform
- **AI prompt caching on Claude** — Repeated Claude calls now reuse their fixed instructions from Anthropic's prompt cache instead of paying for them every time. The Clash of Clans vision player, which made 3,500 Claude Haiku calls in October without one cache hit, now reads its rules from the cache and pays a tenth of the input price for them. Agent conversations cache their growing history too. Input tokens in AI usage now always count the whole prompt, cached part included, so Atlas on MiniMax no longer shows 0 input tokens. · area: ai
- **Settings: one switch style and an Appearance tab** — Every on/off setting is now the same row: a switch, a title and one line of help that never changes when you flip it. Settings has an Appearance tab with Light / Dark / System cards (System now shows a monitor icon), Reduce motion, and Notify me when my runs finish. AI endpoints show what each provider costs, a Get a key link and the fallback order. · TASK-127 · area: platform
- **Run Tests history** — A one-line summary (running, passed, with failures) above the launch form opens into tiles. Last Executions has status filters with counts, a search box and a time range, and the table shows a results bar, pass rate, duration and how long ago the run started. · TASK-127 · area: testing
- **Docs: left navigation, search and a proper reader** — the documentation has a persistent left tree that follows the navbar's Docs menu (same order, names and icons; a Home entry first; only the current section open; two levels), a full-text search on Cmd/Ctrl+K or "/" across guides, FAQ, API and bug reports, a breadcrumb, icon buttons for Copy link, Download and Print, and a text size choice (S, M, L; S by default), an automatic "On this page" for pages with three or more sections, both side panels collapsible from buttons in the page header (a collapsed panel leaves no strip, the text takes the full width), Copy buttons on code blocks, heading link anchors, Previous / Next at the end of a page and an Edit on GitHub link. Headings show no emoji in the menu, outline or search. · TASK-124 · area: platform
- **noVNC tiles show only the desktop, no "Connected to <host>" banner** — the blue top bar was pure noise inside a device tile and pushed the screen down. It is now collapsed by default and expands only when the status text reports a problem (disconnected, connection closed, failed), so errors are still visible. The `Send CtrlAltDel` control became a small translucent overlay in the top-right corner instead of a full-width bar button (it stays reachable, including on Windows hosts). Applied to the shared `vnc.lite.example` template (Linux and macOS installs) and to the live vpt-windows `vnc_lite.html`. In plain words: the desktop tile is all desktop · area: device-control
- **View popover: Custom-grid steppers are chevrons, not mini diamonds** — the two stacked triangles next to each axis input in the Custom column (cols × rows) of the View popover read as a tiny filled diamond at chip size and lost the up / down meaning. Replaced with MUI `KeyboardArrowUpOutlined` / `KeyboardArrowDownOutlined` icons at 10 px so up / down stays legible at any resolution; the disabled state (at the 1 / 9 axis cap) now uses `P.textMuted` colour plus 0.55 opacity instead of opacity alone, so "unavailable" reads as a colour shift, not as the colour looking away from the keyboard. No change to the axis inputs, the `Custom` label or the 100-device cap. In plain words: the spin buttons in the column / row picker look like spin buttons again. · area: device-control
- **Device Control: the REC tile hover tooltip names the host again** — the preview tile's label dropped its ` - host` suffix in TASK-116 to save room in the one-line header, which left two same-named devices on different hosts indistinguishable from the grid. Hovering the device name now reads `device_name - host_name`, matching the stream modal's title, so the tile is still unambiguous without opening anything. VNC tiles are unchanged (they already show only the host). In plain words: hover a tile to see which host the device sits on · area: device-control
- **TASK-95 §8.8: corrected ceiling for 1080p sources on nipogi + a one-shot VAAPI policy helper.** The earlier "39 streams" estimate in TASK-95 §6 was based on a 720p source assumption; the real FR deployment on nipogi is mostly **1080p H.264 HLS** (16/24 devices tagged 1080p in their name, 2/24 at 720p, 1/24 at 477p, 5/24 on Pluto CDN). Measured per-stream CPU at the lean graph: 0.32 core at 1080p vs 0.12 core at 720p — the 2.7× gap is the 720p → 1080p H.264 decode cost, so the real ceiling is **24 streams software / 44 streams mixed VAAPI**, not 39. New helper `scripts/apply_vaapi_nipogi.sh` writes (or `--unset`s) the recommended VAAPI policy on a backend host (`VAAPI_MODE=full`, `VAAPI_MIN_QUALITY=hd`, `SW_THREADS=1` — the 8.6 mixed-mode recommendation): idempotent with timestamped backup, `--check` mode, restart `vpt-stream` once, the script's clean-up is a `POST /host/system/config` + `sudo systemctl restart vpt-stream` if `--unset` is used. The bench script `scripts/bench_vaapi_modes.py` and the evidence files (`docs/tasks/TASK-95-evidence/*.csv`, `nipogi_vaapi_modes.jsonl`) are restored from `origin/tvlite-nipogi-bench` so the same back-to-back scenario harness is reusable. TASK-95 §6 carries a one-line correction pointer at the 39-stream estimate, and §8.8 captures the 1080p math + the diagnostic (`ps -o pcpu --no-headers -C ffmpeg` summing > 250 % with stale tiles = the 24-stream ceiling is breached). The recommended policy is **applied on nipogi** (2026-10-16, 23 ffmpegs at 0.32 core/stream, all `low` streams running at `speed` 0.95-1.0x, segments 25/10s; `VAAPI_MODE=full` is in the .env, watched `hd` tiles will use the GPU when the operator flips one). Rollback: `scripts/apply_vaapi_nipogi.sh --unset vpt-nipogi` · area: monitoring
- **TASK-95 §8.9: pick the 720p HLS variant where the CDN exposes one (10 of 24 FR channels).** The CDN masters for Euronews (Wurl), TV5Monde, Sony One Blacklist/Favoris, Trace Sport, RMC Découverte, L'Équipe, Equidia, TV Monaco, 20 Minutes TV all expose a 1280x720 HLS variant. New helper `scripts/pick_720p_variants.sh` reads the master playlist locally, picks the 1280x720 variant (handling dynamic CDN variant sets), rewrites the matching `DEVICE<N>_VIDEO=` lines in the host's `.env` (timestamped backup, `--check` mode, restart `vpt-stream` once), and leaves the no-720p / single-variant sources alone. Stdlib-only Python resolver at `scripts/hls_pick_720p.py` returns `OK` / `ALREADY_720P` / `NO_MASTER` / `NO_720P` / `FETCH_ERROR`. After the rewrite on nipogi (2026-10-16): 24 streams at 0.28 core/stream (down from 0.32), 6.75 cores total, 17% idle — headroom for 2-3 more 1080p or 5-6 more 720p streams. All 10 720p streams at 0.98-0.995x speed with 22-25 segments/10s. The 14 channels still on 1080p (Pluto, ParaTV, France 24, Francophonie24, LCP, Monte Carlo) need the §8.8 mixed-VAAPI path for further growth. The new ceiling estimate combining both moves: **35-40 real FR streams at 1.0x**. Rollback: `sudo cp /opt/virtualpytest/backend_host/src/.env.bak.<ts> /opt/virtualpytest/backend_host/src/.env && sudo systemctl restart vpt-stream` · area: monitoring
- **TASK-95 §8.10: max-streams layout — nipogi goes from 24 to 35 real FR streams (16 at 720p, 19 at 1080p).** The 24-stream 720p-picked layout of §8.9 was below the host ceiling (2.4 cores of headroom, 4 broken/throttled streams wasting capacity). Replaced the 4 dead/slow sources (LCP Public Senat, Cine Action/Horreur/One Piece Pluto) with verified 720p alternatives and added 12 new 720p devices — all 16 from `docs/agent/iptv-france-streams/STREAMS.md` (the 137/214 iptv-org FR playlist probed 2026-10-16). The new helper `scripts/apply_max_streams.py` is idempotent, writes the full block (NAME + STREAM_TYPE + VIDEO + VIDEO_FPS + VIDEO_CAPTURE_PATH + VIDEO_STREAM_PATH — the last two were missing for new devices and caused OOM kills on the first run), and skips entries that are already present. A small `run_ffmpeg.sh` change adds per-device `DEVICE<N>_USER_AGENT` support so the BFM family (BFM TV, BFM Business, 11 regional BFM, all reject the default `Lavf/<ver>` UA with 403) can be added later when CPU permits — the iptv-org m3u carries the working UA as `#EXTVLCOPT:http-user-agent=Mozilla/5.0 (X11; Linux x86_64) AppleWebKit/537.36 (KHTML, like Gecko) Chrome/108.0.0.0 Safari/537.36`. Live on nipogi (2026-10-16, after `apply_max_streams.py vpt-nipogi` + restart): **35 streams, 781% CPU, 0% idle, 0.22 cores/stream average, 0.71-0.998x per-stream `speed`**. The 35 is the *practical* ceiling — 19 1080p × 0.32 + 16 720p × 0.20 = 9.28 cores exceeds the 7.6 available, so the host saturates. Two follow-ups to go past 35: drop 4-5 of the slowest 1080p streams and replace with more 720p candidates (ADN TV+, Zee One French, Generations TV 576p, Vosges TV 576p, FIFA+ FR, T18, TV Alsace, TV Mulhouse — net 38-40 streams), or switch the 1080p sources to `VAAPI_MODE=decode_only` per §8.8 (offloads the H.264 decode to the iGPU, expected 50+ streams at 1.0x). Rollback: `sudo cp /opt/virtualpytest/backend_host/src/.env.bak.<ts>.pre-maxstreams /opt/virtualpytest/backend_host/src/.env && sudo systemctl restart vpt-stream` · area: monitoring
- **Windows host service_health stops reporting false "down" on non-English Windows (BUG-0576)** — every Windows host running vpt-host as a Scheduled Task was showing Host API red on the dashboard because the schtasks parser hardcoded English strings ("running", "ready", "queued", "disabled"). On a French box the output is `Statut: En cours`, on German `Status: Wird ausgeführt`, etc. — every non-English Windows host was silently red. The check now uses `Get-ScheduledTask | .State` from PowerShell (returns the English enum regardless of UI locale) as the primary path, with a multi-locale schtasks fallback (English / French / German / Spanish / Italian / Japanese) as defense-in-depth. As a follow-up, `_check_windows_service` now returns `not_installed` instead of `unknown` when no candidate service exists — a service the host never had renders as a gray "not installed" badge, not a red "down". And `VERSION.txt` is now copied to the host project root at install time so `deployed_version` shows the real ref (`current:main-2026.10.16-88297`) instead of `unknown`. In plain words: Windows host cards now show what the host is actually doing · [BUG-0576](../bugs/BUG-0576-2026-10-16-windows-service-health-locale-bug.md) · area: monitoring
- **Windows host_vnc devices now show in the All Targets dropdown and the noVNC iframe is reachable (BUG-0580)** — for any device with , the controller now stores the noVNC URL (, defaulting to ) as  instead of the HLS path, so the dashboard's iframe src resolves to a real noVNC page that pi4's nginx proxies to the host. The frontend's host-pill row in the All Targets dropdown buckets host_vnc devices under their owning host instead of under the bare device name, so a host with one host_vnc device and one other device (e.g. vpt-windows: Host_Windows + Mobile_Emulator) now shows up in the dropdown. And  now answers CORS preflight + credentials, so the cross-origin cookie mint that primes nginx's  gate works from any of the public frontends (virtualpytest.angelstreet.io, <host-server>, the Capacitor mobile shell, gcloudstandalone-api.virtualpytest.com). In plain words: the vpt-windows tile in the device grid now shows the live noVNC desktop, and vpt-windows shows up next to the other hosts in the All Targets filter. · [BUG-0580](../bugs/BUG-0580-2026-10-16-windows-host-vnc-stream-url-and-dropdown.md) · area: monitoring
- **Windows host_vnc devices: noVNC actually connects after BUG-0577 / -0580 (BUG-0582)** — even with the right iframe URL and a minted host-session cookie, the vpt-windows noVNC iframe was still showing 'Something went wrong, connection is closed (code 1005)'. Three more layers were wrong: (1) pi4's `/host/vpt-windows/` nginx block was missing `auth_request` and proxied to vpt-host (6109) instead of vpt-websockify (6080), so the noVNC assets and websocket never reached the right place — now matches the vpt-pi4 / vpt-lenovo1 two-block pattern (regex for gated noVNC paths to 6080, prefix for the rest of the host API to 6109); (2) vpt-windows's noVNC `vnc_lite.html` built `wss://<host-server>/websockify` (no host prefix) so the websocket fell through to vpt-server and got 101'd by Cloudflare with no data — vpt-pi4's `getDefaultWebsockifyPath()` helper has been copied into the Windows `vnc_lite.html` and a `?v=20261016b` cache-bust is appended to all core/* imports to escape the 4-hour browser cache during iteration; (3) TightVNC 2.x advertises `[None, Tight]` in its security-types list but rejects None at the protocol level (`UseVncAuthentication=0` only disables the password check, not the auth-type itself) — `core/rfb.js` `_negotiateSecurity` now iterates a client-preferred order with Tight first. In plain words: the vpt-windows tile in the device grid now shows the actual Windows desktop (a separate connect log entry below the vpt-pi4 Pi-desktop tile), and the All Targets dropdown shows the `vpt-windows` pill on the same row as `vpt-pi4` / `vpt-nipogi` / `vpt-lenovo1`. nginx `-t` clean, `systemctl reload nginx` clean, both vpt-windows files updated in place at `C:\virtualpytest\novnc\` · [BUG-0582](../bugs/BUG-0582-2026-10-16-vpt-windows-novnc-still-closed-after-BUG-0577.md) · area: monitoring
- **`vpt-windows` moves from Docker Desktop to a native Windows install (INFRA.md)** — the section at INFRA.md › `### Tunnel-only hosts — vpt-windows` is rewritten to reflect the 2026-10-16 native install: 5 NSSM services (vpt-archiver, vpt-kpi, vpt-monitor-img, vpt-monitor-audio, vpt-websockify — Session 0, automatic) and 3 Scheduled Tasks (vpt-host, vpt-stream, vpt-emulator — Session 1, `InteractiveToken` so ffmpeg's `gdigrab` and the Android emulator have a real desktop). Install root is `C:\virtualpytest\virtualpytest\` (Python 3.12 venv + 195 packages), .env at `C:\virtualpytest\virtualpytest\backend_host\src\.env`. The bring-up steps now use `install_host_windows.ps1` from the Mac (no more Docker Desktop compose). The nginx wiring on pi4 is documented: a regex block at `^/host/vpt-windows/(vnc_lite\.html|websockify|vnc/|core/|vendor/|include/|app/|utils/)` rewrites to `/$1` and proxies to `192.168.x.70:6080` (websockify, NOT vpt-host 6109) behind `auth_request /server/host-session/authorize;`; a prefix block `/host/vpt-windows/` rewrites to `/host/$1` and proxies to `192.168.x.70:6109` for the WHEP / media paths. The noVNC patches (Tight-auth preference + `getDefaultWebsockifyPath()` + `?v=20261016b` cache-bust on the 27 `core/*` imports) are listed per-file. Five new known-quirk entries replace the Docker-era ones: Avast's `Block (Public)` rules preempting port 6109 (fix is the Ethernet → Private profile, not deleting the rules), ImageMagick's 4.2.3 ffmpeg missing `-fps_mode` (fix is `stream_wrapper.ps1` pre-pending a 2025-01-08 gyan.dev build to `$env:Path` and `run_ffmpeg.ps1` using `-vsync cfr`), TightVNC 2.x's [None, Tight] lie (use the patched rfb.js), the vpt-emulator-Avast interaction (tracked, not blocking the install), and the 4 h browser cache on noVNC assets. In plain words: the vpt-windows section is now a recipe you can re-run on a fresh Windows 11 box without re-discovering any of the 5 gotchas · area: platform
- **Agent chat input aligns with the message column** — the message box and the three action buttons (copy / export / clear) sit inside the same 820 px centred column the messages use, so on a wide chat area the row no longer overshoots the text container. In plain words: the input reads as part of the chat, not as a full-width bar · area: ai
- **Agent chat sidebar is wider, panel sizes balanced** — the conversation history opens at 300 px (was 240) and the Target panel at 380 px (was 320), so the chat column starts wider and all three panels feel balanced on a wide screen. Chat-item titles in the sidebar drop from 0.85 rem → 0.8 rem with normal weight and the section labels lose their bold + wide letter-spacing, so the list reads as a list and not as a header bar · area: ai
- **Drop the dormant `workspaces.project_tags` field (BUG-0558)** — the column was added in Phase 2 of the workspaces feature but no code ever read or wrote it for filtering. Project scoping lives on the project's own scope tag (TASK-65 G9 + G10) and on `executable_tags`. The column is dropped, the field is removed from the `Workspaces.tsx` form, the API stops accepting it, and the doc stops promising it. In plain words: the workspace form is one field shorter, and project scoping keeps working as before · [BUG-0558](../bugs/BUG-0558-2026-10-16-workspace-project-tags-field-dormant.md) · 🗄 DB migration `setup/db/migrations/20261016e_drop_workspace_project_tags.sql` · area: platform
- **Mods settings cards: centered, no duplicate label, single-row layout** — every installed mod on **Settings > Mods** is now a one-row card (32 px icon tile on the left, the mod name + a 1-line description centered, switch on the right) so 20+ mods fit on a single page without scrolling; the human name is the only label (the `mod.id` slug like `online-users` that used to sit under the title is gone — no more "Online Users · online-users" double-label); a small gold dot at the top-right lights up when a mod is On for an at-a-glance count of active mods; hover lifts the card 1 px and tints its border gold (same gold-on-hover as the rest of the app). In plain words: the Mods page reads like a list and a single name is shown once · area: platform
- **Top bar hover: per-user picker in the settings panel, line only on the bar** — the top-bar hover ships as three preconfigured styles selectable per user from the **Settings** panel (the row under Language), not from the colour theme. A theme may still ship a default via `theme.uiOptions.navHover`; the user setting wins. **underline** (the default — a 2 px accent line that scales in over 320 ms cubic-bezier 0.65, 0, 0.35, 1, from the left edge, line only — no background wash), **bracket** (two corner reticles — top-left + bottom-right — that draw inward over 260 ms), and **magnetic** (the item lifts -2 px with a soft inset accent ring + drop shadow, 220 ms) all follow the same six rules in `docs/agent/platform/UIUX.md` §"Reference · Hover patterns": colour-only active marker, no font-weight change, no ripple, no reflow, single accent, caret rotates. The AppBar's `root` override now uses the page's background colour, drops the box-shadow, drops the bottom hairline and drops the `backgroundImage` gradient MUI puts behind dark AppBars — the bar reads as one continuous surface and the items as transparent labels, never as a row of differently-sized chips. The panel row shows the three named buttons + a **More** button that opens the colour-theme dialog for fine-grained control. In plain words: the bar feels like one motion and one surface, and the picker lives next to Theme/Colours/Font/Language where the user expects it · area: platform
- **Localize on the video, and cheap when nothing moves** — the device tile and the stream modal show the screen the monitor recognises at the top-right of the video (`home · 96 %`, `Live video`, `No signal`; nothing when it does not know). On a still screen the monitor no longer re-runs the matcher every second: a frame identical to the last matched one keeps its answer, one changed tile (a focus move) re-checks it at once, a still screen is re-checked every 30 s, and a menu with a playing trailer behind it (the only change is the trailer that was already playing) is re-checked every 20 s while any change beside it, or a popup over it, re-checks at once. In plain words: you see where each box is without opening Monitoring, and the Pi spends its CPU only when the picture changes · TASK-115 · area: monitoring
- **Localize names the right screen far more often** — on the Localize benchmark (403 frames) it is right 93 % of the time instead of 52 %: a menu tab that matches only one screen is answered without asking the AI, look-alike screens with the focus on another tile are told apart, a popup over a page is answered as a popup, and a slow live scene (a presenter) is answered as live. The button, the monitor and MCP still give the same answer. In plain words: AI exploration and goto checks can trust "where am I?" without sending a screenshot · TASK-110 · area: ai
- **Localize benchmark measures real screens** — `bench.py harvest-nodes` keeps live frames of a screen after a goto that passed, so drift is measured, not only each node's own screenshot; `scripts/localize/refingerprint.py` rebuilds old fingerprints from their screenshots (with a backup) and `scripts/localize/recapture.py` recaptures a whole interface on a device. · TASK-110 · area: ai
- **One scope picker in the header** — Server, workspace and project are now one fixed-width control: the server name plus a filter badge when a workspace or project is picked. Create and manage projects from the Projects entry in the user menu. · area: platform
- **One typography scale everywhere, with a Size knob** — every label in the web UI reads its size from one role table (`frontend/src/constants/typography.ts`): the navbar item, page title, card title, button label, table header, body, caption and micro-meta are named slots, and consumers do `sx={{ ...t.slot('cardTitle') }}` instead of `sx={{ fontSize: '0.875rem' }}`. MUI's `h1`-`h6`, `subtitle1/2`, `body1/2`, `caption`, `overline` and `button` map to the scale in one place, so existing `<Typography variant="...">` consumers follow without edits. Settings gains a **Size** row (Compact / Default / Large) that rescales the whole UI by one multiplier (0.9375 / 1 / 1.125) via `--app-font-scale`. A `no-literal-font-size` ratchet in `scripts/ui-check.mjs` blocks new `sx={{ fontSize: <literal> }}` files; PR-B migrates the 280 violations it seeded today. In plain words: every place a label can live has one named slot, change one number to move all of them · TASK-100 · area: platform
- **Settings dropdown: minWidth floor, More→Less label, segment density S/M/L** — three quick polish items on the same menu: the paper width was 240 px while the PreferencesRows footer is 292 px so the third option in every row clipped past the right edge (now the Settings dropdown passes `minWidth={296}` and the other five keep the 240 baseline — switching language still does not resize them); the **More** disclosure label did not flip after expanding, so the user only saw the chevron move (now it reads "Less" while open); and the segment buttons in the Theme/Colours/Font/Size/Language/Navbar rows were hard-coded at 32 px with no user choice, so a new **Density** row sits between Colours and More with three options (S = 26 px, M = 28 px — the new default, one notch smaller than before, L = 32 px) persisted in `localStorage` under `virtualpytest:ui:segmentDensity`. Density is a per-user preference and intentionally independent from the existing Size row (Size = how big the whole UI is; Density = how dense this preference list is). New i18n keys: `Less` (fr `Moins`, de `Weniger`) and `Density` (fr `Densité`, de `Dichte`). In plain words: every row fits, the disclosure names itself, and you can pick a denser preference list without making the rest of the UI smaller · [BUG-0572](../bugs/BUG-0572-2026-10-16-preferences-dropdown-minwidth-and-density.md) · area: platform
- **Split view page picker is compact and collapsible on sections** — the dropdown under the sidebar-split top-bar button (and the in-pane page button) renders each page row at 28 px (dense MUI sizing) with a tighter 28 px icon gutter so the visible page count roughly doubles; every section header — Recent, Top, AI, Test › Build, Monitoring, … — is now clickable and toggles its rows in and out, with a chevron rotating 90° as a cue and `aria-expanded` set for screen readers; all sections are expanded by default on first open, and a closed-and-reopened menu keeps the user's last collapse so they can compare two pages without the picker blowing back up.
