In plain words: the picker fits the nav on one screen, and you only see the section you opened · TASK-118 · area: platform
- **Picture-in-picture is draggable and collapsible** — the floating device stream (and remote) can now be dragged anywhere on the page from its header; the offset persists in `localStorage` so the placement survives a reload, and a smaller window can never reopen the panel clipped off-screen (`clampPosition` re-centres a panel wider than the viewport minus twice the 8 px margin). A new **Collapse picture-in-picture** entry in the top-bar menu hides the stream + remote into a small draggable chip with a Show button, and a small "X" pinned to the panel header collapses with one click without opening the menu. Buttons inside the header still fire — minimize / expand / collapse / take-control / release / hide-remote — because the drag handler short-circuits on `closest('button, [role="button"], [data-no-drag]')`. In plain words: a narrow window no longer hides the picture, and the user can park the panel wherever they like · TASK-114 · area: device-control
- **Platform update asks the database VM for the backup** — step 1 no longer runs `pg_dump` on the server (the server's `pg_dump` 15 refuses a PostgreSQL 17 database). It asks the database VM, through its VM agent, to run the nightly backup script now, waits for the file to be reported (up to 20 minutes) and shows `Database · vpt_postgres_….dump · 45M · NFS copy yes`; a failing script shows its last log lines. The new prerequisite **Database backup on request** is OK when the agent answers or the last nightly backup is under 24 hours old (then step 1 reuses it and shows its age). The agent action is opt-in: it needs `--action db-backup` and a token, accepts only the server's IP, and can only run that one script, at most once every 5 minutes. In plain words: the update now takes a real, current database backup made by the machine that owns the database, and tells you exactly what to run if that is not set up · [Docs](../user-guide/update-setup.md#db-backup) · TASK-109 · area: platform
- **Platform update guide: seven short sections** — the guide is now one page with exactly seven sections (Prerequisites to Close), each with what happens, **You do** (three short bullets) and **If it fails**; everything else (the per-check set-up, backup restore, maintenance windows, deploy order) sits in a collapsible **More details** inside its step. A sticky **On this page** panel lists the seven steps and follows your scrolling (a small **Steps** button on a narrow screen), and the page text is one size smaller. Any doc can switch the panel on with `<!-- toc: h2 -->` and the smaller text with `<!-- compact -->` at its top; a link to an anchor inside a closed block opens it first. The Prerequisites dialog links now open the matching check on this page, and the old set-up guide is a one-line pointer. In plain words: the guide shows only what you have to do, and the long explanations stay one click away · [Docs](../user-guide/platform-update.md) · TASK-109 · area: platform
- **Platform update: seven steps, with a backup** — the steps are now Prerequisites, Backup, Bundle, Deploy, Migrations, Testing and Close; the Services step is gone because the deploy already updates and restarts every machine, and each deployment card keeps that machine's result. **Run checks** runs the prerequisite checks and lists them on the page (the step is done when no required check is KO; the lease and paused-queue tick boxes are removed). **Back up** asks the database VM for a fresh backup and saves the `.env` of the server, of every host and of the frontend VM in one timestamped folder on the server, readable only by the service user and never offered as a download; **I snapshotted the VMs** then ends the step, and the database VM makes the database backup on request (see the next entry). The versions before the deploy are now kept in the update itself, so the from → to survives a restart or another browser. An update started before this change must be closed and restarted. In plain words: back up first, then update; it takes seven short steps, and a restore from the backup is in the guide · TASK-109 · area: platform
- **Platform update page is much shorter** — every step card has one short subtitle and short checkbox and button labels instead of paragraphs; the guide behind "How this step works" holds the explanations. A line under the title shows `installed → target` with a warning when machines run different versions, each deployment card shows that machine's version before → after, and the closing message records the from → to. The classic source check only runs when you open its Advanced section, so the unused storage-path error is gone. In plain words: you read less, see which version goes to which, and no longer get an error about a path nobody uses · area: platform
- **Monitoring overlay shows WebRTC health and uses one font** — over a WebRTC picture the Monitoring overlay gets a **STREAM · webrtc** block: FPS and dropped frames, **Freeze** (share of the last 2 s frozen, plus the total count), **Network** (NACK share, jitter, round trip) and bitrate / lost packets, coloured orange or red past a threshold. The overlay's labels, headers and values now all use the same monospace font instead of mixing the theme font with monospace. In plain words: you can see from the live view whether choppy video is the network, not the host · area: device-control
- **Go to node from Take Control** — the Take Control header gets an **Actions** button (AI Agent, Go to node); both panels open in the same spot over the video and the remote stays visible. In Go to node you pick the user interface, variant and node (the node search always stays visible); the node search and the gold Run button share one row, and the path is shown as start → end below it, from the device's current node when known. Verification runs at the end only. **Show steps (N)** lists the steps with the ETA, and each step expands to its actions. Run uses the same progress, failure reports and retry as the user interface page (same backend, no new API). In plain words: send the device to any screen from the live view, see the route and how long it takes, and keep the remote in reach. · area: device-control
- **Run script from Take Control** — the **Actions** menu of the Take Control header gets **Run script** next to Go to node. The panel opens over the video: pick Scripts, Testcases or Virtual (search by name, only items that fit the device), then fill the parameters — the user interface and variant default to the device's preferred ones and appear only when the script has them, required parameters sit inline two per row, the rest under **Show N more parameters**. One gold Run starts it (same start, abort and maintenance check as Run Tests, no new API), the panel shows the live step and a Stop button, then Passed / Failed with the duration, **Report**, **Logs** and **Run again** (reruns at once with the same parameters). The last script and its values are remembered per device in the browser. A busy device is refused with a message instead of being queued. In plain words: run any script or test from the live view without leaving it · area: device-control
- **Bluetooth remote panel opens instantly on a connected STB** — the panel remembers the last BLE status of each device for the browser session, so opening it again (or re-taking control) shows the remote at once instead of "Loading BLE status…"; one background check confirms the link and a real drop still shows within the usual 4 s. In plain words: no loading flash before the remote on an STB that is already connected · area: device-control
- **Phone remote: cleaner layout** — arrow keys form a D-pad with OK in the middle and Back / Home / Menu beside it; a Type Text field with one Enter key that types the text then presses Enter (or presses Enter alone when empty); Refresh Apps is an icon next to the app list; Power and the manual rotate button are gone (orientation is detected automatically). In plain words: the phone remote is easier to read and type with · `0305ffbbc` · area: device-control
- **Phone app launcher shows readable app names** — the Android phone remote's App Launcher lists one short name per app (Outlook, Notes, Netflix…), sorted A–Z, with the full package name in a tooltip on hover, instead of truncated `com.google.android.…` rows. In plain words: you can read the app list in the narrow remote panel · area: device-control
- **Phone remote types text and sends Enter / arrow keys** — the Android phone remote gains a key row (← ↑ ↓ → OK Enter) and a Type text field with Send; in a group-control tile both go to every phone, each with its own ✓. In plain words: type a search once and every phone in the group gets it · `ba043e73c` · area: device-control
- **WEB opens faster and comes back faster after a quality click** — on vpt-pi1 the rpitest nginx wrote one access-log line per request straight to the slow SD card, so a WHEP setup request waited 2.5–15 s behind it; the log is now buffered (POST 0.03–0.3 s; stbv1+ plays 1.9 s after the click). A LOW/SD/HD/HD+ click restarts that capture's ffmpeg: `run_ffmpeg.sh` dropped a fixed 2 s pause after the kill and its audio probe reads 0.1 s instead of 1 s, so the stream is back in 4.0–4.3 s instead of 5.8–6.2 s. vpt-mf1 gets WEB over the public IP (gateway UDP 8191), no tunnel. In plain words: the WEB picture appears in about two seconds and a quality change blacks it out for about four, not six · area: device-control
- **Device stream stays live when opening its control window** — opening a normally-live HLS or VNC preview now moves the same mounted player into the full control window instead of stopping it and creating a second connection. Playback, buffering and the VNC session continue through the transition; mute, quality changes, WEB mode and VNC interaction remain synchronized, while archive and restart modes still switch to their purpose-built players. In plain words: opening a device no longer reloads its live picture · `a46287179` · area: device-control
- **WEB is its own on/off switch next to LOW / SD / HD (HD+)** — the resolution stays highlighted while WEB is on, so it is clear that WebRTC plays at that resolution (the host runs one encode for both); picking another resolution keeps WEB on. Before, WEB replaced the highlight and silently kept whatever resolution ran last, often LOW. In plain words: you can see and choose the resolution of the WEB stream · area: device-control
- **HD+ is back in the stream selector on hosts with hardware encode** — hosts that report VAAPI H.264 encode (`system_stats.hardware_encode`, e.g. vpt-mf1) show LOW / SD / HD / **HD+** / WEB; the Raspberry Pis keep LOW / SD / HD / WEB. HD+ is the 1280×720 @ 30 fps buffered stream. In plain words: the Minisforum gets its smooth HD+ stream back · area: device-control
- **WebRTC live view no longer encodes the picture twice** — `run_ffmpeg.sh` (v4l2) now sends the one HLS H.264 encode to both HLS and the MediaMTX RTSP input with ffmpeg's `tee` muxer, instead of a second 720p x264 encode for WebRTC. VAAPI hosts encode with `-bf 0` (no B-frames, needed by WebRTC; validated at 60 fps on vpt-mf1). WEB now shows the same resolution as the selected LOW/SD/HD/HD+ tier. MediaMTX down no longer stops HLS (`onfail=ignore`); restart `vpt-stream` after restarting `vpt-mediamtx` to bring WEB back. Measured on vpt-pi1: capture ffmpeg CPU LOW 35–40% → 19–22%, HD 50% → 35%, Pi load ~9 → ~5.5 (TASK-79). In plain words: the Pis spend much less CPU on live video, and HLS keeps working when the WebRTC relay is down · area: device-control
- **Live stream keyframes follow the frame rate above 10 fps** — `run_ffmpeg.sh` keeps a 0.4 s keyframe interval for every H.264 encode (HLS on VAAPI and libx264, and the RTSP copy for WebRTC): 8 frames at 20 fps, 24 at 60 fps, unchanged 4 at 10 fps or less. Without it a 60 fps grabber sent 15 keyframes a second and the picture blurred at the same bitrate. Taken from the 2026-10-10 vpt-mf1 60 fps validation (TASK-79). In plain words: hosts can run captures at 60 fps without a blurry stream · area: device-control
- **Targets filter gets host toggle pills** — the Device / Dashboard "All Targets" dropdown now shows a tri-state pill per host (`host-name N/M`) at the top of the menu, just below the existing Select-all / Clear row. Click an empty pill to select every device on that host; click a filled pill to deselect them all; partial selections show the count (2/6) on the accent border, and a fully selected host fills that count. There is no checkbox on the pill. The pill always acts on the host as a whole — independent of the search box — so "give me everything on `vpt-pi1`" is one click. Hidden when fewer than two hosts have more than one target. The menu stays within 360px, so further hosts wrap onto the next line instead of widening the panel off the screen. No new endpoint, no new persisted state. In plain words: pick a whole rack without scrolling through devices one by one, and the host row stays on screen · area: device-control
- **Stream overlay shows live FPS and host load, and says how old it is** — in monitoring mode the device stream gains a bottom-left block: playback FPS, dropped frames and how far behind the live edge the player is, re-read every second off the video element, plus the host's CPU, RAM, 1-minute load and capture write rate with the state of `vpt-stream` and `vpt-monitor`. The two halves are labelled apart because they update on different clocks: the stream numbers are live, the host numbers come from the 60-second heartbeat, so that row carries its age and stops showing anything once the heartbeat is older than 90 s rather than leaving a healthy-looking reading next to a stream that just froze. Load average stays neutral (no core count to judge it against), unmeasurable values show '—' and never 0, and the VNC path shows only the host half because an iframe has no video to measure. No new endpoint, no extra fetch. In plain words: you can tell whether the picture froze because the host was busy or because the player stalled · area: device-control
- **Heatmap page: clearer header and timeline** — the header is now the standard page title with the frame being shown, the All / OK / KO switch, a labelled Latest button and one gold Generate report button, followed by Devices / Active / Missing / Incidents tiles (Incidents filters to KO); the old `Frame N / 1440` counter and `Incidents detected` pill are gone. The timeline sits in its own card with previous / next buttons around a fixed-width time readout, a gold slider, one clickable segment per hour (the selected hour in the frame's status colour) and a single Yesterday | Today divider at midnight instead of repeating the day under all 24 hours. Data analysis shows its device and incident counts as pills. In plain words: the heatmap page reads like the rest of the app and the time you are looking at is obvious · area: monitoring
- **Monitoring card back in the top-left corner** — the live analysis card (time, blackscreen, freeze, macroblocks, audio, screen, motion) returns to the top-left of the stream, where it was before TASK-79 moved it top-right. In plain words: the card is where operators expect it again · area: monitoring
- **CI/CD report cards filter the table** — on the API smoke, Frontend Component Tests and VPT Self-Test Smoke reports, clicking Passed / Failed / Probed / Skipped shows only those rows; a second click or Called/Total shows all (VPT Smoke gains the cards). pytest-html and Playwright reports already filter through their own controls; Lint, Typecheck and Web Script reports have no rows. Applies to runs from the next CI run on. In plain words: click the Failed card to see just the failures · area: testing
- **The floating Ask AI button has a subtle, slow glow pulse** — the bottom-left `Cmd+K` Fab was static at rest, so it blended into long pages and a first-time user could miss it. The accent-coloured shadow now breathes between `~25%` and `~47%` alpha on a 3.2 s ease-in-out cycle (no scale, so the click target stays steady; hover pauses the pulse and runs the existing lift; `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` disables the animation entirely). In plain words: the button reads as alive without being distracting · area: ai
- **Postman: the connect screen GitLab has, and a setup form instead of a wall of text** — Plugins → Postman answered an unconfigured server with a wide amber warning carrying four setup steps on the page, and its Setup button opened the whole operator README as monospace text in a full-screen dialog. It now shows the same centred card as the GitLab page — the real Postman mark, **Connect to Postman**, one gold **Setup** button — and that button opens a small **Connect Postman** dialog: one password field for the Postman API key, a link to Postman's API-key page, and the workspace it will connect to. New routes `GET/POST/DELETE /server/postman/settings`; the key is stored outside the deploy tree (`state_path('postman_settings.json')`, mode 600) so a deploy cannot disconnect a server, is never sent back to a browser, and is **proven against Postman before the page reports success** — a bad key says so instead of leaving an admin believing it worked. A key saved here wins over `POSTMAN_API_KEY`, so an install that only ever used the env var keeps working unchanged. Saving needs the new `plugins.postman:manage` permission (admins always have it); without it the card says an admin has to connect the server, like GitLab's. The page picks up the shared title, card border and palette, Refresh only appears once there is something to refresh, and the workspace cards carry the Postman mark instead of a generic API glyph. No migration. In plain words: an admin can connect Postman from the page with a key, and everyone else gets one clear card and a sentence instead of a screen of setup instructions. · area: integrations
- **Every docs section opens with its menu icon, and docs pages open instantly** — each top-level Docs page now shows a header with the same gold icon as its Docs menu entry (the emoji in the Release Notes and Bug Tracker titles are gone), and a page you have already visited paints at once from memory while it refreshes quietly in the background. In plain words: the docs look consistent with the menu, and revisiting a page no longer flashes a reload · area: platform
- **Host card Services grouped by what the device profiles need** — the Services section of a Dashboard host card now has three groups: Core (Stream, Host API, Remote view = VNC + Websockify), Monitoring (Image, Audio, Subtitle, with how many devices use each) and Optional (Archiver, KPI, Emulator and the rest). A service is **down** (red) only when a core service or a device profile needs it and it is not running; a service no device needs, or any service on a `tv_lite` host, is **idle** (grey, with the reason); services that are not installed collapse into one expandable "Not installed (N)" list. The collapsed header reads `8 running · 1 down · 1 idle`, a host with mixed profiles shows `5 tv_app · 1 ad_meter · 1 tv_lite` under its name, status is a dot + word (no coloured pills), and logs / start / stop / restart moved into a ⋮ menu per row. The host's stats now carry `monitor_profile_services` ({profile: services it turns on}) so the card has it on first paint. In plain words: a service that is off on purpose no longer looks broken, and red means something really needs fixing · TASK-90 · area: platform
- **The docs pages filter by release, and the docs menu is usable again** — the Bugs group in the documentation dropdown listed one row per shipped report: 459 of them, in a menu capped at 400px, which buried every other section and made the dropdown useless on the page that needed it most. It now lists the Overview only, and a report is still one click away — every one of them is linked from the index on the page. `/docs/bugs` and `/docs/release_note` also opened as one scroll of accordions (the bug tracker's Unreleased alone is ~300 reports) with no way to say "just build 9369", so each page now has a dropdown beside **Download PDF** that filters the page to one list — All, Unreleased, or any shipped build. It opens on **All**, so the page still reads as the whole document; picking a build narrows it to that one, and the choice lives in the URL (`/docs/release_note?list=build-9369`), so it survives a reload and can be sent to someone else. A link to a build that no longer exists falls back to the whole page rather than hiding everything but one list. The dropdown opens flush with its own button and has no label in front of it — the value already says what it is. In plain words: the docs page still shows everything by default, and when you want one release you get just that one · area: platform
- **Videos: one top video, a dropdown and 3 thumbnails, only 4 loaded** — the Videos page (and the README) used to list all 26 videos with a thumbnail each. Now the app page shows the best-of "VirtualPyTest in 60 seconds" at the top as a poster that becomes the YouTube player when you press play (so the player is only downloaded on demand), a **Choose a video** dropdown (text only) that picks which video sits at the top, and the 3 newest as thumbnails; the choice is in the link (`#v=<id>`). Videos are numbered by publication (oldest = 1; `#26` is the newest), the dropdown is alphabetical with the number after the title, category badges (Device, AI, Test, Scripts, Stream) filter it (`#c=ai&v=<id>`), and the main video plus the 3 stacked thumbnails (image only) fit on one screen without scrolling. One data file, `docs/videos/videos.json` (newest first), feeds both: `scripts/gen_readme_videos.py` rewrites the videos block of `README.md` and `docs/videos/README.md` (top video, 3 newest, and a collapsed text list of all; `--check` for CI), and `frontend/scripts/copy-docs.sh` ships it to the app. A new video = one entry at the top of the JSON + run the script. In plain words: the page opens fast with just four pictures instead of twenty-six, and every other video is one click away in the dropdown. · area: platform
- **Screenshots: filter badges and a dropdown** — the Screenshots page now has the same category badges as Videos (Device, AI, Test, Scripts, Stream, with counts), a "Choose a screenshot" dropdown (alphabetical) and a grid of thumbnails that shows only the current filter; a click or the dropdown opens the full image with previous/next, and the filter and the open screenshot are in the link (`#c=<category>&s=<id>`). One file, `docs/screenshots/screenshots.json`, feeds the app page and the README (`scripts/gen_readme_screenshots.py`, grouped by category). In plain words: find a screenshot by topic instead of scrolling a long wall, and share a link straight to one. · area: platform
- **Screenshots page loads in a fraction of the time** — the gallery images are now WebP (a 640 px thumbnail in the grid, the full image on click) instead of 22 MB of PNG; `scripts/screenshot_to_webp.py` makes both files for any new screenshot. In plain words: the Screenshots page opens almost instantly · area: platform
- **Android app presents the owner's client certificate (app 1.0.51)** — when the deployment's host asks for a TLS client certificate (Cloudflare mTLS), the app offers Android's certificate picker once and remembers only the chosen alias; the WebView, the runtime-config and update checks, and the phone agent's Socket.IO link through the public host then present that certificate, its key read from Android's KeyChain on every handshake and never stored by the app. Other hosts asking for a certificate get none, LAN host connections are unchanged, and a declined, removed or revoked certificate clears the alias so the picker shows again. In plain words: a phone holding the owner certificate can use the app from 4G, behind an IP whitelist · area: platform
- **Host & Devices form auto-fills `DEVICEn_VIDEO_AUDIO` from the host's USB topology; Host & Devices form auto-fills DEVICE_VIDEO_AUDIO from the host's USB topology** — the `plughw:N,0` ALSA card used to be the only `DEVICEn_*` value the operator had to look up by hand (SSH into the host, run `arecord -l`, copy the right card number, hope the kernel doesn't reshuffle the cards on the next reboot). The Host & Devices page now shows a "Detected Grabbers" panel that calls `GET /host/system/grabbers` (proxied as `GET /server/settings/host-grabbers?host_name=...`) and lists every UVC node pre-paired with its USB UAC sibling by the same `v4l2-ctl` + `/sys` walk the operator-facing `scripts/detect_grabber.sh` does. One click on `Use` fills `DEVICEn_VIDEO` + `DEVICEn_VIDEO_AUDIO` together on the next free device, and changing the `Video Device` field also auto-fills the audio (the dynamic mapping is the source of truth — the operator-typed `.env` value is overridden whenever a detection exists, and the `.env` only remains in effect when no detection exists for that video). A small caption under the audio field tells the operator which source is in effect. Same-port pairings (the common case) are now fully automatic; cross-port pairings (HDMI audio coming from a different physical source) still work as one-shot manual overrides that get saved to `.env` but revert on the next page load. `backend_host/src/.env.example`, `setup/local/linux/backend_host/install_host.md`, and `docs/agent/devices/STREAM.md` §5 are updated to drop the "look it up by hand" guidance Also: registering a device used to require SSHing into the host to copy the ALSA card number (`plughw:2,0` today, `plughw:4,0` after the next reboot); a new "Detected Grabbers" panel above the device list calls `GET /host/system/grabbers` and shows every UVC node already paired with its USB UAC sibling. One click on `Use` fills `DEVICE_VIDEO` + `DEVICE_VIDEO_AUDIO` together on the next free device, and changing the `Video Device` field also auto-fills the audio (the dynamic mapping is now the source of truth — the operator-typed .env value is overridden by detection whenever a match exists, and the .env remains in effect only when no detection exists for that video). A small caption under the audio field tells the operator whether the value is "auto-detected from USB 3-1" or "from .env". Cross-port pairings (HDMI audio coming from a different physical source) still work as one-shot manual overrides that get saved to .env but revert on the next page load — the common same-port case is now fully automatic. The empty case points the operator at `setup/local/linux/backend_host/setup_permissions.sh` (the host service user must be in the `video`/`audio` groups) · [BUG-0458](../bugs/BUG-0458-2026-10-11-host-settings-audio-never-auto-filled.md) · area: platform
- **Release notes list features by area, with improvements folded away** — each build shows its Features grouped by area (with an area filter), Improvements collapsed per area, and Internal changes only for admins; Compare counts features, improvements, fixes and migrations. In plain words: the page tells you what is new in a minute · TASK-108 · area: platform
- **Grafana alerts now use the four-band severity hierarchy (critical / major / minor / warning), and the digest shows start time / NEW since the previous report / resolved end time** — the old two-band hierarchy marked every `Host stopped reporting`, `Capture or monitor not running`, `Fleet health: device DOWN` and `Platform service down` as **critical**, so every isolated hiccup filled the on-call subject line. The new bands reserve **critical** for one rule only — `vpt-system-down` (every host silent) — and split the rest into `major` (one platform component down or one site's whole fleet down via the new `vpt-fleet-all-hosts-offline` aggregation) and `minor` (single host or device failure). Resource thresholds keep `warning`. The daily / periodic alert digest now carries one line per firing alert with a `start=` time, a `NEW` flag (anything not in the previous digest's firing set, persisted in `.grafana_alert_digest_state.json` next to the script), and a `[was X]` band on RESOLVED lines with their `start=` and `end=` times. The new `scripts/grafana_alerts.py report` subcommand renders the same shape that the `vpt-email` notification template sends, so the email body and the on-demand digest match. In plain words: every alert now has a band that means what it says, and you can compare today's digest with yesterday's in one glance · TASK-116 · area: monitoring
- **Dashboard host card declutter** — on a 24-device host, the per-host card was carrying a URL line, a profile-mix line, a per-device info icon, a per-device IP icon, and a `Services 6 running` caption that repeated the green LED six times. The card now: shows the host's IP only on hover of the host name, drops the `24 tv_lite` caption (the mix is on the Dashboard filter bar), keeps the favourite star as the only thing on the right of every device row, lets the device name fit on one line with an ellipsis, and lines the `Linux (x86_64)` platform label up with the RAM column instead of the card edge. Services use the same `Devices (N)` count style, render the active/down/stuck/idle word on hover of the dot (LED is enough), and lay out **Core | Monitoring** in two columns with **Optional** collapsed by default. In plain words: the same data, half the visual noise. · area: platform
- **Android remote text input cleans up** — placeholder `Element ID (number)` → `Element ID`, `<InputLabel>Select an app...</InputLabel>` → `Select an app` (no trailing dots, no parenthetical hint). The MUI Select stopped looking truncated and the input type stays `number` so the numeric keypad still appears · area: device-control
- **Dashboard Android remote Back / Home / Menu are icon buttons (commit `44db78584`, pulled in here for clarity)** — the three text Buttons in a column took ~150 px of vertical panel space. They are now `<Tooltip>`-wrapped `<IconButton>`s using `ArrowBackIosNew` / `Home` / `Menu` icons in a single horizontal row 32 px tall, matching the D-pad's visual weight · area: device-control
- **Rename / Delete on project detail page** — `/projects/<id>` had no way to rename or delete a project once it was created. The page header now shows a pencil and a trash icon button next to the Executive summary link, both gated on `can_edit` (admins or project owners per `projects_members.role = 'owner'`). Rename opens a small dialog with the 60-char cap; Delete opens the shared red `ConfirmDialog` and on success navigates back to `/projects` after clearing the localStorage `virtualpytest:project_id` entry so the top-bar selector stops claiming the deleted project is still active. The underlying `PATCH /server/projects/<id>` and `DELETE /server/projects/<id>` routes were already enforcing the right ACL — the bug was purely UI. The overview endpoint now also surfaces `permissions: { my_role, can_edit }` so the frontend mirrors the server gate, not its own opinion. · [BUG-0577](../bugs/BUG-0577-2026-10-16-project-detail-no-rename-or-delete-button.md) · TASK-65 · area: platform
- **Group control** — One remote drives several devices at once, and a click on a phone's stream taps every phone in the group. · TASK-75 · TASK-76 · area: device-control
- **Device page layouts** — Pick 12 or 24 devices per page, or your own columns and rows (100 devices a page at most), or a layout that fits the screen. · TASK-75 · area: device-control
- **Favorites** — Star a device, script or test case once and it sits first in every list. · TASK-100 · TASK-99 · 🗄 DB migration `setup/db/migrations/20261014d_executable_favorites.sql`, `setup/db/migrations/20261014b_device_favorites.sql` · area: device-control
- **Timeline** — See a device's whole day as a thumbnail grid, and a timeline inside every test report. · TASK-94 · area: monitoring
- **Quickfilter badge area** — The reserved row under the device-control toolbar used to be profile-only. Pick which categories it shows (Tags / Models / Profiles / Favorites) and in which order, up to 2 lines, with a `+N more` chip when there's overflow. The leading bolt icon marks the row as *Quickfilters* and is the sole entry point to its configure popover, which anchors to the right of the bolt. Same filter store the dropdowns write to, so chips and dropdowns stay in sync. In plain words: you no longer have to fight through every device on the default profile to find your favorites · TASK-120 · area: device-control
- **Maintenance windows** — Declare a window for chosen devices so their alerts stay quiet while you work on them. · area: monitoring
- **Screen memory** — The alert triage learns which screens are harmless and stops raising them. A screen Localize cannot name is registered once per device (a device can opt in with `DEVICE{N}_LOCALIZE_SETTLED=1` so a splash or TV guide with a moving part is read too, not taken for live video) as `unknown_1`, `unknown_2`, … (the same picture is the same screen, even a few pixels apart), the AI names it later, and a name is never replaced, so a tile shows the same name for the same picture on every box. · TASK-84, TASK-159, [BUG-0840](../bugs/BUG-0840-2026-10-22-unknown-screens-named-three-ways-by-seven-writers.md) · 🗄 DB migrations `setup/db/migrations/20261012a_screen_fingerprints.sql`, `setup/db/migrations/20261022e_screen_registry.sql`, `setup/db/migrations/20261022f_screen_prune_guard.sql` · area: monitoring
- **Live run progress** — Hover a running script in Run Tests to see its steps as they happen. · area: testing
- **AI usage and budgets** — See where the AI goes and what it costs, get alerts on failing features and set monthly budgets. The page answers in three clicks at most (TASK-147): an overview with four numbers (spend, calls, failing now, wasted), calls per hour and the calls by provider and by feature; a provider or feature opens with a one-line answer ("56 % of calls · Session manager 27 % · Browser agent 79 % of cost"), who uses it, its models and its calls (All / Failed, repeated failures folded "145×"); a call opens with its result, latency, tokens, cost, why it failed, who asked, and its trace. Lists rank by calls, cost or failures; groups also by user and host; the chart shows calls or spend per hour; the 30-day view names the October storm as an incident; budgets show the month total and every provider spending without a budget. A feature's models show their benchmark pass rate next to calls and cost (tested models nobody used are listed too), and the feature list ranks by quality; benchmark calls are one "Model tests" row with their results; budgets and training data open from the overview. In plain words: "why is this key used so much?" is answered on the second screen · [TASK-147](../tasks/TASK-147-ai-usage-page-review.md) · fixes [BUG-0730](../bugs/BUG-0730-2026-10-20-ai-usage-errors-tab-empty-while-calls-failed.md), [BUG-0731](../bugs/BUG-0731-2026-10-20-ai-usage-two-different-cost-totals-for-one-period.md), [BUG-0732](../bugs/BUG-0732-2026-10-20-ai-usage-30-day-view-swamped-by-the-402-storm.md), [BUG-0733](../bugs/BUG-0733-2026-10-20-ai-usage-saved-by-memory-mixes-avoided-calls-with-cache-discount.md), [BUG-0734](../bugs/BUG-0734-2026-10-20-ai-usage-delta-arrows-compare-useful-against-total.md), [BUG-0735](../bugs/BUG-0735-2026-10-20-ai-usage-top-feature-ranks-by-count-not-cost.md), [BUG-0736](../bugs/BUG-0736-2026-10-20-ai-usage-desktop-table-on-mobile-shows-no-numbers.md) · 🗄 DB migration `20261012e_ai_calls_rollup_times.sql`, `20261012d_ai_budgets.sql`, `20261012c_ai_prices.sql`, `20261012b_ai_calls.sql` · area: ai
- **Agent Dashboard** — One card per AI agent shows its real work, calls, errors and logs. · 🗄 DB migration `setup/db/migrations/20261005a_agent_turns.sql` · area: ai
- **Atlas model picker — pick which link of the fallback chain answers** — the model label next to the agent dropdown is now a dropdown listing every hop of the agent's AI route (primary first, then each fallback, with the profile and endpoint on the line below). Pick one and Atlas uses ONLY that model for the rest of the session; pick "Auto" and the chain runs as usual. `/server/agents/` returns the full `model_chain` (each entry: `profile_id`, `profile_name`, `endpoint_name`, `model`, `role` primary|fallback), the agent manager resolves the pinned profile via `router.resolve_profile` (single hop, no fallback chain), and the socket handler validates the id against `ai_routing_db` (enabled + endpoint allowed for the team) before pinning. In plain words: when the primary is down you can land Atlas on a specific fallback without waiting for the chain to find it · area: ai
- **Mods** — Add-ons you switch on: Run Progress, Picture-in-picture, Split view, Online Users and Blast Radius. · 🗄 DB migration `features/mods/db/001_user_mod_preferences.sql` · area: platform
- **Dashboard widgets** — Mods can add widgets to the Dashboard: Team devices, plus the **Info tiles** mod which ships a palette of small info widgets (clock, weather, and more) that share the `InfoTile` body. The clock follows the browser's timezone (or a place you pick, 12-hour or 24-hour). Weather shows today's temperature, conditions and high/low for that same place, from Open-Meteo, refreshed every 30 minutes. New info tiles (calendar, stocks, …) drop in as a sub-folder and a `widgets: [...]` entry on the bundled mod, not a new mod. · 🗄 DB migration `features/mods/db/002_user_widget_layouts.sql` · area: platform
- **Colour themes** — Pick a colour theme or make your own, with one font and one icon set everywhere. · [Docs](../user-guide/themes.md) · TASK-102 · area: platform
- **Release Notes compare** — See what changes between your build and the latest, then roll the update out. · TASK-106 · area: platform
- **AI game players** — An AI plays Clash of Clans on a hard budget, and Pokémon reports when it is stuck. · [Docs](../user-guide/coc-llm-player.md) · TASK-33 · TASK-100 · area: entertainment
- **Run a script against multiple variants at once** — the Run Tests variant dropdown is a checkbox multi-select producing a composition (e.g. `variant1+variant2`) · `bb0370618` · area: testing
- **Release notes and bug reports are browsable in the app** — a new Docs › Release Note page and linked bug reports · area: platform
- **Voice control of the device you hold (TASK-160)** — In the stream window, Actions > Voice listens continuously and runs what you say on the device you hold under Take Control, never on one you do not hold: it repeats the command back with a beep, runs it, then says "Done", and shows what it heard, what it sent and the last 3 commands. Commands are matched to the device's own listed actions, so it works for any device; a Tello keeps a stricter set (English and French, 100 cm / 90 degrees per command, battery checked, a command naming another drone is refused). There is no confirmation step: say "abort" (or press Abort) to cancel the command being processed and keep listening, and "stop" (or press Stop) to close the microphone and stop the device. Beep and read-back can be switched off. Every spoken command is recorded in the audit trail. Flips and emergency stop stay off for a drone. · area: device-control
- **Projects G11 — create in one flow, then pick a range** — the Create Project dialog now accepts `tags[]` and `members[]` in the same payload (the modal can finish a project without follow-up visits), the project's scope tag is mechanically distinct from user-attached tags (a `0 tags` card actually means 0), and the project page gains a `1d / 2d / 7d / 30d` Grafana-style toggle that drives every tile + the embedded runs table + the trend chart (Active users stays "live in last 5 min" — independent of the range). New `setup/db/migrations/20261015b_projects_tags_is_scope.sql` adds the column; apply AFTER `20261006a` and BEFORE the deploy that brings G11 endpoints live. In plain words: creating a project feels like one action, and a project's "1d" view is one click · area: platform
- **Project in-scope + manual linking API (BUG-0579)** — five new routes under `/server/projects/<id>/` expose what was hidden behind the auto-tag-on-create flow: `GET /in-scope` returns devices, executables (flat peer list of scripts + testcases + virtuals), and campaigns with per-item run count and pass rate, plus totals. `POST/DELETE /link` lets an admin or project owner manually attach or detach an artefact by `{type, id}` — idempotent on both sides. `POST/DELETE /tags/names` matches the device-flag pattern: get-or-create a tag by name and link it as `is_scope=false`, with the scope tag (auto from project name) protected from remove. Every write emits a `[PROJECT_AUDIT]` line; the in-scope endpoint reuses the same flag-intersection and `executable_tags` joins the G9 selector already uses. No schema migration. 26 in-memory tests in `test_projects_in_scope_in_memory.py` cover admin/owner gates, idempotency, scope-tag protection, and the per-item stats contract. Front-end component (`ProjectInScope` for the project page) is a follow-on — the API is the deliverable. · [BUG-0579](../bugs/BUG-0579-2026-10-16-project-in-scope-api-surface.md) · TASK-65 · area: platform

### Bug fixes
- **A live video that fell behind no longer stops with "FFmpeg appears stuck" (BUG-0950)** — the player counted the segments a host had already moved into its 24 h recording as failures, and after ten it gave up and asked for a stream restart while ffmpeg was writing fine (seen on the egg line). The playlist lists about two minutes, the host keeps 20 to 80 seconds of them; a player that lagged behind asked for the deleted ones. It now jumps to the live edge and keeps playing; only a stream whose newest segments are missing too is called stuck. In plain words: a hiccup no longer kills the live video · [BUG-0950](../bugs/BUG-0950-2026-10-26-a-lagging-player-called-a-healthy-ffmpeg-stuck.md)
- **The Pokémon runner no longer freezes on the stair it is standing on (BUG-0947)** — on Victory Road 1F the runner marked the one way down as "unusable" while the player was standing on it: the six ticks that did not move were the walk's failure to step onto the square, not the door's failure to open, so the door died for the milestone, every tick answered "no route" and the player stood still until the detector called it a stall (the run of 2026-10-26). A door the player stands on is never marked unusable any more: the way down is marked blocked instead, so the runner walks back, the game resets the boulders of the floor and the runner can solve it. In plain words: the runner keeps playing the last floor before the Hall of Fame instead of freezing on it · [BUG-0947](../bugs/BUG-0947-2026-10-26-the-pokemon-runner-marked-the-door-it-stood-on-as-unusable.md) · [TASK-33](../tasks/TASK-33-handover.md)
- **The Pokémon runner leaves the pocket with the stairs instead of ping-ponging between two floors (BUG-0948)** — on Victory Road 1F the player came down the stair from the floor above into the pocket where the stairs are: the floor's plan could not be set up there, the rock that sealed him in was not the rock the plan pushes, and the only way down had been marked unusable by a walk that never happened — so the lane answered "no route" on every tick and the player stood still until the detector called it a stall (twice, on 2026-10-26). The rock that seals the player in is now pushed aside whichever rock of the floor it is, a door the player stands on is never marked unusable, and the mark of such a door is dropped. In plain words: the runner keeps playing the last floor before the Hall of Fame instead of freezing in it · [BUG-0948](../bugs/BUG-0948-2026-10-26-the-pokemon-runner-was-sealed-in-the-pocket-with-the-stairs.md) · [TASK-33](../tasks/TASK-33-handover.md)
- **The delayed stream plays even when captions are not ready (BUG-0943)** — the stream modal of a caption-playback device sat on "Waiting for the archive…" while the grid showed its live picture, because the player refused to start until the host had transcribed a minute. The picture now always starts from the archive at the profile delay and keeps playing; a badge says when the captions of the current minute are not ready. In plain words: the delayed video is never held back by the captions · [BUG-0943](../bugs/BUG-0943-2026-10-26-delayed-stream-waits-for-captions-before-playing.md)
- **Atlas no longer acts on devices you did not ask about** — on a vague ask Atlas could pick any device the server lists: once it took control of a drone and of two shared host desktops and ran shell commands there. Now a device action (take control, keys, navigation, runs) is refused for a device outside the chat's devices; a drone only moves, and a host desktop is only used, when you name it or it is your selected device; and a shell command runs only when you ask for a command. Atlas then asks you instead of guessing. Screenshots, screen dumps and key lists are not affected. In plain words: Atlas only touches the device you meant · [BUG-0914](../bugs/BUG-0914-2026-10-25-atlas-acts-on-devices-outside-the-page-context.md)
- **vpt-pi1 no longer piles up dead OCR processes or burns a core in bluetoothd (BUG-0934)** — the subtitle monitor gave up on a timed-out tesseract that did not die within 3 s of being killed and marked it finished, so nothing ever collected it: 579 dead processes in a week. A waiter thread now collects it whenever it does end. Separately, the login user's audio session (WirePlumber) re-registered about 150 Bluetooth audio endpoints every 12 s on a host that only emulates a BLE remote, which kept `bluetoothd` at 40-90 % CPU and wrote ~950 journal lines a minute; the BLE installer now turns that monitor off (vpt-pi1: 0 % CPU, 0 lines). In plain words: the Pi that drives the set-top boxes has its CPU back · [BUG-0939](../bugs/BUG-0939-2026-10-26-pi1-tesseract-zombies-and-bluetoothd-cpu.md)
- **The Pokémon runner takes the door the game opens, not the wall beside it (BUG-0938)** — the `second` lane stopped in the gate house at the south of Route 2, one room away from the Viridian Forest, and walked into a wall for five minutes: the room's map lists two warps north — (4, 0) and (5, 0) — but only (5, 0) is a door (the game draws (4, 0) as the wall tile 4A), and the runner kept choosing the wall, because it prefers the warp with the same destination as the one the route had picked. The refused step was then covered by the fallback that tries the other keys, so the player was walked back down and the run ping-ponged between two squares until the detector called it a stall. The warp squares the game shows as walls are now skipped when the door of the hop is chosen, so the runner takes the door that opens. In plain words: the runner walks through the gate instead of bumping into it · [BUG-0938](../bugs/BUG-0938-2026-10-26-the-pokemon-runner-aimed-at-a-door-the-game-draws-as-a-wall.md) · [TASK-33](../tasks/TASK-33-handover.md) · area: games
- **A run whose model stays throttled gives the phone back (BUG-0931)** — the free MiniMax route refused every call of the CoC runs of 2026-10-26 03:00 and 04:00 (HTTP 429). Each run skipped step after step — a throttle is not billed, so the step was skipped and the game went on — until its budget stopped it at 60 steps: 258 refused calls and **56 minutes** holding `vpt-pi1/device1`, while the other lane found the device busy at every slot and the game did not play for two hours. A throttle that lasts three steps in a row now ends the run and gives the phone back in about two minutes; a single throttle still only skips that step · [BUG-0931](../bugs/BUG-0931-2026-10-26-a-run-whose-model-stayed-throttled-held-the-phone-for-an-hour.md)
- **The web content suite opens the catalogue again (BUG-0916)** — the TV web app answers in the language of the browser profile, and a fresh profile starts in German, while the reference tree (and the suite's own labels) are English: the suite pressed "All Shows" on a page that publishes "Alle Sendungen", so the press matched nothing and every web case ended "the tile did not open an item". The suite now speaks the language the app publishes: every label it presses is listed in every language the app ships (the catalogue tile, the VOD tile, the play action), the app's language is read from the page and written into the run's evidence, and the app's own sign-in box — which lies over the page it opens — is dismissed with the app's own guest bypass before anything is driven. The press also tries the platform's own click when the page script pressed nothing, because the page script sees only what is on screen while the platform dumps the whole semantics tree. Live on a free web host, the tile now opens the detail page of a real programme. In plain words: the web cases stopped blaming the app for speaking German · [BUG-0916](../bugs/BUG-0916-2026-10-25-the-web-suite-pressed-the-catalogue-tile-in-the-wrong-language.md) · [TASK-168](../tasks/TASK-168-vod-radio-content-suite.md) · area: testing
- **The content suite reaches the app on the phone (BUG-0928)** — the TV app on the phone stopped on its own login screen, with Android's notification dialog on top of it, so the tree's very first check could never see the app's surface and every mobile case ended "the screen does not match the tree's reference picture". Neither of the two is answered with a credential: the system dialog is denied and the app's own "Skip login" bypass is used, exactly as on the web. The suite then waits for the app's own surface before the tree drives it, and asks again on every poll, because the app may put the login screen up again. The names of the app's controls live in the content description and the dump fills the empty text field with a placeholder, so the names were read from the wrong place and never matched; they are now read where the platform prints them. A guest session plays nothing, and that is the session, not a defect of the app; in plain words: the mobile cases now test the app instead of its front door · [BUG-0928](../bugs/BUG-0928-2026-10-26-the-app-on-the-phone-stopped-on-its-own-login-screen.md) · [TASK-168](../tasks/TASK-168-vod-radio-content-suite.md) · area: testing
- **A missing play button and a timeout are not defects of the app (BUG-0929)** — two rules of the content suite reported our own problems as the product's: a guest session on the web has no play button on a programme page (the app offers the playback to a signed-in user only), and a navigation that ran out of time was reported as a defect of the product although the platform simply did not answer. Both now say what was measured: a guest session is the session, a timeout is the platform. A radio app that is on the screen but that the tree cannot reach is a gap in the tree, not a defect, and the row says so. In plain words: the suite stopped blaming the app for what we could not reach · [BUG-0929](../bugs/BUG-0929-2026-10-26-the-suite-called-our-own-problems-defects-of-the-app.md) · [TASK-168](../tasks/TASK-168-vod-radio-content-suite.md) · area: testing
- **The Pokémon runner no longer stands still for 300 ticks on a stair it never tried (BUG-0925)** — on Victory Road 1F the runner's one way down was marked "unusable" because a rock sealed the corridor to it: the door was never stepped on, but an unusable door is dead for the milestone, so every tick answered "no route" and the player never moved again (the stall of 2026-10-26). A rock in the way now marks the way down as blocked — so the runner goes back and solves the floor — instead of the door. In plain words: the runner keeps playing instead of freezing on the last floor before the Hall of Fame · [BUG-0925](../bugs/BUG-0925-2026-10-26-the-pokemon-runner-stands-still-on-victory-road-1f-with-a-stair-it-never-tried.md)
- **A failed CoC call no longer loses the whole run (BUG-0921)** — the hourly CoC run of 2026-10-25 played for 27 minutes and was then thrown away with `Unexpected error: 'outcome'`: an attack step whose harness raised carries no outcome, and the report read it with a hard lookup. 623 screenshots, a 34 MB video and half an hour of play ended in a row that says "failed". The report now shows such a step as *unknown* instead of crashing, and the failed step carries the outcome every other step has. In plain words: one refused call no longer throws away the game · [BUG-0921](../bugs/BUG-0921-2026-10-25-a-failed-coc-call-lost-the-whole-run.md)
- **The game lanes run the code of main, not the code they were deployed with (BUG-0920)** — the fixes were on main and the lanes did not have them: the stall watcher kept the code that reads only the *latest* lock of the save store while main already read every lock, so the second Pokémon save stayed locked for two days and nobody was asked to fix it; the game dispatcher ran a tree dozens of commits behind, which is why the CoC job logged `past 2026-10-25` every minute. Nothing failed — the lanes were healthy and out of date, the worst kind of failure. A lane that runs from a clone is now brought back to main before every tick, and the lanes run from their own clone (the shared checkout is the working copy of every session on the host, and its branch is switched under them) · [BUG-0920](../bugs/BUG-0920-2026-10-25-the-lanes-ran-the-code-they-were-deployed-with-not-the-code-of-main.md)
- **The Pokémon lane plays again: no run died with "maximum recursion depth exceeded" (BUG-0919)** — not one Pokémon run finished since the morning of 2026-10-25: every run died the same way, about 167 s in, with the save never moving. The walk to a guard asked a boulder to move, the push was refused, and the runner asked to talk to the guard instead — which asked to walk to him, which asked the boulder again, for ever. On Victory Road 1F the guard sits in a pocket the corridor boulder seals off, so the walk could never succeed. A person who stands on the target square is not talked to through the walk any more, the same man is never walked to twice, and a guard a boulder seals off is not the one to talk to. The local replay of the canonical save now plays 833 ticks without an error and wins the trainer battle · [BUG-0919](../bugs/BUG-0919-2026-10-25-every-pokemon-run-died-with-maximum-recursion-depth-exceeded.md)
- **The free Clash of Clans lane plays again, and it no longer buys a paid model (BUG-0918)** — the extra CoC job logged `past 2026-10-25` every minute and played nothing: the date the free MiniMax M3.1-Flash plan was believed to end had been written into the job as the day it stops starting. The same date had turned the hourly run's *auto* model choice into the **paid** Claude Haiku 5.5 — 508 calls and $0.150 on 2026-10-25 alone — so the lane did not stop working, it changed vendor and started billing. The plan is unlimited and free, so `auto` is now always Flash, the job has no end date, and a paid model is only ever a deliberate choice (`--model_choice haiku`). In plain words: the game plays on the free model, and the game costs nothing · [BUG-0918](../bugs/BUG-0918-2026-10-25-the-gate-date-ended-the-free-co-c-lane-and-bought-a-paid-model.md)
- **The egg line: circles on the eggs, every frame analysed, each egg counted once (BUG-0913, BUG-0917, BUG-0915)** — three defects made the egg demo wrong on the owner's screen. **The circles floated off the eggs**: the boxes were letterboxed to the whole player while the analysed picture, a separate image, stayed at its own pixel size in the middle; the picture is now drawn inside the boxes' own SVG, in the same frame pixels, and a new capture is shown only once loaded together with its own boxes — live, 184 circles and none outside the picture. **The line analysed 1.6 frames a second of the 5 it was given**: a square model input that was 44 % padding, `cv2.dnn`, a 40 MB chunk re-encoded every 2 s under the GIL, pure-Python loops over thousands of anchors, a box shared with six capture streams, and a worker that slept a whole extra period after a frame that came a few ms early. Now a 640x352 export of the same weights through onnxruntime (118 ms a pass instead of 390, same eggs found), vectorised decode and duplicate merge, a chunk flush that waits 20x its own cost, counting_cars and the internet TV streams moved off `vpt-lenovo1`, and a worker that waits only until the next frame is due: **4.8 analyses a second, 285 of 288 captures analysed**. **The counter counted the same egg several times** (1 740 "eggs" in two minutes with ~180 on screen): a missed detection ends a track and a new one starts on the same egg, and the profile counted tracks. A profile can now count at a line (`track.count_line_x`, `count_dir`): an egg counts once, when its centre crosses mid-frame going right, so a broken track still counts once. Against the 960 model on every frame of one pass at 10 fps (380 eggs), the profile counts 369 (97 %) offline and 357 per pass live (94 %, the live window also crossing the demo clip's 30 s loop and the 2-minute reset). In plain words: the circles sit on the eggs, the picture moves at the camera's rate, and the number is the eggs that went past · [BUG-0913](../bugs/BUG-0913-2026-10-25-the-egg-overlay-draws-its-circles-in-the-players-space.md) · [BUG-0917](../bugs/BUG-0917-2026-10-25-the-egg-line-analysed-three-frames-a-second-of-five.md) · [BUG-0915](../bugs/BUG-0915-2026-10-25-the-egg-counter-counted-the-same-egg-several-times.md)
- **A screen seen again now gets a picture, so it can be named (BUG-0912)** — screens registered by the old writers kept a host file path as their picture; the file is deleted within minutes, so the hourly namer skipped them for ever. When the screen is seen again, the row now takes the fresh thumbnail. In plain words: screens stuck at "unknown" because their picture was gone get one back the next time they appear · [BUG-0912](../bugs/BUG-0912-2026-10-25-a-matched-screen-row-never-got-a-thumbnail.md)
- **Game and video screens are no longer named "Full-screen poster" or "Splash screen" (BUG-0908)** — the screen namer asked its two set-top-box questions ("a welcome splash?", "a menu bar?") of every device, so a Clash of Clans village on a phone and an Apple TV screensaver were named like an operator poster or splash, and because a name is never replaced the wrong names stayed. The two questions are now asked only of set-top boxes; other devices get an ordinary name. The 124 wrong names were cleared back to `unknown_N` so they are named again. In plain words: a phone game screen is no longer called a TV poster · [BUG-0908](../bugs/BUG-0908-2026-10-25-screen-state-checks-named-game-frames-full-screen-poster.md)
- **An aborted or cancelled campaign is no longer shown as failed (BUG-0904)** — in the campaign results table, a campaign that never reached a verdict was painted as a red **Fail**, exactly like a campaign whose checks really did break, with no way to tell the two apart. It happened to the runs the platform stops rather than the runs that fail: one whose message reads `Execution aborted - scheduler restarted`, and a night that never started because the device was busy. Neither is a failing check — the checks never gave a verdict at all. Both now wear a neutral grey stop icon, with the tooltip naming what happened (**Aborted** or **Cancelled**). A campaign that really failed still shows the red fail icon. In plain words: a stopped night no longer reads as a broken one · [BUG-0904](../bugs/BUG-0904-2026-10-25-an-aborted-campaign-is-shown-as-failed-in-the-results-table.md)
- **A campaign that is still running is no longer shown as failed (BUG-0902)** — the campaign results page used to close a campaign as "failed" once it was 125 minutes old, even while its scripts were still running; nightly runs last up to 170 minutes, so for the last 45 minutes they read as failed. A running campaign now stays running as long as its scripts keep reporting. One that has really gone silent for longer than its timeout is closed as aborted, with the time of its last activity and a message that says how many scripts reported. Past runs are not rewritten. In plain words: a night that is still running now reads as running · [BUG-0902](../bugs/BUG-0902-2026-10-26-reading-the-campaign-results-closes-a-running-campaign.md)

- **A run that never reported a verdict is closed once, not every five minutes (BUG-0901)** — the host closes a run that died before writing its result, but it kept re-closing the same ones every cycle, overwriting their end time and logging the same count for days. Each is now closed once, keeps that end time, and the log only mentions rows it really closed. Rows closed before the fix keep the end time the sweep last gave them. In plain words: the "closed N orphaned rows" line now means what it says · [BUG-0901](../bugs/BUG-0901-2026-10-25-the-script-result-orphan-sweep-re-closes-the-same-rows-for-ever.md)
- **A campaign aborted by a host restart no longer stays "running" for ever (BUG-0900)** — when the scheduler restarted during a campaign, the deployment record was closed as aborted but the campaign's own record, the one the nightly report reads, kept saying *running*. It now reads *aborted* with the reason, and a record left running by an earlier restart is closed at the next host start. In plain words: a dead night no longer looks like a live one · [BUG-0900](../bugs/BUG-0900-2026-10-25-a-campaign-aborted-by-a-scheduler-restart-stays-running.md)
- **A nightly that never ran now says so (BUG-0898)** — before, a nightly that was skipped left nothing behind: no result row at all, or a row that read as a failure, so the nightly report could not tell "the night ran and found nothing" from "the night never ran" — and the log said the run "will retry next trigger", which really meant tomorrow. A skipped nightly now leaves a record of its own that reads as **skipped** and carries the reason — the skip is a flag on the row, the campaign status itself stays a value the database accepts — the nightly report counts it as a finished night instead of a failure, and the log says plainly that the run will not be retried today. Three nightlies that run on the same device no longer lose each other either: a recurring deployment skipped because the device is busy is now retried inside its own trigger window instead of being dropped until the next day. In plain words: a night that never ran is now visible as a night that never ran · [BUG-0898](../bugs/BUG-0898-2026-10-25-a-nightly-that-never-ran-leaves-no-trace.md) · 🗄 DB migration `setup/db/migrations/20261025a_campaign_executions_skipped.sql` · area: monitoring
- **A campaign that failed every check is no longer recorded as a successful deployment (BUG-0899)** — the campaign's own record always told the truth, but the deployment's record said *completed / success* for any campaign that ran to the end, and a manually run campaign showed *completed* on the dashboard even when every check failed. Both now carry the campaign's real verdict, and a run that was aborted keeps reading as aborted. Rows already written are not rewritten. In plain words: a red night now shows red in the deployments list · [BUG-0899](../bugs/BUG-0899-2026-10-25-a-failed-campaign-is-recorded-as-a-successful-deployment.md)
- **A Pokémon run is no longer killed by the no-progress clock of the run before it (BUG-0897)** — the `latest` lane stopped on **Victory Road 1F** and locked the save: the run `20261025-000846` wrote three events and **played 29 seconds** — the plan of the strategist, the rock of the pressure switch pushed out of the doorway (`boulder_push … boulder [9, 14] → [[8, 14]] direction LEFT moved True aside True`, the move that lets the player out of the pocket) and the stall. The report blamed the game for *"no milestone, no new map and no new square for 5 min of game time"*, but the run's own counters say the clock stood at **300.19 s** when it started — that is the clock of the **run before** it, already over the 300 s threshold, inherited through the memo. The run before had died before it could write its stall report and its lock (the report and the lock were written after the long upload of the game report, the memory — the file that carries the clock — long before it), so the dispatcher found an unlocked save, started the next run **without** `--clear_stall yes`, and that run was killed by the clock it inherited **on its very first tick**. A run now starts its own clock when the measurement it inherits is already overdue (a clock under the threshold still carries over — the time of a stuck milestone spans its runs), and the stall report **and the lock** are written **before** the memory and before the upload, so a run that dies in between still leaves the lock behind and the dispatcher waits for the fix instead of starting a doomed run. The floor was not stuck: a local replay of the canonical save of this stall plays it and recovers on its own — the runner leaves the floor and enters it again (which puts the boulders back where they were) and the chain of the pressure switch runs to the end, **(17, 13)**, the switch, **249 ticks, no stall**. In plain words: a fresh run is judged on the time it spends, not on the time of the run before it · [BUG-0897](../bugs/BUG-0897-2026-10-25-the-pokemon-run-was-killed-by-the-clock-of-the-run-before.md) · [TASK-33](../tasks/TASK-33-handover.md) · area: games
- **A virtual script that dies on a library the platform could not fetch now names the library (BUG-0089)** — a virtual script declares the libraries it needs in `_script_libs`, and the platform resolves them best-effort: a library it cannot fetch is **skipped**, on purpose, because a declared library may well be optional. The script then died on a bare `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'x'` — and when the import sat at the top of the file, the run blamed the author's file with *"the file is likely corrupted or has a syntax/import error"*, which was wrong: the file was fine, the platform could not fetch the library. The names the platform skipped now travel to the run's error path, which says **"declared library 'x' could not be found by the platform"** instead, in the error message, in the row's error and in the run's log. The skip stays a skip and the verdict does not move: a run whose script needs a library the platform could not fetch still fails, exactly as before, and a failure that is not about a skipped library keeps its own message. In plain words: the script is not broken — the platform could not find the library, and now it says so · [BUG-0089](../bugs/BUG-0089-2026-09-15-virtual-script-materialize-failure-reported-as-success.md) · area: testing
- **A run no longer leaves its own browser behind when it ends (BUG-0760)** — an hourly run on a game host ended at **17:05:54** with `Cleanup timed out or failed: TimeoutError:` and its Chromium was **still alive on port 9300** until the periodic reclaim pass took it **four minutes later**. The run closed its browser over the Playwright controller loop, and a wedged loop — exactly what made the run fail — meant the close was queued on a loop that would never answer it; and even when the cleanup ran, the process kill sat *behind* the graceful close, so a hang there left the browser alive while the controller believed there was none to close. The end of a run now takes down the browser **that run launched**, without the loop: it finds it by the run's own profile directory (`user_data_runs/<run id>`), checks that no **live** run owns its port, and signals it by pid — individual positive pids only, never a process group, and never the host's own persistent service browser. Two host switches still decide whether a browser dies with its run: **`VPT_KEEP_BROWSER_OPEN`** (the operator asked for the browser to live: it is left open and the run says so, with the pid) and **`VPT_REAP_LEAKED_BROWSERS=0`** (the operator asked for no killing on that host: the run reports *NOT reaped - reaping is disabled*). On such a host the leak comes back by the operator's own choice — that is the switch's meaning, not a defect; neither switch is set anywhere in the fleet today. An **aborted** run gets the same treatment (Chrome sits in its own session, so the abort's tree kill could never reach it), and the cleanup now reaches its kill even when the graceful close hangs. The reports say what really happened: *closed normally*, *taken down by pid*, *already gone*, or **NOT reaped** with the reason — and `close_browser` no longer reports success for a browser that is still running. In plain words: a run that ends takes its browser with it · [BUG-0760](../bugs/BUG-0760-2026-10-22-an-hourly-job-leaks-a-chrome-every-hour-and-the-cap-cannot-see-it.md) · area: testing
- **A browser run no longer outlives its own budget and leaves its driver behind (BUG-0842)** — a scheduled `game_tetris.py` run on `host-clone-2` played for a couple of minutes, then **went silent and never finished**: it was measured alive at **20 minutes** against its own `--max_duration 900`, holding a Chromium on port 9300 and a Playwright driver, and it never reached teardown — there is no `Closing web browser after test execution...` line in the journal for any Tetris run, while the `youtube_video_check` runs on the same host print it every time. Two causes fed it: the waits on the web controller's loop had **no timeout**, so a wedged loop left the caller waiting for ever instead of failing, and `--max_duration` was a **script argument** and nothing else, so nothing outside the action loop ever stopped the run. The waits are now **bounded** and say the loop did not answer; the executor stops the run at **the run's own budget** and reports the timeout **with a reason** instead of never reporting at all; and the browser the run held is closed on the way out, because the script never reached its own teardown. The reclaim pass also learned to see the **Playwright driver**, which holds no port and was therefore invisible to every port-based count — measured at **4 h 49 m** and 74 MB on `host-clone-2` while a healthy run was in flight beside it. A run inside its budget is never killed and a healthy run keeps its verdict · **and the reaper now tells the truth about what it did**: the deployed run on `host-clone-2` logged “took down 0 process(es)” and “reclaimed orphaned Playwright driver(s)” for a driver it was not even allowed to signal — a signal the kernel refuses is now reported as **not reaped**, with the reason, and only the drivers that were really taken down are announced as reclaimed · [BUG-0842](../bugs/BUG-0842-2026-10-22-a-browser-run-outlives-its-own-time-budget-and-never-tears-its-browser-down.md) · area: testing
- **The Pokémon chain of a floor follows the rock and not its own count (BUG-0896)** — the `latest` lane stopped on **Victory Road 1F** and locked the save: the run `20261024-225457` wrote **290 identical `hop_blocked` events** (the runner walking at the stairs of the floor, next map 194, which the corridor boulder seals) interleaved with two wild battles the strategist fled, and the detector wrote `no_progress` after 300 s without a new square. The floor's plan had already been given up, and the chain says why: a push of the plan is **not** one press per square of rock (the game plays the dust of a push and swallows the presses that land in it), and the push counted one *change of the live boulders* as one square of rock — so the second push ended with the rock at **(7, 16)** instead of (9, 16) and the six pushes after it pressed against nothing (`boulder_push … boulder [9,16] → [9,16]`), the pressure switch **(17, 13)** was never pressed, the game never opened the passage of the floor, the stand square **(2, 9)** of the corridor push stayed out of reach and the floor's plan was given up (`seafoam_pushes_done = 8`). The chain is read from the **live boulders** now — the rock that stands on the square a step starts from is the step to do, a rock that stands between two squares of the plan is pushed on to the end of that step, and a rock that may only pull the chain back (a rock that is out of sight is not gone) — the push ends when the rock has really reached the square the step wants, a push that moved nothing does not advance the chain (it is tried again, and given up after three tries), and the generic push respects the plan for a rock that stands between two squares too. In plain words: the Pokémon plays the puzzle of the floor instead of counting the pushes it never made · [BUG-0896](../bugs/BUG-0896-2026-10-24-the-pokemon-chain-counted-pushes-the-game-never-made.md) · [TASK-33](../tasks/TASK-33-handover.md) · area: games
- **The Pokémon pushes the boulder of a Strength plan square by square again (BUG-0895)** — the `latest` lane stopped on **Victory Road 1F** and locked the save: the run `20261024-152049` played 390 ticks and the seven pushes of the floor's plan ran — the boulder west of the entrance was pushed onto the pressure switch **(17, 13)**, and the corridor push was given up anyway, because the stand square **(2, 9)** of that push stays out of reach: the game opens the passage of the floor only while a rock lies on the switch, and the rock never got there. The cause was the push itself: a push of the plan is **not** one press per square of rock — the game plays the dust of a push and swallows the button presses that land in it, and the player cannot step into the square the rock left until the dust is over. Measured on the stalled save, **four presses moved the rock one square**, so the second push of the chain left the rock at (6, 16) instead of (9, 16); the six pushes after that pushed air while their events reported the plan's claim, the chain never reached the switch, and the runner paced the floor until the stall detector wrote `no_progress`. The push watches the rock now and presses again until the rock has really moved — the chain of the floor reaches the pressure switch, the game opens the passage itself, and the floor can be finished · [BUG-0895](../bugs/BUG-0895-2026-10-24-the-pokemon-pushed-the-boulder-one-square-at-a-time.md) · [TASK-33](../tasks/TASK-33-handover.md) · area: games
- **The Pokémon no longer plans a path through a barrier the game keeps closed (BUG-0894)** — the `latest` lane stopped on **Victory Road 1F** and locked the save: the run `20261024-134917` played 11 minutes and wrote **46 events, every one of them a battle** — 22 wild battles (ONIX 42, MACHOPEUR 42, NOSFERALTO 41, GRAVALANCH 41, MACHOC 24, RACAILLOU 26) and **not one walk, not one push** — the player never left the grass of the floor, and the stall detector wrote `no_progress` after 300 s without a new square. The cause was the grid: BUG-0892 opened the barrier on the rocky ridge from the runner's **own count of the chain**, but the game does not open it — measured on the live save, the boulder the chain pushes onto the switch tile **(17, 13)** falls into the hole that tile is (it is gone from the sprite list), the map's **block table stays byte for byte the one of the ROM** and the game's own collision keeps (5, 8) and (5, 9) apart (pressing UP at (5, 9) does not move the player). A grid that believes the barrier is open sends the runner at a square the game will never let it stand on: it ping-ponged in the grass, one wild battle per step, until the lane locked. The grid follows the game's own collision data now — when the game opens a barrier, the tiles of the floor change and the wall pairs go with them — so a push that cannot be set up gives the floor up and the guide takes the runner on to the last mile instead of pacing in the grass · [BUG-0894](../bugs/BUG-0894-2026-10-24-the-pokemon-planned-a-path-into-a-barrier-the-game-keeps-closed.md) · [TASK-33](../tasks/TASK-33-handover.md) · area: games
- **The pressure switch of Victory Road 1F opens a barrier the runner never saw (BUG-0892)** — the `latest` lane worked the floor's Strength plan again and again but never finished it: the chain runs, all seven pushes, and the boulder of the pressure switch lands on the switch at **(17, 13)** — and the game opens the barrier on the rocky ridge while it lies there, which is the only way into the part of the floor the stairs are in. The grid kept that barrier for ever (the player's part of the floor stayed at 129 squares instead of 292), so the stand square (2, 9) of the corridor push was out of reach, the push was given up on every visit and the runner left the floor for ever. The grid now opens the barrier while a boulder lies on the switch, the way the game does · [BUG-0892](../bugs/BUG-0892-2026-10-24-the-pressure-switch-opened-a-barrier-the-runner-never-saw.md)
- **The Pokémon no longer walks at a hop no boulder can reach (BUG-0891)** — the `latest` lane stopped on **Victory Road 1F** and locked the save: the boulder that sealed the one-wide corridor to the stairs was left where it was, the world graph still routed to Victory Road 2F through those stairs, and the runner walked at a square it could never reach — it ping-ponged between two squares of the grass, fought **42 wild battles** in 33 minutes and reached no new square, and the stall detector never saw it because the battle ticks do not count towards the no-progress clock. **A hop the player cannot walk to is now no route at all**: the guide goes back to the waypoint it came from, the player leaves the floor, the game puts the boulders of the floor back where they were and the floor can be solved · [BUG-0891](../bugs/BUG-0891-2026-10-24-the-pokemon-walked-at-a-hop-no-boulder-could-reach.md)
- **The Pokémon no longer pushes the boulder against the plan of the floor (BUG-0890)** — the `latest` lane stopped **twice in a row** on the same floor, **Victory Road 1F** (map 108), and both stalls wrote the lock that stops the dispatcher from playing at all. The run `20261024-093928` worked the floor's Strength plan four times round and never finished it (37 boulder pushes), and the first run **after BUG-0889 was deployed** (`20261024-102539`) stalled the same way 13 minutes in: 40+ times the same `boulder_push … boulder [2, 9] … direction UP … moved false` — the game saying "the boulder won't budge". Three faults fed one loop: the **generic push pushed against the plan** (the corridor boulder has to be pushed **south**, out of the passage to the stairs; the generic push shoved it **north**, into the alcove (2, 9), where the game refuses to move it at all — measured on the stalled save: (2, 8) is a wall tile, so the rock can never be pushed from there), a **refused push was tried again for ever** (the refusal is now remembered and never repeated), and the **chain of the floor was read from the memo** instead of from the floor (the game puts the boulders back where they were when the player leaves it, so the chain starts over by itself now). The plan has to win: a boulder the plan names is only ever pushed the way the plan says. In plain words: the Pokémon plays the puzzle of the floor instead of shoving a rock that will not move · [BUG-0890](../bugs/BUG-0890-2026-10-24-the-pokemon-pushed-the-boulder-against-the-plan.md) · [TASK-33](../tasks/TASK-33-handover.md) · area: games
- **A wall mark no longer outlives the boulder of Victory Road 1F (BUG-0889)** — the `latest` lane stopped again after BUG-0888, on **Victory Road 1F**: the run `20261024-093928` worked the floor's Strength plan four times round and never finished it (`no_progress` after 23 minutes of play, 37 boulder pushes).
