The corridor boulder at (2, 10) has to be pushed **south**, out of the one-wide passage to the stairs (1, 1), and to push it south the player has to stand north of it at (2, 9) — which it could not reach, because the save still carried a **wall mark** on (2, 10): the leftover of the old bug where the runner talked to the boulders until they were walled for good (BUG-0885). BUG-0886 taught the runner to skip such a mark while the rock stands there, but the mark stayed when the rock was pushed away — and a wall on an empty square is the passage to the stairs. The plan gave up, the generic push then shoved the boulder the **wrong way** (north, into a corner), and the floor could not be finished: 4 times round, no new square. - **Atlas is now offered the tool your question was about (BUG-0882)** — "What is on the screen of the phone?" and "Open the TV guide" were answered with the wrong tool, because the right one was **never in Atlas' list**: the tool list is chosen by matching your question against each tool's name, description and category, and the matching counted the small words ("is", "the", "of", "show") that sit in nearly every description — so every tool scored, the eleven slots filled with tools that had nothing to do with the question, and the obvious ones (a screenshot, the navigation tree) were pushed out. Words that carry no meaning are now ignored, "screen" and "showing" count as screenshot questions, "open the guide" counts as a navigation question, and the tool that answers a category of question is always offered, whatever else scores higher — without the list growing, and without pushing out the tool your question names: "open the app on the phone" still gets the tool that launches the app, not the navigation tree · [BUG-0882](../bugs/BUG-0882-2026-10-23-atlas-was-never-offered-the-tool-the-question-was-about.md) · [TASK-163](../tasks/TASK-163-atlas-usecases.md) · area: ai

- **The Pokémon's heal trip can no longer become a dead end (BUG-0877)** — the long play stopped for good: the run `20261023-173029` stood on **Victory Road 1F** at (2, 12) for 300 ticks and the lane's lock stopped the game. - **The game's own picture no longer blocks the game's own reads (BUG-0876)** — the second Pokémon's lane (the parallel save `second`) stalled for the second time in a row, and this time the game was **alive**: the host's journal shows the page answering in **2 ms** right up to the first hang — and the first hang is a **picture**, not a read ("Page screenshot timed out after 5s"). The game asked for its JavaScript through the action executor, which **photographs the page before and after every action**, and a page's command queue is **one single channel**: a page that stops painting (the host's window is occluded and the box is busy) hung the picture for ever, and every read and key press queued behind it waited out its own timeout — **25 s a game action**, the game never played another step, and the run wrote the lock again (and no save, so the lane starts a new game every time). The game now asks the **browser** for its JavaScript directly instead of going through the action executor — only the game is ever queued on the game's channel — and its own evidence comes from the host's screen capture instead of a page picture, so a page that stops painting costs the run its pictures, never its game. In plain words: the game keeps playing even when the screen stops refreshing, instead of waiting for a photograph that never comes · [BUG-0876](../bugs/BUG-0876-2026-10-23-the-games-picture-blocked-the-games-own-reads.md) · [TASK-164](../tasks/TASK-164-flash-play.md) · area: games
- **The new game no longer stands at the title menu for ever (BUG-0875)** — the second Pokémon's lane (the parallel save `second`) never played a single step: every run died the same way — `no_progress` at the milestone `starter`, map 0, position (0, 0), no Pokémon and no money — and the **lock** it wrote stopped the lane for good. The game was alive but **frozen**: the runner's A and START presses landed on a game whose memory never moved (the host's screen showed the title menu with the cursor on "▶ NOUVEAU JEU", and the captures did not change for ten seconds), and the runner could not tell that from a new game that is still starting up. The runner now compares the game's work RAM with the previous tick — the game writes its OAM shadow every frame, so a snapshot that never changes is a game whose keys go nowhere — **restarts the emulator in the page** when that happens (the ROM and the save are loaded again, so the run goes on), and ends the run without a stall and without a lock when the page cannot be restarted at all. The intro of a new game (the title screen, Oak's speech, the names) is no longer counted as "no progress" either: it is a long phase in which the game moves nowhere by design. In plain words: a new game starts playing instead of waiting at the title screen, and a frozen emulator no longer locks the lane · [BUG-0875](../bugs/BUG-0875-2026-10-23-the-new-game-stood-at-the-title-menu.md) · [TASK-164](../tasks/TASK-164-flash-play.md) · area: games
- **The Pokémon's boulders never moved (BUG-0874)** — the long play reached **Victory Road 1F** and stood still for ever: the floor's push plan named **(8, 12)**, the square the player already stands on, while the floor's real boulder is at **(2, 10)** — and the push was never armed at all, because pressing A on a boulder does not move it: **Strength has to be the field move in use**. The runner now arms it the way Surf and Cut are used (START, POKéMON, the Pokémon that knows Strength, FORCE) and walks to the square the push starts from, so the boulder moves. The plan now names the boulder that really blocks the corridor and pushes it the right way (down, with the player north of it). The floor itself is still not passable — the player cannot reach the far side of the boulder because the grid does not model the floor's jump tiles yet — and that is the next piece, written up in the handover · [BUG-0874](../bugs/BUG-0874-2026-10-23-the-victory-road-boulder-never-moved.md) · area: games
- **A host with a stuck game on it is not taken (BUG-0873)** — the Pokémon's lane started a run on a host at 12:09 and it was gone two minutes later: the host had no game process at all. The host was not free — a tetris run whose 15-minute budget had expired 20 minutes earlier was still running on it, and a lease that expires under a stuck run no longer says "this host is free". The lane now asks the host itself whether another game script runs on it (the same check it already uses to notice that its own run is over, turned around) and takes another host. In plain words: two games never share a 2-core box again, even when the registry thinks the box is free · [BUG-0873](../bugs/BUG-0873-2026-10-23-a-host-with-a-stuck-game-was-taken-anyway.md) · [TASK-164](../tasks/TASK-164-flash-play.md) · area: games
- **A dead emulator page ends the run instead of locking the save (BUG-0872)** — the Pokémon's run of 2026-10-23 13:28 stopped for good: the emulator page died mid-run (the host's journal: "Page screenshot timed out after 20s", then no live page) and every read of the game's memory waited out its 15 s timeout and answered with nothing, so the runner was handed 8 KB of zeros that look like an empty game. It took that for a hiccup and read again for ever: the run spent **9 min 35 s** doing nothing, the detector then called it "no progress" at map 0, position (0, 0) — a map the game was not on — and wrote the **lock**, so the dispatcher started no run at all and the whole long play stopped. Three blank reads in a row now mean the page is gone: the run ends there, no stall is written, no lock is set (the dispatcher keeps playing), and the state of a game that is gone is never written over the save. In plain words: when the emulator dies, the run stops and the game carries on the next time · [BUG-0872](../bugs/BUG-0872-2026-10-23-the-run-spun-on-a-dead-emulator-page-and-locked-the-save.md) · area: games
- **The egg line counts the eggs and says nothing about their quality (BUG-0906)** — the egg panel showed a good/bad/unverified breakdown next to the egg count, and that breakdown was a number the picture could not support: `egg_classify` grades a crop of a white egg lying on a blue belt in daylight, where the image carries **no evidence at all** of what is inside the egg, so it reports ~99 % bad because that is what the model says. Nothing on screen said so, and a demo showing "Eggs: 214 / Bad: 212" reads as a quality measurement. A new profile `egg_count_yolo_belt` keeps the part the image *can* support — the belt detector, which finds 98.6 % of the eggs — and drops the grader: no `egg_classify` stage, no good/bad counter, no verdict colouring, and a comment that says a real grade needs **candling** (the egg against a light). The count itself is honest in the way that matters: `egg_seen` fires **once per tracked egg** (finalised when it is seen twice, or when it leaves the frame), so the counter is *distinct eggs*, not detections per frame, and `reset_counters` keeps it to "eggs in the last two minutes" with `counting.since` saying what the number covers. What it is not: it counts what the model **saw** (2 eggs of 147 were missed) and it says nothing about the inside of any of them. The graded profile stays available, with its own disclaimer. In plain words: the demo counts eggs honestly instead of pretending to grade them · [BUG-0906](../bugs/BUG-0906-2026-10-25-the-egg-line-published-a-grade-the-picture-cannot-support.md) · area: monitoring
- **The egg line was waiting for the tracker, not for the model (BUG-0910)** — the egg overlay refreshed **29 times a minute** on a host whose detector can do **1.33 detections a second**, and neither a faster model (BUG-0909) nor a shorter sampling period (BUG-0907) changed the live rate: it stayed at 0.47 → 0.48 analyses/s. A live profile of the running service (`py-spy dump`) put the analysis thread **inside the tracker**, not inside the detector. The monitor matches every detection against every live track, and it did it in pure Python, one pair at a time: the assignment cost matrix is ~175 detections × ~250-345 live tracks = **43 000-60 000 pairs**, each calling the prediction and the IoU, and the assignment itself ran on a pure-Python Hungarian solver worth **0.2 s** on the operator's Mac and about four times that on the box. The detector was 0.24-0.75 s of a cycle that took ~2 s, so the tracker decided how fast the line could run. It is now computed with arrays — every IoU in one operation, the cost matrix built from them, the "same kind?" test a comparison of label groups, and the assignment solved by `scipy.optimize.linear_sum_assignment` (scipy is already on every host, scikit-learn pulls it in; the pure-Python solver stays as the fallback). **The answers did not change**: the new tracker is compared against the original implementation on a moving-belt sequence and matches it pair for pair and track id for track id, and the cost matrix is the same matrix cell for cell. It is **~20x faster** (0.25 s → 0.012 s per frame on the egg line's workload), and every counting profile pays less: the monitor service runs three profiles in ONE python process, so a tracker that burns a second of CPU per analysis slows the other devices' monitoring down too. **Measured live on `vpt-lenovo1` over a 60 s window: the egg line went from 0.48 to 0.85 analyses/s (960, count only), from 0.45 to 0.67 (960, graded) and from 0.48 to 1.57 analyses/s (640, count only)** — the circles now refresh about every 0.6 s instead of every 2 s. In plain words: the egg line was queuing behind its own bookkeeping · [BUG-0910](../bugs/BUG-0910-2026-10-25-the-egg-line-was-waiting-for-the-tracker-not-for-the-model.md) · area: monitoring
- **The egg line runs three times faster on the same box (BUG-0909)** — the egg overlay refreshed **29 times a minute** (0.48 analyses/s, median gap 1.81 s) on a host whose detector can do **1.33 detections a second**, while the picture is written 5 frames a second. Raising the sampling period (BUG-0907) did not help — the live rate went from 0.47 to 0.48 — because the host was never waiting for permission to look, it was **waiting for CPU**: three other device profiles and the archive ffmpegs put the load average at **8.5 on 4 cores**. The lever that buys speed on a busy box is a **smaller input**, and that had never been tried on the belt model. The **same weights** exported at 640x640 (a separate file: the input size is baked into the ONNX graph) find **98.0 %** of the eggs — one egg of 147 less than at 960 — in **0.19-0.30 s** a pass instead of 0.65-0.75 s, i.e. **4.2 detections/s on an idle box instead of 1.33**. Two export details cost an afternoon and are written down so nobody repeats them: the 640 export with the ONNX simplifier on is the one **OpenCV 4.14 cannot even load** ("total(srcShape) == maskTotal" in the DFL reshape), and `dedupe_dist` has to be 0.40 at this size, not 0.35 — the boxes are smaller, the same egg is found twice a few pixels apart, and 0.35 leaves 3 duplicated pairs where 0.40 leaves **0** without merging a single pair of neighbouring eggs. A new profile `egg_count_yolo_belt_fast` runs it, with its own model key (`MONITOR_YOLO_BELT_FAST_MODEL`) and still **no grader**: the count is what the picture can support. In plain words: the same eggs, counted three times faster, for one egg of accuracy · [BUG-0909](../bugs/BUG-0909-2026-10-25-the-egg-line-can-run-three-times-faster-on-the-same-box.md) · area: monitoring
- **The egg line stopped waiting for permission to look (BUG-0907)** — the belt profile asked for `sample_hz: 0.5`, a number inherited from the *old* sliced profile where one pass really did cost 12.4 s (twelve 640x640 forwards) and which was wrong the moment the belt model replaced them with **one** 960x960 forward (0.65-0.75 s on that host). The worker takes the newest pending frame and analyses it once a period has passed, so a cycle costs `max(period, analysis time)`: at 0.5 Hz the period was 2 s against ~1.0 s of work, so the box spent half its time idle. The period is now set from the measured cost of a cycle — **1.0 Hz** for the graded profile, **1.2 Hz** for the count-only one — and the numbers are written down where they can be found again (the profiles, §8c of `docs/agent/devices/MONITOR_PROFILES.md`, the notes in `monitor_profile.py`). **And the honest part: raising the period alone did not make the box faster.** The live rate barely moved (0.47 -> 0.48 analyses/s) because the host was never waiting for permission to look, it was **waiting for CPU**: three other device profiles and the archive ffmpegs put the load average at **8.5 on 4 cores**, and the monitor process is scheduled on 1.2 of them. The lever that actually buys speed on a busy box is a smaller input (BUG-0908). In plain words: the egg line stops asking for permission and starts using the CPU it actually gets · [BUG-0907](../bugs/BUG-0907-2026-10-25-the-egg-line-analysed-at-half-the-speed-the-box-allowed.md) · area: monitoring- **The egg overlay now finds 98.6 % of the eggs instead of 78 % (BUG-0870)** — the circles that were missing were not lost to a threshold, a tile size or a zoom: they were lost to the **wrong model for the scene**. The shipped detector was a YOLOv8s *segmentation* model trained on the public Roboflow `egg-seg-1` set — eggs photographed close up on warm rods in daylight — while the device3 feed is a landscape shot of small white eggs on a saturated blue belt. Somebody else has trained a detector on a conveyor belt of white eggs (`Moein-Talebi/egg-detection-tracking`, public GitHub, no account, no key): a YOLOv8n *detection* model with one class, `egg`. On the same fixed frame it finds **98.6 %** of the eggs where the old one found 78.2 %, and it is not a hair's breadth more: **194 circles, 1 duplicated pair, 97.4 % of them on a white egg, and the ellipse covers 95 % of the egg it sits on**. The number is only worth something because the ground truth was built **without** the new model — the union of four *other* detectors, merged, filtered to the boxes on a white egg, and then canonicalised to one box per distinct egg (the raw pool counted 18 pairs of the same egg twice, and a denominator that counts an egg twice flatters whoever is being measured): 147 eggs, and the belt model covers 98.6 % of them. **It is also 35x faster, because it does not have to be sliced**: a detection model with no mask branch is happy with the whole frame at 960x960, so this is **one forward in 0.36 s** where the old profile needed twelve forwards and 12.4 s — which is why it can sample twice a second and why the good/bad vote window is reachable again (before, an egg crossed the belt between two passes and every egg came out `unverified`, 191 of 191). Each knob is measured: `input_size` 960 (640 gave 98.0 %, 1280 gave 97.3 %), `fill: letterbox` (**stretch** gave 94.5 % — a wide frame stretched into a square turns every egg into a tall ellipse), `min_conf: 0.02` (the head's scores are already activated, so this is a real 2 % confidence), `dedupe_dist: 0.35` (duplicate pairs: 0 → 49, 0.25 → 18, 0.30 → 6, 0.35 → 1). The four circles that sit on no egg are four eggs the ground truth itself missed, not false positives. It ships as a **third profile**, `egg_quality_yolo_belt`, beside the two others, reading its own model key (`MONITOR_YOLO_BELT_MODEL` — the two models have different heads and one key cannot serve both); the weights are **not in git**, the operator fetches and exports them (the repository carries no licence file), and the install steps are in the monitor-profile doc. **Good/bad per egg still means nothing here**: the classifier grades white eggs on a blue belt in daylight, where the image carries no evidence of interior quality, so it reports ~99 % bad because that is what the model says. A real grade needs candling · [BUG-0870](../bugs/BUG-0870-2026-10-23-the-egg-overlay-missed-a-quarter-of-the-eggs.md) · area: monitoring
- **The Pokémon's battle moves are free too (BUG-0871)** — the Pokémon's lane played on the free MiniMax M3.1 Flash, but only half of it: the **strategist** was on Flash while every **battle move** was still decided by Jev, a paid model (~$0.0026 a fight). The chooser is pinned by name, so the free route never reached the decisions. The runner now has a `--chooser flash` — the same free profile, pinned, with **no fallback**: a throttled or failing call returns nothing and the game falls back to its own heuristic for that turn instead of paying for another model — and the volume lane asks for it by default. The dataset deployment keeps Jev. In plain words: a Pokémon's run now costs $0 from the first move to the last · [BUG-0871](../bugs/BUG-0871-2026-10-23-the-pokemons-battle-moves-were-billed-to-a-paid-model.md) · [TASK-164](../tasks/TASK-164-flash-play.md) · area: games
- **A dead browser page is now seen as a stall (BUG-0868)** — the Pokémon's runs stopped moving and stayed stopped: the emulator page stopped answering (every read of the game's memory waited out its 15 s timeout) and the run simply sat there until its budget ran out. The stall detector counted the game time of a tick but capped it at 5 s, so a tick that really lasted a minute was counted as 5 s: the stall needed 60 ticks — an hour — and the run never reported one, so no fixer was called. A slow tick now counts the time it really took, and a dead page is seen as a stall after ~5 minutes: the run ends cleanly, writes its stall report and the lock, and the watcher does what it does for every other stall. In plain words: a run that cannot read the game anymore stops itself and asks for help, instead of sitting there for ever · [BUG-0868](../bugs/BUG-0868-2026-10-23-the-stall-detector-could-not-see-a-dead-browser-page.md) · [TASK-164](../tasks/TASK-164-flash-play.md) · area: games
- **A lab host that is restarting is no longer unreachable for half a minute (BUG-0866)** — a Raspberry Pi lab host (`vpt-pi1`) ran at load 12-17 on its 4 cores and the Atlas / stb benchmark got **502** and timeouts when it asked the server for a picture of a set-top box. Two causes, both in the restart path. **The server was killed instead of stopped:** gunicorn was given the same 30 s to let a busy worker finish as systemd was given to stop the unit, so the arbiter was still waiting when systemd gave up — the unit logged `Failed with result 'timeout'`, the process was killed, and the API was gone for the rest of the restart. It now gives up after 10 s, and the server's own cleanup runs once instead of twice. **The host had the same shape of problem one layer down:** it waited up to 5 s for its ping thread, 5 s for its metrics thread and 10 s to unregister itself from the server — and the server it was unregistering from was usually the server being restarted at the same moment — then did all of it a second time on the way out, so 40 s of waiting sat inside a 30 s budget. The exit is now one pass of ~7 s, and a picture request that meets a host in the middle of its restart is **asked again** instead of being answered 502 on the spot. **And the host was shouting into a full journal:** the capture monitor wrote the same "freeze" line for every frame of a long freeze and the subtitle monitor wrote its decision for every frame of every device — 423,000 lines a day, three quarters of everything on the box — which filled the 500 MB journal quota and made the system throw away four days of history, so the next incident could not be diagnosed. Both now say what changed instead of repeating themselves, and the OCR and the screen matcher no longer run again on a picture that has not changed. In plain words: a host that is restarted stays reachable, the box stops drowning its own history, and a request that meets a restarting host waits for it instead of failing · [BUG-0866](../bugs/BUG-0866-2026-10-23-a-host-that-is-being-restarted-is-unreachable-for-half-a-minute.md) · [TASK-169](../tasks/TASK-169-vpt-pi1-load.md) · area: monitoring
- **The free model lane waited for its turn instead of giving up (BUG-0864)** — every MiniMax M3.1 Flash call of the 08:00 hour answered `rate_limit` (the account is shared with the paid models of the same provider, and another job made 1685 calls in that hour), and the games stopped dead: the Clash of Clans Flash run started at 09:33 and died 21 seconds later on its first call, the Pokémon's strategist call failed the same way and the run sat in a menu for half an hour. A refused request is never billed, so a **free** model is now asked again on the same hop (3 times, 5 s / 20 s / 45 s apart) instead of being written off, and a throttled game step is **skipped, not fatal**: the run notes it and plays on. A model that costs money is never waited for — its caller moves on to the fallback chain as before, so nothing of this can cost anything. In plain words: the free lane queues for its turn instead of quitting at the first refusal · [BUG-0864](../bugs/BUG-0864-2026-10-23-the-free-model-lane-stopped-at-the-first-rate-limit.md) · [TASK-164](../tasks/TASK-164-flash-play.md) · area: ai
- **The lease of a running game was released while the game was still playing (BUG-0863)** — the Pokémon's lease died two minutes after it was claimed: the holder name was `codebox:TASK33-pokemon`, the name of a tmux session that existed and is long over, and the lease watcher releases the lease of a holder whose session **ended**. Another run took the same 2-core host 23 seconds later and the two starved each other: the browser page became unresponsive and the game never moved again. The dispatcher now holds its lease under a name no session ever had, asks for the 10 minutes the registry actually grants, and renews it every 3 minutes (the CoC Flash dispatcher did the same); a lease that cannot be renewed is now **said out loud** instead of being ignored. In plain words: a game keeps its device for as long as it plays on it · [BUG-0863](../bugs/BUG-0863-2026-10-23-the-lease-of-a-running-game-was-released-while-it-played.md) · [TASK-164](../tasks/TASK-164-flash-play.md) · area: platform
- **The health badges are plain text now (BUG-0861)** — the red "Monitoring problem" badge sat next to the amber "N in warning" badge and both wore the same icon, the amber warning triangle, so an open monitoring problem (an error) announced itself as a warning and only the colour told the two apart. The badges now carry no icon at all: "Monitoring problem", "N in error" and "N in warning" are words in their colours, on the desktop and on mobile. In plain words: the row says what it means, nothing more · [BUG-0861](../bugs/BUG-0861-2026-10-23-the-monitoring-problem-badge-wore-the-warning-triangle.md) · area: frontend
- **Atlas can take control of and release devices again, and no longer crashes on a bare key name (BUG-0862)** — "Release all stb" or "Take control of all devices on host X" was answered with "the tool is not available", "Volume up on stb3" crashed the key sender when the assistant sent just the key name, and "Press HOME on stb3" was answered from the documentation instead of pressing the key. The control tools are now offered to the skills that list them, a bare key name is sent as a key press, and a command that names a device never goes to the documentation answers. · [BUG-0862](../bugs/BUG-0862-2026-10-23-atlas-cannot-take-control-and-crashes-on-string-actions.md)
- **Every egg circle is on its egg again in the live overlay (BUG-0859)** — the detector was right and the overlay still showed the circles *beside* their eggs. The monitor's worker analyses one frame and attaches that result to every frame's JSON until the next analysis, so the JSON of capture N usually carries the boxes of an **older** frame — and `captured_at` says so. But the JSON's `filename` named the capture the JSON was *attached to*, while the client draws the picture it fetches from `filename` (BUG-0836's frame-lock: "show the capture the newest analysis was computed FROM"). So the client drew a picture up to one sample period plus one analysis time newer than the boxes, and on a belt the eggs had moved: measured live on the device3 line, the boxes sat a **19 px median** (90th percentile 45 px) from the detections of that capture, and **33 of 223 circles had a mostly-blue interior** — bare belt, not an egg. The client's own compensation cannot cover it either: the eggs on this belt move 2-3 px/s, so only 20 of 223 observations carried a velocity, and in frame-lock mode the boxes are deliberately not carried forward. The frame JSON now names **the frame the boxes were measured on**, exactly like `captured_at` already did, so the picture and the boxes are the same frame again and the circles are on the eggs by construction · [BUG-0859](../bugs/BUG-0859-2026-10-23-the-live-overlay-drew-the-egg-circles-on-bare-belt.md) · area: monitoring
- **The egg line's profile was refused by the host as unusable (BUG-0860)** — the monitor logged `profile 'egg_quality_yolo' is unusable (ui.stats is ...); its devices count as tv_app`. There are two validators for a monitor profile, and they disagreed: the pydantic model accepted a stat that reads a KPI (`{label: Eggs, kpi: count_total}`) and one that reads the observation count (`{label: Detections, observations: true}`), while the shared validator — the one the host uses to decide **which services a device gets** — demanded that `observations` be a bool on **every** stat. A stat that reads a KPI has no `observations` key at all, so the check fired on every row and the profile BUG-0848 shipped was never a valid profile on the host that runs it. The key is now only checked when it is there, and the four broken shapes (a non-bool `observations`, both sources at once, neither source, an unknown key) are still refused · [BUG-0860](../bugs/BUG-0860-2026-10-23-the-shared-validator-refused-any-stat-row.md) · area: monitoring
- **The amber "N in warning" badge could not say which hosts it counted (BUG-0857)** — the badge counts **hosts**, but its tooltip always announced a version mismatch, even for a host that was amber because its disk is over 85 % or because the backend reports it degraded, and it never named the hosts, so "2 in warning" could not be matched with the cards below it. The tooltip now lists every amber host, the server it belongs to and the reason it is amber (different build — with both builds —, degraded backend, disk at N %). The badge is clickable too: it narrows the dashboard to the amber hosts, exactly like the red "N in error" chip narrows it to the hosts in error, and it switches itself off when the last amber host recovers, so it can never leave the page empty. In plain words: the number on the badge is now the list behind it · [BUG-0857](../bugs/BUG-0857-2026-10-23-the-warning-badge-counted-hosts-the-tooltip-could-not-name.md) · area: frontend
- **The Pokémon runner knows the water of every map (BUG-0855)** — Route 23 is crossed by Surf, and the runner's map model only knew the water of the overworld tileset, so on every other map the sea, a river or a lake was a wall to it. Route 23 was therefore cut in two: the runner stood at the foot of the water with no route to Victory Road at all, talked to the people on the road instead and was called `same_spot` after 300 ticks. The game knows the water by its tile id alone, and so does the runner now: the water is a way as soon as a party member knows Surf, the map is one part again, and the way to Victory Road opens. In plain words: the runner surfs over the water instead of standing in front of it · [BUG-0855](../bugs/BUG-0855-2026-10-23-the-pokemon-runner-does-not-know-the-water-of-a-map-that-is-not-the-overworld-tileset.md) · area: game
- **The nightly virtual scripts died on a flag that carried its dashes twice (BUG-0856)** — every night at 00:32 the `game_pokemon` step of the nightly web deployment died before it started: *"Unknown argument(s) for game_pokemon: ----userinterface pokemon-blue-web"*. A campaign stores a step's parameters as a map whose keys already carry their dashes, and the executor prefixed them again. Eleven of that night's thirty scripts carry a parameter map and all eleven were built the same way. The flag is now rebuilt from the normalised name, so the nightly scripts start. · [BUG-0856](../bugs/BUG-0856-2026-10-23-nightly-virtual-scripts-died-on-a-double-dashed-flag.md) · [TASK-164](../tasks/TASK-164-flash-play.md) · area: backend
- **A free model was charged like a paid one (BUG-0853)** — a Clash of Clans run on MiniMax M3.1 Flash, an unlimited free-plan model, reported a spend of $0.0249 a run and $3.92 in the month, while the real cost of the same calls was $0. The budget meter looked the price up on the endpoint the player talks to, found no row for that model, and fell through to another model of the same endpoint (Haiku). The free model now has an explicit zero price and the meter accepts a price of zero as what it is, so the daily allowance no longer refuses the day's free runs. · [BUG-0853](../bugs/BUG-0853-2026-10-23-the-coc-budget-meter-charged-a-free-model-like-a-paid-one.md) · [TASK-164](../tasks/TASK-164-flash-play.md) · area: ai
- **The Pokémon save stayed locked for two and a half days (BUG-0854)** — a stall locked the save and the dispatcher starts no run while it is locked, so nothing played from 2026-10-20 21:47. The watcher that starts a fixer only looked at a three-hour window of stalled runs and never re-armed a report it had already handled, so a lock that outlived the window waited for ever for a human. The watcher now reads the canonical lock first — it names the stall — with no age limit, and re-arms a handled stall every six hours while the save is still locked. · [BUG-0854](../bugs/BUG-0854-2026-10-23-the-stall-watcher-never-re-armed-a-lock-older-than-three-hours.md) · [TASK-164](../tasks/TASK-164-flash-play.md) · area: agent
- **A photo of a wall was named "Full-screen poster" (TASK-162, BUG-0849)** — the rule that recognises a poster asks "is there a menu?" and treats "no menu" as "poster", which is only a fair question when the picture is a device screen at all. On a device pointing a camera at the room the answer is vacuously yes, so a blurry photograph of a wall was confidently named a poster — and because a screen name is never replaced once written, only a person could undo it. A new first question now asks whether the picture is of a screen or of the real world, and a photo is never named. Every such question is also now asked more than once and has to be answered the same way each time, because the free model does not always give the same answer twice: one stored frame came back MOVING, STILL, STILL. The two names written before this fix were put back the way they were. · [BUG-0849](../bugs/BUG-0849-2026-10-23-no-menu-poster-rule-named-photos-of-a-wall.md) · [TASK-162](../tasks/TASK-162-screen-label-process.md) · area: monitoring
- **A short Atlas command is no longer answered by an unrelated list (BUG-0851)** — "Screenshot <device>" or "Dump the UI of <device>" could come back as the page list or the host list, because the fast path accepted any tool the small model picked. It now accepts a list or info tool only when the message is about it, and otherwise hands the request to the full assistant. · [BUG-0851](../bugs/BUG-0851-2026-10-23-atlas-quick-step-picks-an-unrelated-info-tool.md)
- **"Take control of all stb" no longer fails with `unknown command: take_control` (BUG-0850)** — Atlas put "take control" into the keys it sent to the boxes, which the remote refuses. The group skill can now take control of, release and wake every device of a model by itself, a busy device is named and skipped, and a tool name sent as a key is refused with the tool to call instead. · [BUG-0850](../bugs/BUG-0850-2026-10-23-atlas-take-control-all-stb-unknown-command.md)
- **The free AI model works again on a machine whose AI library drifted (TASK-162)** — the platform pins its AI library below version 1.0 because 1.x broke the client. On a machine where 1.x was installed anyway, every call to the free MiniMax models failed with a type error before a byte was sent, because the newer library removed a setting from its call signature — which is why some assistant skills were failing on the live server too, with the cause hidden behind a generic error. The client now checks once what the installed library accepts and sends only that, so the call works on either version and the old behaviour is unchanged where the platform expects it. The screen-naming job also moved onto the free unlimited M3.1-Flash model, and carries a second free profile as backup, because the two MiniMax endpoints have separate allowances and a job that depends on one quota alone stops dead when that quota is dry. When the provider refuses a call — the plan is spent, the key is wrong for that endpoint — the job now moves straight to the other profile instead of asking the one that just refused again, because the answer would be identical. Two more things it learned on its first scheduled run, both of which had it naming nothing at all while looking perfectly healthy: the free model thinks before it answers and the thinking comes out of the same answer allowance, so the job now gives it room to finish — before, every answer came back empty and read as "unreadable frame" — and the question that decides "is this a screen or a photo of the room" now asks where the pixels come from rather than what the picture shows, because a poster of a film shows a person in a forest and was therefore being called a photograph. · [BUG-0865](../bugs/BUG-0865-2026-10-23-the-screen-label-worker-named-nothing-the-model-had-no-room-to-answer.md) · [TASK-162](../tasks/TASK-162-screen-label-process.md) · area: ai
- **A name is not a finished screen: the recurring screens get fingerprints too (TASK-162)** — the daily report was counting the screens with no NAME, and called a screen that had a name but no fingerprint "labelled". It is not finished: the goal is a screen that is labelled AND fingerprinted, and on the lab 21 of the 31 recurring screens had a name and no fingerprint. Two causes, both fixed. **The fingerprint could never be written to an existing row**: the monitor computes it on every sighting and handed it to the insert, while the path a repeat sighting takes threw it away, so a screen registered before fingerprints existed kept a NULL fingerprint for ever however often it was seen. A sighting now fills it — once. And a fingerprint gets its own single writer, like a name: it fills only an empty slot, it never overwrites one, it never renames a row and it never deletes one. The daily job also fills the rows that are already there, from the picture each row still keeps, checked against the row's own measured identity: a picture that does not agree is listed for a person instead of being written. The number the report prints is now **"screens >10 seen, not live, missing a label or a fingerprint"** — 25 on the lab when it landed and **5** after the day's worker run, down from a report that could not see the gap at all. · [BUG-0867](../bugs/BUG-0867-2026-10-23-a-named-screen-with-no-fingerprint-was-reported-as-finished.md) · [TASK-162](../tasks/TASK-162-screen-label-process.md) · area: ai
- **A frozen screen was registered without a fingerprint (TASK-162, BUG-0869)** — a screen row is created in two places, and only one of them had been fixed. The Localize monitor stores the picture's fingerprint when it registers a screen it has never seen, but the freeze detector has a second path: when a device freezes on a screen that Localize never published an id for, the freeze registers that frozen frame itself so the screen can be named later — and it registered it with the identity hashes only, so the row was born with no fingerprint. A poster on a screen is exactly that case (a picture that does not change is a freeze), so recurring posters kept coming back as unfinished in the daily report even after the fingerprint work had shipped. The freeze path now stores the fingerprint it can compute from the frame it is already holding, and when the freeze sees a screen that was registered before fingerprints existed, it fills the missing one through the same single writer that names use — never over an existing fingerprint. · [BUG-0869](../bugs/BUG-0869-2026-10-23-a-frozen-screen-was-registered-without-a-fingerprint.md) · [TASK-162](../tasks/TASK-162-screen-label-process.md) · area: monitoring
- **A deploy said "done" while the screens it registers ran days-old code (TASK-162, BUG-0893)** — the screen registry is written by the image-capture service, not by the host service, and a deploy only restarts the host service: the capture service keeps the code it was started with, and nothing said so. The bookkeeping made it invisible — the deploy's code marker tracks the host service only, so "the new files are on the disk" and "the new code is running" looked identical. It is not identical, and the difference cost a night: the screen-label report went from 5 unfinished screens to 17, because 293 screens were created without a fingerprint by a monitor still running the fix from five hours earlier — and on the same hosts the subtitle, audio, archiver, quality and KPI services were running code from **five days** earlier. A deploy that changes the host code now names the services that are still on the previous code, with the date each one started, in the deploy output and in the summary. It never restarts anything by itself — restarting the capture service drops frames for every device on that host, so that stays a deliberate step under the lease. The three deploy harnesses, including a new one for this report, now run in their own workflow on every push and every pull request, outside the nightly gate: a change merged in the afternoon used to get a green run that tested nothing, and the first version of this report read the banner as a service named "==>" because no test covered that parse. The harness that needs the lease registry can no longer skip for want of it, so a green run means the checks really ran. · [BUG-0893](../bugs/BUG-0893-2026-10-24-a-deploy-left-the-screen-monitor-running-days-old-code.md) · [TASK-162](../tasks/TASK-162-screen-label-process.md) · area: infra
- **A used-up AI plan now says so instead of looking like a blank answer** — when MiniMax's coding plan runs out of tokens, the endpoint answers with a success status but an empty reply, so the call was recorded as a wasted empty answer and the platform kept asking an endpoint that could not reply. The reply's own error field is now read: the call fails with the provider's words ("Token Plan usage limit reached"), the dashboard shows "no credit" instead of "wasted", and the platform stops calling that endpoint for five minutes rather than retrying. Any MiniMax call — a frame description, a screen naming job, an alert triage — now tells you the plan is finished instead of quietly returning nothing. · [TASK-162](../tasks/TASK-162-screen-label-process.md) · area: ai
- **Screens are now fingerprinted when they are registered (TASK-162, BUG-0847)** — the monitor registered every unknown screen with only its identity hashes and left the fingerprint itself empty, so a screen could end up with a name but no fingerprint: 18 of the 30 recurring screens on the lab were in that state. It now stores the fingerprint the matcher actually reads (full-frame hash, per-region hashes, focused-tile crop, focus signature) as part of registration, so every screen registered from now on is fingerprinted. Matching itself is unchanged — it never read that field — and screens already in the table keep working; their picture is simply not recoverable, so they gain a fingerprint the next time that screen is registered. The fingerprint is computed without the two text-recognition passes, so it costs the per-second monitoring loop nothing. · [BUG-0847](../bugs/BUG-0847-2026-10-23-monitor-registers-screens-without-a-fingerprint.md) · [TASK-162](../tasks/TASK-162-screen-label-process.md) · area: monitoring
- **Back / Home / Menu on the phone remote are squares again** — in Take Control on an Android phone the three icon buttons beside the OK/arrow D-pad rendered as tall narrow slivers (~22 px wide against 32 px tall) with an empty strip left on their right, so they looked nothing like the square D-pad buttons next to them. `flex: 1` was set on the `IconButton`, but `Tooltip` forces a wrapping `<span>` to be the flex item, so the rule did nothing and MUI's `IconButton` — which has padding but no width — collapsed to the bare 20 px icon. The grow now sits on the wrapper and each button is squared with `aspect-ratio`, so it stays square at any panel width instead of only when the panel happens to be a certain size. Each button is now 36 × 36, matching the OK button, and the row ends flush with the panel edge. In plain words: Back, Home and Menu look like the rest of the phone remote and are easier to hit · [BUG-0845](../bugs/BUG-0845-2026-10-22-phone-remote-back-home-menu-buttons-render-narrow-not-square.md) · area: frontend
- **The phone remote uses the whole panel width** — the Back / Home / Menu row was still drawn inside a column capped at 238 px while the panel itself is wider, so the three square buttons came out smaller than they could be and a strip of the panel stayed empty on their right. The column now uses the full panel width, so the buttons grow to the space the panel really has. In plain words: the buttons are as big as the panel allows · [BUG-0852](../bugs/BUG-0852-2026-10-23-android-mobile-back-home-menu-buttons-leave-the-panel-width-unused.md) · area: frontend

- **Tapping a phone's stream works again on a host that has its own server** — on a phone attached to a host registered on another server (vpt-pi1), every tap on the stream failed with "Host not found" while the remote keys worked, because the tap went to the server picked in the UI instead of the host's own server. Taps now go where the keys go. In plain words: tap the phone screen in Take Control and it reacts · [BUG-0844](../bugs/BUG-0844-2026-10-22-stream-tap-on-phone-asks-the-wrong-server.md)
- **Take Control reconnects an Android device that dropped off adb** — when a phone or Android TV reached over ADB/TCP disappeared from `adb devices` (Wi-Fi blip, reboot), Take Control reported the remote as unavailable and someone had to run `adb connect` on the host by hand. Take Control now notices the device is gone and recovers it itself, least disruptive step first: `adb connect` (after `adb disconnect` when it shows offline); if the same phone is plugged in over USB (matched by its Wi-Fi address), `adb tcpip <port>` and connect again; and `adb kill-server` + `start-server` only when the adb server itself does not answer, since that drops every adb device on the host. A device with both `DEVICEn_ADB_SERIAL` and `DEVICEn_IP` set now logs a warning at host start saying the USB serial is used. In plain words: take control and the remote comes back on its own · [BUG-0843](../bugs/BUG-0843-2026-10-22-take-control-does-not-reconnect-dropped-adb-device.md)
- **Tello video through a USB Wi-Fi dongle (AIC8800DC) finally arrives** — a host flying Tellos through TP-Link AX300 / AIC8800DC USB dongles could talk to the drone but never receive its video: every frame above about 520 bytes came in cut to 512 bytes of payload (`truncated-ip` in tcpdump, 100 % loss on `ping -s 600`, the device tile stuck on "Starting video..."). The drone fragments every frame that size into three 802.11 fragments and the out-of-tree driver forwarded each fragment as a whole frame. The driver now reassembles fragments before handing them up; `setup/scripts/aic8800dc/install_aic8800dc.sh` installs the patched DKMS package (`6.4.3.0-patched.17+vpt.1`), and a reboot, never an `rmmod`, loads it. In plain words: plug in the dongles, run the installer, reboot, and the drones stream · [BUG-0839](../bugs/BUG-0839-2026-10-22-aic8800-dongle-delivers-fragmented-frames-truncated.md) · [Tello over USB Wi-Fi](../agent/devices/TELLO_USB_WIFI.md)
- **The Tetris bot stopped hammering the browser while it waited for the next piece** — between pieces, the game needs about a second to drop one, and the bot spent that second re-reading the page as fast as the host's browser could answer — no pause at all. It was measured issuing **41,598 reads in 2.5 seconds**, which saturated the one browser connection the host shares, so the screenshot every keypress needs started timing out and a run that normally takes two minutes no longer fit inside its time budget. The bot now checks the page a few times a second instead of thousands, costs nothing in how fast it plays (Tetris drops a piece about once a second anyway), and respects its time cap even while it is waiting. In plain words: the game is played at the same speed, the host's browser is no longer held hostage while it waits · [BUG-0841](../bugs/BUG-0841-2026-10-22-tetris-piece-wait-hot-spins-the-browser-controller.md)
- **Browsers left behind by a run that died are now closed, not just counted** — a browser whose run died keeps its slot forever: the host knows exactly which ports are corpses (`browser_run_status()` said `leaked: 2` with `running: 0` on one host) but the kill had been left out on purpose, because a job that kills the wrong process once took a host's SSH down with it. That caution outlived its reason — every safe piece was already there and tested, and the only path that could have cleaned up was unreachable in practice, since a new run is always handed a fresh port and never visits the corpse. So leaks only ever accumulate, counting against the cap until the host refuses new browser runs while looking perfectly idle. A run that leaves a browser behind now closes it every five minutes, through the same guarded path that refuses a port owned by a live run and that can never signal the service's process group, and never touching the host's own browser on 9222. `VPT_REAP_LEAKED_BROWSERS=0` turns it off. In plain words: the host stops slowly losing the ability to start browser tests · [BUG-0838](../bugs/BUG-0838-2026-10-22-leaked-browsers-are-counted-but-never-closed.md)
- **A run that dies no longer leaves a phantom failure behind for ever** — every run opens with a placeholder result row and closes it when the script finishes. When the run died first, nothing closed it: the host's recovery path only touched a row it could reach through the run's id, and that id is read out of the *finished* run's output, so for exactly the runs that died it was never there. The row kept `success = false` and no reason at all — a failure that never happened, counted on the dashboard for ever (110 such rows by 2026-10-22, 28 of them in a single day, one browser game accounting for most of them). A dead run now finds the placeholder it left behind (same host, same script, un-finished) and closes it with a reason, and a periodic sweep closes anything older than two hours that no run links — comfortably above the longest legitimate run, so a slow-but-healthy run is never rewritten. In plain words: the failure rate you see is now about runs that really failed, not about runs that died · [BUG-0837](../bugs/BUG-0837-2026-10-22-dead-run-leaves-phantom-failure-forever.md)
- **A device now only offers WEB when it can actually do WebRTC** — every device showed a WEB button next to LOW / SD / HD, whatever the host was capable of. On a host with no MediaMTX service (the cloud farm devices on host-clone-1, for instance) every press was a guaranteed six-second timeout and a fallback to HLS. Each device now declares what it can deliver: a capture grabber on a host running MediaMTX can do WEB; a capture that is not a grabber, or a host with no MediaMTX, shows WEB **greyed out with the reason on hover** (“this host runs no MediaMTX”, “this capture is not a grabber”); a **cloud farm** device (Sauce Labs, BrowserStack) says plainly that it runs in the vendor’s datacentre and there is no stream to carry — which is true, and not something installing anything here would fix. If you had switched a device to WEB before, that choice is cleared so it stops asking. Devices on a host that has not reported yet keep working exactly as before. In plain words: the button is only there when pressing it can work · [BUG-0835](../bugs/BUG-0835-2026-10-22-webrtc-offered-where-it-cannot-work.md)

- **A WebRTC fallback now says which device and why** — when the live view drops from WebRTC back to HLS, the notification was the bare words "WebRTC unavailable — back on HLS": no device, no host, no reason, and clicking it took you back to the page you happened to be on (the Heatmap) instead of to the device whose stream had failed. With six devices open they were also indistinguishable, because they all shared one notification. It now reads `host-clone-1 · Sauce_S23_FE: WebRTC unavailable (no frame in time) — using HLS`, every device gets its own entry, and **Show** opens that device on Device Control. The reason comes from the browser itself (no frame in time, the WHEP request blocked by CORS / cookie / proxy, connection dropped), so you can tell a slow start from a blocked request without opening the developer console. In plain words: the picture still worked, but now the app tells you which one went wrong and takes you there. · [BUG-0832](../bugs/BUG-0832-2026-10-22-webrtc-fallback-notification-names-no-device.md)

- **Filtering targets then picking a model no longer empties the grid (BUG-0829)** — the device grid is the AND of four filters (target, model, profile, tag) but every option list was built from the whole fleet, so an axis could offer a value that no device had left after the target filter ran. Select 4 targets, open **More**, pick an STB model, and the page said "No targets match the selected filters" with a filter set that explains nothing. Options are now counted over the devices that survive your current selection, so a value is on offer only while a surviving device still carries it — and the list is recomputed after every click, which also closes the tag trap where a second tag emptied the grid one step after the first. A value you already picked stays visible even if its device goes offline, so you can always clear your way out. **Targets themselves are untouched on purpose:** that filter is a union, so adding a target can only widen the grid, and narrowing it would have broken the "Select all = every target" rule (BUG-0822). Device Control and the Dashboard, which share the same filters, are fixed together. In plain words: every filter you can still click is a filter that keeps showing devices · [BUG-0829](../bugs/BUG-0829-2026-10-22-filtering-then-picking-a-model-empties-the-grid.md) · area: frontend
- **A "runs failed" notification now opens the failing runs** — clicking the bell entry for "78 of 240 runs failed" did nothing useful: it took you back to the dashboard, the very page that raised it. The dashboard's failure-spike alert was the one notification still carrying a placeholder target instead of the page that shows the cause. It now opens the reports page already filtered to the failed runs, over the same time window the counts came from, so the list you land on is the set the alert was describing. In plain words: click the warning, see the failures · [BUG-0830](../bugs/BUG-0830-2026-10-22-failure-spike-notification-links-to-dashboard.md)
- **A dismissed notification stays dismissed, and a toast shows at most twice** — the × in the bell (and Clear) removed an entry only until the platform re-detected the same problem, which put it back, and a re-detected problem could pop its toast again and again. A dismissed or cleared notification is now remembered in your browser and never comes back, and the same notification toasts at most twice. The toast button is now **Skip** (it dismisses the notification for good) instead of Show (clicking the message still opens the page), and toasts appear below the top bar so you can keep navigating. · [BUG-0828](../bugs/BUG-0828-2026-10-22-dismissed-notification-comes-back.md)

- **Caption translation no longer burns paid AI calls** — when MiniMax was rate limited the router fell back to the paid OpenRouter model, which answered empty after thinking (about $1.5 an hour). Caption translation now skips the paid fallback after a rate limit, stops asking an endpoint that keeps answering empty for 15 minutes, and asks OpenRouter not to think · [BUG-0831](../bugs/BUG-0831-2026-10-22-caption-translation-burns-paid-openrouter-calls.md) · area: ai
- **Pairing a Tello on a host with one dongle per drone ([BUG-0827](../bugs/BUG-0827-2026-10-22-pairing-a-tello-fails-on-a-two-radio-host.md))** — the pairing route joined the wrong Wi-Fi radio and never saw the drone as connected. It now joins each drone through its own dongle. In plain words: with two dongles, each drone pairs and shows as connected
- **A device card stuck on "Loading stream..." finally tells you why (BUG-0826)** — when a host is not writing a capture, the HLS manifest answers 404. The handler written to stop on exactly that was erasing its own message: it set the error and then called the cleanup, which sets the error back to null, so the card landed on the one state that draws "Loading stream..." — and then re-requested the same missing manifest every 6 s forever (measured on `vpt-lenovo6`: 60 s+ of "Loading stream...", a 404 on every request, while `getStreamUrl` itself answered in 79 ms). It now stops, says "Stream manifest not found (404)", and comes back on its own once the host starts writing again. Behind it, every API call now has a deadline, so a request that never answers becomes an error instead of a spinner with no end: `getStreamUrl` has 15 s, everything else 60 s. Cards also gained the terminal states they were missing — a device that cannot be reached shows the reason instead of a transient toast and a permanent loader, a broken VNC device no longer hides its error behind "Loading...", and a stream that has given up says "unavailable after 5 retries" rather than "Retrying...". In plain words: if it is never going to play, the card says so instead of waiting forever. · [BUG-0826](../bugs/BUG-0826-2026-10-22-loading-stream-forever-manifest-404.md) · area: frontend
- **The platform is labelled "production" again in Settings (BUG-0783)** — every operator who opened Settings on the public platform read `ENVIRONMENT=development`, while the platform ran as production. The public server now reports `production` (the live `.env` is kept by design — it holds secrets — so the value was corrected in place, one line, with a backup). The key is a label, not a switch — the switch the code uses is `VPT_ENVIRONMENT` (`dev` / `test` / `prod`) — so nothing changed under the hood; what changed is that the platform no longer calls itself a development build. The same page read `DEBUG = 1` next to it, a value the server only honours as `true` — so it never turned debug mode on but it read as if it had; that now says `false` plainly. In plain words: Settings tells the truth about where it runs · [BUG-0783](../bugs/BUG-0783-2026-10-22-settings-reports-environment-development-on-the-live-server.md) · area: platform
- **Two Tello drones on one host both show video ([BUG-0824](../bugs/BUG-0824-2026-10-22-two-tello-video-ffmpegs-bind-the-same-udp-port.md))** — the second drone's video capture died because both captures took the same UDP port on every address. Each now listens only on its own drone's address. In plain words: two drones, two working video tiles
- **The Quality tables are readable on a phone (BUG-0794)** — On a 390 px screen the Flaky, Slow and Not-executed tables squeezed the Test column to nothing: the test names broke to one letter per line ("g / a / m / e …") and the last column was cut off. The Test column now asks for its width and each table asks for a minimum width, so the table scrolls sideways instead of squeezing the names — `stb_android_mobile` stays on one line. In plain words: you can read the test names on a phone · [BUG-0794](../bugs/BUG-0794-2026-10-22-quality-table-on-a-phone-breaks-test-names-one-letter-per-line.md) · area: frontend
- **The Quality page's "Window" label is readable again (BUG-0814)** — The window selector on `/test-results/quality` carried two labels on one control, and the app paints an opaque background on every field, which covered the lower half of the word: only the top row of the letters showed. The label is now plain text next to the select, and it still names the field for a screen reader. In plain words: the word "Window" can be read · [BUG-0814](../bugs/BUG-0814-2026-10-22-quality-window-label-is-painted-behind-the-select.md) · area: frontend
- **The KPI tiles no longer glue two numbers together (BUG-0816)** — The Coverage tile read "1915%" (the count and the rate as one number) and the Localize tile read "78.6%327/416". The shared tile now separates the second figure, and the Localize tile writes its total as "10 / 34". In plain words: the tile shows two numbers, not one strange one · [BUG-0816](../bugs/BUG-0816-2026-10-22-coverage-kpi-tile-prints-count-and-percentage-glued-together.md) · area: frontend
- **The Activity audit fits a phone (BUG-0817)** — On a 390 px screen the activity table was 974 px wide in a 324 px box, so only "When" and a word of "Event" were visible and Target, Details and Actor were off screen. The table now fills its box, the cells wrap, and the longest column (Details) is left out on a phone — it stays in the CSV export. In plain words: the audit reads on a phone · [BUG-0817](../bugs/BUG-0817-2026-10-22-activity-audit-table-is-974-px-wide-on-a-phone.md) · area: frontend
- **The Requirements summary keeps its stats on a phone (BUG-0818)** — The bar above the requirements table was one line that never wrapped, so on a 390 px screen it read "RequirementsTotal 131 | Active 131 | No coverag…" and the P1/P2/P3 chips were gone. The bar wraps now, and all six stats are on screen. In plain words: the summary is complete on a phone · [BUG-0818](../bugs/BUG-0818-2026-10-22-requirements-summary-bar-loses-its-stats-on-a-phone.md) · area: frontend
- **The Interface names are written whole on a phone (BUG-0819)** — On a 390 px screen the Interface table broke the names in the middle of the word ("stb_android_mobil" / "e"), cut "Active" to "Acti" and clipped one cell to 8 px. The table now asks for a minimum width and the page scrolls sideways instead of cutting the table. In plain words: the names are readable · [BUG-0819](../bugs/BUG-0819-2026-10-22-interface-table-breaks-names-in-the-middle-of-the-word-on-a-phone.md) · area: frontend
- **The Status page reads in French and German (BUG-0785)** — On `/status` the four summary tiles (Services healthy, Server CPU, Server RAM, Server disk) stayed English in French and German while the table underneath was translated, so the page looked half broken. The page header (System Status, Last checked, Refresh now) and the uplink tile were English too, and four of the strings it used were stubs — the catalogue had the English word under the French and German keys. Everything on that page is translated now, and the page is on the i18n check's list, so a raw English literal in it fails the build instead of shipping. In plain words: the Status page speaks your language, from the title to the tiles · [BUG-0785](../bugs/BUG-0785-2026-10-22-status-page-tile-labels-stay-english-in-french-and-german.md) · area: frontend
- **The picture-in-picture panel shows the device again (BUG-0777)** — Picking a browser device in the Picture-in-picture menu did nothing but print an error in the console: the player reads a state that only existed on a few pages, so on every other page the panel crashed instead of opening ("useVNCState must be used within a VNCStateProvider"). The state is now provided wherever a player is drawn — the picture-in-picture overlay, the test case builder, the virtual scripts page — while the pages that share it still share it. The panel also lands where it should: it used to be measured starting at its anchor and running past the right edge of the screen; it now ends on the anchor, 20 px from the right edge and 56 px above the bottom, and grows to the left of its anchor when the remote is shown. In plain words: the floating device window opens where you expect it to · [BUG-0777](../bugs/BUG-0777-2026-10-22-picture-in-picture-panel-throws-usevncstate-error.md) · area: frontend
- **The AI chat on a phone has a way out (BUG-0776)** — On a phone the Agent Chat takes the whole screen and the bottom navigation is hidden on that route, so the page had no way back at all: the only controls were the send button and the global run strip. In the native app, which has no browser chrome, that was a dead end. The chat now opens with a back arrow and the page title: the arrow returns to the page the chat was opened from, and when the app was launched straight onto the chat it lands on the dashboard instead of nowhere. The arrow says "Back" in English, "Retour" in French and "Zurück" in German. In plain words: you can always get out of the chat on a phone · [BUG-0776](../bugs/BUG-0776-2026-10-22-agent-chat-on-a-phone-has-no-way-out.md) · area: frontend
- **The top bar no longer prints the "N online" chip on top of the Settings label (BUG-0773)** — In French and German the header's "N en ligne" chip landed on the Settings label on every page ("Paramèt<chip>es", "Einstell<chip>ungen"): the ten nav items carried 12 px of padding each and their labels could not shrink, so in those two languages the row had no free space left and the chip was printed over the word. The nav items now shrink and the label is what gets cut, the row clips so it can never reach the chip, and the padding is halved, which gives the row back the room the chip needs: at 1440 px the chip and the Settings label stand side by side in English, French and German, in dark and light. On a screen too narrow for every label, the word keeps its full text in a tooltip. In plain words: the chip no longer sits on the Settings label · [BUG-0773](../bugs/BUG-0773-2026-10-22-header-nav-overlaps-online-chip-in-french-and-german.md) · area: frontend

- **Capture streams come back by themselves after a host reboot ([BUG-0823](../bugs/BUG-0823-2026-10-22-vpt-stream-does-not-start-after-reboot-on-hosts-with-screencap-loops.md))** — on hosts that run Android screencap loops the capture service was dropped at boot by a systemd ordering cycle, so every tile showed "Loading stream..." until someone started it by hand.
- **Live streams from a host on a slow link stop freezing (BUG-0825)** — On a host reached over weak WiFi (vpt-mf1) the live picture kept stopping for a moment and the overlay showed half the frames dropped, in HLS and in WebRTC. The host now compresses the live playlist (28 KB → 2 KB per refresh). After its first stall, an HLS player moves from 1 s to 3 s behind live and stops speeding up to catch up (1.1× instead of 1.5×, which made the browser drop every other frame). A WebRTC player that sees packet loss holds 500 ms of buffer, so resent packets arrive in time (frozen 32 s → 9 s in 80 s on vpt-mf1). The WebRTC overlay now shows its latency. A host on a good link never stalls and keeps its low delay. In plain words: a far-away box plays smoothly, a little further behind live · [BUG-0825](../bugs/BUG-0825-2026-10-22-live-stream-stalls-and-drops-on-a-slow-host-link.md)

- **Picking a script no longer makes your Run target disappear (BUG-0789)** — On the Run page, tick a target and then choose a script and the target un-ticked itself: the counter went back to "0 selected", Run stayed greyed out and nothing said why. The page was answering two questions with two different sets of rules: "which scripts can I run here?" treated a script that names no target rules as good for any target, while "which targets can this script run on?" treated the very same script as device-only and dropped the host target. The server, which is what actually runs the script, only checks the rules a script really declares — so it would have run it there. Both directions now read the rules the same way, and when a target really does have to go (a script that says "device only", on a host that has nothing but its own screen), the page says so instead of dropping it silently. In plain words: the target you ticked stays ticked · [BUG-0789](../bugs/BUG-0789-2026-10-22-run-page-picking-a-script-silently-unticks-the-target.md) · area: frontend
- **Monitoring > Analytics shows one "open incidents" number on both tabs (BUG-0788)** — the Overview tab and the Incidents tab showed the same metric on two different clocks: the Overview recomputed it every ~21 seconds while the Incidents tab served a copy that could be nearly three minutes old, so an operator reading the two tabs a minute apart saw 212 on one and 206 on the other (and one persona judged it live data). Both now read the same cached figure, so the two tabs cannot disagree — not even for the half hour between two refresh passes. In plain words: one metric, one number, on both tabs The Overview charts keep their 14-day window; only the open figure is shared · [BUG-0788](../bugs/BUG-0788-2026-10-22-analytics-open-incidents-differ-between-overview-and-incidents-tab.md) · area: monitoring

- **The Roles page and the Users page now count a role the same way, and a role can be saved again (BUG-0787)** — the two pages disagreed about the same role (Manager 68 vs 65, Developer 54 vs 51, Tester 48 vs 45, Viewer 27 vs 26): the Roles page counted the stored list, the Users page counted the permissions the app actually knows. The difference was three permissions the projects migration put in the role rows (`team:projects:view` / `:edit` / `:delete`) that no route checks — they gate nothing, and they could be neither granted nor revoked. Worse: `team:projects:create`, the permission the project creation route really requires, was known to the frontend but not to the backend, so **every save of the manager, developer, tester and viewer role was refused** ("Unknown permissions") — an admin could not edit a role at all. The backend now knows the permission the app enforces, the Roles page counts the permissions the app can honour, and the edit dialog says which stored permissions it is ignoring and removes them when you save. Which stored lists change: the first save of the manager, developer or tester role removes `team:projects:view`, `team:projects:edit` and `team:projects:delete` from that role's row (the dialog text says so before you save), and the first save of the viewer role removes `team:projects:view`; `team:projects:create` stays. The admin role and a role you created yourself change only if its row holds one of those permissions, and a role you never save is left as it is. None of the removed permissions gated anything. In plain words: one number per role, and editing a role works again · [BUG-0787](../bugs/BUG-0787-2026-10-22-roles-page-counts-permissions-the-app-cannot-honour.md) · area: platform

- **The server log says what happened instead of repeating that a permission check passed (BUG-0786)** — every allowed request printed a line, so two lines in three of what an operator read while chasing an incident were `Permission check passed: <permission>` — and the most repeated line of the hour on the live server (369 of 6031 lines). The check passing is the normal case, and the activity audit trail already records what each request was allowed to do, so the line is now off by default. Set `AUTH_TRACE=1` (from the Settings page, no restart) to bring it back while chasing an auth problem. The refusals stay in the log — those are the events. In plain words: the log is now readable · [BUG-0786](../bugs/BUG-0786-2026-10-22-server-log-is-mostly-permission-check-noise.md) · area: platform

- **The dashboard's "AI usage" tile prints money as money (BUG-0784)** — the card formatted the 7-day spend by hand, and a bare number format prints **three** decimals: `$22.039`. In German that became `$22,039`, which a German reader parses as twenty-two thousand — a 1000x error on the number that reports the platform's spend. The card now uses the same formatter as the AI usage page (two decimals, the locale's own separators, and a sub-cent call keeps its digits instead of rounding to $0.00): `$22.04`, `22,04` in German. The monthly AI budget line on Settings > AI printed its amounts the same wrong way and is fixed with it. In plain words: the number on the tile is now the amount you spent · [BUG-0784](../bugs/BUG-0784-2026-10-22-ai-usage-dashboard-tile-prints-money-with-three-decimals.md) · area: ai


- **The Remote test page keeps its own panels to itself, and stops asking a question with no answer (BUG-0781)** — On the Remote test page the dashed "Mock AV Stream" box and the remote panel were pinned to the window instead of to the page, so wherever you scrolled they lay on top of the page's own headings and cards, and the remote panel's buttons hung off the bottom of the screen. Both now sit inside the test area they belong to. The page also asked the server for the screen orientation of a device that does not exist — the test page's own placeholder phone — and the server could only answer "host not found", every time the page was opened; the remote panel no longer asks about a host the server has never heard of. In plain words: the test page no longer covers its own content, and its console is clean · [BUG-0781](../bugs/BUG-0781-2026-10-22-remote-test-page-overlaps-itself-and-calls-missing-route.md) · area: frontend

- **A device that is not working no longer looks busy, and the Device page stops asking for a log that never comes (BUG-0780)** — A run whose execution record was left behind in "running" — the run died before it could write its verdict — kept its host marked busy for good: the Run page padlocked the target so new runs queued behind a run that did not exist, and the Device page asked the host for a running log that was never going to appear, every two seconds, for ever. The platform already asks the host what it is really running, once a minute, and uses that answer to free stuck device locks; it now uses the same answer to close the execution record that was left behind, so the host stops claiming to be busy. The page that polls the log backs off instead of giving up: after thirty seconds of grace it asks every fifteen seconds instead of every two, and never stops, so a run that was queued (browser slot, device lock) and writes its log late still gets its overlay; a new device or run starts the grace again. In plain words: an idle device is idle again, and the console stops filling with failed log requests. A later review hardened it: a campaign that is between two steps still counts as busy, a failed or empty busy answer counts as busy, and a run that just finished is never overwritten · [BUG-0780](../bugs/BUG-0780-2026-10-22-device-page-polls-missing-running-log-every-2-seconds.md) · area: platform

- **A Localize frame is asked for once, and is only called gone when it is really gone (BUG-0779)** — Every capture on the Localize and Match-runs pages was requested twice: once without the host-session cookie (the proxy gate answers 401) and once again with a cache-buster. The first answer also decided the verdict, so a frame that was sitting on the host was announced as "no longer on the host" before anyone had even looked for it — and a page with twenty frames sent twenty session requests. The page now mints the session before it asks for the picture, and one session serves every frame of that host: one request per frame, no failed request in the console, and the "gone" message only when the file is really not there. In plain words: the Localize thumbnails load on the first try and stop lying about missing captures. If the host cannot be reached to mint the session, the frame shows its error line with one Retry instead of a grey box for ever · [BUG-0779](../bugs/BUG-0779-2026-10-22-localize-and-match-runs-capture-images-401-then-404.md) · area: frontend

- **The Monitoring timeline asks the host whether it has a Timeline, instead of looking for a file that may not exist (BUG-0778)** — Opening Monitoring > Timeline sent 14 to 17 requests that all failed: the page looked for a thumbnail file on every device of the host, four at a time, and the Timeline service is only enabled for the devices an operator opted in, so every probe for the other devices answered 404 (and the browser aborted them) on every load of the page. The host already knew the answer and had a route for it — the page just never asked. It now asks once per device: on a host where one of four devices has a Timeline, four answered questions replace sixteen failing lookups, and the device that has one still renders its frames. The answer is kept per device while the page is open (it used to be asked again each time the chunk list reloaded), and a host that cannot answer is written to the console before the page falls back to the old file lookup. In plain words: the timeline page no longer hunts for files that cannot be there · [BUG-0778](../bugs/BUG-0778-2026-10-22-monitoring-timeline-probes-missing-json-and-shows-broken-frames.md) · area: frontend

- **A run whose report never reached storage now says why (BUG-0770)** — When the report, the logs or the metadata could not be uploaded, the storage error was printed on the host and then thrown away: the run row showed "No Report" and "No Logs" with no reason at all, and the review pack said only `report_url: n/a`. A green run whose artifacts were rejected by the object store was therefore indistinguishable from a run that simply had no report — which is exactly how a host with stale storage credentials could sit at "0 of 34 runs with a report" for a whole QA sweep without anyone being told why. The reason now travels with the run: the verification review pack gains an "Artifact upload failed: …" line — both the pack the host builds and the one the server falls back to when the host could not upload even that — and the run row keeps the error in its metadata. In plain words: when a report is missing, the platform now tells you what went wrong Only the error code and a short fixed phrase are kept (no endpoints, paths or account detail) · [BUG-0770](../bugs/BUG-0770-2026-10-22-labox-backend-runs-end-with-no-report-and-no-logs.md) · area: testing

- **The Run page could not find its own scripts (BUG-0769)** — Every Run tab (Run, Build, Monitor, Schedule, Deployments and the five legacy `/test-execution/*` routes) asked the server for its list of runnable scripts with a request that carried no `team_id`, and the server answered `400 "team_id is required"` every time. The page swallowed the error, so the script-to-home-server index stayed empty: in "All servers" mode a script had no server to run on, and a fallback built the request against the literal host `http://__all__/server/script/analyze`, which can never answer. The request is now built with the shared URL helper, so it carries the `team_id` like every other call, and the "All servers" sentinel can no longer be turned into a request. The request now goes through the app's API client like every other call, and a server that cannot list its scripts is reported on the page (a red line above the script list) instead of leaving the list silently empty. In plain words: the Run page knows again which server holds which script, and says so when it cannot · [BUG-0769](../bugs/BUG-0769-2026-10-22-run-page-list-executables-without-team-id-400.md) · area: frontend
- **The Targets picker says what it will do: the bar is everything, the host row is that host (BUG-0822)** — on /device-control, *Select all* next to the search box used to change its meaning with the context: it took every searched target when no host was open, but only the active host's devices once you clicked into one, so the rail badge (`vpt-mf1 0/6`), the panel checkbox (the whole host) and the button could all disagree — and the rail row itself only navigated, leaving no way to select a host from it. Now **the bar under the search box is always the whole list**: *Select all* takes every target, *Clear* drops every target, whatever host is open and whatever the search shows, and each greys out when it has nothing left to do. **The host's own pair lives on its panel header** — open `vpt-mf1` and its row shows *Select all* and *Clear all* for those six devices alone (*Clear all* leaves the other hosts alone). The two never share a label, so there is nothing to infer: *Clear* is everything, *Clear all* is the host you are looking at. Clicking a host row cycles **show its devices → select them all → unselect them all**, so one click never both moves the panel and rewrites the selection you were reading, and switching hosts restarts the cycle. Single-target pickers (Atlas, the navigation editor) keep navigation-only rows and get no host pair. In plain words: the top two buttons always mean all of it, and what applies to one host is written on that host · [BUG-0822](../bugs/BUG-0822-2026-10-22-target-picker-select-all-ignores-the-host-you-are-in.md) · area: frontend
- **One half-disabled device no longer freezes every stream on its host (BUG-0821)** — On vpt-mf1 all live streams (Apple TV, the STBs, the phones, the host desktop) kept freezing and going dark for 10 minutes, even in LOW.
