- **Hosts logged "Added to analysis queue" for alerts Redis had refused** — the log now says the alert was not queued when Redis is unreachable · [BUG-0283](../bugs/BUG-0283-2026-10-04-alert-queue-log-said-added-after-redis-failed.md)
- **Charts and tables Atlas rendered never appeared in Agent Chat** — they now open in their own tab, with the export bar, readable dates and a clear empty state · [BUG-0282](../bugs/BUG-0282-2026-10-04-atlas-charts-never-appeared-in-agent-chat.md) · `9d2d590bc`, `1cfacae11`
- **Validate runs the entry edge** — the first step, Entry → home, was always skipped (*could not reposition to 'Entry'*: nothing leads into the entry point) and a tree whose every edge passed was reported failed; the entry edge now runs from wherever the device is · [BUG-0281](../bugs/BUG-0281-2026-10-04-validate-skipped-the-entry-edge.md) · `this commit`
- **AVQ icon in the stream modal closed the modal instead of opening AVQ** — feature links in the modal header now navigate, and Back returns to Device Control · [BUG-0280](../bugs/BUG-0280-2026-10-04-stream-modal-feature-links-closed-the-modal-instead-of-navigating.md)
- **AI Generate on Android checks home by its whole tab bar** — validation took Google TV's search page for home because it also says "Search", saved one BACK where two are needed, and the next item failed; home now means every validated item shows in the app's window · [BUG-0279](../bugs/BUG-0279-2026-10-04-ai-generate-took-the-search-page-for-home.md) · `this commit`
- **Script run form: argument descriptions shown again** — one description written as an f-string hid every description of the script (`dns_lookuptime`, `zap_digit`, `validation`, the game scripts) · [BUG-0278](../bugs/BUG-0278-2026-10-04-script-run-form-lost-every-argument-description.md) · `this commit`
- **10-minute archive chunks hold exactly their 10 minutes again, and no minute is transcribed twice** — each 1-minute piece is named after the minute it was recorded in, not the clock when the busy archiver got to it; on vpt-pi1 one merge in 5 to 23 % wrote a minute slot twice and skipped the next, and chunks came out 540 or 660 s. A host deploy must restart `vpt-archiver` and `vpt-transcript` · [BUG-0277](../bugs/BUG-0277-2026-10-04-archiver-wrote-a-minute-slot-twice-and-skipped-the-next.md) · `this commit`
- **Atlas analytics failed on every question asked through the Pi-hosted server** — servers without a database connection now forward the query to the main server · [BUG-0276](../bugs/BUG-0276-2026-10-04-atlas-analytics-failed-on-the-pi-hosted-server.md) · `47d971fc9`, `06aa2cbd5`, `510bd4281`
- **execute_testcase no longer reports a running testcase as failed** — a status read that times out is read again instead of ending the wait, so a caller does not start the next testcase on a busy device · [BUG-0275](../bugs/BUG-0275-2026-10-04-one-slow-status-read-ended-execute-testcase.md) · `this commit`
- **Text checks on a busy box take a fraction of a second again** — OCR runs single-threaded; on a loaded Raspberry Pi the same check took 0.2 to 30 s and could time out empty, now 0.2 s on an area and 0.7 s on the full frame · [BUG-0274](../bugs/BUG-0274-2026-10-04-ocr-took-up-to-30-s-on-a-loaded-host.md) · `this commit`
- **A slow OCR check no longer fails the image check next to it** — in a verification with several checks, each check's time window counts from when frames were captured, not from how long the other checks took to run; a 17 s OCR on a busy box had closed a 10 s image check before it saw the screen change · [BUG-0273](../bugs/BUG-0273-2026-10-04-a-slow-ocr-leg-closed-a-sibling-image-legs-window.md) · `this commit`
- **A passing image verification made the testcase status answer HTTP 500 and left the device locked** — numpy values are converted to plain JSON types for every verification and execution event; appear-then-disappear image checks can pass again · [BUG-0270](../bugs/BUG-0270-2026-10-03-numpy-value-500d-a-passing-testcase-and-kept-the-device-locked.md) · `9d3bd5b79`, `5f53a098a`
- **The BLE remote woke a stale bond, and keys that reached no box were reported as sent** — the wake aims at the bond the box last connected with and tries the others; a key with no connected box after the wake now fails · [BUG-0271](../bugs/BUG-0271-2026-10-03-ble-remote-woke-a-stale-bond-and-keys-reported-sent.md) · `d8d100372`, `5666223f7`
- **MCP create_node dropped verifications, and edge writes were never validated** — verifications are saved and validated, label is optional, the userinterface lookup uses the real route · [BUG-0272](../bugs/BUG-0272-2026-10-03-mcp-create-node-dropped-verifications-and-edge-validation-never-ran.md) · `e3d7e6b63`
- **AI Generate on Android finds the short way back** — validation stored the whole entry edge (close, launch, tap Home) as each tab's way home although the tap alone worked, and on a screen without the app's Home tab it tapped Android's Home button, left the app and saved no way back; it now keeps only the taps it replayed, and only taps elements of the app's own window · [BUG-0269](../bugs/BUG-0269-2026-10-04-ai-generate-validation-wrote-an-app-restart-as-the-way-back.md) · `this commit`
- **Screenshots showed nginx 401** — capture images under a host's stream path now get their access cookie on their own, also after it expires, and a screenshot opened in a new tab gets it before it opens (vpt-pi1, vpt-pi3, vpt-nipogi) · [BUG-0268](../bugs/BUG-0268-2026-10-04-screenshots-401-on-gated-host-paths.md) · `16f33780a`, `0436f415c`, `af1f318e4`
- **Mobile app showed only one server** — the app re-reads the server list on every launch instead of only at setup (APK 1.0.48) · [BUG-0267](../bugs/BUG-0267-2026-10-03-mobile-app-server-picker-shows-only-one-server.md)
- **Atlas made up the rows of an export and struggled to compare variants** — exports now hand the model the real file; a comparison needs only the two variants; Grafana drafts only on request · [BUG-0266](../bugs/BUG-0266-2026-10-03-atlas-invented-exported-rows-and-could-not-compare-variants.md) · `24dae71f4`, `49e44bd38`
- **Atlas answered chart and statistics questions without its analytics tools** — such questions now always get them · [BUG-0265](../bugs/BUG-0265-2026-10-03-atlas-answered-analytics-questions-without-the-analytics-tools.md) · `26c9672f3`
- **Atlas live status questions always failed** — the live-status adapter crashed on every call · [BUG-0260](../bugs/BUG-0260-2026-10-03-atlas-live-status-questions-always-failed.md) · `8ee5bd2bc`
- **Atlas KPI charts filtered by device flag failed** — the flag filter queried a column that does not exist · [BUG-0261](../bugs/BUG-0261-2026-10-03-atlas-kpi-flag-filter-referenced-a-missing-column.md) · `8ee5bd2bc`
- **Atlas network charts had no latency and mixed test types** — now one source per test with the dashboard's fields · [BUG-0262](../bugs/BUG-0262-2026-10-03-atlas-network-latency-was-always-empty.md) · `8ee5bd2bc`
- **Atlas "degraded hosts" listed devices that had since recovered** — it now reads the newest fleet check, like the dashboard · [BUG-0263](../bugs/BUG-0263-2026-10-03-atlas-degraded-hosts-counted-history.md) · `8ee5bd2bc`
- **Atlas KPI radar and occupancy differed from Grafana** — same grouping, environment, range clipping and PASS/FAIL defaults as the dashboards, checked panel by panel · [BUG-0264](../bugs/BUG-0264-2026-10-03-atlas-kpi-and-occupancy-numbers-differed-from-the-dashboards.md) · `c31a83788`
- **Jira instance settings ignored the plugins.jira permissions** — listing now needs `plugins.jira:view`, adding / editing / deleting needs `plugins.jira:manage`, like TestRail · [BUG-0238](../bugs/BUG-0238-2026-10-03-jira-instance-settings-were-open-to-every-signed-in-user.md)
- **A test case's execution history was always empty** — the query asked for a column `script_results` does not have and hid the error · [BUG-0237](../bugs/BUG-0237-2026-10-03-testcase-execution-history-was-always-empty.md) · `961a0769d`
- **AI Generate on Android proposes the app's own navigation** — it proposed the status bar, Android's navigation bar and the feed's tiles (`Four bars of phone signal`, `Recents`, a movie title) as screens, because the UI dump holds every window on screen; it now reads only the app's window, only what can be tapped, and prefers the tab bar and toolbar over list items (Google TV: Search, Shop, Library, Watchlist) · [BUG-0235](../bugs/BUG-0235-2026-10-02-ai-generate-on-android-proposed-the-status-bar-as-screens.md) · `this commit`
- **A node or edge saved through the API reaches the hosts** — create/update through `/server/navigationTrees/<tree>/nodes|edges` left every host on its cached graph until a manual cache clear (only the editor followed up); the routes now clear the hosts' graph like every other tree write · [BUG-0236](../bugs/BUG-0236-2026-10-02-edge-saved-through-the-api-left-hosts-on-the-old-graph.md) · `this commit`
- **Analyzer worker logs appear as they happen** — `vpt-discard-scripts` (Sherlock) buffered its output, so its lines reached the journal only when it restarted; the unit now sets `PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1`. The unit is hand-installed: add the line to `/etc/systemd/system/vpt-discard-scripts.service` and restart it · [BUG-0234](../bugs/BUG-0234-2026-10-02-analyzer-worker-logs-reached-the-journal-only-at-restart.md) · `790e21623`
- **Test Prompt: an exploration stops at its time limit** — the agent ran until it gave up on its own (15+ minutes on a hard prompt), keeping the device busy and overlapping the next run; it now stops at the skill's limit (5 min) and the run ends as an error saying so · [BUG-0233](../bugs/BUG-0233-2026-10-02-test-prompt-exploration-had-no-time-limit.md) · `this commit`
- **Test Builder: one Undo undoes one change** — the builder recorded a step for every redraw of the canvas (block sizes, selection, each mouse move of a drag), so one change such as an AI edit's Apply needed several Undo presses, and Undo itself added a step that broke Redo; it now records a step only when the blocks or connections change · [BUG-0232](../bugs/BUG-0232-2026-10-02-test-builder-undo-needed-several-presses.md) · `this commit`
- **Test Builder: a testcase keeps its interface when you pick the device** — opening a testcase from a link (e.g. Test Prompt's *Test Builder* button) and then picking a device switched the Interface to that device's first one (`web_tv` instead of `sauce-demo`) · [BUG-0231](../bugs/BUG-0231-2026-10-02-test-builder-loaded-testcase-got-the-wrong-interface.md) · `this commit`
- **The 24 h archive keeps every minute** — the host's stream writer deleted the oldest video segments while the archiver was merging them into each minute of the 10-minute archive, so whole minutes went missing (often a third of a chunk or more) and a click on the A/V quality timeline played black or showed a later moment. The writer now keeps about two minutes of segments, and a merge that loses a file keeps the rest. In plain words: replaying the last 24 h shows the moment you click, on every device; needs a host deploy · [BUG-0230](../bugs/BUG-0230-2026-10-02-archive-chunks-lose-whole-minutes.md)
- **A server deploy now restarts the heatmap and discard workers too** — `update_core.sh --server` restarted only `vpt-server`, so `vpt-heatmap` and the discard workers kept running the code they were started with; on the second server the heatmap ran a three-week-old processor that never wrote its status file, so `/status` and the System Service Status dashboard showed it as failing while it produced frames every minute. They are now restarted after `vpt-server`, only if already running. In plain words: deploying the server updates everything that runs on it, and a service you switched off stays off · [BUG-0229](../bugs/BUG-0229-2026-10-01-server-deploy-left-heatmap-and-discard-on-old-code.md)
- **Script report: the execution video fills the player** — a small recording (a web host's capture) played at its native size in the middle of an empty modal; the video now scales to the modal in both orientations · [BUG-0228](../bugs/BUG-0228-2026-10-01-report-video-modal-small-capture-not-scaled.md) · `this commit`
- **Test Prompt: Explore keeps the device under your control** — the exploring agent was told to force-unlock the device, so it cleared your Take Control on its first action; the next run was refused (*take_control_required*) while the page still showed you in control · [BUG-0227](../bugs/BUG-0227-2026-10-01-test-prompt-explore-force-unlocked-the-users-take-control.md) · `this commit`
- **Test Prompt: Explore reaches the target screen you picked** — the Target Screen dropdown saved the node's database id, which navigation does not know, so Explore failed at pre-navigation (*Target node … not found in unified graph*); it now saves the node id navigation uses. A prompt saved before this shows an empty Target until it is picked again · [BUG-0226](../bugs/BUG-0226-2026-10-01-test-prompt-target-saved-as-uuid-not-found-by-navigation.md) · `this commit`
- **Test Prompt: a saved prompt keeps its target screen** — opening a prompt wiped its Target Screen, so Explore from the page went to the tree's root and failed (*No unified path found to 'Entry'*), and saving stored the prompt without a target · [BUG-0225](../bugs/BUG-0225-2026-10-01-test-prompt-loaded-prompt-lost-its-target-screen.md) · `this commit`
- **Test Prompt: live steps show on the stream whatever the viewer's clock says** — the page compared the host's run start time with the browser's clock, so a browser more than 5 s ahead of the host never saw the step overlay; it now compares host times only · [BUG-0224](../bugs/BUG-0224-2026-10-01-test-prompt-overlay-hidden-by-clock-skew.md) · `this commit`
- **A script that crashes at startup now has a report and logs** — a syntax error, bad import or stray character at the top of a script used to leave the run empty; the host now records it as "script crashed before it started" with the Python error, and uploads the full traceback as its log · [BUG-0223](../bugs/BUG-0223-2026-10-01-script-crashing-at-startup-left-no-report-or-logs.md) · `this commit`
- **Standby measurement now times the wake after a long dwell** — after 5 min or more in standby the BLE cold-wake wakes the STB on its own, so the timed POWER press never happened and no KPI was recorded. The cold-wake is now the measured wake: from the BLE wake signal until the live screen (e.g. Home) appears, on the same wake edge and report · [BUG-0222](../bugs/BUG-0222-2026-10-01-standby-cold-wake-was-never-measured.md) · `this commit`
- **A KPI failure after "scan budget exceeded" shows its real scan window and frames** — the report said Scan Window 0.00s with no scan thumbnails or mosaic, because the frame list was dropped when the budget ran out; the error now also says the budget is processing time and how many frames were verified · [BUG-0221](../bugs/BUG-0221-2026-10-01-kpi-failure-report-scan-window-zero-on-budget-exceeded.md) · `this commit`
- **A device missed by the capture monitor at startup is now picked up within 10 s** — on a cascaded restart `vpt-monitor` could read the list of capture folders while `vpt-stream` was still writing it, and never read it again, so that device's frames were never analysed: no freeze / blackscreen / audio incident could fire, and vpt-pi4's desktop went 37 h unwatched while ffmpeg, the stall watcher and systemd all looked healthy. The monitor now re-reads the list every 10 s and starts watching any folder it was missing · [BUG-0220](../bugs/BUG-0220-2026-10-01-capture-monitor-missed-a-device-listed-after-it-started.md) · `this commit`
- **Deploys that update hosts run again** — after the lease check landed, a full, `--host` or `--restart` `update_core.sh` run stopped at once on *trim_host: command not found* and deployed nothing; `--frontend` and `--server` alone were not affected · [BUG-0219](../bugs/BUG-0219-2026-10-01-update-core-host-deploy-failed-on-trim-host.md) · `this commit`
- **2048 on a phone confirms Game over and finds the game screen reliably** — its checks ran OCR on the whole HDMI capture, which skipped the "Game over!" overlay and sometimes read nothing at all; they now read the board and the title row only · [BUG-0217](../bugs/BUG-0217-2026-10-01-game2048-phone-game-over-overlay-not-read-by-ocr.md)
- **2048 on a phone starts a new run right after a finished game** — New Game's "OK" step fails when there is no confirm dialog (after game over), and the run gave up although the new game was on screen; it now reads the screen and plays · [BUG-0218](../bugs/BUG-0218-2026-10-01-game2048-phone-run-after-a-finished-game-never-started.md)
- **Test Prompt: a saved prompt opens on its own interface** — clicking a prompt picked its device but showed that device's first interface (e.g. `web_tv` for a `sauce-demo` prompt), and the locked Interface picker could not be changed, so Take Control loaded the wrong tree · [BUG-0216](../bugs/BUG-0216-2026-10-01-test-prompt-loaded-prompt-got-the-wrong-interface.md) · `this commit`
- **KPI Measurement: the Action Set filter lists its action sets again** — since BUG-0157 its query failed with a GROUP BY error, so the drop-down was empty. In plain words: you can filter the KPI dashboard by action set again. Grafana dashboard: re-import `kpi-measurement.json` (no deploy script does it). [BUG-0214](../bugs/BUG-0214-2026-10-01-kpi-measurement-action-set-filter-query-error.md)
- **2048 on a phone no longer misreads the board and stops mid-game** — on the S21's HDMI capture the board finder sometimes took a square piece of the grid for the whole board, so a blind read returned tiles that weren't there and the run ended with `input_not_applied`; it now keeps the biggest square match · [BUG-0213](../bugs/BUG-0213-2026-10-01-game2048-phone-reader-took-a-piece-of-the-grid-for-the-board.md)
- **Test Prompt: "Save as testcase" links the testcase to the prompt** — the testcase was created but the link failed, so *Run graph* kept generating a new graph (an AI call) instead of replaying it; the host's testcase save and `execute-from-prompt` (with save) also returned an object where the testcase id belongs · [BUG-0215](../bugs/BUG-0215-2026-10-01-save-as-testcase-never-linked-the-prompt.md) · `5c32ad836`
- **Grafana's built-in admin can pick a workspace on dashboards** — the Workspace drop-down found the viewer by email only, and Grafana's built-in `admin` (email `admin@localhost`) has no VirtualPyTest account, so it offered only *None*. It now also reads the Grafana login and lists every workspace for `admin`, the same rule the device filter already used. In plain words: the admin can narrow dashboards to a workspace again. [BUG-0212](../bugs/BUG-0212-2026-10-01-grafana-workspace-dropdown-empty-for-builtin-admin.md) · 🗄 DB migration `20261001d_grafana_workspaces_builtin_admin.sql`
- **Stream-modal AI panel saves your choice for an ambiguous screen name** — choosing a screen with "remember" posted a route that does not exist, so the panel showed *Failed to apply disambiguation* and asked again next time; it now saves through the same route as the Test Builder · [BUG-0211](../bugs/BUG-0211-2026-10-01-stream-modal-ai-panel-could-not-save-disambiguation.md) · `this commit`
- **Tests generated from a prompt run their actions and checks** — a graph built by the AI (Test Prompt *Run graph*, the stream-modal AI panel, the Test Builder's AI generation) stopped at its first action or verification with *re-create the block*, because the AI builder still wrote blocks in an old shape and never gave a command its parameters (`press_key` with no key). It now writes the blocks the executor runs, with parameters (`press_key(key=OK)`, `waitForTextToAppear(text="Live TV")`), and graphs cached in the old shape are regenerated · [BUG-0210](../bugs/BUG-0210-2026-10-01-ai-generated-graphs-failed-on-every-action-and-verification.md) · `this commit`
- **AI Dataset: freeze strips show their frame times again** — the review page read each strip frame as `{t_rel_s}`, but the host stores plain seconds, so no strip showed its `+52.8 s · after freeze` chips and the reviewer had nothing to tell frozen frames from live ones · [BUG-0209](../bugs/BUG-0209-2026-10-01-freeze-strip-frame-chips-never-shown.md) · `this commit`
- **Running a testcase from a prompt works again** — `/server/testcase/execute-from-prompt` failed on every device with *does not have AIExecutor initialized*, because the host still called an AI executor that had been replaced by the graph builder. It now generates the graph with the builder, optionally saves it, and runs it like any other testcase (sync, or async with live execution events), holding the device for the whole run. `/server/testcase/generate-with-ai` now only generates, as documented, instead of going through that broken route. Neither route is called by a page today; the stream-modal AI panel uses `generatePlan` + `/testcase/execute` and was not affected ([BUG-0208](../bugs/BUG-0208-2026-10-01-execute-from-prompt-always-failed-on-missing-ai-executor.md))
- **One Run click no longer leaves a second "running forever" row** — a second click while the first was still launching queued a copy that waited behind the device lock and never finished; Run is now one launch per click, and a device under your own Take Control runs the script directly instead of queueing it · [BUG-0207](../bugs/BUG-0207-2026-10-01-one-run-click-left-a-phantom-queued-row.md) · `this commit`
- **Standby measurement reports carry their summary again** — the run measured the wake, then crashed building the summary on a helper whose signature had changed in July, so the report said "Execution summary not available". The host log also now says when an early device release was declined because a user holds Take Control · [BUG-0284](../bugs/BUG-0284-2026-10-01-standby-summary-crashed-on-kpi-helper-signature.md) · `this commit`
- **An Apple TV on an IRTrans box had no remote, and a Fire TV always showed an IR panel** — the remotes a device could get were hard-coded per model, so an Apple TV with `DEVICE{n}_IR_IP` set came up as *"Unsupported device: apple_tv"* and an Android TV could not use IR at all; Fire TV drew an IR panel even with no transmitter configured. Every HDMI/camera model now takes whichever IR, IRTrans or BLE remote its `.env` sets, `DEVICE{n}_IR_REMOTE` is optional, and the panels show only the remotes the host created · [BUG-0205](../bugs/BUG-0205-2026-10-01-ir-remote-refused-by-device-model.md) · `d47966813` `0c54bff88` · **client install: `patch-2` ([patch/patch_host_2_irtrans-any-device.sh](../../patch/patch_host_2_irtrans-any-device.sh))**
- **Fire TV IR sent no keys with `IR_TYPE=fire_tv`** — the key-code file was named `ir_conf/firet_v.json`, so the type that showed the Fire TV panel loaded no codes, and the one that loaded codes showed the Samsung panel. Renamed to `fire_tv.json`; **a host still set to `IR_TYPE=firet_v` must change it to `fire_tv`** · [BUG-0206](../bugs/BUG-0206-2026-10-01-fire-tv-ir-profile-misnamed.md)
- **Standby measurement no longer blames a missing KPI reference when the STB woke during the cold-wake** — after a long dwell the WoBLE cold-wake can wake the box all the way to live, so the measured wake edge never runs; the run now says so instead of sending you to edit KPI settings that were fine. KPI rows are also matched by the variant's action set id, not base's · [BUG-0204](../bugs/BUG-0204-2026-10-01-standby-wake-already-awake-blamed-kpi.md) · `this commit`
- **Runs under a variant were querying the database on every internal graph read** — resolving a variant name to its id happened per read, roughly 15 times per navigation step, and each of those queries also dragged every variant's full override JSON that the resolver never looks at. It is now fetched once per interface and held for a few seconds, dropped whenever the tree cache is invalidated so a rename still takes effect. Measured on the lab: **49 ms of database per navigation step down to 2 ms**, and it scales with override size and database latency · [BUG-0203](../bugs/BUG-0203-2026-09-30-variant-map-refetched-per-graph-read.md) · `67f83aad4`
- **Taking control now pre-builds the navigation graph for the variant you are looking at** — it always warmed the base scope, so the first run under a variant rebuilt the whole tree hierarchy mid-run even though the warm-up had just finished. Measured at 68 ms on a 14-tree lab interface, and it grows with hierarchy size and database latency; `scripts/measure_cache_warmup.py` reports it for any host · [BUG-0202](../bugs/BUG-0202-2026-09-30-takecontrol-warmed-the-wrong-cache-scope.md) · `0e0ef4882`
- **The event a run's result travels on is now delivered where it can be heard to fail** — a finished run handed its completion event to a detached thread nobody was left to check, so a rejected delivery produced no trace inside that run. Two silent outages in ten days turned on exactly that. Terminal events are now delivered on the thread that produced the result (which is exiting anyway, so nothing waits longer), progress events still never block the request handler, and a regression test pins the whole contract — credential sent, refusal logged, terminal blocking, progress not · [BUG-0201](../bugs/BUG-0201-2026-09-30-terminal-execution-events-were-fire-and-forget.md) · `9789e90cc`
- **A node's Run recorded its result against the wrong variant** — edge runs have always declared the scope they ran under; node verification never did, so the host used whatever variant an earlier unrelated request had left on the device. Switching the canvas variant and running a node check with no navigation in between filed the metric under the previous scope, and the Localize cross-check rebuilt the whole tree hierarchy every time because it was looking for a scope the cache had not been asked to hold. The scope is now declared with the run, so the metric lands where it belongs and the lookup is a cache hit · [BUG-0200](../bugs/BUG-0200-2026-09-30-node-verification-runs-under-a-leftover-variant.md) · `6ab94a9a0`
- **Script progress, early device release and deployment status were being dropped without a word** — three host→server callbacks either sent no credential or ignored the server's answer, and because a rejection is a response rather than an error they read as delivered. On a server that requires a credential that meant live progress never updated during a run, a device stayed locked through the report/video/upload tail instead of being released early, and a refused deployment notification looked identical to a delivered one. All three now authenticate and say so when they are refused. Same defect as [BUG-0151], whose sweep stopped at four callers · [BUG-0199](../bugs/BUG-0199-2026-09-30-three-more-host-callbacks-swallow-401.md) · `166fc0a5f`
- **A verification that times out or cannot run now says so in the Edit Node panel** — when a run produced no results the panel simply stayed empty and the only explanation was a toast that faded, so it looked as though nothing had happened. The reason now renders where the results would have been, in the same red box, and reads the same in both places — *"Verification timed out — the host did not report a result in time"* rather than a raw error string · `a9b1db893`
- **A host's data disk filled up, the dashboard read 9.8%, and verifications blamed the device for it** — the host only measured `/`, which on a host that keeps captures, references and reports on their own mount is the one filesystem that cannot fill: 960 GB at 100% while the tile stayed green. The Disk figure now tracks the **fullest** of the root, the stream filesystem and each capture's hot tmpfs, and names it — so the tile's red threshold and the Status page's over-90% row finally see it. A screenshot that fails because the disk is full now stops the verification with *"the host is out of disk space on /data"* instead of reporting "0/2 passed" and recording the node as failed. And retention tightens as a mount fills (reports 14d/7d/2d under 20/10/5% free, swept every cycle below 10%) rather than holding a flat 30 days until there is no room left. **On hosts with a separate stream mount the reported Disk % will step up after this deploy — that is the real number** · [BUG-0198](../bugs/BUG-0198-2026-09-30-disk-full-invisible-and-reported-as-test-failure.md) · `2f4ef3287` `c29a6f3c9` `3c3db7ecc`
- **Verification from the Navigation editor always timed out on one install** — actions and navigation on the same host completed, verification never did: it was the only flow that waited solely on the live-update socket, so when a host cannot deliver its execution events the result had no second route home. It now polls the host's status endpoint alongside the socket, the way actions and navigation already do, and completes about a second after the host finishes · [BUG-0197](../bugs/BUG-0197-2026-09-30-verification-waits-on-socket-only.md) · `bc6df8003`
- **Navigation editor no longer reports "user interface not found" for an interface that simply is not published** — the header checks for a prod snapshot on every load, and an interface that only exists in dev made that check log a red error. It is now an ordinary answer (`No prod snapshot published for "x"`), and a failed lookup is no longer kept in the cache, so an interface published elsewhere stops showing as unpublished for the rest of the TTL · `89d3598c2`
- **Switching stream quality took ~30 s and could end on an error though it had worked** — the stream modal checked for the restarted stream with request headers the host's CORS policy rejects, so every check failed: it waited out the 15 s timeout, restarted FFmpeg a second time, waited again and then showed "Stream failed to reload". The checks no longer send those headers; the overlay clears as soon as the new stream is up · [BUG-0196](../bugs/BUG-0196-2026-09-30-quality-switch-poll-blocked-by-cors.md) · `459b0e1a8`
- **A testcase could pass without running the step it tests, and waited 5-10 s before every navigation step** — before each hop the navigation re-checked the next screen even when the previous hop had verified the current one a moment earlier; a next screen verified by text the current screen also shows "passed", so the hop was skipped (a TodoMVC "toggle all back" testcase passed without clicking). A position verified less than 10 s ago is now trusted (`VPT_POSITION_TRUST_SECONDS`, see Upgrade), anything that sends device commands in between ends the trust, and where the check still runs it looks at the current screen first. Five-step web testcases return in ~20 s instead of ~45 s · [BUG-0195](../bugs/BUG-0195-2026-09-30-position-probe-rechecks-a-screen-just-verified.md) · `8107abcb0`
- **A web testcase spent more than half its run waiting** — before every hop after the first it waited out the next screen's full verification timeouts to check a screen that was not there (now capped at 5 s like the other position probes), the browser start slept ~7 s on fixed timers (now waits for the browser to be ready), every typed field paused 3 s (now 0.5 s), and the caller waited for the report upload after the verdict was known (now the report follows a few seconds later; `VPT_TESTCASE_BACKGROUND_REPORT`, see Upgrade). A five-step web testcase returns in ~60 s instead of ~104 s · [BUG-0194](../bugs/BUG-0194-2026-09-29-web-testcase-spends-half-its-run-waiting.md) · `cebf3c363` `8495faa62` `4dad94e82` `0d25c22f0`
- **Web "wait For Title To Appear" verifications always failed** — the step showed an "Element Criteria" field and saved the typed title where the host does not read it, so the run stopped with "No title specified". The step now has a **Title** field (QuickTest, TestCase builder and Navigation editor); steps saved before must have their title retyped · [BUG-0193](../bugs/BUG-0193-2026-09-29-web-wait-for-title-saves-search-term.md) · `cf6705f30`
- **QuickTest crashed as soon as you took control of a web target** — the page showed "useVNCState must be used within a VNCStateProvider" and no step could be added; QuickTest now provides the VNC state the web player needs · [BUG-0192](../bugs/BUG-0192-2026-09-29-quicktest-crashes-on-web-device-control.md) · `06a9cf847`
- **Web hosts ran out of memory after a few hours of tests** — every browser connection that failed, was replaced or timed out on close left a Playwright driver running inside the host service (~30 MB each, ~20 an hour), until the VM ran out of memory, every web test failed and the VM no longer answered SSH. Each driver is now stopped, or killed if it hangs, on every path; restarting `vpt-host` once after the deploy clears the ones already leaked · [BUG-0191](../bugs/BUG-0191-2026-09-29-playwright-drivers-leak-until-host-runs-out-of-memory.md) · `048bd9ad1`
- **Navigation editor opened blank on installs with a long test history** — loading a user interface could fail with a 500 (`canceling statement due to statement timeout`) and an empty canvas, on and off, then work on a retry. The index added for [BUG-0001](../bugs/BUG-0001-2026-07-15-remote-control-metrics-timeout.md) could not actually be used to fetch each edge's latest KPI report, so the metrics call re-read that edge's entire KPI history instead of its newest row — 1.9 s per load on a 2.9M-row table, peaking past the database's 8 s limit. Now 1 ms. A load that does fail shows the error instead of an empty editor · [BUG-0189](../bugs/BUG-0189-2026-09-29-kpi-edge-lookup-index-cannot-probe.md) · `a63f2dc08` · 🗄 DB migration `20260929g_kpi_edge_scope_index.sql`
- **A failed KPI measurement on a conditional edge hid the sibling's report and blamed the KPI service** — the Go To panel lists the target's and the sibling's verification report when neither destination matches; a `kpi_measurement` run showed only the target's, and its summary said vpt-kpi might be lagging. The failed step now lists the sibling's report too, and the Execution Summary names the navigation failure with *View target report* / *View sibling report* links · [BUG-0188](../bugs/BUG-0188-2026-09-29-kpi-report-hides-conditional-sibling-reports.md) · `c4858fb32`
- **Every deploy disconnected TestRail, JIRA and Slack** — the credentials entered in the UI were saved inside the project folder, which the deploy's `rsync --delete` wipes. They now live in `~/.config/virtualpytest/` of the server's service user (`VPT_CONFIG_DIR` overrides; Docker keeps them in the `server-config` volume). **Copy the existing files once before deploying** — see Upgrade below · [BUG-0187](../bugs/BUG-0187-2026-09-29-deploy-wipes-testrail-jira-slack-credentials.md) · `bf56ea42d`
- **Test reports named the same user for every web UI run** — the Trigger line showed one hardcoded id whoever was logged in, because the frontend read a user key nothing ever wrote. Runs now show `vpt_user:<name>` from the login, scheduled deployments `vpt_deployment:script|campaign:<name>`, and API callers keep their own `X-User-ID` · [BUG-0186](../bugs/BUG-0186-2026-09-29-report-trigger-shows-same-user-for-everyone.md) · `a25e643bf`
- **Run Tests showed "No parameters" for Zap Chup, App Install, API Test and Camera Quality** — the server reads a script's arguments from its first 300 lines, and these four declared theirs below that, so the run controls never appeared and every run used the built-in defaults (no way to set Zap Chup's node or iterations). They now declare their arguments at the top of the file like the other scripts · [BUG-0185](../bugs/BUG-0185-2026-09-29-run-tests-shows-no-parameters-for-four-scripts.md) · `4ad48c3d4`
- **Users page: Edit crashed for users without a name, and every user showed 0 permissions** — the Edit dialog read the name before checking it existed. The Permissions column counted only individual grants; it now shows what the user really holds (role + teams + grants − denials), "All" for admins · `fdd859974`
- **Users page: admins could not delete a user** — the Delete button was switched off in code even though the server supported it. It now shows for anyone with `org.users:delete`; you cannot delete yourself, and only an admin can delete an admin · `2444b595a`
- **Edit User showed controls that did nothing** — the Profile tab kept greyed-out name/email fields (now a subtitle), the Teams tab could not change anything (removed; the table still lists each user's teams), and the Permissions tab left every box unticked. It now ticks what the user actually has: untick a role or team permission to deny it, tick another to grant it · `33145b2ba` `ce4a227b0`
- **Dialogs jumped up and down when switching tabs** — every dialog is now anchored near the top instead of centred, so only its height changes (Edit User, Edit Team and all others) · `a2dd3ea22`
- **Teams: the "Add member" field had a thick dashed border** — now a plain outlined pill matching the member chips · `5f1ffb70e`
- **Dashboard showed testers host restart/reboot buttons that always failed** — they are shown only with `system.services:restart`, the permission those actions need · `e674b7654`
- **Tokens without a platform-admin claim were never looked up in the database** — the fallback meant for older login tokens could not run, so such a super admin was refused the Tenants pages until they signed in again · `e32c37bec`
- **Verification timeouts are milliseconds for every check type** — web and video checks read `timeout` as seconds while image, text, adb and appium read milliseconds, and the editor labelled all of them "Timeout (ms)"; a 5 s image check could run as 5 ms. All types now read ms and show ms in the editor and reports, and stored web/video values are converted · [BUG-0184](../bugs/BUG-0184-2026-09-28-verification-timeout-units-mixed.md) · `a757ca11b` · 🗄 DB migration `20260928d_verification_timeout_web_video_to_ms.sql`
- **Supabase would not start behind a firewall** — the one-VM installer enabled Edge Functions and Storage image transforms, whose images come from `public.ecr.aws`; neither is used by the platform, so a VM without outbound access failed `supabase start` for nothing. Both are now off by default; existing installs add the two `enabled = false` blocks to `config.toml` by hand · [BUG-0183](../bugs/BUG-0183-2026-09-28-supabase-start-fails-behind-firewall-on-unused-images.md) · `0d582527a`
- **Device info runs disappeared entirely instead of showing a report** — the new `device_info_latest` trigger fired on the row a script writes when it *starts*, where the info field is legitimately absent; a NULL-unsafe guard let it through and the resulting constraint error aborted the script's own database record. The run executed and uploaded its report, but nothing was recorded, so Run Tests had no row to link. The guard is NULL-safe now and the trigger can only ever warn — a derived cache must never break the write it observes · [BUG-0182](../bugs/BUG-0182-2026-09-28-device-info-trigger-aborts-every-get-info-run.md) · `9fba11247` · 🗄 DB migration `20260928c_fix_device_info_track_null_guard.sql`
- **Device Info listed every device on the server, ignoring the active workspace** — the page was the one device surface that never applied the workspace filter, so a restricted workspace could still read and correct devices it does not contain. Switching server or workspace now also drops a selection that has left scope and re-reads its values from the newly selected server · [BUG-0180](../bugs/BUG-0180-2026-09-28-device-info-dropdown-ignores-workspace-scope.md) · `dd6ae17d0`
- **A gateway correction could overwrite device info on the device-information dashboard** — its five panels merge corrections in their own SQL rather than reading the corrected view, and were missing the device/gateway discriminator, so a fix entered on the Gateway tab showed up as the device's value for any key the two families share (`serial_number`). Only that dashboard was affected · [BUG-0181](../bugs/BUG-0181-2026-09-28-gateway-override-bleeds-into-device-info-dashboard.md) · `6139cee95`
- **Web scripts no longer spend five minutes waiting for a browser nobody will close** — a script that finds a Chromium already on the debug port now reuses it, or kills it and launches its own, instead of waiting up to 300 s for a run that had already finished; and teardown now really closes the browser (CDP `Browser.close`, then the process group, then whatever still listens on the port), so the next script normally finds the port free · [BUG-0179](../bugs/BUG-0179-2026-09-28-goto-waits-300s-for-an-orphan-chromium.md) · `29a289e81`
- **Docker host: archive, captures and VNC live audio never loaded** — the host now answers the nginx-style `/host/<name>/stream/…` itself and gates it with the login session cookie, so a Docker host behind a tunnel or on a LAN serves media like a native host · [BUG-0171](../bugs/BUG-0171-2026-09-27-docker-host-archive-and-captures-unreachable.md) · `31a5766a99`
- **The AI agent guessed web commands the platform does not have** — the tool router offered screen analysis and `execute_device_action` without the tools that feed them, so a web chat called invented commands and failed. A tool now travels with its prerequisites, and the web action docs list what actually exists · [BUG-0170](../bugs/BUG-0170-2026-09-27-router-offers-tools-without-their-prerequisites.md) · `b4b81aa22`
- **The AI agent could not read a web page** — `dump_ui_elements` asked for a remote controller, which a web host does not have, and neither the element list nor a script's output ever reached the model. Web dumps now go through the browser, and both paths put the elements in the text the model reads · [BUG-0172](../bugs/BUG-0172-2026-09-27-agent-dump-ui-elements-blind-on-web.md) · `3431decfe`
- **The AI agent picked the wrong field on a web page** — screen analysis returned no CSS selectors and let any element with a unique id win, then answered with a command web does not support. Ids and selectors are now judged against the intent, and the answer is a ready-to-run click or input action · [BUG-0173](../bugs/BUG-0173-2026-09-27-web-selector-scoring-ignores-css-and-intent.md) · `9384a9c4c`
- **AI Agent Target panel showed a black VNC stream** — the panel rendered the stream before minting a host session, so it worked only if another page had opened that stream in the last 10 minutes. It now waits for the session, showing "Loading stream..." until it resolves · [BUG-0174](../bugs/BUG-0174-2026-09-27-agent-target-panel-vnc-black-without-host-session.md) · `94c99c42b`
- **Typing into a web field failed unless the agent named it one exact way** — `input_text` accepted only `selector`; it now also takes `element_id` or `xpath`, falls back to the focused input when the field is not named (the box a click just focused), and honours `press_enter` so the query is actually submitted · [BUG-0175](../bugs/BUG-0175-2026-09-27-web-input-text-rejects-natural-field-forms.md) · `7b3a2fb2b`
- **With a Claude model, every agent chat that loaded a skill died on a bare "400 Bad Request"** — a text LOAD SKILL left the conversation ending on the assistant, which the provider rejects; the manager now appends the same continue turn the tool path uses, and a provider error carries its response body instead of a naked 400 · [BUG-0177](../bugs/BUG-0177-2026-09-27-agent-skill-load-prefill-rejected-by-claude.md) · `aab2c4dfe`
- **Viewers got 403 on every stream URL lookup** — `av/getStreamUrl` / `av/getStatus` are read-only POSTs, now exempt from the viewer floor · [BUG-0168](../bugs/BUG-0168-2026-09-27-viewer-blocked-from-stream-url-lookup.md) · `0c11b8fab1`
- **AI Agent chat never rendered its reply** — the page sent several CONNECTs for `/agent` on one connection while the authenticated handshake was pending; the server refused the duplicates and the client destroyed its own socket, so the agent's answer went to a room nobody listened in. One handshake per connection now, rooms are re-joined on every reconnect, and a refused handshake rebuilds the socket instead of leaving a dead one · [BUG-0166](../bugs/BUG-0166-2026-09-27-agent-chat-duplicate-socket-connect-never-renders-reply.md) · `bcd8e9c7d2`
- **Agent looped on a closed browser context for 40 minutes** — a dead Playwright context is now dropped and reconnected on the host, the agent ends its turn after three identical tool failures, and a chat queued behind another conversation is told it is waiting · [BUG-0167](../bugs/BUG-0167-2026-09-27-agent-loops-on-closed-browser-context-for-40-minutes.md) · `b660a964d8`
- **Docker host's noVNC asks for a password** — opt-in `HOST_VNC_AUTOCONNECT` replaces the broken `ef8e0fc4ca` hand-off · [BUG-0164](../bugs/BUG-0164-2026-09-27-docker-vnc-password-prompt-autoconnect-broken.md) · `becb88e027`
- **Regression: emulator streams stalled at the first frame** — since emulator audio shipped (build 9151, `1d3581ffe`), the wall-clock timestamps meant for the audio-synced stream also reached the capture and thumbnail outputs. Two frames inside one tick got the same timestamp, the encoder rejected it, and ffmpeg restarted about once a minute without producing a stream. Restarting did not help. The wall clock now applies to the stream branch only · [BUG-0165](../bugs/BUG-0165-2026-09-27-emulator-stream-stalls-on-duplicate-capture-timestamps.md) · `d38adc77a`
- **Emulator streams went black ~17 minutes after starting** — same origin as BUG-0165. The capture and thumbnail outputs ran on a clock about 1.4% slower than real time, and ffmpeg reads its inputs only as fast as its slowest output, so the emulator audio was read too slowly. After ~17 minutes samples were lost, audio and video drifted apart and the browser showed black. All outputs now share the real-time clock · [BUG-0169](../bugs/BUG-0169-2026-09-27-emulator-audio-timestamps-run-ahead-black-player.md) · `f85e19817`
- **An emulator screen turned off after 30 minutes and its stream stayed black** — the emulator setup script set a 30-minute screen timeout, and nothing wakes a sleeping emulator. New emulators never sleep. Existing ones need `adb shell settings put system screen_off_timeout 2147483647` once (emulator troubleshooting §12) · [BUG-0176](../bugs/BUG-0176-2026-09-27-emulator-screen-sleeps-stream-black.md) · `cf9e5eb08`
- **A failed "press until" step hid what each check saw** — every repeat_until iteration read "✅ Success (0ms)" because it only recorded the key press, and script reports linked no comparison report. Each iteration now shows the press, the wait and the check outcome, and when the loop fails every check links its own comparison report (frame vs reference) in the script report · [BUG-0178](../bugs/BUG-0178-2026-09-28-repeat-until-report-hides-each-check.md) · `eeec22af1`

**In plain words:** with `HOST_VNC_AUTOCONNECT=true`, the desktop view in the app connects by itself. Opening the VNC page directly still asks for the password. Other installs are unchanged.

Android emulator devices show a live stream again.

**Upgrade:** 🗄 **Database migrations — apply all of these, in this order, to every database this build reaches.** `update_core.sh` deploys code but never runs migrations, and shipping this code without them means every device info run is recorded but never surfaced.

1. `20260928b_device_info_overrides_prereq.sql` — **only if the database never received 035** (no `device_info_overrides` table). Use this rather than 035 itself on any TASK-10-hardened DB: 035 ends with a `FOR ALL TO public USING (true)` policy that would re-open the table to the anon key.
2. `20260928a_device_info_latest.sql` — the table, trigger, backfill and the two recreated views.
3. `20260928c_fix_device_info_track_null_guard.sql` — **mandatory, never apply 2 without it.** Without the hotfix the trigger aborts the database record of every `get_info` / `device_get_info` run (SQLSTATE 23502 on `device_info_latest.info`), so those runs execute and upload their reports but vanish from Run Tests entirely (BUG-0182).
4. `20260928d_verification_timeout_web_video_to_ms.sql` — converts stored web/video verification timeouts from seconds to milliseconds (BUG-0184). **Deploy the code with it:** old code reads the converted web values as seconds (capped at 60 s), new code without it would run a 15 s web wait as 15 ms. One-shot and guarded (`public.data_migrations`), so a second run is skipped; tables a database lacks are skipped. Applied on node1 and QualiAI on 2026-09-28 (backups in `~<operator>/backup_20260928d_before_timeout_ms_*.sql` on each DB VM); **still outstanding on every customer install.**
5. `20260929a_role_permissions.sql` — the editable role defaults table (Roles page). **Apply before deploying the code:** the code reads Tester/Viewer defaults only from this table, so without it every tester and viewer resolves to no role defaults and loses every page their role granted. Seeded with the previous hard-coded values; idempotent, never overwrites edited rows. Rollback: `20260929a_role_permissions_rollback.sql` (only together with a code rollback).
6. `20260929b_role_page_view_permissions.sql` — gives Tester and Viewer the view permissions of every page they could open before page visibility followed permissions, so the deploy hides nothing. Apply after 5, before deploying the code; harmless on older code (these view permissions gate no backend route). 5 and 6 applied on node1 and QualiAI (node3) on 2026-09-29, both verified; **still outstanding on every customer install.**
7. `20260929c_role_catalog.sql` — role catalog (display names, external names) and the Manager / Developer roles; `profiles.role` becomes a foreign key to `roles`. **Apply after 5 and 6, before deploying the code:** the code reads the catalog on every Roles and Users page load and answers 500 without it. External names are **not** seeded: an admin adds each provisioning system's names on the Roles page (or `PUT /server/roles/<role>` with `aliases`) before that system sends `role`. Rollback: `20260929c_role_catalog_rollback.sql` — moves Manager and Developer users to Tester first.
8. `20260929d_access_ceilings.sql` — team permission ceilings, device ceilings, and the complete claim sync. **Apply after 7, then deploy the code straight away:** it resets every team's permission list to *no limit* and moves the team claim from `team_permissions` (grants) to `team_ceiling`. Old code left running would lose every team grant (they are gone); new code *before* the migration enforces nothing wrong (no claim = no limit) but its Users page reads the old memberships function and shows every permission as blocked by the team limit. Users pick up a ceiling change at their next sign-in. Also restores the `tenant_ids` / `is_platform_admin` claims that `20260925a` stopped refreshing. Rollback: `20260929d_access_ceilings_rollback.sql` (team limits become empty grant lists). 7 and 8 applied on node1 and QualiAI (node3) on 2026-09-29 (no team held grants on either, so the reset removed nothing; backups `~<operator>/backup_20260929{c,d}_*.sql` on each DB VM); **still outstanding on every customer install.**
9. `20260929e_ai_routing.sql` — AI routing tables (`ai_endpoints`, `ai_profiles`, `ai_routes`, `ai_usage_stats`), seeded with the pre-upgrade defaults. **Order matters:** (a) apply the migration; (b) on the server run `python3 scripts/ai_routing_import_env.py --dry-run --host-env <a host .env>`, check the plan, then run it without `--dry-run` — it turns the old `AI_*` / `LOCAL_AI_*` / `VLM_*` settings into routes and moves the provider keys into `~/.config/virtualpytest/ai_secrets.json`; (c) deploy the code to the server **first**, then every host (new host code calls the server's `/server/ai/complete`; if one `update_core.sh` run covers both, the server must be updated before the hosts); (d) remove `AI_PROVIDER`, `AI_{AGENT,VISION,TEXT}_{PROVIDER,MODEL}`, `LOCAL_AI_*`, `ANALYZER_OPENROUTER_MODEL`, `DOM_MODEL`, `DOM_EFFORT`, `VLM_PROVIDER`, `VLM_PROVIDER_FALLBACK`, `VLM_TIMEOUT_SECONDS`, `*_VLM_MODEL` and the provider keys (`ANTHROPIC/OPENROUTER/OPENAI/MINIMAX/GOOGLE/GEMINI_API_KEY`) from server and host `.env` files; (e) check Settings → AI → Routing shows the same models as before and run the Test buttons. Rollback: `20260929e_ai_routing_rollback.sql` (only with a code rollback; keep the old `.env` keys until the new code is verified). Applied on the node1 lab DB and deployed on 2026-09-29 (see TASK-30 status); **still outstanding on QualiAI and every customer install.**
10. `20260929f_grafana_device_scope.sql` — the functions every dashboard calls to find the viewer's devices. **Apply before re-importing the dashboards** (scoped dashboards call it). It grants `grafana_reader` itself; if the Grafana datasource logs in as another non-superuser, grant it `EXECUTE` on `grafana_device_scope(text,text,text)` and `grafana_user_workspaces(text)`. Then **re-import every dashboard** (`_push_dashboard.py` or the Grafana UI — no deploy script does it), and run `python3 scripts/grafana_sync_org_roles.py` (dry run) then `--apply` once on the server, so existing Grafana users become Viewers; the built-in admin is left alone. A deployed `backend_server/config/integrations/grafana_config.json` that still maps a role to `Editor` keeps it — remove those entries. Customer dashboard folders go through `_apply_device_scope.py` before they are imported. Migration applied on node1 and QualiAI on 2026-09-29. Dashboards re-imported on node1 only (21 repo dashboards; the 5 dashboards that exist only in that Grafana were scoped from their live JSON; backups taken first); roles re-synced on 2026-10-01 (`--apply`: the one VirtualPyTest admin with a Grafana account went Viewer → Admin; a second dry run changes nothing). QualiAI dashboards scoped on 2026-10-01 from their live JSON (11 dashboards, System Server Monitoring skipped; backups taken first); every panel and variable query run through its Grafana before and after: no new error, identical rows for an admin, zero rows for a viewer with no devices. Its Grafana has no user to re-sync (dry run: 0 to change). QualiAI's server `.env` has no `GRAFANA_URL`, so it does not mirror users into Grafana at all; the sync ran with the Grafana settings passed inline. Its KPI Measurement dashboard pointed at a datasource that does not exist there (`ceunuuxwxvy80e`) and was replaced by the repo version node1 runs (BUG-0214 fix included). **still outstanding on every customer install.**
11. `20260929h_role_preferred_names.sql` — one preferred name per role, unique ignoring case; drops the per-provider `role_aliases` table of step 7 (unused: an installation serves one tenant). Apply with the code. Before applying, make sure no two roles share a name ignoring case, or the unique index fails. Rollback: `20260929h_role_preferred_names_rollback.sql`. Applied on node1 and QualiAI on 2026-09-29; **still outstanding on every customer install.**
12. `20261001a_device_models_catalog.sql` — adds the six missing core device models to every team and drops `device_models.controllers`. **Apply after deploying the code:** older code still reads `controllers` and the Device Models page fails to load until it is replaced. Then restart `vpt-server` (or wait 24 h), which caches the model list. Idempotent. Rollback: `20261001a_device_models_catalog_rollback.sql`, before rolling the code back. **Outstanding on every database.**
13. `20261001d_grafana_workspaces_builtin_admin.sql` — the Workspace drop-down also takes the Grafana login, so the built-in `admin` sees every workspace (BUG-0212). Apply after 10, then **re-import every dashboard** (`_push_dashboard.py` or the Grafana UI); the 5 node1 dashboards that exist only in Grafana need the same Workspace query change. Either order is safe: the login defaults to empty, so a dashboard not yet re-imported keeps working (the built-in admin still sees only *None* until it is). A non-superuser datasource login other than `grafana_reader` needs `EXECUTE` on `grafana_user_workspaces(text,text)`. Rollback: `20261001d_grafana_workspaces_builtin_admin_rollback.sql`, after re-importing the earlier dashboards. Applied on node1 and QualiAI on 2026-10-01. On node1 the Workspace query was patched in place in all 25 scoped live dashboards (only that variable; backups taken first) and checked to list every workspace for `admin`. QualiAI's dashboards were scoped afterwards with the fixed transform, so they carry the login-aware query (step 10). **Still outstanding on every customer install.**

Applied on the lab DB (node1 192.168.x.102) and on QualiAI (node3 192.168.x.102) on 2026-09-28, both verified; **still outstanding on every customer install.** Rollback: `20260928a_device_info_latest_rollback.sql`. Re-import `infra/monitoring/grafana/dashboards/device-information-get-info.json` into Grafana (BUG-0181) — no deploy script does this. New optional keys in `setup/docker/.env`: `HOST_VNC_STREAM_PATH`, `HOST_VNC_AUTOCONNECT`, `SUPABASE_PUBLIC_URL`, `SITE_URL`, `SMTP_*`, `ENABLE_EMAIL_AUTOCONFIRM`, `HOST_URL`, `HOST_SESSION_SECRET`. New optional keys in `frontend/.env`: `VITE_ENABLE_SIGNUP`, `VITE_ENABLE_RESET_PASSWORD` (and `VITE_ENABLE_OAUTH`); an admin-provisioned site sets all three to `false`, plus `[auth] enable_signup = false` in Supabase (leave `[auth.email]` on). Restart `backend_host` and rebuild the frontend. On emulator hosts, restart `vpt-stream` after the deploy (`update_core.sh <branch> --host --restart vpt-stream`). A plain code deploy leaves the old ffmpeg running.

**Integration credentials move out of the project folder (BUG-0187) — one-time copy, on every server, *before* deploying this build.** The new code reads `testrail.json`, `jira_instances.json`, `slack_config.json` and `slack_threads.json` from `~/.config/virtualpytest/` of the server's service user, and the deploy deletes the old copies in `backend_server/config/integrations/`. Copy them first or TestRail, JIRA and Slack come up disconnected (recoverable by re-entering the credentials in the UI). On each server, as a sudoer (the service user is `vpt_user`, home `/var/lib/vpt_user`, on installer-built servers):
```
D=~vpt_user/.config/virtualpytest; SRC=/opt/virtualpytest/backend_server/config/integrations
sudo install -d -m 700 -o vpt_user -g vpt_user "$D"
for f in testrail jira_instances slack_config slack_threads; do [ -f "$SRC/$f.json" ] && sudo install -m 600 -o vpt_user -g vpt_user "$SRC/$f.json" "$D/"; done; sudo ls -l "$D"
```
Docker (untested): the server now keeps these files in the `server-config` volume. Before upgrading, save the ones that exist; after the new container is up, copy them in and hand them to `vpt_user` (`docker cp` writes them as root, which the server cannot read):
```
mkdir -p integrations-backup; for f in testrail jira_instances slack_config slack_threads; do docker cp vpt-server:/app/backend_server/config/integrations/$f.json integrations-backup/ 2>/dev/null; done
# upgrade, then:
for f in integrations-backup/*.json; do docker cp "$f" vpt-server:/var/lib/vpt_user/.config/virtualpytest/; done
docker exec -u root vpt-server sh -c 'chown vpt_user:vpt_user /var/lib/vpt_user/.config/virtualpytest/*.json && chmod 600 /var/lib/vpt_user/.config/virtualpytest/*.json'
```

**New optional host key `VPT_TESTCASE_BACKGROUND_REPORT`** (`backend_host/src/.env`, BUG-0194) — nothing to set: by default (`pass`) a passing async testcase reports `completed` once its browser is closed and its verdict is written, and its report and log paths reach `script_results` a few seconds later; a failed one still waits for its report. `all` backgrounds failed runs too, `off` restores the old order. Restart `vpt-host` after changing it.

**New optional host key `VPT_POSITION_TRUST_SECONDS`** (`backend_host/src/.env`, BUG-0195) — nothing to set: by default (`10`) a navigation step does not re-check a position whose verifications passed less than 10 s earlier; `0` checks before every one-step hop, as before (now current screen first, a fraction of a second when it is showing). Restart `vpt-host` after changing it. **Behaviour change to expect:** a testcase that used to pass because its last step was skipped on a look-alike screen now runs that step, and can fail if the step or its destination check is wrong.

- [BUG-0560](../bugs/BUG-0560-2026-10-16-stale-user-guide-permissions-md-removed.md) Stale `docs/user-guide/permissions.md` replaced by the unified `docs/agent/platform/ACCESS_MODEL.md` (the new doc audits every layer's implementation status). Links in `user-guide/README.md`, `sharing-reports.md`, `grafana-alerting.md` updated. · area: docs
### Security
- **The backend trusted a certificate root whose signing keys are known to be compromised** — GitHub's dependency scanner reported one **critical** advisory against `certifi` ([GHSA-6q48-6c7w-9gw2](https://github.com/advisories/GHSA-6q48-6c7w-9gw2)): the CA bundle `certifi` ships still carried the `emSign ECC Root CA - G3` root, and **eight eMudhra intermediate CA certificates** chaining to it had **compromised private keys** — the CA revoked them. An attacker holding one of those keys can issue a valid-looking certificate for any hostname and, if they are also on-path, intercept or modify the TLS traffic of every client that trusts the bundle. `certifi` is not a library the backend uses on the side — it is the trust store: `requests` (pinned at `2.33.0`) reads its CA bundle from `certifi/cacert.pem` at import time, and so does `httpx` and everything built on `urllib3`, so every HTTPS call the backend makes (Supabase, the AI providers, the GitHub API, the model hosts) was validated against a bundle that still carried that root. The root itself is cryptographically intact, which is exactly why the problem cannot be seen from the outside: a certificate chaining to it keeps validating, and the only symptom is a connection that should have been rejected being accepted. The pin was never revisited, so the advisory surfaced only as one of the six open dependabot alerts on the default branch. `certifi` is now pinned to `2026.10.19`, the version the advisory names — the measured bundle difference confirms it is the right one and not the over-removing `2026.10.18` (which dropped all emSign roots): exactly the affected root is gone and the three unaffected emSign roots are still there. The advisory also asks for container images that vendor certifi to be rebuilt, because the bundle is baked into the image. In plain words: the backend was willing to believe a certificate signed by a key chain that is known to be broken · [BUG-0944](../bugs/BUG-0944-2026-10-26-the-backend-trusted-a-certificate-root-with-compromised-signing-keys.md)
- **The frontend dependency tree shipped a URI parser with four known parsing flaws** — GitHub's dependency scanner reported **four** advisories at once against `fast-uri`, one transitive package that nobody asked for by name: it arrives with `ajv@8.18.0`, which `serve@14.2.6` pulls in, and `ajv` uses it to resolve `$ref` URIs while compiling a JSON Schema. Two are medium (host confusion through unchecked WHATWG host canonicalization, and URI scheme spoofing through percent-encoded scheme decoding) and two are low (scheme injection through an unescaped first-segment colon in suffix references, and improper path equivalence in relative-reference normalization). All four cover `3.0.0 <= v < 3.1.9`, and the lockfile pinned `3.1.8`, so the tree sat inside all four vulnerable ranges at the same time. The exposure was limited, and it was worth saying so before touching anything: these are URI *resolution* flaws, so reaching them needs a schema whose `$ref` an attacker controls — `serve` compiles its own fixed config schema and the platform never accepts a schema from a caller, and the lockfile entry is marked dev-only. So this was never an exploitable path in the running platform; it was a known-bad version of a parser sitting in the tree, reported four times over on every push. `fast-uri` is now at `3.1.9`, the version that closes all four at once — `ajv@8.18.0` already asks for `^3.0.1`, so the three-line lockfile change adds, removes and moves nothing, and `npm audit` on the tree now reports **0 vulnerabilities**. The sixth open alert, `datasets`, stays open on purpose: `unsloth 2026.10.3` requires `datasets <5.0.0` and the patch is `5.0.1`, and the platform never imports `datasets` at all · [BUG-0945](../bugs/BUG-0945-2026-10-26-the-frontend-dependency-tree-shipped-a-uri-parser-with-four-known-parsing-flaws.md)
- **Every Node.js runtime on the platform could be driven to remote code execution by a crafted Brotli payload** — CVE-2026-111497 ("HTTP2Shell") is an out-of-bounds heap write in the Brotli decoder Google ships inside Node's `node:zlib` (Brotli 1.0.3 to 1.2.0). Any server that accepts a `Content-Encoding: br` request body decodes it **before** routing and **before** authentication — Express 5 / `body-parser` 2.x calls `zlib.createBrotliDecompress()` — and any client that fetches a Brotli response decodes it too: measured on Node 18.20.7, 23.6.0 and 24.21.0, the client sent `Accept-Encoding: gzip, deflate` and **still ran the response through the vulnerable decoder**, so a hostile upstream server reaches the client process with no negotiation needed. Every Node.js on the platform shipped the vulnerable decoder. Node fixed it in **22.23.4 / 24.22.1 / 26.12.1** — as a cherry-pick of the upstream Brotli commit, so `process.versions.brotli` still reports `1.1.0` / `1.2.0` on the fixed builds and the Node version is the only reliable signal; Node 18, 20, 23 and 25 are EOL and get no fix at all. The platform's own installers could not deliver the fix: they compared **only the major version**, so a host on `22.22.2` was declared healthy forever and never pulled the patched build. A new shared guard (`setup/local/linux/shared/ensure_node.sh`) now compares **full versions**, upgrades a host below the fixed build of its own line, moves an EOL line onto the supported LTS, and **fails the install** rather than shipping a vulnerable host; the frontend, storage and database installers all use it. Container base images are pinned to a patched build (`node:22.23.4-alpine`) because a floating `node:22-alpine` tag cannot be read as "patched". The public entry points were checked one by one and were never reachable: the edge is nginx **without** the brotli module, the backend is Python with **no** `brotli` package (so `requests` advertises `gzip, deflate` only), and the frontend is a static file server with no body parsing. · [BUG-0905](../bugs/BUG-0905-2026-10-24-nodejs-brotli-decoder-rce-cve-2026-111497.md)
- **The MCP docs tools no longer serve internal-only docs** — `list_docs`, `search_docs` and `read_doc` served every file under `docs/`, including gitignored runbooks and the internal-paths list, and the agent docs they served still carried lab addresses, a public gateway address, a database password and an operator login name. Internal docs are now refused (and skipped in listings and searches), and the served docs use placeholders, now checked on every push. `read_doc` and `list_docs` also now publish their `path` and `section` parameters. · [BUG-0623](../bugs/BUG-0623-2026-10-18-mcp-docs-tools-served-internal-docs.md)

### Internal

- **The stall watcher now clears the lock when its fixer ends (BUG-0949)** — an unattended Pokémon lane only plays again when the lock of a stalled save is cleared, and the last link of that chain was the fixer agent's own work: step 6 of its brief ("clear the lock"). An agent that ends without it — it died, it was closed after the 75 min mark, it went another way — left the save locked for ever, and nothing reported it: the watcher's retry window is 6 h, so the same stall was not even looked at again. On 2026-10-26 the second lane was dark for **4 h 30** and the first for **54 min**, both with their fix already on main. The watcher now clears the lock itself when the fixer it started is over, through the dispatcher's own command (`clear-stall --save <save>`) so the clear lands with the lane that plays that save — but only for the lock that still names that very report, never for a newer stall and never for a lock whose fixer is still working, and a failed clear keeps the session so the next minute tries again · [BUG-0949](../bugs/BUG-0949-2026-10-26-the-stall-watcher-left-the-save-locked-when-its-fixer-ended.md)

- **A server deploy ships the code but never installs the Python packages (BUG-0946)** — a deploy pulls the code, rsyncs it and restarts `vpt-server.service`, and the Python packages in the venv are none of its business: the venv is explicitly outside the rsync (`--exclude='venv'`) and only the host installer ever fills it. So when a pinned dependency changes in `backend_server/requirements.txt`, the deploy copies the new file to the target, restarts the service on the **old** packages and reports success. This is not a theory: the `certifi` bump that closed the critical advisory (BUG-0944) was deployed as `main-2026.10.26-90121`, the deploy was green, and all three live servers still ran the old trust store — the bundle that still trusts the revoked eMudhra chain. The only way to find that out was to look at the venv on the host. It was worse than a missing step, because a green deploy is exactly what a person relies on to decide that a fix shipped, and because the file on the target always says the new version — the rsync replaces it before anybody compares anything, so a file-to-file diff says "nothing changed" precisely when the venv is the thing that is out of date. A server deploy now compares the pin the repository declares against the version the venv actually has (read from the target with `pip list --format=freeze`), installs what is behind one package at a time, and does it on every deploy rather than only when the code changed, so a venv that drifted can catch up on its own. Only exact pins are handled: a range would need a full resolve of the whole file, which is a bigger decision than a deploy should make on its own. The venv's ownership is put back first, because on one host 136 entries in `site-packages` had drifted to root and pip as the service's own user could not write the distribution metadata — it left an invalid distribution behind, and the result was a venv where the import worked but `pip show` said the package was not installed. A failed install fails the deploy and the service is not restarted on a half-installed dependency. A new harness covers it, and it is in the CI list — a harness that is not in that hardcoded list never runs · [BUG-0946](../bugs/BUG-0946-2026-10-26-a-server-deploy-shipped-the-code-but-never-installed-the-python-packages.md)
- **The clear of a stall now reaches the lane it is about (BUG-0932)** — the fixer of a Pokémon stall is told to run `pokemon_dispatch.py clear-stall`, and that command only ever clears the **first** lane's save: the dispatcher reads its save from `POKEMON_SAVE` and its state from `POKEMON_STATE_DIR`, and the plain command uses the defaults. The second save therefore kept its lock while the fix for it was on main and deployed on its host: **5 h 30 without a run**. The clear command now takes `--save <name>`, a clear for another save travels through the canonical store (the one place both lanes share) and the lane that plays that save takes it, and the brief names the save the stall is about. Both lanes were cleared the repo's own way and played again at 06:51 · [BUG-0932](../bugs/BUG-0932-2026-10-26-the-clear-of-a-stall-reached-the-wrong-lane.md)

- **The Pokémon stall fixer now works from the current main (BUG-0923)** — the session that fixes a stall was started in `~/virtualpytest`, the shared checkout of every Claude Code session on codebox: its HEAD is whatever the last session left behind, and on 2026-10-26 it was a day old. The fixer of the `hall_of_fame` stall therefore replayed the save and "found" the recursion `walk → unblock → talk_at → walk` that main had fixed 16 hours earlier (BUG-0919), while the stall it was sent to fix was a stall. The fixer now gets a clone of its own, put on `origin/main` right before it starts, and the brief names it · [BUG-0923](../bugs/BUG-0923-2026-10-26-the-pokemon-stall-fixer-worked-from-a-checkout-that-was-a-day-old.md)
- **The Virtual Scripts page can be typechecked again, and the nightly check that would have caught it (BUG-0924)** — the typecheck job on `main` was red with one error: the helper that hides the system scripts asked for a folder as a string, while the interface says a script with no folder has `null` for it, so passing a script to the helper was a type error (`string | null` is not a `string | undefined`). The page itself worked, because the code already treated a missing folder as an empty one — only the typecheck saw it. The helper now takes the folder the interface has. The error was not new: the heavy suite only runs inside the 00:00-07:00 UTC night window, and every push to `main` on the evening of 2026-10-25 landed outside it, so every one of those runs was reported green with all the jobs skipped and the typecheck never ran. In plain words: the gate that was supposed to catch this never ran, and when it finally did, it pointed at one line · [BUG-0924](../bugs/BUG-0924-2026-10-26-the-virtual-scripts-page-could-not-typecheck.md)

- **The nightly check on main now survives the next push (BUG-0926)** — the regression suite that is supposed to run once a day on main was dropped before it started: measured over the last 100 workflow runs, one was scheduled. The nightly run shared the queue of every push to main, and the queue keeps only the newest waiting run, so a push that landed while the nightly run was still waiting threw it away. The first nightly run that really happened found a red gate on main that had been sitting in the tree the whole time. The nightly run now has a queue of its own, like a manual run already had, so a push can no longer throw it away. In plain words: the daily check now happens · [BUG-0926](../bugs/BUG-0926-2026-10-26-the-nightly-check-on-main-was-dropped-by-the-next-push.md)

- **The CI runner disk is emptied after every build again (BUG-0941)** — the `Docker stack builds and starts` job failed twice on `main` with `no space left on device`, on a code error that had nothing to do with the code: the runner's 31 GB root was at 96 % and held 12 GB of images, one of them a 5 GB image with no name left on it. Every build tags a new `virtualpytest-host:local` and leaves the previous one behind without a tag, and the clean-up only removed images **older than a day**, so those leftovers stayed until the disk was full. The clean-up now also removes the untagged leftovers right away — no job can be using an image that has no name and no container — while the images a running job just built keep their one-day protection. In plain words: the runner disk is reclaimed after every build instead of filling up and killing the build · [BUG-0941](../bugs/BUG-0941-2026-10-26-the-ci-runner-root-filled-up-and-the-docker-build-died.md)

- **Two sessions can no longer take the same bug number (BUG-0942)** — three times in one day two sessions picked the same free number while both were working, and the collision reached `main`: the check that looks for it only reads the working tree, so each session saw a free number and the clash only appeared in the merged tree. `main` was then red for every agent until somebody renamed a file, and the one who noticed had to guess which of the two files was allowed to keep the number. A new gate now refuses the push that would cause it: it compares the numbers the push adds with the numbers the branch it will land on already carries, names the file that already holds the number, the file that wants it, and the next free number to take. A push that adds the same number twice is refused too. In plain words: the clash is caught at the push that causes it, not after it lands · [BUG-0942](../bugs/BUG-0942-2026-10-26-two-sessions-picked-the-same-bug-number.md)

- **The run record no longer calls a run in flight "failed" (BUG-0922)** — the per-run record of the free-Flash game lanes wrote two runs that were **still playing** as failures with a wall time of zero. The platform writes the row of a starting run with `completed_at = started_at` (a placeholder) and leaves the duration NULL, and the reader asked for the one signal that column never carries (`completed_at IS NULL`), so a run that had just started read as *finished, failed, no reason given* — the CoC run of 01:03 was called a failure and then played for another quarter of an hour on Flash. The reader now uses the platform's own rule (the placeholder timestamps together with the missing duration) and prints *running* for a run in flight. In plain words: the table no longer counts a game that is still being played as a game that broke · [BUG-0922](../bugs/BUG-0922-2026-10-26-the-run-record-called-a-run-in-flight-failed.md)

- **The CI runner cleanup can no longer delete the workspace of a running job, and no longer runs twice at the same time (BUG-0903)** — the script that reclaims the disk of the self-hosted runners was installed by hand on the runner VMs, so nothing reviewed it and nothing tested it. Its guard looked for `bin/Runner.Worker` while the runner really runs the versioned `bin.2.338.0/Runner.Worker`, so it never matched and every runner looked idle; it then picked the workspaces to delete by the age of the directory, which says when the tree was created and not whether a job is running inside it. A cleanup that landed on a running job deleted its checkout and the job died in `actions/checkout`. The script is now in the repo with its units and its documentation, the guard matches the versioned worker, the liveness is checked again right before every delete, the age filter asks about the job's own activity instead of the directory, and a workspace a live process holds is never deleted - not even when the disk is nearly full. Every runner now gets a decision line, so the next incident can be read from the VM. the retention script is driven by two different systemd units: a daily full cleanup and a 15-minute check that only acts when a disk is filling up. systemd does not serialise two different units, so the daily sweep and the 15-minute check could walk the same runner workspaces at the same time, and two passes deleting the same tree is a race nobody wants on a disk that is already full. The script now takes a lock file (`/run/ci-runner-retention.lock`) for the whole run; a run that does not get it logs `another run in progress, skipping` and deletes nothing. In plain words: the nightly cleanup no longer pulls the ground from under a job that is running, and the daily sweep and the 15-minute check no longer tread on each other · [BUG-0903](../bugs/BUG-0903-2026-10-25-the-ci-runner-retention-deleted-the-workspace-of-a-running-job.md)


- **The frontend test tooling gets its own npm cache** — `vitest` and the `@testing-library` packages are not in `package-lock.json`, so the typecheck and component-test jobs fetch them separately, and the runner's npm cache is persistent and shared by the three runners on a VM. An interrupted install can leave an entry in that cache pointing at a removed blob, and the next `npm install` then dies with `ENOENT: ... npm/_cacache/content-v2/sha512/...` even though the registry fetch itself is fine — which is how Frontend Typecheck failed in run 4397. The old guard was `npm cache clean --force`, but that blanks the cache the whole VM shares, so it also destroyed the cache a sibling job was reading; the two jobs overlap often.
