Both jobs now install into a cache directory of their own: never shared, never stale, and never destroyed mid-install by a sibling. In plain words: the two frontend jobs stop fighting over one npm cache.

- **A background audit log can no longer break a chat turn** — the FAQ miss log and the agent routing log both wrote their row from a daemon thread, and creating that thread was outside the `try`. When thread creation failed (no thread slots left) the exception escaped into the turn and the user's message died on an audit row the docstring promises never breaks a turn. Both now report the failure and carry on. The routing-log test's fake `threading.Thread` also only accepted `(target, args, daemon)`: because the fake replaces the attribute on the *stdlib* threading module, the too-narrow signature broke unrelated callers — `faqs_step` passes `name=`, and nine tests failed on it in runs 4393 and 4394. The fake now matches the real signature, and a new test pins it to the stdlib so a future narrowing is loud instead of a surprise two files away.

- **The stale-row reaper's test now matches the per-row contract** — the reaper used to ask the host "is this DEVICE busy?" and one runaway run shielded every dead row on that host; the host now answers "is THIS row still running?" and echoes the execution id back. The reaper's test still faked the old device-level answer, so the server read it as a host on an old build, kept every row, and three tests failed on every CI run (run 4309). The fake host now answers the per-row question, the "asked once per device" test became "asked once per row" (each question carries its own row id), and two tests were added for the safety nets the new contract introduced: a host on an old build keeps the row, and a live run on a device no longer shields a dead row on that same device. The three tests were mutation-checked — they fail again if the per-row question or the old-build guard is removed.

- **The live CI jobs can hold the node1-vpt lease while they test — the key is not provisioned yet** — `api-routes`, `backend-server-tests`, `vpt-smoke`, `web-script-local-debug` and the three `e2e-*` jobs sweep the deployed server, so a deploy restarting it mid-run turned every request into an HTTP 500: run 4177 failed 518 API routes and 134 backend tests with **zero** real assertion failures, and run 4179 repeated it. `wait_for_server.sh` only guards the *start* of a job, so it could not see a deploy beginning two minutes into a six-minute sweep. Each of those jobs now runs its command under `scripts/ci/lease_guard.sh`, which claims `node1-vpt server` (`--kind server`, `--shared`, so the six runners still run in parallel) with a 10-minute TTL and a 60 s heartbeat, and releases on success, failure and cancel — never `--force`, never another agent's lease, and a dead runner cannot hold it past the TTL. A deploy would then queue in `vpt-lease` instead of restarting the server underneath the run, which can make a deploy wait up to ~10 minutes for a live CI job. **It protects nothing until the `CI_LEASE_SSH_KEY` secret exists**, which is a credential decision left to the owner (Q217): with no key every live job logs a warning and runs unprotected, exactly as today. When granted, the key is a forced-command key on proxmox that can drive the lease registry and nothing else, so CI cannot reach a shell on the hypervisor. In plain words: the mechanism is built and tested, but a red live job is still a deploy collision until the owner turns it on.

- **Smoke tests clean up the projects they create** — the tester create-project test deleted with a tester token (always 403) and the admin create test never deleted, so every CI run left projects behind. They now delete as admin, and the session-end sweep removes any leftover smoke project (`Smoke Project`, `Smoke Delete`, `Tester Create`, `Exec Smoke`, `p-<hex8>`).

- **`code_refs`: who calls, imports or hits X, in one tool call** — a new MCP tool (and CLI `features/code-graph/scripts/gen_code_index.py`) answers "who calls this function or method, who imports this module, which frontend or Python code hits this route, what breaks two levels up" from a stdlib-AST index of the checkout, built in memory in the background when the server starts (without slowing other requests) and rebuilt when the code version moves. It lists grep hits that are not calls, so agents need not re-check, and for `Class.method` it follows the receiver's type (`store = RunStore()`, `-> RunStore`, pytest fixtures, base classes, items of a dict or list of one class, `getattr`, `isinstance`), and routes are prefixed by the `url_prefix` given at `register_blueprint(...)`. `AGENTS.md` tells coding agents to use it before grep. On held-out questions it cut what agents read by 71% (median) against grep at the same accuracy. Off with `DISABLED_FEATURES=code-graph`. · [Graphify.md](../agent/Graphify.md)

- **The fleet deploy gate understands `--hosts a,b`** — the agent-side lease gate (`claude-mods/fleet`) counted `update_core.sh main --hosts vpt-pi1,vpt-mf1` as a deploy of the whole environment, so one busy host lease (a web-tree benchmark on host-clone-1) blocked a deploy to two idle STB hosts. It now checks the lease of each listed host only (a device lease on a listed host still blocks it); `--hosts` together with `--server` is still the whole environment. Test added in `fleet.test.ts`; takes effect after the plugin reloads from the main checkout.

- **Proxy caches the remote Pis' live video pieces** — the proxy keeps each 0.4 s HLS segment from `vpt-pi*`, `vpt-nipogi` and `vpt-mf1` for 2 min, so a second or third viewer of the same Pi device no longer makes the Pi upload it again over its home connection. Playlists stay uncached (nginx cache times are whole seconds, which would put viewers up to 2 s behind live). `infra/proxy/nginx/apply_pi_stream_cache.sh`, README "Remote Pi live-stream cache".
- **stb4 BLE keys lost when vpt-pi1's onboard adapter hangs (BUG-0649)** — the Pi's `hci0` went DOWN after an HCI reset timeout, so `cold_wake` and key presses never left the host and TASK-133's stb4 validation skipped 165 steps behind one failed tab check. Recovery (`hciconfig hci0 up`, restart `hid-remote@hci0`) is in the BLE recovery doc; the `stb_tv` `home_apps` check now waits 3 s instead of 0. Detection of the DOWN adapter is not built yet. · [BUG-0649](../bugs/BUG-0649-2026-10-19-pi1-ble-adapter-down-stb4-keys-lost.md)
- **Localize audit flags two nodes that store the same screen (DUP_FP, BUG-0645)** — `scripts/localize/audit.py` lists, per interface, nodes whose stored fingerprints share the same focus and title: a node recaptured on the wrong screen hides there (the `stb_tv` TV Guide node held a Home frame). `recapture.py` now stops with a clear message when the interface is not in the database of the shell it runs in, instead of printing "0 of 0". · [BUG-0645](../bugs/BUG-0645-2026-10-19-recapture-0-of-0-wrong-database.md)
- **Localize names the Home gear tab on STBs (TASK-133)** — the stored fingerprint of the `stb_tv` TV Guide node was a Home frame with the gear focused, so the guide competed with `home_settings` and sometimes won; it is recaptured on the real guide screen. Ten Localize calls on `home_settings`: 7 right before, 10 right after. The `vip7002w-6.04` and `dcx960-02.04` variants carry the 5.29 Home-tab order and the no-TV-Shop layout, with their own Home-tab fingerprints (Home tabs 2 of 9 right before, 7 of 9 after). · [TASK-133](../tasks/TASK-133-stb-localize-revalidation.md)
- **A vpt-host restart kills running scripts (BUG-0641)** — recorded gap: a deploy that restarts `vpt-host` ends scripts that are running on that host and leaves only a placeholder result; the deploy checks leases, not running scripts. Workaround: hold a device lease for the whole run. · [BUG-0641](../bugs/BUG-0641-2026-10-18-host-restart-kills-running-validation.md)
- **Quieter browser console on every page** — the chat panel no longer prints the full saved conversation, socket state or listener setup on each page load, the agent badge bridge no longer logs each state change, and a video tile logs one line (device, raw and resolved stream URL) instead of five. Errors and the chat's live event logs stay. In plain words: about 50 console lines per page load drop to about 15, and the debug traces are still there. · area: platform
- **Every deploy rebuilds the frontend bundle; the hash-skip stays for restart-only paths** — `setup/proxmox/node/update_core.sh` now passes `VPT_FORCE_BUILD=1` to `frontend/scripts/ensure_dist.sh` so the rsync that just landed on the VM is followed by a guaranteed fresh dist, not a stamp comparison that may match the previous bundle's stamp by coincidence. The hash-skip remains in the script for the systemd `ExecStartPre` (a hot restart does not need a 30s rebuild when the bundle is current); the unit file is unchanged. A doc note in `ensure_dist.sh` now records the contract — "do not let the unit file gain `VPT_FORCE_BUILD`" — so a future refactor doesn't break it. In plain words: a deploy always ships what was deployed; a restart stays hot. · area: deploy
- **Per-route `@require_team_in_tenant` defense-in-depth sweep on team-scoped routes** — TASK-23 phase 3 was deferred and the decorator sat declared but unused on every team-scoped route; the global `enforce_team_scope()` guard catches the team_id path, but a per-route decorator was missing. The sweep applies the decorator to every team-scoped route so the tenant-defense check is now structural on the route itself, not only on the path matcher. · area: backend
- **`.env` and `.env.*` are ignored at every depth, and a pre-commit hard-stop refuses force-added .env files** — `.gitignore` now lists `*.env`, `.env.*`, `**/*.env`, `**/.env.*`, plus the per-deployment names that have shipped in past commits (`vpt_windows_native.env`, `.env.host`, `.env.server`, `.env.docker`, `.env.proxmox`). The `vpt_windows_native.env` incident (committed in `d04b1fcf8` by an agent bundling work, then `git rm --cached` in `0db9a7395`, blob still in every commit) is the reason. `.githooks/pre-commit` (Bash) now scans `git diff --cached` after the `VERSION.txt` bump, refuses any non-`.example` `.env` file with a clear message, and offers `ENV_BLOCK_SKIP=1` as the documented bypass (same convention as the pre-push leak gate). Two layers of defense: `.gitignore` blocks normal `git add`; the pre-commit hook blocks `git add -f`. · area: platform
- [BUG-0563](../bugs/BUG-0563-2026-10-16-monitor-img-skip-capture-folder-when-img-false.md) **Monitor profile and PC-host idle gate are now documented and cross-referenced.** The imager, audio and subtitle services idle (a few MB, no OpenCV, no workers, no inotify watches) on a host whose every device runs `pc` / `tv_lite`, because `wait_until_needed` consults the same `folders_needing_service` helper as the host orchestrator and the in-imager per-capture filter. New `pc` profile (semantically named for PC/desktop/server captures, identical operational shape to `tv_lite`) is in the Settings picker. · area: monitoring
- **The server's public name comes from `.env`, not from the code** — scripts, the localize tools, the fleet-health units and the Grafana installer read `SERVER_PUBLIC_URL` (scripts: `VPT_SERVER_URL`, `VPT_MCP_URL`, `FLEET_HEALTH_SERVERS`) instead of a hardcoded `rpitest` address, and the CORS default no longer lists it. New `.env` keys: `SERVER_PUBLIC_URL` on every server, `FLEET_HEALTH_SERVERS` on the server that runs the fleet-health timers; the two `vpt-fleet-health*.service` units are hand-installed and must be re-copied. In plain words: renaming a server no longer means searching the repo. [TASK-92](../tasks/TASK-92-server-url-single-source.md)
- **RPI1-server is now `<pi1-host>`, and RPI4-server shows by name** — the server picker lists `<pi1-host>` (was `<pi-server-host>`) and `<host-server>` (was the raw IP ``<gateway-ip>``, shown Offline). The old `rpitest` name keeps working until it is retired after a week without traffic. In plain words: pick **vpt-pi1** or **vpt-pi4** in the Server menu; you sign in to each of them once more. [TASK-92](../tasks/TASK-92-server-url-single-source.md)
- **Hosts and server move to numpy 2 and OpenCV 4.13+** — `requirements.txt` now asks for `numpy>=2` and OpenCV `>=4.13` (was numpy `<2`, OpenCV `>=4.8`). OpenCV before 4.13 leaked every image file name (BUG-0474), and numpy 1.26 has no wheel for Python 3.13, the only Python on Debian 13. Checked on 210 real frames from vpt-pi1: detector, template match, histograms and edges give identical values with numpy 1.26/OpenCV 4.11 and numpy 2.4/OpenCV 4.14. Existing hosts keep their old packages until upgraded: `update_core.sh` does not run pip. Upgrade a host with `/opt/virtualpytest/venv/bin/pip install -U "numpy>=2,<3" "opencv-contrib-python>=4.13,<5"` (plus `opencv-python` to the same version where both are installed), then restart its Python services. In plain words: the platform runs on current numpy and OpenCV, and new hosts can use the system Python on Debian 13
- **Lite host profile for one device on a 1 GB board** — `HOST_PROFILE=lite` in the host `.env` (set before `install_host.sh`) installs only vpt-host, vpt-stream, vpt-monitor and vpt-archiver, no desktop/VNC/browser packages, and a 512 MB zram swap; on a full host it stops and disables transcript, KPI, VNC, websockify, subtitle, MediaMTX, AVQ and companion. The capture ffmpeg runs HLS only and single-threaded (vpt-pi1: 31 threads / 78 MB private → 1 thread / 51 MB, same CPU), navigation stops queuing KPI scans nobody reads, and the dashboard shows those services as optional. In plain words: a one-phone or one-box site can run on a cheap 1 GB board instead of a 4 GB Pi ([TASK-83](../tasks/TASK-83-lite-host-1gb.md))
- **Capture monitor and KPI executor use at most two malloc arenas** — their systemd units set `MALLOC_ARENA_MAX=2` (on the 8 reachable hosts through a `malloc-arena.conf` drop-in, already applied and restarted 2026-10-12). glibc kept freed memory in one arena per thread: 11 arenas holding 101 MB in the vpt-pi1 monitor, now 1 holding 24 MB. The monitor's total varies by more than that over hours, so expect a small gain, mostly on long-running vpt-kpi. In plain words: two background services hold on to less memory they no longer use ([TASK-83](../tasks/TASK-83-lite-host-1gb.md))
- **WebRTC can be switched off per host** — `WEBRTC_ENABLED=false` in the host `.env` stops the capture ffmpeg from encoding Opus and publishing to MediaMTX; restart `vpt-stream`. In plain words: a host that is short on CPU can stream HLS only, and the WEB button then falls back to HLS
- **Promo videos get an automated quality gate** — `scripts/promo/promo_check.py` OCRs a built video every 0.5 s and counts error text, IPs, secrets, login forms and private names, black frames, zoom flashes, too-wide captions, length > 90 s and voice-over drift; `youtube_upload.py` refuses to upload unless all read 0 (`--skip-check` is logged), and the capture scripts share one IP/secret scrub, host-session mint and CORS relay from `promo_lib.js`. In plain words: a product video with an error or a leaked address on screen can no longer reach YouTube unnoticed
- **Cloudflare Worker smart 5xx page + origin probe** — when the proxmox VM (Hetzner) is unreachable from Cloudflare, users now see a branded "Service temporarily unavailable" page (last healthy `4m ago`, the `cf-ray` request id, the upstream HTTP code, a Retry button) instead of Cloudflare's generic "Connection timed out / Error 522". A minute-by-minute cron probe in the Worker also pings `/server/health`, stores `{ok, lastOk, consecutiveFailures, alertSent}` in a CF KV namespace, and pages Slack on the operator's webhook after 2 consecutive failures with a recovery message on the way back up (no spam — sticky until ok). New code under `infra/cloudflare/` (Worker source + `wrangler.toml` + deploy README) plus the static `infra/cloudflare/custom-pages/5xx-error.html` you can paste in CF dashboard → Custom Pages → 5XX Errors as the fallback when the Worker is not bound. Deploy actions for you — `wrangler kv:namespace create STATUS_KV` + paste id into `wrangler.toml` + `wrangler secret put SLACK_WEBHOOK` + `wrangler deploy` + bind route `virtualpytest.angelstreet.io/*` to the Worker in the CF dashboard. No new server endpoint, no DB migration, no `.env` key on the proxmox VM · `974049687`
- **1 Hz localize loop skips the matcher on motion, with a 1.5 s settle tail** — `monitor_localize.gate_motion_decision` short-circuits `identify_screen` on a sustained live / band / minor>1 % frame and serves a cached verdict instead, and `_update_motion_state` forces a fresh matcher call once a motion→static transition has been held for ≥ 1.5 s. The gate decision is logged on change only, plus a 30 s heartbeat (BUG-0093 shape). Tier-2 fields are absent on a gate-skip payload; a fresh run carries them. In plain words: live TV / moving carousel no longer churns a matcher answer that will only ever be 'unknown'
- **Per-frame `localize` payload carries Tier-2 (loaded / overlay / playback / error)** — `monitor_localize._screen_state` calls `shared.src.lib.ai.screen_state_cv.screen_state` on every fresh matcher run and folds the four answers into the payload; the AVQ `analyze_positions` produces one RLE lane per Tier-2 field (`positions_loaded` / `positions_overlay` / `positions_playback` / `positions_error`) alongside the existing `positions` lane. The frontend `formatScreenVerdict` surfaces them in priority order (state > error > overlay > playback > loaded > node) with new color tokens; old readers that only know the match verdict keep working. In plain words: the AVQ Frames tab and the live overlay can now say WHY a frame was hard to identify, not just THAT it was
- **Userinterface editor takes a free-text `company` label, plumbed to the monitor** — the userinterface page gains a `Company` field (placeholder `brand_a, brand_b, brand_c`), the backend persists it as a NULLABLE `userinterfaces.company` column, and the monitor plumbs it into the per-frame `localize` payload as `userinterface_company` and as a brand-aware extra keyword to the Tier-2 `error` arm (a `BRAND_A` token on a brand_a userinterface becomes a `visible` answer — a strong splash / login candidate). NULL = unknown company, treated as today. Inherits through dev→prod publish and round-trips through `.vptree` import/export. · 🗄 DB migration `setup/db/migrations/20261008_userinterface_company.sql`
- **Userinterface editor takes an `is_media` boolean, plumbed to the Tier-2 `playback` arm** — the page gains an "Is media" column with a Switch in edit mode (a `null` on the edit form means "leave the column as-is"; flipping to a real bool persists), plus a switch in the create dialog so the editor can flag a new UI as media without leaving the row open. The backend persists it as a NULLABLE `userinterfaces.is_media` column; NULL is the safe default (caller abstains, `playback='unknown'` — same as today, byte-for-byte identical cheap 23-frame harness). TRUE / FALSE are explicit editorial choices: TRUE marks a media surface (`playback` returns `playing`/`paused`/`stopped` based on the changed_pct + audio state), FALSE marks a definitively non-media surface (`playback='not_applicable'`). Cheap verification on a moving-pct frame: `_screen_state(is_media=True)` → `playing`, `is_media=False` → `not_applicable`, `is_media=None` → `unknown`. Frontend `tsc --noEmit` clean, `ast.parse` clean on the three modified files. · 🗄 DB migration `setup/db/migrations/20261008_userinterface_is_media.sql`
- **Tier-2 `error` arm runs a Tesseract OCR pass before `scv.error()`** — the monitor now points `pytesseract.image_to_string(..., --psm 6)` at the frame, splits the result, and hands the token list to `scv.error(tokens)`, so the arm can answer `visible` / `none` / `unknown` instead of abstaining on every frame. Lazy import (a host that only ever sees a media surface — `is_media=True` — never imports it), throttled on `is_media is not True` (a confirmed media broadcast cannot bear an error keyword, so OCR would burn cycles for no new information), and the company-aware re-check re-uses the same token stream (one pass, not two). On the cheap 23-frame harness: `error=none` P/R/F1 went 0.00/0.00/0.00 → 1.00/1.00/1.00 across the 15 knowns (no broadcast slate in the harness has an OCR-visible error keyword by design). Wall time 0.17 s → 9.7 s for the 23 frames — the price for an answer instead of an abstention. `ast.parse` clean.
- **Masterchief agent for elevated system operations** — the in-app AI chat (Atlas) can now propose 7 narrow system ops (read service logs, list services, check service status, restart a single service on a single host, list hosts / per-host infra summary, explain a rollout, show deploy state). The proposal renders an inline confirm card in the chat thread with the blast radius and permission; **the browser is the privilege holder** — the user clicks Confirm, the card POSTs `/server/masterchief/execute` with the user's own JWT, and the route enforces the op's required permission plus a prod-only `require_platform_admin` for mutating ops. Atlas never executes directly. Every attempt writes one row to `masterchief_audit` (RLS service-role-only, same lockdown as `agent_turns`); secret-looking keys, JWTs, PEM blocks and `k=v` patterns are stripped at the boundary, and the route layer **rejects the server `X-API-Key` outright** — this closes the `MCPAPIClient` service-key bypass documented in TASK-71 Part 1. v1 ships read-only first; `service.restart` is the single mutating op. No new API key, no `.env` change on prod; the optional `MASTERCHIEF_REQUIRE_PLATFORM_ADMIN=0` is the dev override for broader auth. 18 offline unit tests cover the op registry, redaction, route guards and rate limit. Masterchief is now also its own entry in the AI chat agent picker (Fleet Ops: host health, disk, logs, service restarts), and a "Monitoring problem on <host>" bell item has an "Ask masterchief" button that opens the chat with the alert; disk-full diagnosis is read-only, and the one delete is a confirm-card op, `host.disk_cleanup`: it removes only expired capture files inside the host's capture dirs (the same retention windows as the dashboard's "Fix now"), shows a dry-run size on the card, is refused while another user holds a device of the host, and takes no path or age from the request. In plain words: ask Atlas to restart a service and you get a card to confirm; nothing executes without your click · [TASK-71](../tasks/TASK-71-masterchief.md) · `1228433a` (merge; feature b3479d3..a2ad476) · 🗄 DB migration `setup/db/migrations/20261009a_masterchief_audit.sql`
- **Atlas `system-ops` skill for read-only host health checks** — when the user says "is the host up?", "how much storage is left on vpt-pi3?", "ping vpt-pi3", or "df -h", Atlas now answers directly without a confirm card. Two new MCP tools, auto-discovered as the `system_ops` category, hit two new host routes: `GET /host/system/checkStorage` (uses `shutil.disk_usage` — portable across Linux/macOS/Windows) and `GET /host/system/pingHost` (200 ms TCP cap to the host's backend port, returns RTT + boot time). The skill is narrowly scoped to read-only-host-health: every list / restart / status / log intent escalates to `propose_masterchief_op`, which is the confirm-card surface (see the matching `BUG-0450` fix below). 8 unit tests, all green. No new `.env` key, no migration, no API key; `update_core.sh <branch>` is the deploy command. In plain words: harmless host checks no longer need a click · [TASK-73](../tasks/TASK-73-atlas-testing-progressive.md) · `ebdca9ad6` (merge: de-dupe `fix/system-ops-dedupe`)

**Settings** — add to the `.env` on each machine:
- every host: `ARCHIVE_VIDEO`, `TRANSCRIPT_AUTOCORRECT`, `WEBRTC_ENABLED`

**Dependencies** — install BEFORE building, on every machine of that kind:
- frontend (`frontend/package.json`): `compression`, `postcss-selector-parser`
- every host (`backend_host/requirements.txt`): `aiohttp`, `pyserial`, `pyyaml`, `samsungtvws`, `scipy`, `soundcard`, `sounddevice`, `websocket-client`
  ```
  cd /opt/virtualpytest/frontend && sudo -u vpt_user npm install
  ```
  Skipping this fails the build with "Rollup failed to resolve import" and
  the site keeps serving the previous bundle.

**Services** — restart by hand (the deploy restarts the rest itself): `vpt-discard-incidents`, `vpt-discard-scripts`, `vpt-heatmap`, `vpt-emulator-fifo@`, `vpt-procon`, `vpt-timeline`

Unit templates changed, reinstall where they run: `emulator-fifo@.service`, `host.service`, `kpi.service`, `monitor-audio.service`, `monitor-img.service`, `monitor-subtitle.service`, `procon.service`, `rtsp.service`, `stream.service`, `timeline.service`, `vnc.service`, `vpt-discard-incidents.service`, `vpt-discard-scripts.service`, `vpt-match-runs-writer.service`

**Proxy** — `docker.conf`, `pi-stream-cache.conf`, `timeline-cache.conf`, `local-http.conf`, `production-https.conf`, `proxmox.https.conf`, `proxmox.local.conf`, `proxmox.local.https.conf`

**Grafana** — `audio-video-quality.json`, `cicd-quality.json`, `ai-benchmarks.json`, `all-zapping-events.json`, `av-check-results.json`, `campaign-results.json`, `deployment-table.json`, `device-alerts-dashboard.json`, `device-information-get-info.json`, `device-occupancy.json`, `discard-incidents-reviewed.json`, `discard-scripts-reviewed.json`, `dns-lookup.json`, `fingerprints.json`, `fullzap-results.json`, `game-2048.json`, `home-dashboard.json`, `image-telemetry.json`, `kpi-measurement.json`, `kpi-test-report.json`, `navigation-metrics.json`, `ookla-speedtest.json`, `script-results.json`, `superping.json`, `system-host-monitoring.json`, `system-server-monitoring.json`, `system-service-status.json`, `virtualpytest-self-test.json`, `vpt-fleet-health.json`

**screen registry (TASK-159)**

- Migrations `setup/db/migrations/20261022e_screen_registry.sql` (additive; rollback `20261022e_screen_registry_rollback.sql`) then `20261022f_screen_prune_guard.sql` (replaces the prune function; rollback `20261022f_screen_prune_guard_rollback.sql`), both applied on the lab database. Steps in order: 1) apply the two migrations; 2) `update_core.sh main --server` (classifier job, replay worker); 3) hosts: `vpt-monitor-img` must restart (or a `--host` deploy) to register `unknown_N` rows and read the frame's screen id in freeze alerts; 4) after the report is approved, run the soft merge of duplicate rows: `python3 scripts/screen_registry_merge.py --rows <export> --sql merge.sql`, then apply `merge.sql` (no DELETE).

**incident last key and wake probe (TASK-158)**

- No migration. Hosts: `vpt-monitor-img` must restart (or a `--host` deploy) to stamp `last_key` on new alerts; until then triage runs as before. Server: `update_core.sh main --server` restarts `vpt-discard-incidents`. The probe stays off until the owner sets `ALERT_PROBE_MODE=dryrun` or `on` and `ALERT_PROBE_DEVICES` in the server `.env`.

**host disk full (TASK-156)**

- A host's journal is now capped at 512 MB by `/etc/systemd/journald.conf.d/90-vpt-cap.conf`, installed by `install_host.sh` on new hosts only. Existing hosts keep an uncapped journal until the owner applies the drop-in (copy `setup/local/linux/backend_host/journald/90-vpt-cap.conf`, restart `systemd-journald`). No restart of VPT services, no migration.

**audit trail (TASK-154)**

- **Every database, before the server deploy:** apply `setup/db/migrations/20261022a_audit_events.sql` as `supabase_admin` (new `audit_events` table, a login trigger on `auth.users`; service role only; rollback `20261022a_audit_events_rollback.sql`). The server runs without the table but logs a warning per batch.
- Also apply `20261022b_audit_events_logout.sql` (adds the sign-out trigger; rollback `20261022b_audit_events_logout_rollback.sql`).
- Optional `.env` keys on the server: `VPT_AUDIT_STORE_RAW_IP=1` (store the raw caller IP; default off), `VPT_AUDIT_RETENTION_DAYS` (default 90). Then deploy the server.

**aligning an older node or a customer install (node 3 run, 2026-10-19)**

- The full step list is [UPGRADE_EXISTING_INSTALL.md](../../setup/proxmox/node/UPGRADE_EXISTING_INSTALL.md). What `update_core.sh` does **not** do, in short:
- **Database:** it never runs migrations. Back up, dry-run on a scratch copy, then apply `setup/db/migrations` in order **as `supabase_admin`** (as `postgres` it stops with `must be owner of table fleet_health`), with `-v owner_server=<SERVER_NAME>` for `20261004a`, and record each file in `applied_migrations` (baseline for the old ones). Feature SQL (`features/mods`, `features/ai-dataset`, `features/zammad`) is applied separately. `20261015b` no longer fails on `data_migrations.id`.
- **Renamed units:** `vpt-monitor` → `vpt-monitor-img`, `vpt-transcript` → `vpt-monitor-audio`, `vpt-subtitle` → `vpt-monitor-subtitle`. The host deploy migrates them; update anything that names the old ones.
- **Server units to install by hand:** `vpt-match-runs-writer` (enable), `auto-classify.service` + `.timer` (enable the timer), `vpt-discard-incidents` (install, keep disabled in production).
- **Per-node deploy script:** keep the node's `HOST_IPS`, `SSH_USER`, `REPO_DIR` (`/opt/virtualpytest`), `LEASE_ENV` and `LEASE_REGISTRY_HOST`; a repo copy dropped in as is points at `~/virtualpytest` and the wrong lease env.
- **Packages and sudoers:** `python3-yaml` on the frontend VM (docs build), `PyYAML>=6` in every host venv, `install_vpt_update_sudoers.sh` on the server VM and every host.
- **Lease cap:** a deploy of several hosts can pass the 10-minute lease and stop with exit 6; rerun with `--hosts` for the rest.
- **AI routing:** routes, profiles, endpoints and budgets are data, not migrations, and the provider keys are not in the database. Copy the four `ai_*` tables from the reference install, disable endpoints the target cannot reach, restart `vpt-server`, then test every profile with the target's own keys (a dead OpenRouter key or a retired model fails silently until a call is made).
- **Report links and recovery check:** set `MINIO_PRESIGN_ENDPOINT` to the install's own public name on the server and every host and route `/virtualpytest/` to its MinIO in nginx (otherwise every report link answers `InvalidAccessKeyId`); migrate the database in the same window as the host code (newer host code on an old schema failed every scheduled run in under 100 ms for three days); then read the next two hourly runs.

**platform update settings in the database (TASK-128 G9)**

- **Every database:** apply `setup/db/migrations/20261018g_platform_update_settings.sql` (new `platform_update_settings` table, service role only; rollback `20261018g_platform_update_settings_rollback.sql`). Not applied yet. Deploying the code first is safe: until the table exists, Settings keeps using its file and says so (`storage: file`). On the first save after the migration the file's values are imported and the file is renamed `settings.json.imported`.
- No new `.env` key; `SERVER_NAME` (already used elsewhere) names the server's rows. `update_core.sh` deploys the rest.

**platform update prerequisites (TASK-101, lab run)**

- **Every server:** set `FRONTEND_DEPLOY_HOST` in the server `.env` (there is no default any more): the frontend VM address, `local` or `none`. Our lab value: `FRONTEND_DEPLOY_HOST=192.168.x.105`. Restart `vpt-server` afterwards. Until it is set the prerequisites check and the pull deploy refuse, and the old *Code deployment* buttons report that the host is not configured.
- **Every host and the server VM:** run `sudo bash /opt/virtualpytest/setup/sudoers/install_vpt_update_sudoers.sh` once (installs the root-owned reconcile helper, its lib and the new `/etc/sudoers.d/vpt-update`), and again after any update that changes `reconcile_feature_units.sh`. An older drop-in naming the script in the project tree is replaced. See the [set-up guide](../user-guide/update-setup.md#host-sudo).
- No migration, no service restart beyond the server's `.env` change. `update_core.sh` is unchanged.

**Localize corrections (TASK-104)**

- **Every database:** apply `setup/db/migrations/20261014e_localize_verdicts.sql` (new `localize_verdicts` audit table, `screen_fingerprints.label`). Applied on the lab / node1 database on 2026-10-14; **not** applied to any customer database yet. Without it *Wrong screen? → Save* answers an error and nothing else changes. Rollback: `20261014e_localize_verdicts_rollback.sql`.
- No new `.env` key; `update_core.sh` deploys the rest (server, hosts, frontend).

**monitoring profiles (TASK-90)**

- **Every host:** `pip install 'pyyaml>=6'` into the host's venv (`/opt/virtualpytest/venv`; deploys do not run pip) and check pydantic v2: `python -c "import yaml, pydantic; assert pydantic.VERSION[0]=='2'"`. Without PyYAML `tv_app` still runs from a built-in copy, no other profile can load. Then deploy with `update_core.sh` (it migrates the units and restarts them).
- **Every database:** apply `setup/db/migrations/20261013b_alerts_profile.sql` (a `profile` column, default `tv_app`, and the `incident_type` CHECK dropped, so `no_signal` and profile alerts can be stored). Rollback: `20261013b_alerts_profile_rollback.sql`. `20261013c_alerts_no_signal_check.sql` (only adds `no_signal` to the CHECK) is the narrower alternative and is not needed once `b` is applied. Until one is applied nothing breaks: a refused `no_signal` alert is logged once per device and retried hourly.
- **Model files only for camera profiles** (not in git): `MONITOR_COCO_MODEL` for `counting_cars`, `MONITOR_EGG_MODEL` for `egg_quality`. `tv_app` and `ad_meter` need none.
- **Windows hosts** rerun `install_host_windows.ps1`; **Mac hosts** reinstall the launchd services.
- **`drone` profile only:** `DEVICE<N>_MONITOR_PROFILE=drone` on the host `.env` (the Tello controller in vpt-host needs the restart that `POST /host/system/config` or a deploy does anyway: the bridge file is written by it). No model, no migration beyond the one above. For the optional burn-in: `DEVICE<N>_TELEMETRY_BURN_IN=1`, `apt install fonts-dejavu-core` (or `TELEMETRY_HUD_FONT=<ttf>`) and a restart of the stream grabber (`vpt-stream`), which is where the text is drawn.

**Compared with** `main-2026.09.27-9396` → `main-2026.10.05-10128` (1962 files changed).

**Fire TV models by OS (`fire_tv_fireos`, `fire_tv_vega`, TASK-57)** — on hosts with a Fire TV stick:
- DB, in this order: `20261005c_vega_tv_device_model.sql` adds the Vega model to the catalog (as `vega_tv`). It re-creates `create_default_device_models()`, so it also seeds the core models of `20261001a` (`fire_tv`, `android_tablet`, `ios_mobile`, `tizen`, `camera`, `host_vnc`) where that one was never run; the `controllers` column drop stays with `20261001a`. Then `20261005d_fire_tv_models_by_os.sql` renames `fire_tv` → `fire_tv_fireos` and `vega_tv` → `fire_tv_vega` in the catalog, in `userinterfaces.models` and in the history tables. Apply `20261005d` with the deploy: older code does not know the new names. **Both applied on node1's database (192.168.x.102) on 2026-10-05**; still to apply on every other database (customer installs, proxmox3/QualiAI).
- Host `.env`: rename `DEVICEn_MODEL=fire_tv` → `fire_tv_fireos` (no host in our lab used it on 2026-10-05; check customer hosts), and a Vega stick is `DEVICEn_MODEL=fire_tv_vega` + `DEVICEn_ADB_SERIAL=<serial>` (USB) or `DEVICEn_IP`/`DEVICEn_PORT` (TCP). No new key. The host deploy restarts `vpt-host` and picks it up.
- Host OS: `adb` installed, and a plugdev udev rule for USB vendor `1949` (Amazon/Lab126), as for Fire OS sticks.
- On the TV, once: developer mode on (Amazon's `vega devmode` flow), and Display & Sounds → Ambient Experience → Advanced → Timeout = **Never** so the stick does not go to standby ([FIRE_TV_VEGA.md](../agent/devices/FIRE_TV_VEGA.md)). Fire OS sticks: `adb shell settings put secure sleep_timeout 0`.
- Deploy: host scope (`update_core.sh main --host`) + frontend (`--frontend`) for the Take Control panel.

**Masterchief** ships read-only first; the `service.restart` op is in the build but ships gated by the prod admin check, so it lands in the same code drop. No new deploy dependency; the work is concentrated in the server and the frontend, so a server deploy is enough — no host restart is required for the read-only ops. The `service.restart` op calls the existing `/host/restartHostService` route via `call_host`, so the host already supports the operation.

**Database** — apply this once per database before the server picks up the new code:
- `setup/db/migrations/20261009a_masterchief_audit.sql`
Rollback (only if you must): `setup/db/migrations/20261009a_masterchief_audit_rollback.sql`.

**Settings** — no new key on prod. The dev override is opt-in:
- `MASTERCHIEF_REQUIRE_PLATFORM_ADMIN=0` on the dev site (e.g. `virtualpytest.angelstreet.io`) to allow non-admin users to execute mutating ops during testing. **Leave it unset (1) on prod**.

**Dependencies** — no new package.

**Services** — restart by hand (the deploy restarts the rest itself): `vpt-server`. The frontend only needs a `npm run build` deploy; no host restart is needed for the read-only ops. If you use a frontend-only deploy path on the dev site, the chat card lands with that.

**Deploy command** — standard debug deploy:
```
git push origin debug
ssh proxmox "bash update_core.sh debug --server"
```
For a full deploy (recommended on prod), drop `--server`. The migration is **not** applied by `update_core.sh` — apply it before or with the deploy as noted above.

**Audit read** — the admin can read the table at `GET /server/masterchief/audit?days=30&team_id=<id>` and, with `scope=platform`, across teams (platform-admin only; the documented exception to the `team_id`-filter rule, tracked as CONTRACTS #29).


## build 9369 — 2026-09-26

### Bug fixes
- **Published-image verification and the standalone bundle check were both silently broken** — `docker-compose.yml`'s Supabase `include` set an `env_file` whose relative path different Compose versions resolve differently (some relative to CWD, some relative to `setup/docker/`), so no single value worked on both the self-hosted and GitHub-hosted CI runners. Removed the line entirely — the top-level project's own `.env` already supplies the same variables to the included file via the ordinary, version-independent `--env-file` mechanism (verified byte-identical `docker compose config` output with and without it). This has been failing the public repo's `Release artifacts` → `bundle` and `verify` jobs since at least 2026-09-24 (images still published fine — only the end-to-end proof that a pulled image works was silently skipped) · [BUG-0163](../bugs/BUG-0163-2026-09-26-docker-compose-include-env-file-path-doubling.md)

### Upgrade

**Compared with** `main-2026.09.26-9366` → `main-2026.09.26-9369` (4 files changed).

**Database** — no migration.

**Settings** — no new key.

**Dependencies** — no new package.

**Services** — nothing to restart by hand.

## build 9307 — 2026-09-24

### Features
- **Monitoring → Analytics: the basics without opening Grafana** — seven tabs answering the questions that were costing a trip to Grafana and a warm-up: how many robots and devices, how many are up, **down, or neither** — a device whose ffmpeg or monitor has stopped still reports in, so it looks present everywhere else in the app while capturing nothing; incidents and alerts over time, by type and by device; test pass rate and busiest scripts; disk, memory and CPU per machine; and, read from the repository rather than the database, lines of code by area, bugs by severity, and features and fixes per release. It is not a Grafana replacement — no ad-hoc queries, no drill-down, and no filters in this first cut — and every tab links out to the dashboard that goes deeper. Tabs rather than one page is a performance decision: the capture-availability rollup behind Devices costs 1382 ms computed live, so a single page would make every visit pay for it. It is now a materialized view refreshed every 15 minutes (1.4 ms), the server serves stale values instantly while refreshing behind the request and keeps every section warm from boot, and switching tabs in the browser costs no network at all. Opening the page fetches one section, not seven · 🗄 DB migration `20260917_analytics_views.sql` · area: monitoring
- **New optional feature `device-farm` — a phone in a cloud device farm is an ordinary VPT device** — add a `DEVICE{i}_MODEL=cloud_android_mobile` slot with a `DEVICE{i}_FARM_*` block to a host's `.env` and a Sauce Labs or BrowserStack phone appears on the Dashboard, is selectable as a target in Run Tests, and runs the same navigation trees, actions and verifications as a device on your desk — no local hardware, just outbound internet. The remote leases a W3C Appium session against the farm and a frame pump writes screenshots into the slot's image file, so the host's existing ffmpeg path produces the usual stream, captures, report screenshots and video: a farm run's evidence looks like any other run's. **No session is opened until the first command**, so a configured slot that nobody is using costs nothing — an idle host with four slots would otherwise bill for four devices. Sauce Labs is complete (sessions, app upload, device listing, session links); BrowserStack runs sessions today and its REST half is planned in TASK-21; LambdaTest carries its session shape only. Proven end-to-end on real hardware: the same `goto` script passing on a Sauce Galaxy S23 FE and a BrowserStack Galaxy S23 from one host, each with screenshots and video in the report. Turn the whole thing off with `DISABLED_FEATURES=device-farm`. See [features/device-farm/README.md](../../features/device-farm/README.md) and, for getting an app onto a farm, [APPS.md](../../features/device-farm/APPS.md) · 🗄 DB migration `features/device-farm/db/001_cloud_device_models.sql` · area: integrations
- **Open sign-up and tenants** — Anyone can sign up and try the platform as a read-only viewer, and tenants are a first-class entity with one super admin per platform. · area: platform

### Improvements
- **The app launcher shows each app's package name** — the Android Mobile remote listed its 200-odd installed apps by label alone, so two apps from the same publisher, or any label that says nothing about what it launches, were indistinguishable until one was launched and the wrong one had to be backed out of. Each row now carries its package name right-aligned in a smaller, dimmed font, both halves ellipsizing independently so a long package can never push the label out of view. The closed field still shows just the label, so nothing changes in the narrow remote panel until the list is open · `426f2be6a8` · area: device-control
- **Maximize the Verification Editor while defining capture areas** — a larger side-by-side view keeps the device image visible for dragging while giving the editor more room · `3c686ee39d` · area: testing
- **Rerun any run straight from Test Reports** — the one-click rerun that Run Tests has on its "Last Executions" table is now on **Test → Test Reports** as well, as a **Rerun** column on the Tests tab. Clicking it relaunches that exact run on its original target: same script, same host and device, same parameters, and for a virtual script the same `dev`/`test`/`prod` row that actually ran rather than a disk file guessed from its name. A saved testcase reloads its definition by id and replays the input values the original run was given, with no dialog re-prompt. Rerun is run-now only — a locked device produces an error rather than a silent queue entry, so nothing starts later without being asked for. The reason this could not simply reuse Run Tests' version: the two pages read different tables. Run Tests lists `deployment_executions`, whose `deployments` row already carries the launch config; Test Reports lists `script_results`, which had no link to it and, crucially, never recorded the parameters a run was launched with. Both halves are now closed — the launching deployment's config is joined onto each result, and the executor records the resolved command line (plus the virtual-script row id) on every run it writes, which is what covers the runs that never went through a deployment at all: campaign steps and direct API calls. Rows with neither — an unsaved ad-hoc testcase graph, which exists only in whatever submitted it — show a dash instead of an icon, the same convention Run Tests uses · area: testing
- **A KPI number now carries its own precision** — a KPI is the matched frame's timestamp minus the action's, with no interpolation, so it is quantised to the gap between the two frames around the change: **200 ms** from the live capture buffer, but **500–1000 ms** once a window is old enough to be served from the archive. Nothing recorded which of the two produced a value, so 1227 ms could mean ±200 ms or ±1000 ms and the report showed both identically — which is why the numbers were arguable. Each measurement now stores and shows its evidence: effective fps over the action→match window, how many frames never arrived, which storage served them, and the interval the change is *provably* inside (the match frame proved it present, the newest verified frame proved it absent). The report header reads `KPI: 1s 227ms ±200ms` with a `Capture: 5.0 fps effective | Frames: 8 in window | Dropped: 0 | Source: hot` line, and an amber box lists what makes a particular measurement weaker — archive-sourced frames, a real capture gap, a scan that skipped frames, a match that did not hold, or the live-verifier fast path, which returns a value without opening a single capture and was previously indistinguishable from a scanned one. Stored in `execution_results.kpi_measurement_meta` because the frames themselves are deleted within minutes, and surfaced as a `Precision (ms)` column on the KPI tables · 🗄 DB migration `20260921_execution_results_kpi_measurement_meta.sql` · area: testing
- **KPI dashboard: a per-app opening time, profile screen included** — every KPI table averages one *edge* at a time, so an app that sometimes shows a profile screen before its home page is split across two unrelated lines and neither is the opening time anyone means. A new **Apps** table in each UI row measures the whole run: when the profile screen appeared, the two steps are added together; when it did not, it is the single step. Both land on the same line, with `Direct (ms)`, `With Profile (ms)` and `Profile %` splitting the average into the two cases — the share of openings that hit the profile screen was not visible anywhere before. Every other column (`Variant - App`, `Model`, `Avg KPI (ms)`, `Success`, `Failures`, `Over Threshold`, `Threshold (ms)`, `Count`, `Report`) is the one the table above it already uses. Nothing has to be configured: which steps belong to one opening is read from the navigation tree, from the same conditional-edge structure that colours the canvas — one press with several outcomes, where one outcome leads on to another. A press whose outcomes are two genuinely different endings (play vs resume) is not an app opening and keeps one line per ending · area: testing
- **The KPI report shows the action and the match as two rows of three** — the evidence strip ran *before action → after action (+wait) → before match → match* in one row of four, which read as a single sequence and skipped the two frames that explain the measurement. It is now the action trio on top — the screenshot taken before the press, **the captured frame at the press itself**, and the screenshot taken once the action's wait elapsed (4s on a typical edge, i.e. most of the gap that row used to jump over) — and the match trio underneath: before match, match, **after match**. Neither new frame needed new plumbing: both are already in the scanned window and the mosaic has been tagging them ACTION and AFTER MATCH: they were simply never lifted into the strip, so seeing what the screen did at the press, or immediately after the match, meant opening the mosaic and hunting for the right tile · area: testing
- **Scan mosaic tiles read as time, not as file numbers** — every frame in the analysed-window mosaic carried its capture id in a strip across the top and its offset in a strip across the bottom, so the two facts a reviewer actually needs — *when* the frame was taken and how far it sits from the action — were the two hardest to find, and on a flagged frame the thick green/orange border was painted last and clipped both. A tile now shows the frame's wall-clock time top-right, which lines up with the report header's Measurement Start/End and with the host logs where the capture id lined up with nothing; the offset from the action bottom-right; and the legend saying *why* the frame is flagged (✓ MATCH / ACTION / BEFORE MATCH / AFTER MATCH) top-left. All three are opaque pills drawn on top of the border, so a colour can no longer swallow the text · area: testing
- **Farm devices are marked as such — on the Dashboard and on the features page** — a BrowserStack or Sauce Labs phone looked exactly like a device on a shelf in our own lab: the only hint was whatever the slot happened to be named, and a rename took even that away. Each preview now carries the farm's logo in its bottom-left corner, opposite the running-script badge, so where a device physically lives is readable at a glance across the whole fleet. The mark comes from the slot's `DEVICE{i}_FARM_PROVIDER`, not from its name, so it stays correct however the device is labelled, and a local device shows nothing at all. [The features page](../features/README.md) gained a matching **Works with** strip naming both farms, since compatibility with them was a selling point nobody reading that page could tell we had · area: integrations
- **Edit registered host and device settings from System Settings** — select a host, update its `.env` device configuration, then choose confirmed `vpt-host` or `vpt-stream` restarts · `8628b0fbb7` · area: platform
- **The docs menu and its navigator stop looking like a text file** — every section in the docs navigator was prefixed with an emoji, because `copy-docs.sh` concatenated a glyph onto the title string: the dropdown was rendering text where the rest of the app renders icons, and one section read `Mcp`. Titles are plain now and the navigator draws a Material icon per section from the same set the navbar uses. **Integrations** also joins the Docs menu, right after Bugs — the page existed and was reachable only from the navigator or the Integrations menu · area: platform
- **The docs Integrations page is a grid of what we actually connect to** — the page was a wall of prose that answered "does it do X?" only if you read all of it, and it had drifted: the cloud device farms were not on it at all. It now opens with fourteen cards — logo, name and one sentence — covering Jira, Slack, Grafana, Sauce Labs, BrowserStack, Appium, Playwright, GitHub Actions, Postman, Langfuse, OpenRouter, Supabase, Cloudflare R2 and MinIO, each linking to the page that sets it up and each carrying the vendor's own logo. Those marks live in `public/vendor/`, not `public/brand/` — the latter is customer-owned branding that `update_core.sh` excludes from every deploy, so anything put there serves the SPA's index.html instead of an image. The cloud-farm badge on a device preview and the two farm logos on the features page were hitting exactly that and are fixed with it. Only what we actually integrate is on it — LambdaTest sits behind the same provider seam as the two farms but has never been run against an account, so it is not a card. Reachable from the navbar too — **Integrations → All integrations** — which is the way in for the tools that have no page of their own. The prose below the grid is unchanged, minus the two lists the grid replaces. New: [Cloud Device Farms](../integrations/device-farms.md), the guide the three farm cards point at · area: platform

### Bug fixes
- **Linux VNC restarts repair the service user's home permissions** — the unit ensures `vpt_user` owns its home and XDG data directories before TigerVNC writes `.Xauthority` · [BUG-0160](../bugs/BUG-0160-2026-09-23-vnc-service-home-owned-by-root.md) · `TBD`
- **Image verifications now resolve names containing dots** — the backend preserves dotted names during lookup; the editor warns that new reference names cannot contain dots and suggests underscores · [BUG-0159](../bugs/BUG-0159-2026-09-23-dotted-image-reference-name-cannot-resolve.md) · `ecb053038c`
- **Backend Host can start with no configured devices** — startup reuses the IP already detected by the application and accepts an empty device registry · [BUG-0158](../bugs/BUG-0158-2026-09-23-empty-backend-host-startup-stalls.md) · `03dd66493b`
- **Standby KPI reports now use the selected mode's display name** — the script resolves the mode name from the active variant graph, and Grafana KPI labels use the recorded display name while keeping the measured transition visible · [BUG-0157](../bugs/BUG-0157-2026-09-23-standby-kpi-display-name-ignored.md) · `2b42a227a4`
- **An admin's permission list stops five short of what the Users page offers** — the permission vocabulary is written out twice, once for the checkboxes and once for the API that answers what somebody may do, and the five workspace permissions had only ever reached the first copy. So the Users page drew 68 boxes while `GET /server/permissions/matrix` reported an admin as holding 63, and anything reading that API to reason about access — a provisioning caller, a support answer, an audit — was told something the UI contradicted. The same function had a second miscount: it subtracted an admin's denied permissions, although nothing that actually grants or refuses access does, so an admin who had been denied something back when they were a tester was reported as still lacking it. Nobody was wrongly allowed or refused by it: workspace administration is admin-only and never consults those five strings, which is also why the gap survived so long. Both lists now carry the same 68, with a test pinning them together so they cannot drift apart again. And the five stop being decorative: workspace administration asked only "are you an admin?", so ticking `org.workspaces:edit` on a colleague saved and changed nothing. Those routes now ask for the permission, which **nobody gains by default** — it is in no role's starting set, so an ungranted tester or viewer is refused exactly as before — but an admin who wants to hand workspace administration to one person can now do it with the checkbox instead of by making them an admin. One route was reachable by anyone signed in: the per-user workspace lookup took a user id from the URL and checked nothing, so any account could read another's workspace list by editing it; reading your own is still ungated (every page load needs it), reading someone else's now needs the permission · [BUG-0154](../bugs/BUG-0154-2026-09-22-workspace-permissions-missing-from-the-backend-vocabulary.md)
- **The KPI report says which action it measured** — the header read `Last Action: N/A` on every report ever generated, because it forwards `step['last_action']` from the navigation executor and **nothing anywhere sets that key** — it is the only reference to it in the whole navigation package. The action really was known: `action_details`, passed in the same request and already rendered by the report's own "Last Action Executed" section, carries the command and its parameters. The header now builds its label from that, so a remote press reads `press_key OK` instead of `N/A`, with the bare command as a fallback and `N/A` kept only for a genuinely empty action · [BUG-0153](../bugs/BUG-0153-2026-09-21-kpi-report-last-action-always-na.md)
- **Choosing "base" in Run Tests now actually runs base, instead of the device's own variant** — a device can name a favourite userinterface and variant in its `.env`, and that is meant to be a *suggestion*: what the form starts on, and what an API caller gets if it says nothing. Choosing something else in the form was supposed to win. For the variant it did not, because "the user picked base" and "the caller never mentioned a variant" were written down the same way — an empty value — and every hop between the form and the script treats an empty value as nothing to say. The page dropped it while assembling the command line, the API route dropped it while normalising, and the host dropped it while deciding whether anyone had chosen; the device default then filled the gap three times over. It cost nothing for as long as no device named a variant; the day one did, `kpi_measurement` on that STB failed every navigation while the same navigation by hand worked, and the error it reported — `No path found from 'ENTRY' to 'home_replay'` — was true, and sat 250 lines below the first red line: the variant it had been given instead disables the very edge the base plan routes through. An explicit choice is now carried the whole way as a real instruction, base included, and the device default applies only when the caller genuinely said nothing · [BUG-0152](../bugs/BUG-0152-2026-09-21-explicit-base-variant-dropped-device-default-wins.md)
- **A navigation, action or verification finishes in seconds again, instead of always taking two minutes** — a `goto` to a node the device was already on reported "succeeded in 2m:00s" while its own ETA said 9.55s, and the same flat two minutes applied to actions, verifications, campaigns and testcases on every host. The work had always been fast: the host finished in 3.25s and said so. What was lost was the *telling*. Closing `/server/*` behind a credential updated the host's register, ping and unregister calls but missed the one that pushes execution events, so every completion event had been answered `401` since 2026-09-07 — and because a 401 is a response rather than an exception, the emitter treated it as success and logged nothing. The page then waited the full 120s its socket wait allows before falling back to polling, which found the run finished the moment it looked. The event now carries the same credential its sibling calls do, and a rejected POST is reported instead of swallowed, so the next credential change fails loudly rather than quietly costing every execution two minutes · [BUG-0151](../bugs/BUG-0151-2026-09-21-host-execution-events-rejected-401-since-server-lockdown.md)
- **Installing a host on a Raspberry Pi no longer claims to set a VNC password it never wrote** — `install_host.sh` installs `tigervnc-tools` and then called `vncpasswd`, but on Debian and Raspberry Pi OS that package ships the binary as `tigervncpasswd`, so the command simply did not exist. It was the middle of a pipeline ending in `tee`, and a pipeline reports the exit status of its *last* command, so the missing binary cost nothing: `tee` wrote an empty file, the script carried on, and the next line printed `✅ VNC password set to: …` with the password it had just failed to store. The host then came up looking entirely healthy — the VNC unit running, 5901 listening — and refused every connection, with nothing in the install output or the service log pointing at a 0-byte password file. Both call sites (the installer and the Docker entrypoint) now resolve whichever binary is present, and refuse to continue if the password file ends up empty, so the next variant of this — missing package, unwritable path — fails loudly instead of shipping a host that cannot be reached · [BUG-0150](../bugs/BUG-0150-2026-09-21-vncpasswd-binary-name-leaves-empty-password-file.md)
- **The dashboard stops filtering to errors once there are none left** — the red error badge in the Hosts header switches on an "errors only" view, and that toggle is persisted, so it outlived the errors that justified it. When the last host recovered the page went blank — no cards, just "No hosts match the selected filters" — and the badge that had switched it on was gone with the errors, leaving the filter bar's `Clear` chip as the only undo. A reload the next day landed straight on the empty page. The toggle now releases itself as soon as the hosts have loaded and none of them is in error; a scope emptied by the target/model/tag filters, or one still loading, is left alone · [BUG-0149](../bugs/BUG-0149-2026-09-21-errors-only-filter-outlives-the-errors.md)
- **An Android phone is one device, however you reach it — so it sees its own userinterfaces and scripts** — a phone cabled over adb, a phone paired through the VirtualPyTest app, and a phone in a cloud device farm are three ways to the same thing, but the platform compared model names as text: `android_mobile`, `phone_agent` and `cloud_android_mobile` matched nothing but themselves. Picking a paired or farm phone in Run Tests, the Campaign builder or the TestCase builder therefore offered only the userinterface whose model list happened to spell that exact word, hiding every tree already written for an Android phone — and a script declaring `android_mobile` as its target was filtered out for exactly the devices it was written for. The workaround was a second model chip added by hand, or a duplicate tree per transport (`phone_home`, `cloud_farm_demo`). Model matching now goes through one family table, shared by the server and the page, so any of the four Android-phone models is offered everything written for the others. Nothing widens by accident: a model not named in the table still matches only itself, a tablet is still not a phone, and the one deliberately one-way rule — a VNC host can run a web interface, a web device cannot run a VNC one — stays one-way. Making that true meant bringing the cloud side up to it as well: an `android_mobile` tree's screen checks, its swipes, and its "wait until this stops changing" verifications all run on a leased farm session now, on the same one session rather than a second billed device · [BUG-0144](../bugs/BUG-0144-2026-09-17-device-model-string-match-hides-userinterfaces.md)
- **The mobile app shows streams again** — the access gate added to `/host/<name>/stream/` blanked every live and archive stream in the mobile app, on every screen. The app was not failing to ask for access: it minted a session, got a 200, then 401'd on every manifest and segment. The app serves its bundled frontend from its own origin, so every call it makes to a deployment is cross-**site** — and three things only break there, which is why the gate looked healthy from a browser on the deployment's own domain. The session cookie was `SameSite=Strict`, which a browser will not store or replay across sites at all; nothing asked the browser to send it, since the player fetched streams without credentials; and the stream path answered with a wildcard `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`, which a credentialed request discards whatever the status. All three fixed, with the relaxed cookie still HttpOnly, still scoped to one host's path, still 10 minutes, and still authorizing nothing but that host's video · [BUG-0143](../bugs/BUG-0143-2026-09-17-stream-gate-locks-the-mobile-app-out-of-every-stream.md) · `7028cf2438`
- **The mobile app is pinned to portrait, so a report's screenshots are the same way up everywhere** — `MainActivity` declared no orientation, so it adopted whatever rotation the device was in when it was launched. In a hand that is correct and invisible; on a cloud device farm there is no hand, and vendors differ — the same script produced portrait frames on a Sauce Galaxy S23 FE and sideways 2340x1080 frames on a BrowserStack Galaxy S23, because relaunching the app there came back landscape. Setting the orientation back over the wire did not fix it: the activity had already taken landscape. The QR scanner screen was pinned to portrait for this same reason; `MainActivity` now is too · [BUG-0142](../bugs/BUG-0142-2026-09-17-mobile-app-unlocked-orientation-rotates-farm-evidence.md)
- **Adding a cloud-farm device could stop every other device on that host from streaming** — the ffmpeg grabber will not start until its source image exists and waits two minutes for it, while a farm slot's image is only written when a session is open (and the pump must never open one, or an idle host bills for every slot). A slot that had never run therefore had no image, and because grabbers start in sequence the one waiting held up all the others — the host went quiet with no error. It was quiet in the worst way: runs still passed, with a black screenshot and no video. The pump now seeds one small placeholder frame at startup, without opening a session, and never overwrites a real frame from an earlier run · [BUG-0141](../bugs/BUG-0141-2026-09-17-new-farm-slot-blocks-every-grabber.md)
- **A device whose name has a space had no name inside the stream service** — `run_ffmpeg.sh` loaded the host `.env` with `source`, and to bash `DEVICE2_NAME=Phone slot 1` is an assignment followed by the command `slot 1`: the variable came out **empty** and the rest of the line was executed (`slot: command not found` in the journal). Streams still ran, so it read as noise for as long as any slot has had a two-word name. It also meant a value in that file was shell — `FOO=a $(cmd)` would run `cmd` as the service user. The loader now reads `KEY=VALUE` literally, keeping only the inline-comment and quote handling `source` legitimately did; verified identical on all 36 keys of a real host `.env` except the three it previously blanked · [BUG-0140](../bugs/BUG-0140-2026-09-17-stream-env-sourced-executes-values-with-spaces.md)
- **A queued API run finished and the caller was never told** — `/server/script/execute` queues a run when the device is busy and hands back a `task_id`, promising to complete it with the run's real result. The run executed, the deployment was marked completed, the script result was written and the lock released — but the task stayed `started` forever, and tasks never expire, so an API client polling it waited on an answer that was never coming. The route reads the waiting task's id out of the deployment's `rerun_payload`, and that column was missing from its `select`. The two paths that resolve the same task on *failure* — the stale-queue sweep and a manual abort — both selected it correctly, so a caller was told when its run was dropped but not when it ran · [BUG-0137](../bugs/BUG-0137-2026-09-17-queued-api-run-never-finishes-its-task.md) · `389df6ce21`
- **Switching workspace kept the target you had picked in the old one — and its lock** — the workspace filter reached the device *lists* (RunTests' picker, QuickTest's dropdown) but never the selection itself, which for every single-target page lives in one place none of that filtering touched. So switching workspace emptied the dropdown of a device that was still named in the Target field, still showed a green **Release**, and was still locked on the host with no UI left to release it from — and **Run** would have executed on it, outside the workspace the user had just switched to. A workspace switch now releases that lock and clears the target and its panels; on RunTests, the selection restored from the previous session is re-checked against the workspace instead of being replayed wholesale · [BUG-0139](../bugs/BUG-0139-2026-09-17-workspace-switch-keeps-the-old-target.md) · `812eb51bc5`
- **A tap on an Appium-driven device was told the button was not there** — `click_element` looked the label up in a list of screen elements that only a previous *dump* ever fills, and nothing in the click path dumped. So on a freshly built controller it searched an empty list and reported "Element not found" for a button plainly on screen, while the verification controller — which takes its own dump — found the same element in the same second. The adb remote never had this (it dumps first and always did), so a locally attached phone was fine and only Appium-driven devices were affected; on a cloud farm device, where there is no adb, every click in a navigation tree failed. It now dumps once per click, before searching · [BUG-0138](../bugs/BUG-0138-2026-09-17-appium-click-searches-an-empty-cache.md) · `5fb30719b7`
- **A UI dump on an Appium-driven device found nothing on a screen that was plainly there** — `dump_elements` parsed the page source with a pattern written for ADB's `uiautomator dump`, where every element is a `<node …>`. Appium names each element after its class instead, so the pattern matched nothing and the dump returned an empty tree — not an error, because a screen with nothing selectable on it is a legitimate answer, so every selector and element verification simply failed to find anything. A locally attached phone is driven over ADB and was never affected, which is why this survived: the broken parser only becomes the only path where there is no ADB. Same screen on a real cloud device, before and after: 0 elements, then 38 · [BUG-0146](../bugs/BUG-0146-2026-09-17-appium-page-source-parsed-as-zero-elements.md) · `f6dc609472`
- **The script API took a device id and ran the script somewhere else** — `POST /server/script/execute` accepts `device_id`, locks that device and files the result against it, but the script process never saw it: it picks its device from `--device`, which defaults to `device1`, so a caller who did not hand-write the flag into `parameters` ran on `device1` — or, on a host that has no `device1`, on the host's own VNC desktop, which is how an Android navigation tree came to be driven through Chromium and recorded as a failed phone run. The route now appends `--device <device_id>` unless the caller already supplied it, the way the scheduler always has; an explicit flag still wins. `goto.py` and `goto.md` also drop a documented positional argument that does not exist — a bare positional is ignored and `--userinterface` keeps its default, so a call copied from the usage line loads the wrong tree and says only that `example_mobile` was not found · [BUG-0135](../bugs/BUG-0135-2026-09-17-script-execute-ignores-device-id.md) · `33e9a03067`
- **The daily fleet-health snapshot had not been written for nine days, and said it was fine every morning** — the timer ran, built its report and uploaded it, then failed the one line that writes the table and exited 0, so systemd recorded a clean run each day while the `vpt-fleet-health` dashboard quietly served nine-day-old data. The write used the anon key on the strength of a comment saying "RLS policy is public", which stopped being true the day anon was locked out — the same day the table went quiet. It now writes as `service_role` and a failed write exits non-zero, because a scheduled job that fails silently and reports success is indistinguishable from a healthy one · [BUG-0133](../bugs/BUG-0133-2026-09-17-fleet-health-stopped-writing-and-reported-success.md) · `ccd610d20a`

- **Typing the server URL left the app in open mode, with no real login and no JWT** — the URL bar on the ThisPhone page accepted any text as a server URL and saved it without validation, leaving the app in open mode with no JWT and every `ProtectedRoute` open to unauthenticated callers. The page now validates the URL format before saving, rejecting anything that is not a valid `https?://` origin, and shows an inline error for an invalid URL · [BUG-0145](../bugs/BUG-0145-2026-09-17-typed-server-url-leaves-the-app-unauthenticated.md)
---

### Security
- **Live updates and agent chat were readable by anyone who could open a socket** — the server's HTTP API is closed by default, but none of that applied to its realtime connection, which is served on a different path and so never passed the gate. Anything that could reach the server could therefore open a socket and listen: the fleet's live state — every host, every device, who holds which lock, what is deploying — and, worse, an agent conversation. Conversation rooms were addressed by id and joined on request without a check, so a client that learned a session id received that chat's whole stream (prompts, tool calls, results) and could act in it: send messages as that person, approve an action the agent was waiting on, or wipe the history. The connection is now authenticated at the handshake, with exactly the credentials the page already sends on every ordinary request and the same rules — a deployment that is closed to the web is no longer open over its socket — and a conversation may only be joined or driven by the person it belongs to · [BUG-0156](../bugs/BUG-0156-2026-09-22-socketio-namespaces-accept-any-client.md)
- **Five endpoints accepted anything that reached them; now they carry a credential** — `/server/*` is closed by default behind an allowlist of paths reachable without logging in, and that allowlist is the server's entire public surface. Every entry is supposed to either return nothing worth having (the health probes, the pre-login auth check) or prove who it is with a credential of its own — an MCP bearer, the CI ingest token, the streaming session cookie. Five did neither. The four completion callbacks a host uses to report a finished run were open because, when they were written, a host had no way to identify itself; it has had one for a long time, and register, ping, unregister and the execution events all use it, but the callbacks were never moved over. So anything that could reach the server could report a run finished that was not: releasing the device for someone else to take, ending the run on screen, attaching a report of its choosing — and, for a campaign, handing the server a web address to call afterwards. The fifth was the Slack webhook, which the docs recorded as signature-verified and which verified nothing, so a message could be pushed into an agent chat session by anyone who copied Slack's event shape. All five now authenticate, and the allowlist is pinned by a test that fails on any new entry until someone writes down what guards it · [BUG-0155](../bugs/BUG-0155-2026-09-22-host-callbacks-and-slack-webhook-accept-forged-requests.md)
- **A security gate that had been reported as shipped was not actually running** — the VNC and stream session gate (BUG-0107 Steps 2-3, deployed 2026-09-16) was gone from the live proxy vhost four days later: no `auth_request` anywhere in it, leaving the earlier two-address IP allowlist as the only thing between the open internet and a device's remote desktop. The application half had never broken — the authorize endpoint answered correctly, the signing secret resolved, and the shipped frontend bundle still minted the cookie — so every check that reads the code said the gate was healthy, and this file and the bug report both said it had shipped, which is why the nginx config was the last place anyone looked. It surfaced as an operator getting a bare `403` on consoles seemingly at random; the randomness was the operator changing networks, since only two egress addresses were admitted and a refusal renders as a blank nginx error page rather than as "you are not allowed". Re-deployed in both vhosts as a single server-level directive, which also moves it out of the root-owned locations snippet that a redeploy can overwrite, and verified from an address that is deliberately *not* allowlisted: consoles answer `401` with no session and `200` with the service key, app root and static assets unchanged. The IP allowlist is retired from the request path rather than widened, so the cookie gate now carries this alone. **Not fixed by this entry:** the second environment serves the same `/host/<name>/…` surface through a different proxy and has no gate at all — it answered `200` with no credential on 2026-09-20 — and cannot take this change unchanged, because the platform embedding those streams never mints a session · [BUG-0107](../bugs/BUG-0107-2026-09-15-vnc-console-and-websockify-open-to-the-internet.md)
- **The security dashboard stops claiming it scans Python dependencies for known CVEs** — it listed `backend_host/requirements.txt` and `backend_server/requirements.txt` as covered by Safety, and that scan never ran anywhere: the tool was declared in a requirements file the frontend VM does not install (that VM has no virtualenv at all — its bandit is a Debian package), and on a machine that *did* have it the output was written to a temp file no parser ever opened and deleted at the end of the run. A skipped step and a discarded result look identical from outside — no card — which is why the gap survived a warning printed on every single build. Safety is removed and the claim with it; the page now says plainly that Python dependency CVEs are **not** scanned. `pip-audit` was integrated and tested as the replacement and deliberately backed out: the VM has no venv, Debian 12 refuses system `pip` installs, and there is no apt package for it, so it could only arrive via pipx or a purpose-built venv — more machinery than this page justifies today. Its one real run is worth recording, since the gap is not hypothetical: four vulnerable packages across the two files, including `pillow 10.4.0` with 33 advisories whose fix lives in 12.x, held open by the `<11.0.0` ceiling in our own requirements file. **Nothing about those packages changed in this release** · [BUG-0148](../bugs/BUG-0148-2026-09-17-python-dependency-cves-never-scanned.md)
- **Any logged-in user could read another team's data by editing the address bar** — every table that matters is separated by a team id, and every query dutifully filtered on it, but the value came from the caller: 140 endpoints read it from the query string and nothing checked it belonged to them. Demonstrated on our own deployment — an account in one team fetched another team's test results, and got them. Filtering by a team id is not isolation unless something validates the team id first, so the check now happens once, centrally, for every endpoint at once rather than in 140 places: name a team you are not in and the answer is a refusal. Which teams you belong to now travels in your session, kept current whether you are moved between teams or added to a second one. Administrators and internal service traffic are unaffected · 🗄 DB migration `20260917g_team_ids_in_app_metadata.sql`
- **A logged-in user could make itself an administrator** — the browser's Postgres role held `UPDATE` on *every column* of `profiles`, including `role`, under a policy (`uid() = id OR is_admin()`) that restricts which **row** you may edit but not which **column**. The row you may edit is your own, and a trigger copies `profiles.role` into the JWT claim the backend trusts — so one `PATCH /supabase/rest/v1/profiles` promoted any account to platform admin over a live device fleet, no server involved. Public signup was already enabled and auto-confirming, so this was reachable by anyone on the internet, not just by an invited tester. `authenticated` is now `SELECT`-only; profile and membership edits go through the server on the service role, where `/server/users` is already admin-gated. The SPA never used those write grants — all three of its direct table calls are reads — so nothing in the product changes. Verified live: an account signed up through the public endpoint reads its own profile (`200`) and is refused on `PATCH role=admin` and on writing any other table (`403`, `42501`) · 🗄 DB migration `20260917c_authenticated_read_only.sql`
- **A table on the second environment was writable by anyone, with no login** — `userinterface_publishes` carried full insert/update/delete for the anonymous browser key on the QualiAI deployment, while the *same table on the main deployment* carried nothing of the sort.
